# Verifier Data — Full Reasoning for Every Claim

_The complete, untruncated adversarial-verifier verdict for all 283 claims raised during the gap analysis (273 confirmed incl. 4 completeness-critic finds, 10 refuted). This is the raw per-claim reasoning that `GAP-REPORT.md` summarizes. One claim (KMS org service agent) has a stub `test` verifier explanation — flagged inline._

## Refuted claims — full reasoning (why these are NOT gaps)

### [BOTH] Dedicated/Partner Interconnect support missing entirely
_Area: 3-networks · claimed severity: major · verifier confidence: high_

**Claim:** TF ships 3-networks-{svpc,hub-and-spoke}/modules/dedicated_interconnect and partner_interconnect (VLAN attachments, interconnect routers) and base_env supports enable_dedicated_interconnect / enable_partner_interconnect toggles that (a) add a VPC-SC egress policy to the interconnect project for the networks SA and (b) switch the BGP ASN to 16550 for partner interconnect. The Go port has no interconnect components (no InterconnectAttachment anywhere in the port or pulumi-library/go; BGP ASN hardcoded to 64514 in loadNetConfig) even though 1-org still creates the prj-net-interconnect project. TS port/library also has no InterconnectAttachment.

**Verifier (refuted):** TF side confirmed: 3-networks-{svpc,hub-and-spoke}/modules/dedicated_interconnect and partner_interconnect are real modules creating google_compute_interconnect_attachment resources (VLAN attachments) tied to Cloud Routers, and modules/base_env/main.tf:18-35 does contain enable_dedicated_interconnect/enable_partner_interconnect-gated VPC-SC egress policy and bgp_asn_number = 16550 switch (both variables default to false).

But the claim's central assertion -- 'no InterconnectAttachment anywhere in the port or pulumi-library/go' and 'missing entirely' -- is false. Both ports ship complete, ready-to-activate scaffolding that uses the actual GCP InterconnectAttachment resource, field-for-field matching the TF modules:
- Go: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/interconnect.go.example and partner_interconnect.go.example, plus pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/interconnect.go.example -- all call compute.NewInterconnectAttachment with CandidateSubnets, VlanTag8021q, Type DEDICATED/PARTNER, EdgeAvailabilityDomain, exactly mirroring the TF resource.
- TS: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/{interconnect,partner_interconnect}.ts.example and 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/{interconnect,partner_interconnect}.ts.example -- same pattern using gcp.compute.InterconnectAttachment.
The reviewer's grep of only '*.go' files missed these because the extension is '.go.example'/'.ts.example', not '.go'/'.ts' -- a search-methodology gap, not a real code gap. This mirrors TF's own pattern of shipping the wiring/invocation as *.tf.example / *.auto.tfvars.example files requiring rename-and-customize (real onprem DC names, VLAN tags, interconnect URLs must be filled in either way); only the underlying module logic is committed non-example code upstream, and Pulumi's per-file example achieves the same net effect (working code, gated behind an explicit activation step).

What IS genuinely missing in both ports: the automatic side effects wired to the TF toggles -- (1) no config flag equivalent to enable_dedicated_interconnect/enable_partner_interconnect that auto-adds a VPC-SC egress policy for the interconnect project to the networks SA (VpcScEgressPolicies is never populated from config in either port's 3-networks main file), and (2) BGP ASN is hardcoded to 64514 in both Go (3-networks-svpc/main.go:413-414, hub-and-spoke:538-539) and effectively unconfigurable in TS, with no switch to 16550 for partner interconnect. Additionally the Go port's hub-and-spoke stage only ships interconnect.go.example (dedicated) and is missing a partner_interconnect.go.example, whereas the TS port has both for hub-and-spoke -- an asymmetry between the two ports.

However this narrower gap does not support 'major' severity or 'missing entirely': the feature is opt-in and disabled by default in upstream TF itself (both enable_* variables default false, and even the module invocation must be activated from a .tf.example), so a Pulumi port that requires the same opt-in activation is not diverging from default behavior. Both ports also self-document this in their README ('Networking: Interconnect and VPN' section) and ERRATA.md as an intentional scope trade-off with pointers on where to extend. The remaining real gap (auto egress-policy + ASN-switch convenience logic, and Go's missing hub-and-spoke partner example) is at most a minor/cosmetic parity gap, not a major one.

**Correction:** Refuted as stated. InterconnectAttachment resources DO exist in both ports (interconnect.go.example/partner_interconnect.go.example in Go; interconnect.ts.example/partner_interconnect.ts.example in TS), matching the TF modules field-for-field -- the reviewer's grep pattern '*.go' simply didn't match '*.go.example' files. The real, narrower gap is: (1) no config-driven equivalent to enable_dedicated_interconnect/enable_partner_interconnect that auto-injects the VPC-SC egress policy for the interconnect project and (2) BGP ASN is hardcoded to 64514 with no 16550 switch for partner interconnect, plus (3) Go's hub-and-spoke stage lacks a partner_interconnect.go.example that the TS port does have. Since the entire feature is opt-in and disabled-by-default in upstream TF too, and the bulk of the functionality (the actual attachment resources) is present as activatable example code in both ports, severity should be downgraded from 'major' to minor/informational, not 'missing entirely'.

### [TS] Empty stub BaseEnv component modules (dead placeholders)
_Area: 3-networks-svpc / 4-projects · claimed severity: info · verifier confidence: high_

**Claim:** 3-networks-svpc/modules/base_env/index.ts and 4-projects/modules/base_env/index.ts define ComponentResources whose constructors create nothing (the latter contains the comment "It should orchestrate single_project, peering, etc." followed by registerOutputs({})). They are imported by nothing — the real logic lives inline in env stacks — but they are leftover unfinished scaffolding that misleadingly mirrors TF's modules/base_env.

**Verifier (refuted):** The claim is not describing a missing-functionality gap but confirmed dead/unused scaffolding, and the reviewer already labeled it correctly as such (severity: info). I verified every part of the evidence:

1. TF side: terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/base_env/ and 4-projects/modules/base_env/ both exist with substantial real logic (main.tf, business_unit_folder.tf, example_shared_vpc_project.tf, example_peering_project.tf, etc.) — confirmed.

2. TS side: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/base_env/index.ts and 4-projects/modules/base_env/index.ts are exactly as described — empty ComponentResource constructors ending in registerOutputs({}), with the 4-projects one literally containing the comment "It should orchestrate single_project, peering, etc." Confirmed byte-for-byte.

3. Grepped the entire TS port for `BaseEnv` and `base_env` — zero imports/usages anywhere outside the two stub files themselves. The only other `base_env` string hits are unrelated comments in 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/*/index.ts referencing a TF file path, not this module.

4. Traced where the real functionality lives: 3-networks-svpc/envs/production/index.ts directly instantiates `SharedVpc` from the pulumi-library, and 4-projects/business_unit_1/production/index.ts directly calls `deploySingleProject` and creates folders inline — fully bypassing BaseEnv. This confirms the reviewer's own description that "the real logic lives inline in env stacks."

5. Checked pulumi-library/ts for any BaseEnv equivalent — none exists (not needed, since logic is inline).

6. Confirmed no package.json, Pulumi.yaml, or barrel/index file references these two files; they are unreachable dead code that doesn't even affect the build/deploy since they're never imported.

Since functionality equivalent to TF's base_env module (shared VPC + DNS + router orchestration, and single_project + peering + folder orchestration) genuinely exists and works in the TS port, just implemented inline in env-stack files rather than as a reusable BaseEnv component, there is no functional gap. The finding is accurately characterized as leftover/misleading dead code, not missing functionality — hence isRealGap=false with severity info being the correct classification, matching what the reviewer already assessed.

### [GO] Gcloud ignores DestroyCmdBody (and ServiceAccountKeyFile/ProjectID) — no destroy-time command runs
_Area: pulumi-library/gcloud · claimed severity: minor · verifier confidence: high_

**Claim:** The TF gcloud module runs both create_cmd_* and destroy_cmd_* (foundation uses it in 0-bootstrap build_cb.tf, 1-org iam.tf for the KMS service-agent creation, and 4-projects image build). The Go component only sets local.Command Create; DestroyCmdBody, ServiceAccountKeyFile, and ProjectID are accepted but never referenced, so destroy-time cleanup silently never happens. TS wires destroyCmdBody to the command's delete.

**Verifier (refuted):** The claim mischaracterizes the TF baseline and misidentifies where the port's actual behavior lives.

1) TF premise is false: an exhaustive `grep -ri destroy_cmd` across the ENTIRE terraform-example-foundation repo returns zero matches. None of the three cited TF call sites (1-org/envs/shared/iam.tf:69-79 `create_kms_organization_service_agent`, 0-bootstrap/build_cb.tf:187-219 `bootstrap_csr_repo`/`build_terraform_image`, and 4-projects/business_unit_1/shared/example_infra_pipeline.tf:146-161 `build_confidential_space_image`) set destroy_cmd_body/destroy_cmd_entrypoint at all — every one of them only sets create_cmd_*. So there is no destroy-time behavior in TF for any of the cited usages to fail to port.

2) The Go port doesn't even route these three cases through the shared pulumi-library Gcloud component: `grep -rln "pkg/gcloud\|NewGcloud\|GcloudArgs"` across pulumi_go-example-foundation returns nothing except the library's own go.work file — the component is never imported/instantiated anywhere in the port. Instead: the KMS org service-agent case (1-org/iam.go ~L203-220) is implemented via the native `gcp.projects.NewServiceIdentity` resource (no shell-out, no destroy semantics needed, matching upstream which also never destroys it); the CSR push/build-trigger case (0-bootstrap build_cb.tf) is replaced by an entirely different CI/CD architecture (build_github_actions.go, with build_cloud_build.go.example as an alternate), not a literal port of the CSR+gcloud workflow; and the confidential-space image build (4-projects) has no gcloud/Command invocation anywhere in the Go port's example_infra_pipelines.go or confidential_space.go.

3) The TS 'counterpart' cited as doing it right (ts/packages/gcloud/src/index.ts) is likewise never imported by pulumi_ts-example-foundation (same grep pattern, only the library file itself matches) — the actual TS KMS-agent code (1-org/iam.ts ~L194-197) uses a raw `command.local.Command` with only `create` set, no `delete`, mirroring TF's own behavior (no destroy). So TS doesn't actually demonstrate 'destroy-time cleanup' for this scenario either; the asymmetry is confined to an unused library component's field list, not to any executed code path.

So the underlying observation that Go's gcloud.go accepts DestroyCmdBody/ServiceAccountKeyFile/ProjectID but never references them is technically true (dead/inert fields in a component that is itself unused anywhere in either port's stage code), but the load-bearing claim that this causes 'destroy-time cleanup [to] silently never happen' for 0-bootstrap build_cb.tf, 1-org iam.tf KMS creation, or 4-projects image build is false, because (a) TF never runs a destroy command in any of those three cases, and (b) the port doesn't implement those three features via this component in the first place.

**Correction:** Not a real functional gap. The TF reference never sets destroy_cmd_body in any of the three cited files (repo-wide grep confirms zero destroy_cmd usages in terraform-example-foundation), so there is no destroy-time behavior to lose. Additionally, the shared pulumi-library Gcloud component (Go or TS) is never actually invoked anywhere in either port's stage code for these three scenarios — the KMS org service-agent is done via a native `gcp.projects.ServiceIdentity`/plain `command.local.Command` (no delete set, matching TF), the CSR-push/build-trigger flow is replaced by a different CI/CD architecture, and the confidential-space image build has no gcloud/Command call in the Go port at all. At most this is a very-minor dead-code observation about unused struct fields in a component with zero real callers, not a case of 'silently missing destroy-time cleanup' in shipped foundation behavior.

### [GO] Go port has no policy pack implementation — policy-library/ contains only a README pointing at the TS repo
_Area: policy-library · claimed severity: major · verifier confidence: high_

**Claim:** TF's policy-library (112 files: OPA constraints + templates) is validated against every stage plan in every integration test via tft.WithPolicyLibraryPath (gcloud terraform vet). The Go port's policy-library/ directory contains only README.md, which documents 7 CrossGuard policies and instructs users to clone the TypeScript repo to get the actual pack. There is no policy pack code in the Go repo and nothing in the Go repo (Makefile, CI workflows, deploy.sh, gitlab-ci.yml) invokes `pulumi preview --policy-pack`, so the preventive-control validation layer is entirely absent from the Go port.

**Verifier (refuted):** TF-side citation checks out exactly: terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/ has 112 files (policies/constraints, policies/templates, lib/), and tft.WithPolicyLibraryPath is used in 8 integration test files including test/integration/bootstrap/bootstrap_test.go:84.

But the claim that the Go port's equivalent is "entirely absent" is refuted by evidence in the sibling repo the reviewer was told to check. pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts contains a real, non-stub CrossGuard PolicyPack with 7 fully implemented `validateResource` policies (no-default-network, project-labels-required, no-sa-key-creation, no-public-access, no-public-ip, require-private-google-access, require-flow-logs) that map directly onto the OPA constraint categories the TF policy-library encodes, keyed off actual GCP resource types (gcp:organizations/project:Project, gcp:compute/subnetwork:Subnetwork, etc.). The Go port's policy-library/README.md is not empty vaporware — it correctly documents that Pulumi's CrossGuard/Policy SDK has no Go-authoring surface (policies must be written in TypeScript or Python), so the pack is implemented once in the TS repo and applied to any stack — Go or TS — via `pulumi preview --policy-pack <path>`, which is architecturally how CrossGuard is designed to work (the pack inspects the resource-registration protocol, independent of the source language of the program being previewed). This is the same cross-repo sharing pattern the task setup calls out for pulumi-library, just applied to the policy pack instead.

The one genuinely accurate residual observation is that neither port wires `--policy-pack` into an automated CI/build gate: grep across .github/workflows, Makefile, build/gitlab-ci.yml, and deploy.sh in BOTH the Go and TS ports found zero invocations of `--policy-pack`. Both ports' docs/ERRATA.md explicitly acknowledge this ("Does not include policy validation infrastructure... Pulumi does not have a direct equivalent of terraform vet"). So the "not automated as a preventive gate in CI" gap, to the extent it's real, is shared equally by both ports — it is not a Go-specific deficiency, undercutting the claim's framing and its "major, Go-only" severity.

**Correction:** The claim conflates two different things. (1) "No policy pack code exists for the Go port" is false — a complete, working 7-policy CrossGuard pack exists in pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts, and is intentionally shared rather than duplicated because Pulumi's Policy SDK only supports authoring in TypeScript/Python, not Go; the Go port's README correctly documents how to point `pulumi preview --policy-pack` at it. That is an equivalent-behavior-implemented-differently case, not a missing feature. (2) The real, narrower gap — that no CI workflow in either port automatically runs `pulumi preview --policy-pack` as a merge/PR gate (the terraform-vet-in-CI equivalent) — does exist, but it affects the TS port identically (verified via grep of its own workflows/Makefile/gitlab-ci.yml, and its own ERRATA.md admission), so it is not a Go-specific gap and does not warrant "major" severity scoped to "Affected port: go." At most this should be filed as a minor/informational cross-port item about CI automation, not as a Go-port-specific missing feature.

### [TS] No TS equivalents for Go-only components: cicd (CloudBuild/GitHub/GitLab triggers + Artifact Registry + CSR), app (CloudRunApp), data (DataPlatform)
_Area: pulumi-library/cicd · claimed severity: info · verifier confidence: high_

**Claim:** The Go library ships pkg/cicd (Cloud Build plan/apply trigger pairs for CSR/GitHub/GitLab plus builder Artifact Registry, mirroring tf_cloudbuild_source/builder/workspace usage), pkg/app, and pkg/data. The TS library has none of these; the TS port covers CI/CD inline in 0-bootstrap build files (build_cb.ts.example etc.), and app/data have no TF counterpart and are unused by the Go port. Functional coverage exists on both sides for the foundation flows, so this is component-inventory drift rather than a deployment gap.

**Verifier (refuted):** Confirmed the TF side: 0-bootstrap/build_cb.tf really does wire up tf_cloudbuild_source, tf_cloudbuild_builder, and tf_cloudbuild_workspace (plan/apply trigger pairs per stage) plus an Artifact Registry, and 4-projects/infra_pipelines uses tf_cloudbuild_workspace too. Confirmed pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd/{cloudbuild.go,github.go,gitlab.go} exist and implement CloudBuild trigger pairs (CSR/GitHub/GitLab sources) + Artifact Registry, GitHub OIDC/WIF, and GitLab OIDC/WIF respectively, and that pulumi-library/go/pkg/app and pkg/data exist with no matches under pulumi-library/ts/packages.

Crucially, I traced actual usage in the Go port: only pkg/cicd/github.go's NewGitHubOIDC is used in the active default path (0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.go). The CloudBuild-trigger component (cloudbuild.go) and the GitLab component (gitlab.go) are only invoked from non-default, opt-in example files: build_cloud_build.go.example and build_gitlab.go.example. pkg/app and pkg/data are listed in go.work but are imported by zero .go files anywhere in the Go port (grep for 'pkg/app'/'pkg/data' outside pulumi-library returns nothing) and have no Terraform counterpart (CloudRunApp and BigQuery DataPlatform are not part of the foundation architecture at all).

On the TS side, I verified the 'inline' claim is functionally real, not hand-waved: build_github_actions.ts (default/active) inlines the same WIF pool/provider/per-SA bindings/GitHub secrets that Go's github.go component provides; build_cloud_build.ts.example inlines CSR repo creation plus real per-stage plan/apply gcp.cloudbuild.Trigger resources plus Artifact Registry (functionally matching Go's cloudbuild.go, just not factored into a reusable library package); build_gitlab.ts.example inlines the GitLab WIF pool/provider equivalent to Go's gitlab.go. README-CloudBuild.md exists in both ports with parallel switch-over instructions. So the CI/CD functional surface is present in TS, just duplicated inline in each *.ts.example file instead of factored into a shared pulumi-library/ts package.

This matches the reviewer's own characterization: it is real component-inventory drift (TS lacks reusable pkg/cicd, pkg/app, pkg/data packages) but not a deployment/functional gap, since (a) the equivalent CI/CD behavior is fully implemented inline on the TS side for all three provider options (GitHub Actions default, Cloud Build alternative, GitLab alternative), and (b) app/data are dead weight on the Go side too (unused, no TF counterpart), so there is nothing for TS to be "missing" there. The claimed severity of 'info' is well calibrated and needs no correction — if anything the only legitimate follow-up would be a DRY/maintainability nit (TS reimplements the same project/API/CSR/trigger boilerplate in each example file rather than sharing a package), but that is a code-quality observation, not a functional deficiency.

**Correction:** Confirmed as info-only inventory drift, not a functional gap; no correction needed to the claim's own conclusion. The only refinement worth noting: this was independently verified rather than assumed — the TS build_cloud_build.ts.example and build_gitlab.ts.example were checked line-by-line and do create real gcp.cloudbuild.Trigger plan/apply pairs and WIF pool/provider/OIDC resources, so functional parity is substantiated, not just asserted.

### [BOTH] Storage bucket constraints missing: uniform bucket-level access; public-bucket check only partial
_Area: policy-library · claimed severity: major · verifier confidence: high_

**Claim:** TF enforces storage_bucket_policy_only.yaml (GCPStorageBucketPolicyOnlyConstraintV1 — buckets must enable uniform bucket-level access / bucketPolicyOnly, severity high) and storage_denylist_public.yaml (GCPStorageBucketWorldReadableConstraintV1 — no allUsers/allAuthenticatedUsers access). The port has no check on gcp:storage/bucket:Bucket uniformBucketLevelAccess at all, and its public-access check covers only gcp:storage/bucketIAMMember:BucketIAMMember — not BucketIAMBinding, BucketIAMPolicy, BucketAccessControl or DefaultObjectAccessControl, all of which can make a bucket world-readable.

**Verifier (refuted):** The claim's line-level observations are accurate but its conclusion — that the ports are missing UBLA and public-bucket enforcement — is wrong, because equivalent (and stronger) enforcement exists elsewhere in both ports. TF side confirmed: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/policies/constraints/storage_bucket_policy_only.yaml and storage_denylist_public.yaml exist and are consumed via 'gcloud beta terraform vet' (build/tf-wrapper.sh:278). And yes, the shared CrossGuard pack (/home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts; the Go port's policy-library README defers to it, and /home/user/pulumi-library has no policy pack) has no uniformBucketLevelAccess check and its no-public-access iamTypes includes only gcp:storage/bucketIAMMember:BucketIAMMember for storage (line 75). HOWEVER, both ports deploy org-wide GCP organization policies enforcing exactly these two behaviors, mirroring TF's own 1-org/envs/shared/org_policy.tf:40-41: Go port at /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/policies.go:63-64 (policy.NewOrgPolicy, boolean enforced) and TS port at /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:77-78 (OrgPolicyBoolean). 'constraints/storage.uniformBucketLevelAccess' forces UBLA on all new buckets (and disables ACLs, mooting BucketAccessControl/DefaultObjectAccessControl), and 'constraints/storage.publicAccessPrevention' blocks allUsers/allAuthenticatedUsers grants server-side via EVERY mechanism the claim lists (IAMBinding, IAMPolicy, ACLs) — coverage the TF vet check itself doesn't exceed. Additionally, the shared library's bucket components default UBLA to true (/home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/cloud-storage/src/index.ts:44) or hardcode UBLA=true plus publicAccessPrevention=enforced (/home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/storage/storage.go:73-74). In TF the two vet constraints are a redundant plan-time pre-check of what its org policies (also ported) already enforce; the ports preserve the enforced outcome, losing only part of the redundant shift-left layer.

**Correction:** The real residual gap is only defense-in-depth/DX, not enforcement: the CrossGuard pack lacks a preview-time UBLA check and only covers BucketIAMMember among storage IAM types, so such violations surface at apply time (rejected by the org policies both ports deploy in 1-org) instead of at preview time as TF's 'terraform vet' would. Severity is minor, not major.

### [GO] VPN-HA module missing from Go library and port (TS library ships vpn-ha)
_Area: 3-networks · claimed severity: minor · verifier confidence: high_

**Claim:** TF ships 3-networks-*/modules/vpn-ha and 0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/vpn_ha.tf as the documented on-prem HA-VPN connectivity option. The TS library has packages/vpn-ha; the Go library and Go port contain no VPN resources at all (grep 'vpn' finds nothing in pulumi-library/go/pkg or pulumi_go-example-foundation Go sources), so the on-prem VPN option has no Go building block — Go/TS drift.

**Verifier (refuted):** TF side confirmed: 3-networks-svpc/modules/vpn-ha, 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/vpn-ha, and 0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/vpn_ha.tf exist, and the top-level vpn.tf.example files show it's an optional, manually-activated add-on (module 'shared_vpn' { source = "../vpn-ha" }) rather than something wired into the default deployment.

The Go port DOES contain the equivalent building block: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/vpn.go.example and .../3-networks-hub-and-spoke/vpn.go.example (135 lines each) implement HaVpnGateway (x2, one per region), ExternalVpnGateway (on-prem peer, TWO_IPS_REDUNDANCY), and VPNTunnel resources with BGP/Secret-Manager-PSK wiring — a direct, resource-for-resource analog of TF's vpn-ha module and of the TS port's own /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/vpn.ts.example (208 lines, same HaVpnGateway/ExternalVpnGateway/VPNTunnel pattern). Both ports mirror TF's own '.example, rename to activate' convention (Go: interconnect.go.example, partner_interconnect.go.example, vpn.go.example all sit alongside main.go).

The claim's evidence ('grep -rli vpn over pulumi_go-example-foundation/**/*.go returns nothing') has a methodology bug: the glob **/*.go does not match files ending in .go.example, so it necessarily misses vpn.go.example — verified directly (ls *.go vs ls *.go.example in 3-networks-svpc shows the VPN file only under the .example extension). A plain grep -rli vpn over the whole repo (no extension filter) immediately surfaces it, plus 0-bootstrap/onprem.md (which documents the exact activation steps for both 'vpn.ts.example → vpn.ts (TypeScript) or vpn.go.example → vpn.go (Go)') and docs/ERRATA.md.

One genuine nuance: pulumi-library/go/pkg has no standalone componentized 'vpn_ha' package mirroring pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpn-ha's VpnHa ComponentResource class — that structural asymmetry is real. But it is much less significant than claimed: the TS library's VpnHa component is itself not consumed anywhere in the TS port's actual code (grep for 'VpnHa'/'foundation-vpn-ha' imports in pulumi_ts-example-foundation/**/*.ts returns nothing) — it's declared as a package.json dependency but the port's real vpn.ts.example reimplements the same gcp.compute.* calls inline rather than importing it. So functionally the two ports are at parity (both ship an inline, equivalent, opt-in VPN example); the only difference is that TS additionally has an orphaned/unused library package that Go lacks a counterpart to.

**Correction:** The on-prem HA-VPN option is NOT missing from the Go port — vpn.go.example in both 3-networks-svpc and 3-networks-hub-and-spoke fully implements HaVpnGateway/ExternalVpnGateway/VPNTunnel, matching TF's optional vpn-ha module and the TS port's own vpn.ts.example line for line in approach. The reviewer's grep evidence was a false negative caused by an extension-restrictive glob (*.go excludes *.go.example). The only real, narrow gap is that pulumi-library/go/pkg lacks a reusable componentized VpnHa package like pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpn-ha — but that TS library package is itself unused/orphaned in the TS port (never imported), so this is a cosmetic library-packaging asymmetry, not a functional capability gap. If reported at all, severity should be trivial/informational, not 'minor' framed as 'no Go building block for on-prem VPN.'

### [TS] VpnHa missing router_advertise_config (custom BGP advertisement) and ike_version — required by cb-private-pool VPN and vpn.tf.example
_Area: pulumi-library/vpn-ha · claimed severity: minor · verifier confidence: high_

**Claim:** terraform-google-vpn//vpn_ha accepts router_advertise_config (0-bootstrap cb-private-pool passes mode=CUSTOM advertising the peered pool IP range) and per-tunnel ike_version/bgp_peer_options. TS VpnHa creates the router with only an ASN — no custom advertise config can be expressed — and tunnels have no ikeVersion. It also derives peerIpAddress by splitting peerBgpSessionRange rather than taking the TF bgp_peer.address, so asymmetric session/peer addressing cannot be expressed. Go has no VPN component at all (the Go port implements HA VPN inline in vpn.go.example), so this is the only library VPN surface.

**Verifier (refuted):** The TF side checks out: 0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/vpn_ha.tf really does set router_advertise_config{mode=CUSTOM, ip_ranges, groups=[ALL_SUBNETS]} and each tunnel has ike_version=2 with bgp_peer.address distinct from bgp_session_range (confirmed via independently-settable vars tunnel0_bgp_peer_address vs tunnel0_bgp_session_range). And pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpn-ha/src/index.ts genuinely only sets router bgp={asn}, never sets ikeVersion on VPNTunnel, and derives peerIpAddress via peerBgpSessionRange.split('/')[0] — the cited code observations are all literally accurate in isolation.

However, tracing actual consumers refutes the claim's premise that this component is "required by" cb-private-pool VPN and vpn.tf.example in the ts port:

1. VpnHa is never imported/used anywhere in either port outside its own test file (confirmed via repo-wide grep across pulumi_ts-example-foundation, pulumi_go-example-foundation, and pulumi-library). It's declared as a package.json dependency in pulumi_ts-example-foundation but has zero import statements anywhere in stage code — effectively orphaned library code today.

2. The ts port's cb-private-pool equivalent (pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool/src/index.ts, consumed by 0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.ts as CbPrivatePool) accepts a `vpnConfiguration` arg but never reads it in the constructor body — it creates zero VPN resources today regardless of enableVpn. So VpnHa isn't actually "required by" cb-private-pool VPN in this port; that feature isn't wired to any VPN component at all (a different, larger gap than the one claimed).

3. The ts port's actual vpn.tf.example equivalent, 3-networks-svpc/modules/vpn.ts.example, does NOT use the library's VpnHa component either — it implements HA VPN inline with raw gcp.compute resources (HaVpnGateway/ExternalVpnGateway/VPNTunnel) attached to pre-existing routers by name, exactly the pattern the claim itself says the Go port uses ("implements HA VPN inline"). Contrary to the claim's assertion that TS relies on the library for this ("so this is the only library VPN surface"), TS also bypasses the library here.

4. The router_advertise_config CUSTOM capability the claim says is unreachable already exists elsewhere in the same ts library: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cloud-router/src/index.ts (CloudRouter) sets advertiseMode/advertisedGroups/advertisedIpRanges on gcp.compute.Router — the exact capability, just exposed through a different, already-existing component rather than through VpnHa.

5. The "asymmetric session/peer addressing cannot be expressed" sub-claim is inaccurate: VpnHa's tunnel config has two independent string fields (bgpSessionRange for the local interface, peerBgpSessionRange for the peer), and the component's own test demonstrates setting them to different values (169.254.0.1/30 vs 169.254.0.2/30) — different local vs peer addresses are expressible today, just via an address-embedded-in-CIDR-string convention rather than a dedicated address field.

6. Missing ikeVersion is inconsequential in practice: GCP's VpnTunnel.ikeVersion API defaults to 2, which is exactly the value the TF module hardcodes (ike_version = 2 in both cb-private-pool/vpn_ha.tf and the 3-networks vpn-ha wrapper module) — omitting the field produces identical resulting behavior.

Net: the isolated code-reading is correct, but the claim's framing — that this is missing, required functionality blocking cb-private-pool VPN or vpn.tf.example in the ts port — is refuted. Neither cited consumer actually routes through VpnHa in the ts port; the equivalent advertise-config capability already exists in the library under CloudRouter; the ikeVersion omission matches the GCP default; and asymmetric addressing is already expressible via the two independent fields present.

**Correction:** Refuted as framed. VpnHa's own field set does lack a router_advertise_config-equivalent and an explicit ikeVersion knob, but (a) VpnHa is not actually invoked by either cited consumer in the ts port — CbPrivatePool ignores its vpnConfiguration arg entirely (no VPN resources created at all), and the port's own vpn.ts.example bypasses the library and implements HA VPN inline, same as the Go port; (b) custom BGP advertisement is already supported elsewhere in the same ts library via the CloudRouter component (bgp.advertisedGroups/advertisedIpRanges, mode CUSTOM); (c) omitting ikeVersion is a no-op since GCP defaults it to 2, matching TF's hardcoded value; and (d) asymmetric peer/local addressing is already expressible via VpnHa's two independent bgpSessionRange/peerBgpSessionRange fields. The real, more severe gap is that CbPrivatePool (ts) never wires vpnConfiguration to any VPN creation logic at all — but that is a different finding from the one claimed, and this claim's "minor" severity for a currently-unused component's incomplete API is, if anything, an overstatement of practical impact.

### [BOTH] _Default sink exclusion in the logging project (exclude_external_logs) has no Pulumi equivalent
_Area: 1-org/log_sinks · claimed severity: major · verifier confidence: high_

**Claim:** Terraform issues a PUT to the Logging API (terracurl_request.exclude_external_logs) adding an exclusion to the logging destination project's _Default sink so logs routed in from the org sinks are not double-ingested into that project's default bucket. Neither port nor either library implements any equivalent (no _Default sink update, exclusion, or API call), so every exported org log is also stored a second time in the logging project's _Default bucket — ongoing duplicate ingestion cost for all org audit logs.

**Verifier (refuted):** The TF resource exists as cited (terracurl_request.exclude_external_logs, 1-org/modules/centralized-logging/main.tf:188-216), and the reviewer is correct that no _Default sink update/exclusion exists in either port or the library. But the conclusion does not follow, because the exclusion is only needed for TF's routing architecture. TF routes org sinks to a bare PROJECT destination (module.destination_project -> log-export//modules/project -> logging.googleapis.com/projects/PROJECT_ID, main.tf:124-131); logs routed to a project destination are re-processed by the destination project's Log Router, so its _Default sink would double-ingest them into the _Default bucket — hence the exclusion. Both Pulumi ports instead route org sinks DIRECTLY to the AggregatedLogs log bucket (Go: pulumi-library/go/pkg/logging/centralized_logging.go:253-306, destination logging.googleapis.com/projects/{p}/locations/{l}/buckets/{bucket}, consumed by pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/logging.go; TS: pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts:88, consumed by pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:328). Per GCP Logging semantics, sink destinations of type log bucket store entries directly in that bucket and are NOT processed by the destination project's sinks (including _Default) — only bare project destinations cause re-processing. Exhaustive greps confirm no bare logging.googleapis.com/projects/ID destination exists anywhere in either port or the library, and no code path recreates the TF project-destination pattern. Therefore the claimed failure mode (org logs stored a second time in the logging project's _Default bucket, ongoing duplicate ingestion cost) cannot occur in either port; the resulting state is equivalent to TF-with-exclusion (_Default bucket holds only the logging project's own logs, which TF's exclusion filter also deliberately retains). This is equivalent behavior implemented differently, which refutes the gap.

**Correction:** Not a functional gap: the _Default exclusion is an artifact of TF's project-destination routing, which the Pulumi ports replace with direct log-bucket routing that bypasses the destination project's _Default sink entirely; no double ingestion occurs. At most this is an informational architecture difference worth a code comment. (Separately and out of scope of this claim: the TS library's CentralizedLogging omits the internal '-la' project-level sink that TF creates, so the logging project's own logs are not routed into AggregatedLogs in the TS port — a different, smaller gap the reviewer did not raise.)

### [TS] test/mocks.ts (setupPulumiMocks) is dead code — no resource-level Pulumi-mock tests exist despite the scaffolding
_Area: test-unit · claimed severity: minor · verifier confidence: high_

**Claim:** The TS port ships a shared mock harness (setupPulumiMocks with pulumi.runtime.setMocks simulating Project/RandomString/Folder outputs) whose header says it 'must be called before importing any Pulumi component modules', but no test file imports it. Consequently there are no unit tests that instantiate any stage program or component and assert on resources — the exact test style the file was built for. The mock also only simulates 4 resource types, suggesting an abandoned plan for resource-level stage tests.

**Verifier (refuted):** The narrow factual claim is true but the substantive conclusion is refuted. Verified: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/test/mocks.ts does export setupPulumiMocks() and grep confirms zero references to it anywhere else in the port (all five test/*.config.test.ts files test config-loading logic via inline vi.mock("@pulumi/pulumi", ...) instead, mirroring the Go port's config_test.go pattern one-for-one — the Go port has no mocks.go and no resource-level tests either, confirming this is the deliberate, consistent cross-port test strategy rather than an abandoned plan unique to TS).

Critically, the claim's core assertion — 'no resource-level Pulumi-mock tests exist ... the exact test style the file was built for' — is false at the project level. /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/test/mocks.ts contains a byte-identical setupPulumiMocks() harness that IS actively imported and used by 26 component test files (network.test.ts, project-factory.test.ts, budget.test.ts, kms.test.ts, cloud-dns.test.ts, etc. — every component package the port consumes). That is precisely the resource-level, Pulumi-mock-driven unit testing style the reviewer describes as missing; it exists and is exercised extensively, just in the shared library repo where the actual resource-composing components live, which is architecturally correct since the port's stage directories only wire config into pre-built library components. The TF reference also has no analogous 'resource-level mock unit test' concept at all (its only unit-style tests are Go helper tests under helpers/foundation-deployer and testutils; actual infra verification is Terratest integration tests against real GCP), so there's no upstream requirement being unmet either.

What remains true is a much smaller, purely cosmetic finding: the port ships an orphaned duplicate copy of the library's mocks.ts that nothing in the port imports. That's dead code / repo hygiene noise, not a missing capability or a testing-coverage gap — the claimed severity framing ('no resource-level tests exist ... abandoned plan') overstates a leftover duplicate file into an implied coverage hole that doesn't exist.

**Correction:** Resource-level Pulumi-mock component tests are not missing — they exist and are actively used via the identical setupPulumiMocks harness in /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/test/mocks.ts, imported by 26+ component test files (network, project-factory, budget, kms, cloud-dns, etc.). The port's own test/mocks.ts is a genuine unused duplicate of that file (confirmed dead code, zero imports), but this is a trivial repo-hygiene nit (stray leftover file), not evidence of missing test coverage or an abandoned testing plan — the Go port shows the same 'config-tests-only-in-the-port, resource-tests-in-the-library' pattern by design, with no mocks.go at all. Severity should be downgraded from 'minor gap' to 'cosmetic/no functional impact' — at most, delete the unused file.

## Confirmed claims — full reasoning

### CRITICAL (31)

#### [TS] Authoritative projectCreator enforcement (org_project_creators) missing
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The TF foundation's core bootstrap security control — an authoritative IAM binding restricting roles/resourcemanager.projectCreator at the org/parent-folder level to only the 5 step service accounts + org admins group (removing all other members) — has no TS equivalent. TF main.tf's comment explicitly calls this enforcement out. The Go port implements it (sa.go section 9, bindParentIAMBinding "org-project-creators"), so this is also Go/TS drift. Without it, any default org-wide projectCreator grants remain in place silently.

**Evidence:** TF: terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/main.tf lines 20-30 + upstream terraform-google-bootstrap v12 main.tf google_organization_iam_binding.project_creator / google_folder_iam_binding.project_creator. Checked: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/index.ts, sa.ts, config.ts and pulumi-library/ts/packages/bootstrap/src/bootstrap.ts — no projectCreator binding anywhere (grep only matches README prose).

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF: terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/main.tf lines 20-30 document the enforcement and line 59 passes org_project_creators (the 5 step SAs) to terraform-google-modules/bootstrap v12, which creates an authoritative org/folder IAM binding for roles/resourcemanager.projectCreator. Go port implements it (0-bootstrap/sa.go lines 346-362, bindParentIAMBinding "org-project-creators" with 5 SAs + org admins group), confirming Go/TS drift. TS port: exhaustive search of pulumi_ts-example-foundation (sa.ts, index.ts, local modules parent-iam-member/parent-iam-remove-role, scripts, all .ts/.sh/.yaml/.json for "Creator", "projectCreator", "IAMBinding", "authoritative") and pulumi-library/ts (including the imported @vitruviansoftware/foundation-bootstrap package, which only manages seed project/bucket/KMS) found no projectCreator binding of any kind. The only authoritative binding in TS bootstrap is the roles/editor removal on seed/cicd projects. The gap is actually slightly worse than claimed: the TS port also never grants projectCreator to the 5 step SAs even non-authoritatively (in TF that grant comes from the same authoritative binding), so pre-existing org-wide projectCreator grants remain AND the pipeline SAs lack an explicit projectCreator grant. Claimed severity (critical) is defensible for a security-foundation port: it silently leaves default/broad projectCreator grants in place, which is precisely the control the TF foundation calls out.

#### [TS] CAI monitoring Cloud Function source code (index.js) missing
_Area: 1-org · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The TS 1-org/cai-monitoring-function directory contains only package.json (main: index.js) — the actual 195-line function implementation shipped by TF (function-source/index.js) and by the Go port (1-org/cai-monitoring-function/index.js) is absent. When enable_cai_monitoring=true, the CAIMonitoring component archives this directory and deploys a Cloud Function with entryPoint caiMonitoring; the build fails because index.js does not exist. Feature is unusable.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/modules/cai-monitoring/function-source/index.js (195 lines). Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/cai-monitoring-function/ contains index.js (6899 bytes) + package.json. TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/cai-monitoring-function/ contains ONLY package.json; consumed by 1-org/index.ts:467 (functionSourcePath: "./cai-monitoring-function") and pulumi-library/ts/packages/cai-monitoring/src/index.ts:166.

**Verifier reasoning:** Fully confirmed. TF (/home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/modules/cai-monitoring/function-source/index.js) has the real 195-line Cloud Function implementation. The Go port (/home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/cai-monitoring-function/) ships an identical index.js (6899 bytes) alongside package.json. The TS port's directory (/home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/cai-monitoring-function/) contains only package.json (524 bytes) — no index.js exists anywhere in the TS repo (find -iname index.js returns nothing), nor is one bundled in pulumi-library/ts (no cai*.js found there either). Git history confirms this isn't a working-tree artifact: the single commit that introduced this directory in the TS repo (fe71529, "feat: add Assured Workload support, IAM/contact modules, and CAI monitoring function") added only package.json, never index.js.

Consumption is exactly as described: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cai-monitoring/src/index.ts builds a gcp.storage.BucketObject via `new pulumi.asset.FileArchive(args.functionSourcePath)` and deploys a gcp.cloudfunctionsv2.Function with `entryPoint: "caiMonitoring"`, `runtime: "nodejs20"`. Without index.js exporting a `caiMonitoring` handler, the archived source is missing the entry file and the Cloud Function build/deploy will fail. 1-org/index.ts:462-473 wires this up, gated behind `if (cfg.enableCaiMonitoring)` (config.ts:97, default false — opt-in via `enable_cai_monitoring`).

The one nuance: since enable_cai_monitoring defaults to false, this doesn't break a default TS deployment — it only breaks the CAI monitoring feature specifically when a user opts in. That's a fair basis for softening "critical" to something like "high" at the overall-stack level, but for the feature itself the claim's own framing ("feature is unusable") is accurate — it's a 100% hard failure with no workaround, not a degraded/partial state.

**Verifier note / correction:** Severity is accurately described for the feature itself (complete, unconditional failure — not inflated), but worth noting the feature is opt-in (enable_cai_monitoring defaults to false), so default/out-of-the-box TS deployments are unaffected; only deployments that explicitly enable CAI monitoring hit this failure.

#### [GO] Duplicate 'access-policy' resource registered twice — pulumi up fails with default config
_Area: 1-org · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Both deployOrgPolicies (called at step 5 of main) and main.go step 9.5 create gcp:accesscontextmanager:AccessPolicy with the identical logical name "access-policy", both gated on cfg.CreateAccessContextManagerPolicy which defaults to TRUE (conf.Get("create_access_context_manager_policy") != "false"). Registering two resources of the same type and name produces a duplicate-URN error, so the 1-org stage fails to deploy out of the box. The TS port creates the policy exactly once (index.ts:482).

**Evidence:** TF: terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/org_policy.tf:153 creates google_access_context_manager_access_policy once. Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/policies.go:159-166 AND pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/main.go:183-191 both call accesscontextmanager.NewAccessPolicy(ctx, "access-policy", ...).

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim fully confirmed by direct inspection.

1) TF baseline: terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/org_policy.tf:153-154 creates exactly one google_access_context_manager_access_policy resource, gated by var.create_access_context_manager_access_policy (a single count-based conditional).

2) Go port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/policies.go:159-166 registers accesscontextmanager.NewAccessPolicy(ctx, "access-policy", ...) inside deployOrgPolicies, which is invoked at step 5 of main() (main.go line ~86). Separately, main.go:181-194 (the '9.5 Access Context Manager Policy' block) registers accesscontextmanager.NewAccessPolicy(ctx, "access-policy", ...) again with the identical logical name, same resource type, same parent/title shape. Both are gated on the identical field cfg.CreateAccessContextManagerPolicy, which is set at main.go:353 via `conf.Get("create_access_context_manager_policy") != "false"` — an unset config key returns "" from conf.Get, so this evaluates to true by default. There is no mutual exclusivity or short-circuit between the two call sites; both execute unconditionally whenever the (default-true) flag is true, producing two RegisterResource calls with identical type+name (URN), which Pulumi rejects as a duplicate resource registration, failing 1-org's deploy out of the box.

3) I checked pulumi-library/go for any component that might centralize this logic and make one of the two call sites a no-op or differently-named resource — no such abstraction exists; both call sites directly invoke the raw gcp:accesscontextmanager:AccessPolicy resource from the pulumi-gcp SDK.

4) TS port confirmed as a control: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:481-490 creates the AccessPolicy exactly once, gated the same way, with no duplicate second registration elsewhere in the file.

Severity: 'critical' is accurate, not inflated — this is not a cosmetic or informational issue; it breaks `pulumi up` for the 1-org stage under default configuration (the flag defaults to creating the policy), which is a core, load-bearing part of stage 1. The reviewer's cited line numbers (policies.go:159-166, main.go:183-191) match the actual file contents exactly.

#### [GO] KMS org service agent never provisioned; org-level IAM binding targets nonexistent principal
_Area: 1-org · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Upstream commit 1edf793 (enable kms centralized key usage tracking) creates the ORG-level KMS service agent via 'gcloud beta services identity create --service cloudkms.googleapis.com --organization <org_id>' before granting roles/cloudkms.orgServiceAgent to service-org-<org_id>@gcp-sa-cloudkms.iam.gserviceaccount.com. The Go port instead calls projects.NewServiceIdentity(Project: OrgKMSProjectID), which creates the PROJECT-level agent (service-<project_number>@...), then grants the org role to the org-level agent that was never created. On an organization where that agent does not yet exist, the organizations.IAMMember 'kms-usage-tracking' fails (principal does not exist) and the 1-org stage deploy fails — and the feature is on by default (conf.Get("enable_kms_key_usage_tracking") != "false"). The TS port does this correctly with a command.local.Command running the org-level gcloud command (iam.ts:194-205), so this is Go-only drift.

**Evidence:** TF: terraform-example-foundation 1-org/envs/shared/iam.tf module.create_kms_organization_service_agent + google_organization_iam_member.kms_usage_tracking (commit 1edf793). Go port: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/iam.go:216-229 (projects.NewServiceIdentity with Project, then grant to service-org-<orgId>@gcp-sa-cloudkms), default-on at /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/main.go:362. Correct TS counterpart: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/iam.ts:194-205.

**Verifier reasoning:** ⚠️ _verifier returned a stub/placeholder explanation during a resume run; finding flagged isRealGap but reasoning text is not substantive. Independently plausible; treat reasoning as unrecorded._ (recorded text: `test`)

#### [TS] CAI monitoring Cloud Function source code (index.js) missing from the TS port
_Area: 1-org/cai-monitoring · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The TS port's cai-monitoring-function/ directory contains only package.json — no index.js. The CAIMonitoring component zips this directory (FileArchive) and deploys it as a nodejs20 function with entryPoint caiMonitoring; with no source file the function build fails, so enabling enable_cai_monitoring makes the deployment fail and the IAM-escalation-detection security feature is undeployable. The Go port ships an index.js byte-identical to the TF function-source.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/modules/cai-monitoring/function-source/index.js (6899 bytes). Go port: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/cai-monitoring-function/index.js (identical). TS port: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/cai-monitoring-function/ contains only package.json; consumed by /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:467 (functionSourcePath "./cai-monitoring-function") and /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/cai-monitoring/src/index.ts:163-167.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified in full. TF reference has function-source/index.js (6899 bytes, nodejs20, entrypoint caiMonitoring); the Go port ships a byte-identical copy (cmp confirms). The TS port's 1-org/cai-monitoring-function/ contains only package.json (whose "main" points to the missing index.js). Exhaustive search of the TS port and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/, excluding node_modules) finds no index.js under any name, no embedded/StringAsset source, and no copy/generation script. The library component (pulumi-library/ts/packages/cai-monitoring/src/index.ts:163-167) archives args.functionSourcePath via FileArchive and deploys it as a nodejs20 function with entryPoint caiMonitoring, so the archive would contain only package.json and the Cloud Function build would fail, failing pulumi up when enable_cai_monitoring is set. Root cause identified: 1-org/.gitignore line 7 has a blanket "*.js" pattern (meant for compiled TS output) that git check-ignore confirms blocks cai-monitoring-function/index.js; git ls-files shows only package.json was ever committed. The gap is real and deploy-breaking for the feature.

**Verifier note / correction:** Severity is high rather than critical: enable_cai_monitoring defaults to false (1-org/config.ts:97), so default deployments succeed; the failure only occurs when the opt-in feature is enabled, at which point the 1-org stack update fails and the IAM-escalation-detection SCC feature is undeployable. Fix is trivial: commit the TF/Go index.js into the TS port with a gitignore negation (!cai-monitoring-function/index.js).

#### [GO] Access Context Manager policy created twice with the same Pulumi resource name — pulumi up fails
_Area: 1-org/org_policy · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The Go port creates the AccessPolicy resource in two places, both gated on the same flag (default true): deployOrgPolicies() creates accesscontextmanager.NewAccessPolicy(ctx, "access-policy", ...) and main.go step 9.5 creates a second accesscontextmanager.NewAccessPolicy(ctx, "access-policy", ...). Two resources of the same type and name produce a duplicate-URN error, so deployment fails whenever create_access_context_manager_policy is enabled (the default). Even with distinct names, GCP allows only one access policy per org, so the second create would fail at the API. Terraform creates it exactly once.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/org_policy.tf (google_access_context_manager_access_policy.access_policy, count=1). Port: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/policies.go:159-166 AND /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/main.go:183-191 — both create name "access-policy" when cfg.CreateAccessContextManagerPolicy (default true, main.go:353).

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified: the Go port registers accesscontextmanager.NewAccessPolicy(ctx, "access-policy", ...) at two call sites in the same 1-org program — policies.go:160 (inside deployOrgPolicies, invoked unconditionally at main.go:84) and main.go:184 (step 9.5, whose output feeds the access_context_manager_policy_id export). Both are gated on the same cfg.CreateAccessContextManagerPolicy flag, which defaults to true (main.go:353: conf.Get("create_access_context_manager_policy") != "false"). Neither call sets a parent resource, so both produce the identical URN (same type gcp:accesscontextmanager/accessPolicy:AccessPolicy, same name "access-policy"), causing a duplicate-URN error that aborts pulumi preview/up under default config. Exhaustive grep of the port and pulumi-library/go found no third definition, build-tag guard, or library component that deduplicates. Terraform side confirmed: org_policy.tf:153-157 creates the policy exactly once via count. The secondary claim also holds: GCP allows one access policy per org, so even distinct Pulumi names would fail at the API. Claimed severity "critical" is justified — 1-org fails to deploy with defaults.

**Verifier note / correction:** Framing nuance only: this is a duplicate-resource correctness bug, not missing functionality. The fix is to remove one of the two creations (the policies.go one, since main.go's instance feeds the export), not to add anything.

#### [TS] Default compute service account is never deprivileged on any 1-org project
_Area: 1-org/projects · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform sets default_service_account = "deprivilege" on all seven 1-org project-factory modules, stripping the Editor role from each project's default compute SA. The TS ProjectFactory component supports a defaultServiceAccount arg, but index.ts never passes it for any project, so every project (logging, billing-export, kms, secrets, interconnect, scc, net-hub, 3x shared-vpc) keeps a privileged default SA — a security control silently absent. (Additionally, the library's deprivilege implementation is a destructive empty authoritative IAMBinding on roles/editor rather than removing only the default SA grant, so even wiring it would misbehave.) The Go port passes "deprivilege" and its library uses projects.DefaultServiceAccounts correctly.

**Evidence:** TF: default_service_account = "deprivilege" in every module of /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/projects.tf (e.g. lines 41, 75, 109, 144, 178, 212, 247). TS: no defaultServiceAccount in any ProjectFactory call in /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:130-323; component support at /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/project-factory/src/index.ts:39,116-123. Go equivalent: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/projects.go:62 + /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/project/project.go:175-188.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified. TF sets default_service_account = "deprivilege" in all seven 1-org project modules (projects.tf lines 41, 75, 109, 144, 178, 212, 247). In the TS port, none of the ProjectFactory calls in 1-org/index.ts (lines 130-323) pass defaultServiceAccount, and the library component (pulumi-library/ts/packages/project-factory/src/index.ts:116) only acts on the exact string "deprivilege" with no default — so the deprivilege path is dead code. Exhaustive search of the TS port and pulumi-library/ts for DefaultServiceAccounts, deprivilege, compute@developer, roles/editor, and automaticIamGrants found no per-project equivalent; the only gcp.projects.DefaultServiceAccounts usage in the TS port is in 0-bootstrap for the CICD project. The secondary observation is also accurate: the TS library implements deprivilege as an authoritative empty IAMBinding on roles/editor (members: []), which would destructively wipe all editor grants if enabled. The Go port correctly defaults to "deprivilege" (1-org/main.go:424-425, projects.go:62) and its library uses projects.NewDefaultServiceAccounts with DEPRIVILEGE (project.go:175-188).

**Verifier note / correction:** Gap is real but "critical" is inflated. Both TF and the TS port enforce the org policy iam.automaticIamGrantsForDefaultServiceAccounts (TS 1-org/index.ts:74; TF org_policy.tf:38), which prevents the automatic Editor grant to default SAs going forward — so in steady state the missing deprivilege is a lost defense-in-depth/remediation layer, not the sole control. Residual risk: the org policy does not remove already-granted roles, and the TS stack has no dependsOn ordering between the org policies and the projects, so on a fresh deploy the 5 compute-enabled projects (interconnect, net-hub, 3x shared-vpc) could get privileged default SAs that are never remediated; the other projects do not enable compute.googleapis.com and have no default compute SA. Severity is better rated medium/high.

#### [GO] All spoke environments hardcode identical subnet CIDRs — second spoke peering to the hub fails
_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The spoke branch hardcodes 10.8.64.0/18 and 10.9.64.0/18 primaries, 10.26.2.0/23 / 10.27.2.0/23 proxy subnets, and 100.72-75.64.0/18 secondary ranges for every environment stack; there is no config key to override them. In TF each env uses distinct ranges (dev 10.8.64.0/18, nonprod 10.8.128.0/18, prod 10.8.192.0/18, etc.). Because GCP rejects VPC peering when a new peer's subnets overlap those of an already-peered network, deploying the second and third spoke against the shared hub fails. TS varies CIDRs per env directory.

**Evidence:** Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go:234-269 (hardcoded spoke subnets), loadNetConfig has no subnet range config. TF: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/{development,nonproduction,production}/main.tf subnet_primary_ranges differ per env. TS: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/*/index.ts uses 10.0.64/21, 10.0.128/21, 10.0.192/21.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all fronts. TF side (terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/{development,nonproduction,production}/main.tf) genuinely uses distinct per-env subnet_primary_ranges (10.8.64.0/18 & 10.9.64.0/18 for dev, 10.8.128.0/18 & 10.9.128.0/18 for nonprod, 10.8.192.0/18 & 10.9.192.0/18 for prod), distinct subnet_proxy_ranges (10.26.2/4/6.0/23, 10.27.2/4/6.0/23), and distinct subnet_secondary_ranges (100.72/73.{64,128,192}.0/18) — exactly as the reviewer describes.

Go port confirmed broken: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke is a single Pulumi project (one Pulumi.yaml, one main.go) driven by three stack config files (Pulumi.development/nonproduction/production.yaml.example). In main.go's spoke branch (lines ~234-269, matching the cited range), the primary CIDRs (10.8.64.0/18, 10.9.64.0/18), proxy CIDRs (10.26.2.0/23, 10.27.2.0/23) and GKE secondary ranges (100.72/73/74/75.64.0/18) are all literal string constants — identical regardless of which stack (dev/nonprod/prod) is running. loadNetConfig() (same file, lines 470-543) has no config key for any of these — only env, env_code, project ids, regions, domain, policy id, PSC IP, VPC-SC/firewall/DNS knobs. The three *.yaml.example stack files and the README also contain no CIDR override keys. I checked pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking — SubnetArgs.CIDR is purely a caller-supplied string with no per-env derivation logic, so the library isn't secretly supplying differentiation either; the gap is squarely in the port's main.go. (Also worth noting: 3-networks-svpc/main.go in the same repo has the identical hardcoded values, though that stage is out of scope for this claim.)

TS port confirmed to differ per env as claimed: envs/development/index.ts uses 10.0.64.0/21, envs/nonproduction/index.ts uses 10.0.128.0/21, envs/production/index.ts uses 10.0.192.0/21 (plus per-env pscAddress). So the TS port does vary ranges while the Go port does not — this is a genuine cross-port inconsistency, not just a stylistic difference from TF.

One nuance for severity calibration: the claim's causal mechanism ("GCP rejects VPC peering when a new peer's subnets overlap those of an already-peered network") is slightly imprecise. Per GCP's peering-creation semantics, the overlap check that outright blocks peering fires between the two networks being directly peered — the hub's own primary/secondary ranges (10.0.x/10.1.x/100.64-67.x) never overlap any spoke's ranges, so 'gcloud/pulumi up' for the second and third spoke would likely not hard-fail with an explicit peering-creation error. The actual failure mode is that hub-to-spoke routing becomes ambiguous/non-deterministic once two peered-but-not-directly-connected spokes share identical subnet CIDRs (GCP's own docs describe an internal, non-configurable next-hop selection algorithm in this case), and this is compounded by the same file's hub BGP routers advertising RegionalAggregates of the full 10.8.0.0/16 and 10.9.0.0/16 blocks — which is meaningless/broken once all three spokes occupy the identical sub-range within those blocks. So the bug is real and severe (broken/undefined cross-environment routing, defeats the whole point of per-env address planning implied by the hub's own aggregate-route design), just not necessarily a hard deploy-time API rejection as literally described.

**Verifier note / correction:** The mechanism described ("peering fails") is likely slightly overstated — GCP's peering-creation overlap check is pairwise between the two directly-peered networks, and the hub's own ranges never overlap the spokes', so the pulumi/gcloud calls to create the 2nd/3rd spoke peering would probably succeed rather than hard-error. The real-world consequence is silent/non-deterministic routing ambiguity between spokes that share identical CIDRs behind the same hub (GCP resolves same-destination overlaps via an internal, non-configurable algorithm) plus meaningless hub BGP aggregate-route advertisements of 10.8.0.0/16 and 10.9.0.0/16 that now cover all three identical spoke subnets. That's still a critical, ship-blocking networking defect for a multi-env foundation — just via broken/ambiguous traffic routing rather than an outright deploy-time rejection.

_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

(1) TF reference: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/shared_vpc/main.tf does contain `module "peering"` (count = var.mode == "spoke") using the terraform-google-modules network-peering submodule for bidirectional spoke<->hub peering, plus a `data "google_compute_network" "vpc_net_hub"` lookup of the hub network — exactly as cited. (2) Go port: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go:277-301 creates `compute.NewNetworkPeering` twice (spoke-to-hub and hub-to-spoke, with correct depends-on ordering and custom-route export/import flags) plus a DNS peering zone (~line 344), matching the claim. (3) TS port: read the full /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/shared_vpc.ts (337 lines) end to end — `netHubProjectId`/`netHubNetworkSelfLink` are declared in SharedVpcArgs (lines 36-38) and never referenced anywhere in the constructor body; the module creates the network, firewall policy, NAT, DNS zones and PSC, but no gcp.compute.NetworkPeering and no DNS peering zone. (4) Checked pulumi-library (both go/ and ts/) for an equivalent under any name: go/pkg/network_peering has a NewNetworkPeering component, and ts/packages/network-peering (@vitruviansoftware/foundation-network-peering) has an equivalent NetworkPeering ComponentResource — the capability exists in the shared library and is even listed as a dependency in the TS port's root package.json — but grepping the entire pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke tree for 'NetworkPeering'/'network-peering'/'peering' (excluding an unrelated BGP-peer VPN example file and a doc comment) returns zero usages; it is simply never imported or wired into this stage. The only gcp.compute.NetworkPeering usages anywhere in the TS repo are in 4-projects/business_unit_1/*/index.ts, which is project-to-shared-VPC service-project peering for a different stage/purpose, not spoke-to-hub connectivity. Severity assessment: critical is correct, not inflated — in hub-and-spoke mode each environment gets its own independent VPC (unlike SVPC/shared-VPC mode), so peering is the only mechanism giving spokes connectivity to the hub (centralized NAT/egress, DNS forwarding, on-prem access via the hub, etc.). With zero peering resources and no alternative connectivity path wired in, the spoke VPCs in the TS port are genuinely isolated islands — the hub-and-spoke topology does not exist at runtime as claimed.
