The Go port (/home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go:358) reads policy_id with conf.Get (optional) and only creates the vpc_sc.NewVpcServiceControls component when cfg.PolicyID != "" (lines 261-283). All three shipped Pulumi.*.yaml.example files omit policy_id entirely and the README never documents it, so a default-config deployment gets no perimeter and no access levels, silently. This is not compensated elsewhere: the shared library component exists (/home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/vpc_sc/vpc_sc.go, accepts PolicyID as pulumi.StringInput) and the TS port creates the perimeter unconditionally from the org StackReference output (envs/*/index.ts) — the Go port even reads access_context_manager_policy_id from the same org StackReference (main.go:244-255) but only for the export, never for perimeter creation, proving the equivalent wiring was available and unused. However, the secondary sub-claim is refuted: exporting the zero-value accessLevelName/accessLevelDryRunName StringOutputs does NOT fail or panic. In pulumi SDK v3.231.0 (the version in go.mod), a nil OutputState is treated as resolved-and-unknown (go/internal/types.go awaitOnce), and an empirical mock-harness test exporting a zero-value StringOutput completed with no error and no panic — the exports simply resolve as unknown/absent. So the default-config deployment succeeds quietly, which actually makes the silent-skip worse, not a crash.

**Verifier note / correction:** Gap is real but severity is high, not critical, and the runtime-failure half of the claim is wrong. (1) Exporting the uninitialized StringOutputs does not panic or error in SDK v3.231.0 — nil OutputState awaits as unknown and the stack exports are just blank/absent; verified empirically. (2) Out-of-box Terraform creates only a dry-run (monitoring, non-enforcing) perimeter since enforce_vpcsc defaults to false in modules/shared_vpc/variables.tf:182-186; enforcement is opt-in in both implementations. The silently missing control in a default deployment is therefore the dry-run perimeter plus pre-provisioned access levels, not an enforced boundary. The worst realistic case: a Go-port user sets enforce_vpcsc=true but not the undocumented policy_id and still gets no perimeter with no warning. Also note the shipped Go examples omit the required env_code key, so the literal example files fail before this code path; the gap manifests once users fill in only the keys the examples/README surface, which never include policy_id.

#### [GO] BU folder parent resolved from nonexistent '{env}_folder_id' output on the 1-org stack
_Area: 4-projects · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF parents the business-unit folder on the environment folder from 2-environments remote state (local.env_folder_name = environments_env.outputs.env_folder). The Go port reads orgStack output '{env}_folder_id' (e.g. 'development_folder_id'), but the Go 1-org stack exports only common_folder_id/network_folder_id, and Go 2-environments exports the env folder as '{env}_env_folder' (which 4-projects never reads, despite creating the envStack reference). The parent resolves to 'folders/' + empty, so folder creation fails or lands under an invalid parent — the whole stage cannot deploy.

**Evidence:** TF: 4-projects/modules/base_env/business_unit_folder.tf + remote.tf (env_folder_name from environments_env). Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/main.go:57 (GetStringOutput '%s_folder_id' on orgStack); pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/main.go:125-165 exports no per-env folder ids; pulumi_go-example-foundation/2-environments/main.go:63 exports '{env}_env_folder' which 4-projects never consumes.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on every point. TF (4-projects/modules/base_env/remote.tf:28 + business_unit_folder.tf:23) parents the BU folder on the env folder read from 2-environments remote state (outputs.env_folder = google_folder.env.name). The Go port's 4-projects/main.go:57 instead reads '{env}_folder_id' from the 1-org stack, but 1-org (main.go:125-204, folders.go) exports only common_folder_id/network_folder_id (it creates no per-env folders, matching upstream). The env folder is exported by Go 2-environments as '{env}_env_folder' (main.go:63), yet 4-projects uses its envStack reference only for '{env}_env_kms_project_id' (main.go:81) and never reads the folder. No config fallback exists in loadProjectsConfig, and /home/user/pulumi-library contains no component that could supply this stack output. Consequence: GetStringOutput on the missing output errors at 'pulumi up' (or resolves empty to parent 'folders/' in preview), so BU folder creation fails and deployBusinessUnitProjects (main.go:88) which depends on buFolderID cannot run — the whole 4-projects stage fails to deploy. The TS port correctly reads 'env_folder' from the environments stack (business_unit_1/*/index.ts:21), confirming the defect is Go-specific. Critical severity is justified; only trivial nuance is that modern Pulumi SDKs hard-error on the missing output rather than concatenating 'folders/' + empty string.

#### [GO] Business-unit folder parent resolved from a stack output that does not exist
_Area: 4-projects · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** 4-projects resolves the environment folder via orgStack.GetStringOutput(fmt.Sprintf("%s_folder_id", cfg.Env)) (e.g. "development_folder_id"), but the Go 1-org stack only exports common_folder_id and network_folder_id; environment folders are created by 2-environments and exported as "<env>_env_folder". Pulumi SDK v3.231 GetStringOutput returns an erroring output for missing outputs ("stack reference output %q does not exist"), so 4-projects cannot deploy. The TS port correctly reads env_folder from the 2-environments stack.

**Evidence:** Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/main.go:57 (GetStringOutput "%s_folder_id") vs 1-org exports at pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/main.go:130-149 and 2-environments export "%s_env_folder" at 2-environments/main.go:63. SDK behavior: /root/go/pkg/mod/github.com/pulumi/pulumi/sdk/v3@v3.231.0/go/pulumi/stack_reference.go:108-127. TS: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/development/index.ts:21.

**Verifier reasoning:** Fully confirmed on every point of the claim.

1. Terraform reference: 4-projects/modules/base_env/remote.tf:28 sets env_folder_name = data.terraform_remote_state.environments_env.outputs.env_folder (read from the 2-environments state, NOT from 1-org/bootstrap). Confirmed.

2. Go port bug: pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/main.go:57 does `folderID := orgStack.GetStringOutput(fmt.Sprintf("%s_folder_id", cfg.Env))` against `orgStack` (the 1-org stack reference). But 1-org (pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/main.go:125-141) only exports `common_folder_id`, `network_folder_id`, `%s_network_project_id`, etc. — there is no `%s_folder_id` export anywhere in 1-org. Meanwhile 2-environments (pulumi_go-example-foundation/2-environments/main.go:63) exports exactly `%s_env_folder` (env folder's `.Name`, i.e. `folders/{id}`), which is what's needed. Critically, the same main.go file already instantiates `envStack` (the 2-environments reference) two lines later for KMS project id (`envStack.GetStringOutput(fmt.Sprintf("%s_env_kms_project_id", cfg.Env))`) — so the correct stack reference is right there but the wrong one/wrong key is used for the folder parent.

3. SDK behavior confirmed: /root/go/pkg/mod/.../stack_reference.go:107-124 shows GetStringOutput returns an Output that resolves to an error ("stack reference output %q does not exist on stack %q") when the key is absent — this surfaces as an apply-time failure, blocking the folder/resource creation that depends on it.

4. TS port confirmed correct: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/development/index.ts:19-20,29 reads `envRef.getOutput("env_folder")` from the 2-environments stack and passes it straight through as `parent`, matching Terraform.

5. Searched pulumi-library/go (project, iam, bootstrap packages) for any alternate/compensating folder-resolution helper — none exists; folder ID/parent resolution is entirely the caller's (port's) responsibility, and no other code path in the Go port supplies it correctly.

6. 4-projects/main.go is the single generic program parameterized by cfg.Env across the development/nonproduction/production stacks (per the .example Pulumi.<env>.yaml files), so this bug blocks 4-projects deployment for every environment in the Go port, not just development.

Severity assessment: 'critical' is not inflated — this is a hard deploy-blocking bug (wrong stack reference + nonexistent output key) for an entire stage across all environments in the Go port, not a cosmetic or informational gap.

#### [GO] Peering targets host VPC name 'vpc-{env}-svpc-base' which the network stage never creates
_Area: 4-projects · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** deployPeeringNetwork hardcodes the shared-VPC host network as projects/{net-project}/global/networks/vpc-{env_code}-svpc-base, but 3-networks-svpc creates the host VPC as vpc-{env_code}-svpc (no '-base' suffix). The bidirectional NetworkPeering therefore references a nonexistent network and deployment fails whenever peering_enabled (default true). The network stack even exports network_self_link, which 4-projects ignores. TF reads network_self_link from remote state instead of hardcoding.

**Evidence:** Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/peering.go:90 ("vpc-%s-svpc-base") vs pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go:80 (netName "vpc-%s-svpc") and its network_self_link export (main.go:290). TF: 4-projects/modules/base_env/example_peering_project.tf peers to local.network_self_link from remote state.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF side: terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc/main.tf sets network_name = "vpc-${environment_code}-svpc" (no '-base' suffix), and 3-networks-svpc/modules/base_env/outputs.tf exports network_self_link. 4-projects/modules/base_env/remote.tf reads network_self_link = data.terraform_remote_state.network_env.outputs.network_self_link, and example_peering_project.tf uses local.network_self_link (not a hardcoded name) as peer_network. So TF never hardcodes a '-base'-suffixed host VPC name; it always resolves the real network via remote state, exactly as the reviewer describes.

Port side (pulumi_go-example-foundation): 3-networks-svpc/main.go:80 creates netName := fmt.Sprintf("vpc-%s-svpc", cfg.EnvCode) (no '-base') and exports ctx.Export("network_self_link", vpcModule.VPC.SelfLink). 4-projects/peering.go:90 hardcodes hostVpcRef := pulumi.Sprintf("projects/%s/global/networks/vpc-%s-svpc-base", networkProjectID, cfg.EnvCode) — a name that is never created anywhere. Grepping the entire go port and terraform reference for 'svpc-base'/'svpc_base' finds it in exactly one place: this hardcoded string in peering.go. 4-projects/main.go does open a StackReference to the network stack (netStack, aliased 'network') but only pulls service_perimeter_name from it — network_self_link is never fetched or threaded through deployBusinessUnitProjects/deployPeeringNetwork. Checked pulumi-library/go/pkg/network_peering (the shared NetworkPeering component) — it's a generic wrapper taking caller-supplied LocalNetwork/PeerNetwork self-links with no naming logic of its own, so no equivalent-but-differently-named correct implementation exists there either; the port's peering.go doesn't even use that shared component, calling compute.NewNetworkPeering directly with the bad hardcoded ref.

Severity check: cfg.PeeringEnabled defaults to true (main.go loadProjectsConfig, no override found in Pulumi.yaml), and the NetworkPeering resources are gated only on that flag, so this executes by default for every environment. A GCP NetworkPeering resource pointing at a nonexistent peer network self-link will fail at apply time (assuming 3-networks-svpc is deployed first, the expected stage order). The claimed 'critical' severity is justified, not inflated — this is a hard deployment blocker on the default path, not an unused informational output.

#### [TS] Stage exports 'shared_vpc_project_id' but active 5-app-infra consumes 'shared_vpc_project'
_Area: 4-projects/outputs · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF 4-projects exports 'shared_vpc_project', which 5-app-infra consumes. The TS 4-projects stacks export 'shared_vpc_project_id' instead, while the ACTIVE TS 5-app-infra index.ts reads getOutput("shared_vpc_project") — a name that does not exist — so stage 5 receives undefined and fails. (The 5-app-infra index.ts.example uses the _id name, confirming the drift.) Go exports 'shared_vpc_project' and its 5-app-infra reads the same name, so only TS is broken.

**Evidence:** TF: business_unit_1/development/outputs.tf output "shared_vpc_project". Port: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/development/index.ts:399 (shared_vpc_project_id) vs pulumi_ts-example-foundation/5-app-infra/business_unit_1/development/index.ts:17 (getOutput("shared_vpc_project")).

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified across all repos. TF 4-projects exports 'shared_vpc_project' (terraform-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/development/outputs.tf:32) and TF 5-app-infra consumes it (5-app-infra/modules/env_base/main.tf:21). The TS port's 4-projects stacks (all three envs, index.ts:399) export only 'shared_vpc_project_id' — the returned outputs object (lines 397-419) contains no 'shared_vpc_project' key, and no equivalent export exists in pulumi-library. The ACTIVE TS 5-app-infra index.ts in all three envs (line 17) reads getOutput("shared_vpc_project"), a nonexistent output, while the index.ts.example files (line 16) correctly use 'shared_vpc_project_id'. The Go port is consistent (4-projects/main.go:112 exports 'shared_vpc_project'; 5-app-infra/main.go:56 reads the same name), so only TS is affected. The name mismatch is real and the claim's file/line citations are accurate.

**Verifier note / correction:** The gap is real but severity is inflated. The broken file is an explicit resource-less scaffold whose header says to copy index.ts.example over it to enable the actual workload — and the example uses the CORRECT name 'shared_vpc_project_id', so the functional deployment path works. getOutput (not requireOutput) on a missing name returns Output<undefined>, so pulumi up does not fail; the scaffold just silently exports an undefined/missing 'project_id', which nothing in test/build/scripts consumes. Severity should be moderate (silent wrong-name read in an inert scaffold, confusing but non-blocking), not critical.

#### [GO] Peering references host VPC by wrong hardcoded name ('vpc-{e}-svpc-base' vs actual 'vpc-{e}-svpc')
_Area: 4-projects/peering · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF consumes network_env remote-state output network_self_link for the peer network. The Go port ignores the network stack's exported network_self_link and hardcodes the host network URL as 'projects/{net-prj}/global/networks/vpc-{envCode}-svpc-base', but the Go 3-networks-svpc stage names the host VPC 'vpc-{envCode}-svpc'. Both peering directions therefore reference a nonexistent network and deployment fails whenever peering_enabled (default true).

**Evidence:** TF: modules/base_env/example_peering_project.tf module "peering" peer_network = local.network_self_link (remote.tf). Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/peering.go:90 (hostVpcRef 'vpc-%s-svpc-base') vs pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go:80 (netName 'vpc-%s-svpc') and main.go:290 (network_self_link export, unconsumed by 4-projects).

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified on all points. TF (4-projects/modules/base_env/example_peering_project.tf) peers using peer_network = local.network_self_link, sourced from the network_env remote state output (remote.tf:23), so it always references the real host VPC. The Go port hardcodes the host VPC URL as 'projects/{net-prj}/global/networks/vpc-{e}-svpc-base' (4-projects/peering.go:90), but the Go 3-networks-svpc stage creates the host VPC named 'vpc-{e}-svpc' (3-networks-svpc/main.go:80), and the shared library component uses VPCName verbatim with no suffix (pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/networking.go:83). A repo-wide search of the Go port and pulumi-library shows no network named '*svpc-base' is ever created, and the hub-and-spoke variant ('vpc-{e}-svpc-spoke') doesn't match either. 4-projects/main.go creates a network StackReference but consumes only service_perimeter_name; the 3-networks-svpc 'network_self_link' export (main.go:290) is unconsumed. Both peering directions use the bogus ref (the reverse peering even uses it as its Network), so creation fails against the GCP API, and PeeringEnabled defaults to true (4-projects/main.go:276-280), making this a hard deploy failure of the default path. The TS port confirms the correct pattern: business_unit_1/*/index.ts:146-150 reads netRef.getOutput('network_self_link') and passes it as peerNetwork. Claimed severity 'critical' is justified: pulumi up on 4-projects fails by default.

#### [TS] Shared VPC sample project never attached to the VPC-SC perimeter
_Area: 4-projects/vpc-sc · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF attaches the shared_vpc_project (and conf-space project) to the service perimeter in ENFORCED mode when enforce_vpcsc, else in dry-run mode (vpc_service_control_attach_enabled/attach_dry_run through single_project/project-factory). The TS env stacks attach only the confidential-space project; the SVPC-attached sample project (and CMEK project) are left entirely outside the perimeter in every env. A data-exfiltration security control present in TF is silently absent. (Go attaches the SVPC project when enforce_vpcsc=true.)

**Evidence:** TF: modules/base_env/example_shared_vpc_project.tf (vpc_service_control_attach_enabled/dry_run, perimeter_name). Port: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/development/index.ts — only line 323 creates a ServicePerimeterResource (confidential-space); no perimeter attach for sharedVpcProject (lines 37-60); modules/single_project.ts has no VPC-SC support at all. Same in nonproduction/production index.ts.

**Verifier reasoning:** The core claim is verified. TF's example_shared_vpc_project.tf (lines 43-46) always attaches the shared VPC sample project to the VPC-SC perimeter — ENFORCED when enforce_vpcsc, dry-run otherwise — via single_project/project-factory (vpc_service_control_attach_enabled/attach_dry_run/perimeter_name). In the TS port, all three env stacks (4-projects/business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts) create exactly one ServicePerimeterResource, for the confidential-space project only (line 323); sharedVpcProject (lines 37-60) is never attached. modules/single_project.ts and the pulumi-library ts project-factory package have no VPC-SC support under any name. The library's vpc-service-controls package only creates the perimeter in 3-networks with the env network project number plus manually configured vpc_sc_project_numbers — an operator config escape hatch, not equivalent automatic behavior, and 3-networks deploys before the 4-projects project exists. Exhaustive sweeps of the TS repo and pulumi-library (ServicePerimeterResource, perimeter, gcloud perimeter-update patterns) found no alternative mechanism. The Go port does attach the SVPC project when enforce_vpcsc=true (business_unit.go:107-115), matching the claim's parenthetical.

**Verifier note / correction:** Two refinements: (1) The "(and CMEK project)" portion of the claim is incorrect — TF's example_storage_cmek.tf has no vpc_service_control_* arguments, so the CMEK project being outside the perimeter matches TF behavior and is not a parity gap; only the shared VPC sample project attach is missing. (2) Severity is better rated high than critical: the perimeter itself is still created and enforced around the network host project, and the unattached project is a sample/example project — the missing attach silently drops a demonstrated data-exfiltration control (and the TF dry-run mode entirely), but does not break the foundation or remove perimeter protection where it is primarily applied.

_Area: 5-app-infra · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** main.go and the example file headers say to enable workloads via `go build -tags=example`, but that build fails: example_confidential_space.go uses fields ProjectId, InstanceName, Subnetwork that do not exist on compute_instance.ComputeInstanceArgs (library defines Project, Zone, Hostname, InstanceTemplate, NumInstances, DeletionProtection); reads inst.SelfLink/inst.Name/inst.Zone which do not exist on the ComputeInstance component (only Instances []*compute.InstanceFromTemplate); passes a pulumi.StringOutput (the tee-image-reference derived from the CloudBuild project) into the library's Metadata map[string]string; and has an unused gcp/compute import. So even opting in, the confidential-space workload cannot be built, let alone deployed.

**Evidence:** Verified: `go build -tags=example ./...` in /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/5-app-infra fails with 8 errors (example_confidential_space.go lines 29, 161, 170, 172, 174, 182-184) against /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/compute_instance/compute_instance.go v0.2.0 (matches go.mod requirement). Default build (no tag) succeeds.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim fully verified by direct reproduction. Upstream TF: 5-app-infra/modules/confidential_space exists (WIF pool, attestation-verifier OIDC provider, IAM binding, confidential VM) and is instantiated unconditionally in all three business_unit_1 environments — it is default stage-5 functionality. Go port: the only implementation is example_confidential_space.go behind //go:build example; main.go and the README document `go build -tags=example` as the enable path, and the README advertises the Confidential Space VM, config key, and outputs. Running `go build -tags=example ./...` in /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/5-app-infra (workspace go.work resolves to the local /home/user/pulumi-library checkout, confirmed v0.2.0 via CHANGELOG) fails with exactly the 8 claimed errors at the claimed lines: unused gcp/compute import (l.29), pulumi.StringOutput passed into the library's Metadata map[string]string (l.161), unknown fields ProjectId/InstanceName/Subnetwork on compute_instance.ComputeInstanceArgs (l.170/172/174, library only defines Project/Zone/Hostname/InstanceTemplate/NumInstances/DeletionProtection), and inst.SelfLink/Name/Zone undefined on the ComputeInstance component (l.182-184, which exposes only Instances []*compute.InstanceFromTemplate). Default build succeeds. Exhaustive search found no alternative: 5-app-infra/modules/confidential_space/ and modules/env_base/ are empty 3-line package stubs, and pulumi-library (go and ts) has no confidential-space component. 4-projects/confidential_space.go is the separate project-creation stage and builds fine. docs/ERRATA.md admits Confidential Space is "not included" in stage 5, but that entry is stale (it also says stage 5 deploys Cloud Run + BigQuery, contradicting current code) and contradicts the 5-app-infra README's parity claim.

**Verifier note / correction:** Gap is real as stated; only nuance is severity. "Critical" is defensible because upstream deploys confidential_space unconditionally and the port's sole path to it cannot compile, but "high" is arguably more accurate since the default (untagged) build and the rest of stage 5 deploy fine — the breakage is confined to the opt-in confidential-space workload.

#### [BOTH] GitLab CI template performs no GCP authentication; run_gcp_auth.sh not ported
_Area: build · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's build/gitlab-ci.yml configures id_tokens (GITLAB_OIDC_TOKEN) and calls build/run_gcp_auth.sh, which exchanges the GitLab OIDC token for GCP credentials via 'gcloud iam workload-identity-pools create-cred-config' and 'gcloud auth login --cred-file ... --update-adc'. The ports' build/gitlab-ci.yml documents GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER and GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT as required variables but never uses them: there is no id_tokens block, no auth step, and no run_gcp_auth.sh equivalent anywhere in either port. A pipeline run would fail (or fall back to no GCP credentials) on any stack that touches GCP.

**Evidence:** TF: build/run_gcp_auth.sh and build/gitlab-ci.yml (id_tokens + 'bash run_gcp_auth.sh ...' in every job). Ports: pulumi_go-example-foundation/build/gitlab-ci.yml and pulumi_ts-example-foundation/build/gitlab-ci.yml — .pulumi_base before_script only installs pulumi and deps; grep for 'run_gcp_auth', 'id_tokens', 'create-cred-config' across both ports returns nothing.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed exactly as described: build/run_gcp_auth.sh exists and build/gitlab-ci.yml uses `id_tokens: GITLAB_OIDC_TOKEN` + `bash run_gcp_auth.sh ...` in every job (terraform-plan, terraform-plan-mr, terraform-apply). TF's own 0-bootstrap/README-GitLab.md instructs copying both build/gitlab-ci.yml AND build/run_gcp_auth.sh together into every stage repo, confirming this is the canonical, load-bearing pattern.

Port side: build/gitlab-ci.yml in both pulumi_go-example-foundation and pulumi_ts-example-foundation genuinely lacks any id_tokens block or auth step — confirmed by direct read. The header comments document GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER and GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT as 'required variables' that are never referenced in the file's jobs. No .sh script equivalent to run_gcp_auth.sh exists anywhere in either port or in pulumi-library (exhaustive grep for create-cred-config, id_tokens, cred-file, update-adc, OIDC_TOKEN turned up only doc/comment hits, no scripts).

However, the reviewer's stronger claim — 'no auth step ... anywhere in either port' — is not fully accurate:
- Go port: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/README-GitLab.md contains a complete, functionally-equivalent pattern (id_tokens: GITLAB_OIDC_TOKEN + inline `gcloud iam workload-identity-pools create-cred-config` + GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS export) — i.e. the auth exchange logic is present in the repo, just inlined into a documented YAML snippet instead of a separate run_gcp_auth.sh, and using different variable names (WIF_PROVIDER_NAME/SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL) than what build/gitlab-ci.yml advertises (GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER/GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT). This is inconsistent and not wired into the actual template file, but it refutes 'no equivalent anywhere.'
- TS port: no such equivalent exists anywhere, including its own 0-bootstrap/README-GitLab.md, which just says to `cp build/gitlab-ci.yml .gitlab-ci.yml` and mentions only a legacy static-key variable (GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY) with no WIF exchange documented at all. So for TS the gap is essentially as described.

Severity is inflated. GitHub Actions is the default CI/CD provider in both ports and correctly implements WIF auth (build/pulumi-preview.yml and pulumi-up.yml use `google-github-actions/auth@v2` with `id-token: write` permission, wired to real secrets). GitLab is explicitly documented as a non-default 'Alternative' in both ports (Go's README-GitLab.md literally states 'GitLab is NOT the default'). Even upstream TF flags its own GitLab guidance as 'no longer actively tested or maintained.' So this bug sits entirely in an optional, secondary, docs-driven CI template — not in the ports' primary/default deployment path — making 'critical' overstated.

**Verifier note / correction:** The gap is real for build/gitlab-ci.yml in both ports (it documents GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER/GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT but never wires them into an auth step), but two corrections to the claim: (1) severity should be downgraded from 'critical' to 'medium' — GitLab is an explicitly-labeled non-default/alternative CI path in both ports (GitHub Actions + WIF is the default and is correctly implemented with id-token permissions and google-github-actions/auth@v2), and even upstream TF marks its own GitLab support as unmaintained; (2) the claim that there is 'no run_gcp_auth.sh equivalent anywhere in either port' is false for the Go port — pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/README-GitLab.md documents a working inline equivalent (id_tokens + `gcloud iam workload-identity-pools create-cred-config`), just under different variable names and not wired into build/gitlab-ci.yml itself. For the TS port this secondary claim holds — no equivalent exists anywhere, including its own README-GitLab.md.

#### [BOTH] No policy-check gate anywhere in the CI/CD pipeline templates
_Area: build · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** In the reference, every pipeline runs 'gcloud beta terraform vet' against policy-library before apply: build/tf-wrapper.sh tf_validate() (lines 234-284) is invoked by cloudbuild-tf-plan.yaml, cloudbuild-tf-apply.yaml (step 'tf validate' before 'tf apply'), github-tf-apply.yaml (validate step before apply), github-tf-pull-request.yaml, github-tf-plan-all.yaml, and gitlab-ci.yml. In the ports, none of the shipped pipeline templates run the CrossGuard policy pack: build/pulumi-preview.yml and build/pulumi-up.yml invoke pulumi/actions without --policy-pack, and build/gitlab-ci.yml runs bare 'pulumi preview' / 'pulumi up --yes'. The policy pack exists (pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts) but is only referenced in README prose. A mandatory security/compliance gate present in every TF pipeline is silently absent from every port pipeline.

**Evidence:** TF: build/tf-wrapper.sh (validate/plan_validate_all), build/cloudbuild-tf-apply.yaml lines 54-69, build/github-tf-apply.yaml lines 74-82. Ports checked: pulumi_go-example-foundation/build/{pulumi-preview.yml,pulumi-up.yml,gitlab-ci.yml}, same in pulumi_ts-example-foundation/build/ — zero occurrences of 'policy' (grep). pulumi-library has no pipeline wiring for policy packs either.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed exactly as described: build/tf-wrapper.sh's tf_validate() (which runs `gcloud beta terraform vet ... --policy-library=...`) is invoked as a distinct 'tf validate'/'validate' step between plan and apply in cloudbuild-tf-apply.yaml, github-tf-apply.yaml, github-tf-pull-request.yaml, github-tf-plan-all.yaml, and gitlab-ci.yml (all confirmed by direct grep/read).

Port side: exhaustively searched both pulumi_go-example-foundation and pulumi_ts-example-foundation build/ directories (pulumi-preview.yml, pulumi-up.yml, gitlab-ci.yml), plus scripts/, test/, helpers/, any Makefiles, and the whole repo tree for 'policy', 'vet', 'crossguard', 'policy-pack'/'policyPack'. Also checked pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) for any pipeline wiring — it only contains unrelated GCP org-policy/firewall-policy resource components, not a CrossGuard CI gate. Confirmed: none of the shipped pipeline templates pass --policy-pack (or the GitHub Action's policyPacks input) to `pulumi preview`/`pulumi up`, and the GitLab CI templates run bare `pulumi preview --diff` / `pulumi up --yes`.

A real CrossGuard policy pack does exist in pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts (7 policies mapped to the TF OPA constraints), but the go port's policy-library/ is just a README pointing at the TS one, and in neither port is it actually invoked by CI — only documented in README prose as something the user could add ("Add the --policy-pack flag to your preview and apply steps"). Clinching evidence: the port's own docs/ERRATA.md explicitly states "This port: Does not include policy validation infrastructure... Policy enforcement is handled via organization policies deployed in Stage 1 and via Pulumi's CrossGuard policy-as-code framework, which can be adopted separately" — a self-admitted, undisputed gap.

Severity: 'critical' is reasonable and not inflated — this is a security/compliance gate (blocking policy-violating infra from reaching apply) that exists as an enforced, non-optional step in every TF pipeline variant, and is completely absent (opt-in only, unwired) from every shipped port pipeline. I did not find any softening factor (e.g., a lint/test-time policy check elsewhere) that would downgrade it.

#### [BOTH] Policy validation is not wired into either port's CI/CD pipeline
_Area: policy-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** In Terraform, policy validation is a mandatory, build-failing gate: build/cloudbuild-tf-plan.yaml runs 'tf-wrapper.sh plan_validate_all' and build/cloudbuild-tf-apply.yaml runs 'tf-wrapper.sh validate', which executes 'gcloud beta terraform vet --policy-library=...' and exits 33 on any violation before apply. In both ports the CrossGuard policy pack is never executed by any pipeline: build/pulumi-preview.yml, build/pulumi-up.yml and build/gitlab-ci.yml run plain 'pulumi preview/up' with no --policy-pack flag or pulumi/actions 'policyPacks' input. The compliance gate that blocks non-compliant plans in the upstream pipeline is silently absent from the ports' pipelines (both READMEs only describe manual usage).

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/build/cloudbuild-tf-apply.yaml:53-60, build/cloudbuild-tf-plan.yaml:35-42, build/tf-wrapper.sh:236-278 (exit 33 on violation). Ports checked: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/build/{pulumi-preview.yml,pulumi-up.yml,gitlab-ci.yml} and /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/build/{pulumi-preview.yml,pulumi-up.yml,gitlab-ci.yml} — grep for policy/policy-pack/policyPacks returns nothing.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

TF side: verified as cited. build/cloudbuild-tf-plan.yaml runs `tf-wrapper.sh plan_validate_all ... ${_POLICY_REPO} ...`, and build/cloudbuild-tf-apply.yaml runs `tf-wrapper.sh validate ... ${_POLICY_REPO} ...`. Both invoke `tf_validate()` in build/tf-wrapper.sh, which runs `gcloud beta terraform vet "${tf_file}.json" --policy-library="${policy_file_path}" ... || exit 33` — a mandatory, build-failing gate before apply, exactly as described.

Port side: read every cited pipeline file in full for both ports.
- pulumi_go-example-foundation/build/{pulumi-preview.yml,pulumi-up.yml,gitlab-ci.yml}: plain `pulumi/actions@v5` with `command: preview`/`up` (no `policyPacks` input) and plain `pulumi preview --diff` / `pulumi up --yes` in GitLab CI — no policy flag anywhere.
- pulumi_ts-example-foundation/build/{pulumi-preview.yml,pulumi-up.yml,gitlab-ci.yml}: identical situation, no policy-pack invocation.
- Grepped both port repos and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) for policy-pack/policyPacks/crossguard/"terraform vet" — the only hits are in policy-library/README.md (manual instructions), policy-library/index.ts (the pack definition itself), CHANGELOG.md, and docs/ERRATA.md. No CI file references it. pulumi-library itself has zero hits, so the shared component library doesn't wire it in either.
- Confirmed a real (non-stub) CrossGuard PolicyPack exists in pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts with ~7 concrete mandatory/advisory validateResource policies (no-default-network, project-labels-required, no-sa-key-creation, no-public-access, no-public-ip, require-private-google-access, require-flow-logs). The Go port's policy-library/README.md explicitly defers to this shared TS pack (CrossGuard SDK is TS/Node-only) — this is a documented cross-port reuse pattern, not a missing feature in the Go port per se.
- docs/ERRATA.md ("Policy Validation" section) in both ports explicitly states the deviation: policy enforcement is "handled via organization policies deployed in Stage 1 and via Pulumi's CrossGuard... which can be adopted separately" — i.e., the ports' own documentation confirms it is NOT wired into the pipeline by default, matching the claim.
- No cloudbuild*.yaml or any other CI mechanism exists in either port (only the three build/ files already checked) that could be wiring this in under a different name.

So the claim is accurate: the CrossGuard pack is a real, implemented artifact, but no shipped CI/CD pipeline (GitHub Actions or GitLab) in either port invokes it — `pulumi preview`/`up` run with no `--policy-pack`/`policyPacks` gate, unlike the TF pipeline's build-failing `terraform vet` step.

**Verifier note / correction:** Directionally correct but one nuance: the gap is not "silent" — it is an explicitly documented, intentional deviation. Both ports' docs/ERRATA.md call this out by name in a "Policy Validation" section, and policy-library/README.md gives exact copy-paste snippets (`policyPacks: policy-library` / `--policy-pack ./policy-library`) for an operator to wire it in manually. So this is better characterized as "a real, working CrossGuard policy pack is provided but left as an opt-in manual step rather than auto-wired into the shipped CI templates" rather than a silently-dropped feature. That's a documentation/labeling nuance, not a refutation — the core claim (no pipeline enforces it out of the box, unlike TF's mandatory gate) stands, and "critical" severity is reasonable given a security/compliance-enforcement gate is off by default in the reference pipeline templates.

#### [TS] Private Google APIs DNS zones created empty (no record sets) — breaks API resolution in every VPC
_Area: pulumi-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's private-service-connect module and the Go library PSC create private zones for googleapis.com/gcr.io/pkg.dev WITH wildcard CNAME + A records pointing to the PSC/restricted address. The TS PrivateServiceConnect creates only the GlobalAddress + GlobalForwardingRule, and the TS SharedVpc separately creates five private ManagedZones (googleapis, gcr, pkg-dev, notebooks-*) with NO RecordSets. An empty private zone shadows public DNS, so googleapis.com etc. become unresolvable from any VPC deployed by the TS port — silently breaking all Google API access.

**Evidence:** Go: /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/psc.go:116-230 (NewRecordSet CNAME/A records). TS checked: /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/private-service-connect/src/index.ts (only GlobalAddress + GlobalForwardingRule), /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc.ts:193-213 and 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/shared_vpc.ts:193-213 (ManagedZone loop, no RecordSet anywhere: grep RecordSet in TS repo/library only hits cloud-dns package which is unused by the port).

**Verifier reasoning:** Fully confirmed on both sides.

TF side: 3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc/private_service_connect.tf and the hub-and-spoke equivalent delegate to the upstream registry module terraform-google-modules/network/google//modules/private-service-connect. I fetched that module's dns.tf from GitHub (terraform-google-modules/terraform-google-network, master branch): it creates exactly 3 private DNS zones (googleapis.com., gcr.io., pkg.dev.) and for each one a wildcard CNAME (`*` -> restricted.googleapis.com./private.googleapis.com. or gcr.io./pkg.dev.) plus an apex/named A record pointing at the PSC IP. So the TF reference implementation genuinely populates these zones with records, not just empty zones.

Go port: /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/psc.go's NewPrivateServiceConnect creates the 3 zones (apis, gcr, pkg-dev) inline and, for each, calls dns.NewRecordSet twice (wildcard CNAME + A pointing at the PSC address) — a faithful reimplementation of the upstream TF module's behavior, confirmed by reading the full 116-230 line range.

TS port: /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/private-service-connect/src/index.ts creates only GlobalAddress + GlobalForwardingRule (no DNS resources at all). Separately, /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc.ts (lines 193-213, byte-identical in 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/shared_vpc.ts) loops over 5 domains (googleapis, gcr, pkg-dev, plus two extra notebooks domains not even present in the upstream TF module) and creates a gcp.dns.ManagedZone for each with only name/dnsName/visibility/privateVisibilityConfig — no RecordSet, CNAME, or A record anywhere. I grepped the entire ts port repo and both go/ and ts/ trees of pulumi-library for RecordSet/record_set/recordSet: the only hit is an unrelated, unused cloud-dns package (which does support recordsets) that is never imported by shared_vpc.ts or anywhere in the port (only listed in package.json as a stale dependency). I also checked env-level index.ts files and the test suite (3-networks.config.test.ts, private-service-connect.test.ts) — no record-set creation or assertions anywhere; the PSC unit test doesn't even instantiate DNS resources.

Since Cloud DNS private zones are authoritative and shadow public resolution for their domain tree, an empty private zone for googleapis.com/gcr.io/pkg.dev with no records genuinely returns NXDOMAIN for any query in that VPC (e.g. storage.googleapis.com), which is exactly why the upstream module bothers to add the wildcard CNAME. There is no compensating mechanism (no forwardingConfig, no peeringConfig, no separate resource creating these records by referencing the zone name string) anywhere in the TS port or pulumi-library. This is a genuine, unmitigated, critical functional regression that would break Google API/container-registry/artifact-registry resolution in every VPC the TS port deploys.

**Verifier note / correction:** None on substance/severity. One minor nuance for accurate attribution: the 'Area: pulumi-library' framing is only half right — the TS pulumi-library's PrivateServiceConnect component doesn't own DNS zone creation at all (unlike Go's, which does it internally), so the empty zones actually live in the port repo's shared_vpc.ts (which duplicates zone-creation logic outside the library). Both the library component and the port-level duplication are missing the record sets, so the gap is real regardless of which repo is charged, but a precise fix would need to touch pulumi_ts-example-foundation's shared_vpc.ts (or move zone+record creation into the ts PrivateServiceConnect library component to mirror Go's design) rather than pulumi-library alone.

#### [GO] Log-bucket sink writer identity granted roles/logging.logWriter instead of roles/logging.bucketWriter — aggregated audit logging silently broken
_Area: pulumi-library/centralized-logging · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Go CentralizedLogging routes org/folder sinks directly to the AggregatedLogs log bucket URI but grants the sink writer identity roles/logging.logWriter on the destination project. Log-bucket destinations require roles/logging.bucketWriter (that is what upstream log-export//logbucket grants; TF only grants logWriter for its project-type destination, which Go does not use). The billing-account log-bucket sink has the same wrong grant. Result: org sinks to the log bucket are rejected and centralized audit-log aggregation silently fails. The TS component correctly grants roles/logging.bucketWriter. The Go port 1-org actively uses this path (ProjectOptions set).

**Evidence:** TF: 1-org/modules/centralized-logging/main.tf (module destination_aggregated_logs = log-export//logbucket which grants bucketWriter; google_project_iam_member.project_sink_member grants logWriter only for the project-URI destination). Port: pulumi-library/go/pkg/logging/centralized_logging.go:258-286 and 520-543 (bucket URI destination + roles/logging.logWriter); consumed by pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/logging.go:75-101. Correct TS counterpart: pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts:88-97 (bucketWriter).

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all fronts. (1) TF side: 1-org/modules/centralized-logging/main.tf does route to an 'AggregatedLogs' log bucket and does use the log-export//logbucket submodule (grant_write_permission_on_bkt=false there, since TF's actual mechanism is a two-hop relay: org/folder sink -> destination PROJECT [roles/logging.logWriter, correct since that hop's destination is a project URI] -> a same-project internal sink -> log bucket [no explicit grant needed because sink and bucket share a project]). Fetching the upstream terraform-google-log-export v11.1.0 module confirms roles/logging.bucketWriter is exactly the role the logbucket submodule grants when enabled. (2) Port side: pulumi-library/go/pkg/logging/centralized_logging.go does NOT replicate this two-hop design — it builds the org/folder sink's DestinationURI directly as a log-bucket URI ('logging.googleapis.com/projects/.../locations/.../buckets/AggregatedLogs') at lines 258-286, and again for the billing-account sink at lines 505-545 — yet grants only roles/logging.logWriter (lines 279 and 537) via projects.NewIAMMember, with no compensating roles/logging.bucketWriter grant anywhere in pulumi-library/go or pulumi_go-example-foundation (verified by repo-wide grep). (3) Official GCP documentation (docs.cloud.google.com/logging/docs/export/configure_export_v2), fetched live, explicitly states that for a log-bucket destination the sink's writer identity needs BOTH roles/logging.logWriter (for all destinations) AND roles/logging.bucketWriter (log-bucket-specific) — Go only grants the former. (4) pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts confirms the same direct-to-bucket architecture but grants roles/logging.bucketWriter (lines ~89-97), showing the Go port's role choice is the odd one out. (5) pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/logging.go does pass a non-nil ProjectOptions, so this vulnerable path is actively exercised in the real stack. Net effect: writes from the org/folder (and billing-account) sinks to the AggregatedLogs log bucket will be rejected for lack of bucketWriter, and this fails silently (Pulumi apply succeeds; only Cloud Logging export-error metrics would reveal it) — exactly as described.

**Verifier note / correction:** One nuance worth flagging for severity calibration: this break is scoped to the dedicated AggregatedLogs log-bucket destination (and its linked BigQuery/Log-Analytics dataset) specifically — the same CentralizedLogging call in 1-org/logging.go also configures Storage and Pub/Sub destinations, whose sinks correctly grant roles/storage.objectCreator and roles/pubsub.publisher respectively and are unaffected by this bug, so org audit logs still land in the storage bucket and pubsub topic. 'Aggregated audit-log aggregation silently fails' is accurate for the log-bucket/Log-Analytics path specifically, not for every centralized-logging destination the module configures — but that IS the path the claim calls out by name, so the claim's wording is not really overstated, just worth noting for anyone triaging blast radius. Severity of 'critical' is reasonable given this silently breaks a compliance-relevant log-bucket/analytics pipeline with no deploy-time signal.

#### [TS] PSC component creates no DNS zones/records and hardcodes target 'all-apis' instead of 'vpc-sc' — Google API DNS blackholed and restricted VIP bypassed
_Area: pulumi-library/private-service-connect · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Upstream network//private-service-connect (used by 3-networks shared_vpc with forwarding_rule_target="vpc-sc") creates the PSC address+forwarding rule AND private DNS zones for googleapis.com/gcr.io/pkg.dev with wildcard CNAMEs and A records pointing to the PSC IP. The TS component creates only the address and forwarding rule, hardcodes target: "all-apis" (foundation requires the vpc-sc restricted VIP for VPC-SC enforcement), and omits noAutomateDnsZone/serviceDirectoryRegistrations. The TS port stage does not compensate: shared_vpc.ts creates the private zones with NO record sets (grep finds zero dns.RecordSet in ts 3-networks), so *.googleapis.com, gcr.io and pkg.dev resolve to nothing inside the VPC — API access breaks and the VPC-SC restricted-VIP control is absent. Go networking/psc.go implements zones+records and the port passes ForwardingRuleTarget "vpc-sc".

**Evidence:** TF: 3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc/private_service_connect.tf (forwarding_rule_target = "vpc-sc", dns_code). Port: pulumi-library/ts/packages/private-service-connect/src/index.ts:23-39 (no DNS, target "all-apis"); pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc.ts:193-222 (empty zones, no RecordSet anywhere). Go counterpart: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/psc.go:116-248; pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go:142-147.

**Verifier reasoning:** Every element of the claim was verified directly. (1) TF reference: 3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc/private_service_connect.tf and the hub-and-spoke variant both call terraform-google-modules/network//modules/private-service-connect with forwarding_rule_target = "vpc-sc" and dns_code set. I fetched the actual upstream module source (dns.tf) from GitHub and confirmed it creates three private DNS zones (googleapis.com, gcr.io, pkg.dev), each with a wildcard CNAME plus an A record pointing at the PSC IP, and that forwarding_rule_target="vpc-sc" maps the CNAME target to restricted.googleapis.com (vs private.googleapis.com for "all-apis") — exactly as the claim describes. (2) pulumi-library/ts/packages/private-service-connect/src/index.ts creates only a GlobalAddress and GlobalForwardingRule, hardcodes target: "all-apis", and its PrivateServiceConnectArgs interface has no field at all for forwardingRuleTarget/dnsCode/serviceDirectory — so the stage cannot even opt into vpc-sc. No DNS resources appear anywhere in that file, and its own unit test only checks that the class exists (no assertion on target or DNS). (3) pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc.ts creates 5 private ManagedZones (googleapis, gcr, pkg-dev, notebooks-api, notebooks-gke) directly via gcp.dns.ManagedZone but adds zero gcp.dns.RecordSet resources — confirmed via a repo-wide grep for "RecordSet" across the entire pulumi_ts-example-foundation tree (zero hits) and across pulumi-library/ts (the library does have a generic CloudDns component that supports recordsets, and it's even listed as a dependency in the port's package.json, but it is never imported/used anywhere in the TS port). (4) The Go side is a genuine, correctly-implemented counterpart, not itself a gap: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/psc.go implements the address, forwarding rule, and all three DNS zones with CNAME+A recordsets matching the TF module logic (restricted vs private googleapis URL keyed off ForwardingRuleTarget), and pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go passes ForwardingRuleTarget: "vpc-sc". So the asymmetry between the two ports is real, and the TS gap is not compensated anywhere else in the port or in the shared library. On severity: the claimed "critical" rating is justified rather than inflated — this silently creates authoritative-but-empty private DNS zones for googleapis.com/gcr.io/pkg.dev inside the shared VPC, which blackholes those domains for any workload in the network (worse than having no private zone at all, since the private zone shadows public DNS resolution for the same names), and it separately drops the VPC-SC restricted-VIP enforcement that is a core security control of the foundation blueprint (using "all-apis" instead of "vpc-sc" means traffic is not constrained to the perimeter's allowed services).

#### [TS] defaultServiceAccount not implemented: disable/delete silently ignored, deprivilege implemented as a destructive empty editor IAMBinding
_Area: pulumi-library/project-factory · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF project-factory manages the default compute SA via google_project_default_service_accounts; the foundation passes default_service_account="deprivilege" for every 1-org project and the bootstrap seed uses "disable". TS ProjectFactory only reacts to "deprivilege" and does so incorrectly: it creates an authoritative gcp.projects.IAMBinding on roles/editor with members: [] (strips ALL editor members project-wide, and does not deprivilege/disable the default SA itself). "disable"/"delete" are silently no-ops — the TS Bootstrap component passes "disable" and nothing happens, and the TS port stages never pass the arg at all, so default service accounts retain privileges everywhere. Go implements it correctly via projects.NewDefaultServiceAccounts with action DEPRIVILEGE/DISABLE/DELETE.

**Evidence:** TF: terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/projects.tf:41,75,109,144,178,212,247 (default_service_account = "deprivilege"). Port: pulumi-library/ts/packages/project-factory/src/index.ts:116-123 (only 'deprivilege' branch, empty IAMBinding); pulumi-library/ts/packages/bootstrap/src/bootstrap.ts:46,69 (passes 'disable', ignored); grep of pulumi_ts-example-foundation for defaultServiceAccount: no stage passes it. Correct Go counterpart: pulumi-library/go/pkg/project/project.go:175-188.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified end-to-end. TF side checks out exactly as cited: `default_service_account = "deprivilege"` appears at all 7 lines in 1-org/envs/shared/projects.tf (also present in 2-environments/modules/env_baseline/{kms,secrets}.tf). In pulumi-library/ts/packages/project-factory/src/index.ts:116-123, the only branch handles `defaultServiceAccount === "deprivilege"`, implemented as an authoritative `gcp.projects.IAMBinding` on `roles/editor` with `members: []` — this wipes the entire project's editor binding (not just the default SA) rather than targeting the default compute SA, and "disable"/"delete" fall through as no-ops. bootstrap.ts:46/69 defaults to "disable" and passes it to ProjectFactory where it's silently ignored. Grepping every TS port stage (1-org, 2-environments, 3-networks, 4-projects) confirms none ever pass `defaultServiceAccount` to ProjectFactory, unlike Go's 1-org/main.go:367 which defaults to "deprivilege" and threads it through, and project.go:175-188 which correctly uses `projects.NewDefaultServiceAccounts` with an Action/RestorePolicy mirroring `google_project_default_service_accounts`. The project-factory.test.ts test for "disable" only asserts a static object shape, never exercises actual resource creation, confirming no real test coverage catches this. One nuance not in the original claim: 0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.ts:84 (the active CI/CD build file) directly instantiates the raw `gcp.projects.DefaultServiceAccounts` resource with action DISABLE for the CICD project specifically, bypassing the broken ProjectFactory arg — so it is not quite true that "default service accounts retain privileges everywhere" in the TS port; the CICD project is handled correctly via a different code path. But the seed project, all 7 org-level shared projects, and the environments-stage KMS/Secrets projects remain affected, and the one wired-up path ("deprivilege") is destructively wrong. This is a real, security-relevant gap warranting the claimed critical severity, with a minor overstatement in the "everywhere" phrasing.

**Verifier note / correction:** Mostly accurate as stated; one overstatement: it is not true that TS port stages "never pass the arg at all, so default service accounts retain privileges everywhere." The active 0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.ts (line 84) directly creates a raw gcp.projects.DefaultServiceAccounts resource with action:"DISABLE" for the CI/CD project, correctly bypassing the broken ProjectFactory arg for that one project. The gap is otherwise real and severe: the seed project (via Bootstrap component), all 7 shared 1-org projects, and the 2-environments KMS/Secrets projects have no default-SA handling at all, and the one implemented path ("deprivilege") is an overly broad, destructive empty roles/editor IAMBinding rather than a targeted default-SA action.

### MAJOR (110)

#### [TS] Authoritative billing.creator restriction missing
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Upstream bootstrap module creates an authoritative org-level binding restricting roles/billing.creator to only the billing admins group (removing the default org-wide grant). The Go port implements it (sa.go section 10, iam.NewOrganizationIAMBinding "org-billing-creator"); the TS port has nothing, leaving default billing-account-creation permissions in place.

**Evidence:** TF: terraform-google-bootstrap v12 main.tf google_organization_iam_binding.billing_creator (invoked by 0-bootstrap/main.tf module seed_bootstrap). Checked: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/index.ts, sa.ts, pulumi-library/ts/packages/bootstrap/src/bootstrap.ts — grep for billing.creator returns no matches.

**Verifier reasoning:** The claim is CONFIRMED on all three legs. (1) Terraform side: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/main.tf lines 46-57 invoke module "seed_bootstrap" (terraform-google-modules/bootstrap/google ~> 12.0) passing group_billing_admins; the upstream module source (verified by fetching terraform-google-bootstrap v12.0.0 main.tf lines 160-166) contains an authoritative `google_organization_iam_binding "billing_creator"` granting roles/billing.creator at org level to ONLY group:${var.group_billing_admins}, which removes the default domain-wide billing.creator grant. (2) Go port implements it exactly as the reviewer said: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/sa.go lines 364-379, section 10, iam.NewOrganizationIAMBinding("org-billing-creator") with roles/billing.creator and members = [group:GroupBillingAdmins], with a comment explicitly citing the TF binding. (3) TS port: exhaustive case-insensitive searches of /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation and /home/user/pulumi-library (both go/ and ts/) for billing.creator, billingCreator, billing_creator, "creator", OrganizationIamBinding/IAMBinding, IAMPolicy/setIamPolicy, and gcloud iam-policy-binding commands found no equivalent under any name. The TS 0-bootstrap (index.ts, sa.ts) only creates additive gcp.organizations.IAMMember grants (billing.user etc. to org admins) and gcp.billing.AccountIamMember grants (billing.user/billing.admin to step SAs); the only authoritative org binding mechanism (parent-iam-remove-role / gcp.organizations.IAMBinding at index.ts:19 of that module) is used solely for role removal elsewhere, not for billing.creator. The shared library's Bootstrap component (/home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/bootstrap/src/bootstrap.ts, read in full — 187 lines) creates only the seed project, lien, one org policy, KMS, and state bucket, with no org-level IAM whatsoever. Severity "major" is fair: the missing resource is an authoritative security control whose absence leaves Google's default org-wide (domain-scoped) roles/billing.creator grant intact, meaning any user in the domain can create billing accounts — a genuine security-posture regression in a security-foundation blueprint, not a cosmetic or output-only gap.

#### [BOTH] Automated runner-image build pipeline (tf_cloud_builder) has no equivalent
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF automates producing the pinned runner Docker image: tf_cloud_builder module (GAR repo + Cloud Build trigger on the tf-cloudbuilder repo + Cloud Workflow + Cloud Scheduler), google_project_service_identity for workflows, bootstrap_csr_repo (push-to-repo.sh pushes the Dockerfile), and build_terraform_image (runs the trigger once). Neither port has any of this: TS creates an empty Artifact Registry repo "tf-runners" with nothing publishing to it and no artifactregistry.reader grants for the SAs; Go's default path creates no AR at all. No Workflow/Scheduler/image-build trigger exists in either port or the library. TF's 4-projects consumes cloud_builder_artifact_repo, and Go's 4-projects example expects CloudBuilderArtifactRepo, so the Cloud-Build-based app-infra pipelines have no image to run.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/build_cb.tf lines 137-219 (workflows_identity, tf_cloud_builder, bootstrap_csr_repo, time_sleep, build_terraform_image) + Dockerfile + scripts/push-to-repo.sh. Checked: pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/*.go(.example), pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/*.ts(.example), pulumi-library go/pkg/cicd/*, ts/packages/* — grep for Workflow/cloudscheduler resources returns only API-name strings.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed exactly as cited: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_cb.tf lines 137-219 contain google_project_service_identity.workflows_identity, module.tf_cloud_builder (upstream tf_cloudbuild_builder: GAR repo + Cloud Build trigger on the tf-cloudbuilder repo + Cloud Workflow + Cloud Scheduler), module.bootstrap_csr_repo running scripts/push-to-repo.sh (exists, pushes the Dockerfile), time_sleep, and module.build_terraform_image (one-time 'gcloud beta builds triggers run'); lines 261-271 grant artifactregistry.reader to the env SAs, and 4-projects consumes cloud_builder_artifact_repo. Refutation attempt on the ports failed: grep across both ports and /home/user/pulumi-library for gcp.workflows.Workflow / cloudscheduler.Job returns only API-name strings ("cloudscheduler.googleapis.com", "roles/cloudscheduler.admin"); the library's cicd component (/home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd/cloudbuild.go) creates only an AR repo, reader IAM, and per-stage plan/apply triggers — no image-build trigger, workflow, or scheduler. TS's build_cb.ts.example, build_cloud_build.ts.example, and default build_github_actions.ts all create an empty "tf-runners" AR repo with no publisher and (in TS) no reader grants; Go's default GitHub Actions path creates no AR. No push-to-repo equivalent, no 'gcloud builds submit'/'docker push' automation, and not even a documented manual image-build step exists in any .md/.sh/.yaml of either port; the ports' 0-bootstrap Dockerfile is a local dev container that nothing publishes. README-CloudBuild.md's example cloudbuild YAMLs reference '${_GAR_REGION}-docker.pkg.dev/${_GAR_PROJECT_ID}/pulumi-builders/pulumi:latest', an image nothing builds, and Go's 4-projects/example_infra_pipelines.go (line 35, CloudBuilderArtifactRepo) and TS's 4-projects/modules/infra_pipelines.ts.example (cloudBuilderArtifactRepo) both expect it. The gap is real everywhere it could live.

**Verifier note / correction:** Two calibration points, neither refuting the gap: (1) Go's Cloud Build example DOES grant artifactregistry.reader to the stage SAs via the library component (ArtifactRegistryReaders in pkg/cicd/cloudbuild.go lines 177-188) — the "no reader grants" statement is accurate only for the TS port, as the claim scoped it. (2) Severity: in TF Cloud Build is the default CI/CD path, but in both ports the default is GitHub Actions (runs-on ubuntu-latest, no runner image needed) and Cloud Build is an opt-in .example. The missing pipeline therefore breaks only the opt-in Cloud Build path and the 4-projects infra-pipelines example, not the ports' default flow — "major" is defensible for Cloud Build feature parity (the documented option references an image nothing builds, with no manual fallback documented), but it does not block the default deployment path.

#### [BOTH] Billing admins group never granted roles/billing.admin at org level
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Upstream bootstrap module unconditionally grants group_billing_admins roles/billing.admin at the organization level (google_organization_iam_member.org_billing_admin). Neither port grants the billing admins group anything: both only grant billing.user/billing.admin to the 5 pipeline SAs on the billing account. The billing admins group is collected in config in both ports but only used for the billing.creator binding (Go) or group creation.

**Evidence:** TF: terraform-google-bootstrap v12 main.tf google_organization_iam_member.org_billing_admin. Checked: pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/sa.go (section 6 grants only SA-level billing roles), pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/sa.ts lines 194-213, pulumi-library go/pkg/bootstrap/bootstrap.go and ts/packages/bootstrap/src/bootstrap.ts — grep for billing.admin in pulumi-library returns nothing.

**Verifier reasoning:** Upstream confirmed: terraform-example-foundation 0-bootstrap/main.tf (line 57) passes group_billing_admins into terraform-google-modules/bootstrap/google ~>12.0, and that module (verified in v12.0.0 source, main.tf lines 200-203) unconditionally creates google_organization_iam_member.org_billing_admin granting the group roles/billing.admin at the organization level. Ports: an exhaustive grep for all roles/billing.* grants across pulumi_go-example-foundation, pulumi_ts-example-foundation, and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) shows roles/billing.admin is granted only to the 5 pipeline SAs at billing-account scope (Go sa.go:250-252; TS sa.ts:201-203). The billing admins group receives no billing.admin anywhere: in Go it is used only for the authoritative org-level billing.creator binding (sa.go:371-375), group creation, outputs, and essential contacts; in TS only for group creation, outputs, and essential contacts. pulumi-library's bootstrap components (go/pkg/bootstrap/bootstrap.go, ts/packages/bootstrap/src/bootstrap.ts) create no IAM bindings at all, and go/pkg/iam/billing.go is just a generic billing-account-scope IAM wrapper. The roles/billing.user org grants in TS index.ts:142/152 go to the org admins group, not billing admins. No equivalent under any name exists. Severity 'major' is reasonable: the Go port even replicates the authoritative billing.creator binding that strips default billing permissions org-wide, but omits the compensating billing.admin grant upstream pairs with it, leaving human billing admins without billing-account administration rights; pipelines themselves are unaffected since the SAs hold billing.admin on the billing account.

#### [BOTH] Cloud Build alternative is unusable out of the box: pipeline yamls not shipped, hardcoded stack, undefined substitutions
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The Cloud Build build type creates triggers whose Filename points at cloudbuild-pulumi-plan.yaml / cloudbuild-pulumi-apply.yaml (Go, via pulumi-library cicd.CloudBuild defaults) or cloudbuild-tf-plan.yaml / cloudbuild-tf-apply.yaml (TS build_cloud_build.ts.example), but neither port ships these files — TF ships build/cloudbuild-tf-plan.yaml and build/cloudbuild-tf-apply.yaml to be copied into each stage repo. The only Pulumi versions are inline README snippets (0-bootstrap/README-CloudBuild.md), and those snippets (a) hardcode '--stack production' instead of mapping ${BRANCH_NAME} to a stack, so every branch would deploy production, and (b) reference substitutions ${_GAR_REGION}, ${_GAR_PROJECT_ID}, ${_TF_BACKEND}, ${_PULUMI_CONFIG_PASSPHRASE} that are never defined — the library CloudBuild component has no Substitutions support at all (TF tf_cloudbuild_workspace passes _ORG_ID/_BILLING_ID/_GAR_* substitutions).

**Evidence:** TF: build/cloudbuild-tf-{plan,apply}.yaml; 0-bootstrap/build_cb.tf tf_workspace substitutions block (lines 243-250). Ports: no cloudbuild-*.yaml in pulumi_go-example-foundation/build or pulumi_ts-example-foundation/build (grep 'cloudbuild-pulumi' matches only READMEs and pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd); pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/README-CloudBuild.md lines 98-128 ('pulumi preview --stack production'); pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd/cloudbuild.go CloudBuildTriggerConfig has no substitutions field.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all points. TF side: terraform-example-foundation ships build/cloudbuild-tf-plan.yaml and build/cloudbuild-tf-apply.yaml, and 0-bootstrap/build_cb.tf's `tf_workspace` module (lines 243-250) passes a real `substitutions` map (_ORG_ID, _BILLING_ID, _GAR_REGION, _GAR_PROJECT_ID, _GAR_REPOSITORY, _DOCKER_TAG_VERSION_TERRAFORM) that the yaml files consume, and Cloud Build is TF's active default (build_cb.tf, not a .example file; GitHub/GitLab/local/TFC are the .tf.example alternates there).

Port side: neither pulumi_go-example-foundation/build nor pulumi_ts-example-foundation/build contains any cloudbuild-*.yaml file (both build/ dirs only have gitlab-ci.yml, pulumi-preview.yml, pulumi-up.yml). The only place these pipeline definitions exist is as markdown code fences inside 0-bootstrap/README-CloudBuild.md in both ports — not files a user can copy-paste-use, and not referenced by any script/template elsewhere in either repo (verified via repo-wide grep for the substitution names — only the two READMEs match). The Go port's real (non-README) code path, build_cloud_build.go.example, calls pulumi-library's `libcicd.NewCloudBuild`/`CloudBuildTriggerConfig`, which I inspected directly (pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd/cloudbuild.go): it has no Substitutions field or wiring at all, even though the underlying pulumi-gcp SDK's cloudbuild.TriggerArgs does support Substitutions (verified in the vendored SDK) — so the library chose not to expose it. The Go README (lines 98-128) hardcodes `pulumi preview/up --stack production`, confirmed verbatim. There is no equivalent CloudBuild component in pulumi-library/ts at all (only Go has a pkg/cicd); the TS port's Cloud Build support is purely the build_cloud_build.ts.example / build_cb.ts.example files plus README-CloudBuild.md.

One correction to the claim's evidence: the TS README does NOT hardcode `--stack production` — it uses `--stack ${_STACK}` — so the "(a) hardcode '--stack production'" detail is Go-specific, not true of TS as stated. However this doesn't refute the gap: `${_STACK}` is itself just as undefined as the Go version's hardcoded stack (no trigger in either README or the .ts.example sets a _STACK substitution), so the underlying "unusable out of the box" defect holds for both ports, just manifesting slightly differently.

On severity: Cloud Build is explicitly documented in both READMEs as NOT the default ("Cloud Build is NOT the default... The default is GitHub Actions with WIF"), and the default GitHub Actions path is fully wired and ships working build/pulumi-preview.yml and build/pulumi-up.yml. This is a real, verified gap in an opt-in alternative CI/CD path (on par with the similarly-incomplete GitLab/Jenkins/Terraform-Cloud .example alternates in the same ports), not a defect in the primary/default deployment flow.

**Verifier note / correction:** Core claim holds, but severity is likely overstated as "major": this gap is confined to an explicitly-optional, non-default CI/CD path (both READMEs state Cloud Build is NOT the default; GitHub Actions is, and that path ships fully working build/pulumi-preview.yml + build/pulumi-up.yml). A more accurate severity is moderate/minor — it degrades an alternative feature that a subset of adopters would opt into, not the out-of-the-box default experience. Also, the "(a) hardcode '--stack production'" sub-point is accurate only for the Go port's README; the TS port's README instead uses an equally-undefined `${_STACK}` placeholder rather than a hardcoded stack name — the substance of the gap (undefined substitutions / no shippable yaml) is identical across both ports, but the specific "hardcoded to production" framing shouldn't be generalized to TS.

#### [GO] Cloud Build private worker pool (cb-private-pool) missing entirely from Go port and Go library
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF 0-bootstrap runs all foundation builds on a private worker pool (module tf_private_pool = ./modules/cb-private-pool: peered VPC, service-networking peering, DNS policy, firewall, WorkerPool, optional HA VPN; build_cb.tf sets enable_worker_pool=true for builder and every workspace; cloudbuild yamls set options.worker_pool). The TS side has @vitruviansoftware/foundation-cb-private-pool and uses it in build_github_actions.ts/build_cb.ts.example. The Go port has no cb-private-pool component anywhere: not in 0-bootstrap/modules (only parent-iam-member, parent-iam-remove-role), not in pulumi-library/go/pkg (no worker-pool code; grep 'WorkerPool' matches only the TS package), and the library CloudBuild component has no worker-pool option on its triggers.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/{main.tf,network.tf,vpn_ha.tf}, build_cb.tf lines 146-165, 179-180, 229-230. Go port: 0-bootstrap/modules contains only parent-iam-member and parent-iam-remove-role; pulumi-library/go/pkg has no cb_private_pool package and cicd/cloudbuild.go has no worker pool field. TS: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool + used at 0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.ts line 129.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF: 0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool exists (main.tf creates google_cloudbuild_worker_pool, network.tf does service-networking peering/DNS policy/firewall, vpn_ha.tf for optional HA VPN), and build_cb.tf (the DEFAULT, non-.example build file in TF, sitting alongside build_github.tf.example/build_gitlab.tf.example/etc.) sets enable_worker_pool=true / worker_pool_id at lines 179-180 and 229-230, exactly as claimed.

Go port: 0-bootstrap/modules contains only parent-iam-member and parent-iam-remove-role; pulumi-library/go/pkg has ~25 component packages (compute_instance, networking, network_peering, cicd, project, etc.) with nothing resembling cb_private_pool/worker_pool, and go/pkg/cicd/cloudbuild.go's CloudBuildArgs/CloudBuild struct has zero WorkerPool-related fields. Grepping the whole Go port for worker-pool/private-pool keywords turns up only: a dangling `PrivateWorkerPoolID` struct field (4-projects/example_infra_pipelines.go) that is never populated anywhere; a comment in build_cloud_build.go.example referencing a `cloud_build_private_pool_subnet` config key that the example's actual deployCloudBuild() function never reads or wires into libcicd.NewCloudBuild; and prose in onprem.md/README-CloudBuild.md describing private pools conceptually without any implementation backing it.

TS side confirmed accurate too: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool implements the module (VPC peering, VPN config, worker pool), and — notably going beyond what the claim states — it's instantiated not only in build_cb.ts.example but directly in build_github_actions.ts line 129, a file explicitly commented 'This is the default CI/CD approach for the Pulumi foundation.' So in TS the private pool is part of the DEFAULT bootstrap path (providing network-isolated build execution regardless of CI tool chosen), whereas Go's equivalent default-path files (projects.go deployCICDProject, build_github_actions.go deployGitHubActionsBuild) contain no private-pool/peering logic at all — confirmed by reading those functions directly. This makes it a real parity gap between the two sibling Pulumi ports' default code paths, not merely an upstream-Terraform-vs-Go difference confined to an opt-in alternative.

**Verifier note / correction:** Severity should be tempered, not dismissed. The Go port's own docs/ERRATA.md explicitly discloses this as a known, deliberate deviation ('Cloud Build Integration: ... Cloud Build triggers are not provisioned. CI/CD is handled entirely by GitHub Actions...'), so it is a documented architectural decision rather than a silent defect, and Go's default CI/CD flow (GitHub Actions, no Cloud Build) does not strictly require a Cloud Build worker pool to function — unlike TF, where build_cb.tf is the default. However, the gap is more significant than 'just an unported alternative path': the TS port includes the private worker pool (VPC peering + firewall + optional VPN) as infrastructure in its default bootstrap flow for network-isolated/on-prem-reachable build execution, and Go has no analogous network-isolation capability anywhere, default or optional (the one Go file that gestures at it, build_cloud_build.go.example, doesn't actually implement the wiring either). 'Major' is defensible given this cross-port parity gap and the complete absence of any working code path to get it in Go, but it should be understood as a disclosed/intentional-scope difference relative to Terraform specifically (per ERRATA.md), which weakens the 'silently missing' framing implied by treating it purely as an accidental oversight.

#### [GO] Cloud Build private worker pool infrastructure entirely missing in Go
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF 0-bootstrap always creates a private Cloud Build worker pool with a peered VPC (subnet with flow logs, DNS policy, reserved global address, service networking connection, custom-route peering config, ingress firewall rule). The TS port creates this by default via the library's cb-private-pool component; the Go port creates nothing — no worker pool in main/projects/build_github_actions.go, the Cloud Build example component (go/pkg/cicd/cloudbuild.go) has no worker-pool support, and the Go library has no cb-private-pool package at all. Go's own downstream stages expect it: 1-org reads allowed_worker_pool_id for the cloudbuild.allowedWorkerPools org policy and 4-projects example_infra_pipelines.go declares PrivateWorkerPoolID.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/build_cb.tf module tf_private_pool + 0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/{main,network,vpn_ha}.tf; consumed by 1-org/envs/shared/org_policy.tf and 4-projects/business_unit_1/shared/remote.tf. Checked: pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/*.go, pulumi-library/go/pkg/ (no cb-private-pool; cicd/cloudbuild.go has no WorkerPool). TS equivalent exists: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool + build_github_actions.ts.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified on all three legs. (1) TF: 0-bootstrap/build_cb.tf module "tf_private_pool" (lines 146-165) uses modules/cb-private-pool (450 lines: google_cloudbuild_worker_pool, peered VPC+subnet, global address, service networking connection, DNS policy, peering-routes config, firewall, optional VPN); pool ID feeds tf_cloud_builder and all tf_workspace triggers, and is consumed downstream by 1-org/envs/shared/org_policy.tf and 4-projects remote.tf/infra_pipelines. (2) TS port: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool implements CbPrivatePool (gcp.cloudbuild.WorkerPool + full network stack) and pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.ts creates it unconditionally in the default path (line 129), exporting privateWorkerPoolId. (3) Go port: exhaustive case-insensitive greps (workerpool, private_pool, cb-private, NewWorkerPool, cloudbuildv2, peered network terms, CIDRs 10.3.0.0/192.168.0.0) across pulumi_go-example-foundation and pulumi-library/go find NO worker pool creation anywhere: no cb-private-pool package in go/pkg/, cicd/cloudbuild.go creates only triggers/AR/repos, build_github_actions.go creates nothing pool-related, and even build_cloud_build.go.example (mirror of TF's default build_cb.tf) omits the pool while its README-CloudBuild.md diagram advertises "Private Worker Pool (optional), VPC-peered, 10.3.0.0/24" and the example comments reference an unconsumed cloud_build_private_pool_subnet config key. Go downstream code confirms the expectation: 1-org/policies.go applies cloudbuild.allowedWorkerPools only from a user-supplied allowed_worker_pool_id config, and 4-projects/example_infra_pipelines.go declares PrivateWorkerPoolID (line 36). Major severity is fair: TF runs every foundation build in the pool and enforces it org-wide; TS preserves this by default; Go cannot enable its own guardrail without manual out-of-band pool creation and its docs advertise nonexistent functionality.
