**Verifier note / correction:** Two minor nuances to the claim's framing, neither reducing severity below major: (a) the Go port's active default CI/CD is GitHub Actions (build_cloud_build.go.example is the opt-in Cloud Build variant), so the missing pool does not break the default build flow outright — though the TS port creates the pool by default under the same GitHub Actions default, so the cross-port parity gap stands; (b) 4-projects PrivateWorkerPoolID is a declared-but-never-used dead field rather than an active consumer, and 1-org's allowedWorkerPools policy degrades gracefully behind the enforce_allowed_worker_pools config gate.

#### [BOTH] Cloud Build workspace parity: no per-stage log/artifact buckets, wrong default apply branches, plan trigger not invert-filtered
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's tf_cloudbuild_workspace (build_cb.tf lines 221-259) creates per-stage build-log and build-artifact buckets (plan files are archived: artifacts.objects in cloudbuild-tf-apply.yaml uploads tmp_plan/*.tfplan) and sets tf_apply_branches = [development, nonproduction, production]. The Go library CloudBuild component creates no log or artifact buckets, its ApplyBranchPattern defaults to '^main$' (the foundation has no main-based flow — the Go example never overrides it, so apply triggers would never fire on the real environment branches), and its plan trigger fires on '.*' with no invert-regex, so pushes to environment branches double-run plan and apply. TS's build_cloud_build.ts.example gets the branches right but likewise creates no log/artifact buckets and retains no plan artifacts.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/build_cb.tf tf_workspace (log_bucket_name, artifacts_bucket_name, tf_apply_branches), build/cloudbuild-tf-apply.yaml artifacts+logsBucket (lines 71-76). Go: pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd/cloudbuild.go lines 202-204 (applyBranch default '^main$'), 258-264 (plan Push Branch '.*', no invertRegex), no bucket resources; 0-bootstrap/build_cloud_build.go.example sets no ApplyBranchPattern. TS: 0-bootstrap/build_cloud_build.ts.example lines 110-156.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

TF side (verified in /home/user/terraform-example-foundation):
- 0-bootstrap/build_cb.tf `module "tf_workspace"` sets `log_bucket_name`, `artifacts_bucket_name`, and `tf_apply_branches = ["development", "nonproduction", "production"]`, exactly as cited.
- build/cloudbuild-tf-apply.yaml and cloudbuild-tf-plan.yaml both have `artifacts.objects` (uploading `tmp_plan/*.tfplan`) and `logsBucket: gs://${_LOG_BUCKET_NAME}`.
- I additionally pulled the underlying `tf_cloudbuild_workspace` module (buckets.tf/cb.tf) to check the "invert-filtered plan trigger" claim precisely: for CSR repos it sets `invert_regex = each.key == "apply" ? false : true` on `trigger_template`, i.e. plan fires on branches that do NOT match `apply_branches_regex` and apply fires only on branches that DO match it — genuine mutual-exclusion via invert-regex, not a "both fire" design.

Go port / library (pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd/cloudbuild.go):
- No bucket resources of any kind are created in the CloudBuild component (grepped the whole repo/library — the only log/artifact bucket code anywhere is unrelated 4-projects app-infra-pipeline logic, stage 4, not 0-bootstrap CI/CD).
- `ApplyBranchPattern` defaults to `"^main$"` (line ~204) and 0-bootstrap/build_cloud_build.go.example never overrides it — confirmed the example's `CloudBuildTriggerConfig` literal omits the field entirely.
- Plan trigger branch is hardcoded to `".*"` for both CSR and GitHub source types, and `InvertRegex` (which exists on the pulumi-gcp SDK's `TriggerTriggerTemplateArgs`/`TriggerGithubPushArgs`, confirmed in the vendored SDK) is never set anywhere in `buildTriggerArgs`.
- The library's own README-CloudBuild.md documents the resulting (broken) behavior verbatim: "Cloud Build Plan Triggers ... runs on any branch push" / "Cloud Build Apply Triggers ... runs on main branch only" — since the rest of the foundation uses `development`/`nonproduction`/`production` branch names (confirmed via grep across the whole go port), the default apply trigger as shipped would never fire in real usage, and if a user corrected the branch pattern to match real env branches, plan (`.*`, uninverted) would fire on every one of those pushes too, i.e. genuine double-run.
- Neither `logsBucket` nor a `logging` mode is set on triggers that use a custom service account, which is an additional real-world risk (GCP requires one or the other for non-default-SA triggers) not even flagged in the claim.

TS port (0-bootstrap/build_cloud_build.ts.example):
- No pulumi-library ts equivalent of a CloudBuild component exists at all (grepped ts/packages — nothing cicd/cloudbuild-related); the port builds `gcp.cloudbuild.Trigger` directly.
- Apply branches are correct (`development`/`nonproduction`/`production`), matching the claim's "gets the branches right."
- No bucket creation, no `artifacts`/`logsBucket` fields set anywhere on the triggers — confirmed via grep across the whole ts port.

Both ports' README-CloudBuild.md present this Cloud Build path as a faithful mirror of `build_cb.tf` with no disclaimer about the missing buckets/branch semantics, so the gap isn't a documented simplification.

**Verifier note / correction:** Substance of the claim is accurate; only nuance worth adding is scope/severity framing. This Cloud Build path is an explicitly opt-in alternative CI/CD provider in both ports (default is GitHub Actions + WIF; Cloud Build code ships as `.go.example`/`.ts.example` and isn't compiled/used unless a user follows the README migration steps), so it doesn't affect the default deployment path. Within that opt-in path, however, the bug is at least as severe as "major" — likely worse: because triggers use a custom (non-default) Cloud Build service account with no `logsBucket` and no `logging` mode set, real GCP deployments could hit hard trigger-creation/build failures, not just lose log/artifact retention.

#### [BOTH] GitLab build type: no CI/CD variable provisioning and no branch protection
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's build_gitlab.tf.example uses the gitlab provider to auto-provision masked CI/CD variables in every stage repo (PROJECT_ID, CICD_RUNNER_REPO, WIF_PROVIDER_NAME, TF_BACKEND, SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL, token) and creates gitlab_branch_protection for the image, plan, production, nonproduction, and development branches. Neither port's GitLab example touches GitLab at all (no gitlab provider): pulumi_go 0-bootstrap/build_gitlab.go.example and pulumi_ts build_gitlab.ts.example only create the WIF pool/provider/bindings, and README-GitLab.md instructs users to set all variables manually ('Set these in each project's Settings → CI/CD → Variables'). Branch protection — which prevents direct pushes to environment branches, a control the TF foundation enforces — is absent. Contrast: the GitHub path in both ports does auto-provision Actions secrets.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/build_gitlab.tf.example gitlab_project_variable.variables (lines 118-126) and gitlab_branch_protection resources (128-159). Ports: build_gitlab.go.example / build_gitlab.ts.example contain no gitlab provider resources (imports are gcp-only); pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/README-GitLab.md line 167.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified on both sides.

TF side (confirmed as described): /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_gitlab.tf.example lines 118-126 define `gitlab_project_variable.variables` (for_each over all stage repos, masked=true) provisioning PROJECT_ID, CICD_RUNNER_REPO, WIF_PROVIDER_NAME, TF_BACKEND, TF_VAR_gitlab_token, and per-config SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL. Lines 128-159 define five `gitlab_branch_protection` resources (image, plan, production, nonproduction, development) via the `gitlab` provider (declared line 78).

Port side: both pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_gitlab.go.example and pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_gitlab.ts.example implement only steps 1-4 (WIF pool, WIF OIDC provider, SA→WIF attribute IAM bindings, bootstrap SA workloadIdentityPoolAdmin grant) using gcp.iam/serviceaccount resources — confirmed by reading both files in full. Checked the shared component library (/home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd/gitlab.go, 134 lines) — it contains only `GitLabOIDC`/`NewGitLabOIDC`, a WIF-pool/provider/binding component; no variable- or branch-protection-related type or function exists anywhere in the library (grepped go/ and ts/ trees for "gitlab", "branch_protection"/"BranchProtection"/"branchProtection" — zero hits outside WIF code). No `pulumi-gitlab`/`@pulumiverse/gitlab` provider dependency appears in go.mod/go.sum/package.json in either port — the gitlab provider itself is never even imported, so the resources genuinely could not exist. README-GitLab.md (both ports) instructs manual "Settings → CI/CD → Variables" entry for only WIF_PROVIDER_NAME and SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL (doesn't even mention PROJECT_ID/CICD_RUNNER_REPO/TF_BACKEND) and never mentions branch protection at all. Confirmed the asymmetry: build_github_actions.go in the same repo does auto-provision equivalent secrets via `ghactions.NewActionsSecret` (WIF_PROVIDER_NAME, SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL, PROJECT_ID, PULUMI_BACKEND_URL), so GitHub path has parity with TF while GitLab path does not.

The gap is real and matches the description closely, including the contrast with GitHub. Severity "major" is reasonable, not inflated: branch protection is a genuine security control (prevents direct pushes to production/nonproduction/development/plan/image branches) and its complete absence — with no manual-setup instructions even mentioning it — plus incomplete/manual variable provisioning together represent a real operational/security regression for any team choosing the GitLab path, not merely a cosmetic or informational omission.

#### [GO] Go docs reference stack output names that do not exist (copy-paste commands fail)
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The Go 0-bootstrap program exports {bootstrap,org,env,net,proj}_step_terraform_service_account_email, cloudbuild_project_id, and gcs_bucket_tfstate (main.go:78-121, keys from sa.go:40-46). But the docs reference different names: 0-bootstrap/README.md:224-226 uses 'cicd_project_id' and 'tf_state_bucket' (actual: cloudbuild_project_id, gcs_bucket_tfstate); README.md:390-394 documents outputs 'bootstrap_sa_email'..'proj_sa_email' which are never exported; README-GitHub.md:129 and README-GitLab.md:172 say 'pulumi stack output {stage}_sa_email'; ONBOARDING.md:200 uses 'cicd_project_id'; ONBOARDING.md:300 tells the operator to run 'pulumi stack output organization_step_terraform_service_account_email' — the Go export is 'org_step_terraform_service_account_email' (long-form names exist only in the TS port). Every one of these copy-paste blocks fails with 'no output found', blocking the documented Step 1 SA handoff and CI/CD secret setup.

**Evidence:** Actual exports: pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/main.go:78-121 with granularSAs keys in sa.go:40-46; grep for 'organization_step' across Go .go files returns nothing. Wrong references: 0-bootstrap/README.md:224-226,390-394; 0-bootstrap/README-GitHub.md:129; 0-bootstrap/README-GitLab.md:172; ONBOARDING.md:200,300.

**Verifier reasoning:** Fully confirmed against the actual source, exact line numbers included.

1. TF reference (0-bootstrap/outputs.tf) exports the long-form names the claim assumes: bootstrap_step_terraform_service_account_email, organization_step_terraform_service_account_email, environment_step_terraform_service_account_email, networks_step_terraform_service_account_email, projects_step_terraform_service_account_email, gcs_bucket_tfstate, projects_gcs_bucket_tfstate.

2. Go port's actual exports (main.go:78-121, using sa.go granularSAs map keys "bootstrap","org","env","net","proj"): seed_project_id, cloudbuild_project_id, gcs_bucket_tfstate, projects_gcs_bucket_tfstate, state_bucket_kms_key_id, {bootstrap,org,env,net,proj}_step_terraform_service_account_email, common_config, required_groups, optional_groups, wif_pool_name, wif_provider_name. `grep -rn "organization_step" --include="*.go" .` across the whole Go repo returns nothing; no `_sa_email`-style export key appears anywhere in Go .go files (only an unrelated config key `networks_sa_email` in 1-org).

3. Docs verified line-for-line as cited: README.md:224-226 literally reads `cicd_project_id` and `tf_state_bucket` (real names: cloudbuild_project_id, gcs_bucket_tfstate). README.md:388-394 has an Outputs table listing tf_state_bucket, bootstrap_sa_email, org_sa_email, env_sa_email, net_sa_email, proj_sa_email — none of which are ever exported. README-GitHub.md:129 and README-GitLab.md:172 both say `pulumi stack output {stage}_sa_email`. ONBOARDING.md:~200 uses `cicd_project_id`; ONBOARDING.md:~300 has `pulumi ... stack output organization_step_terraform_service_account_email`, but the Go key is `org_step_terraform_service_account_email`.

4. Cross-checked the TS port (pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/index.ts:175-211): it exports the correct long-form names (organization_step_terraform_service_account_email, cloudbuild_project_id, gcs_bucket_tfstate, etc.) matching TF exactly — confirming the long names exist only in TS, and the Go docs appear to have been copied from TF/TS without adjusting for Go's shorter key convention.

No alias/wrapper exporting the doc-referenced names exists anywhere in the Go port or in pulumi-library (go/ or ts/). The underlying data (SA emails, project ID, bucket name) does exist in Go under different keys, so this is technically a doc/naming-consistency defect rather than an absent capability, but every specific fact in the claim — wrong names, exact locations, TS-only long-form names — checks out exactly. Severity "major" is reasonable: it affects the primary documented onboarding flow (Step 1 SA handoff, CI/CD secret setup) across four separate doc files, not a single cosmetic typo, so no downgrade correction is warranted.

#### [BOTH] Group creation lacks the quota/billing project mechanism (user_project_override)
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's provider.tf configures google-beta with user_project_override=true and billing_project=var.groups.billing_project, and variables.tf validates that billing_project is set whenever create_required_groups/create_optional_groups is true — Cloud Identity group creation requires a quota project. The TS port reads groups_billing_project into config but never uses it; the Go port doesn't even read it; neither port configures a provider with user_project_override/billing_project nor validates the setting, so the opt-in group-creation feature will fail with quota-project errors.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/provider.tf + variables.tf groups validation block. Checked: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/config.ts line 122 (read, unused), groups.ts; pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/main.go loadConfig + groups.go; pulumi-library go/pkg/group/group.go and ts/packages/google-group/src/index.ts (no provider/billing project handling).

**Verifier reasoning:** Every element of the claim verified. (1) TF side exists as described: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/provider.tf lines 17-20 configure google-beta with user_project_override=true and billing_project=var.groups.billing_project, and variables.tf lines 142-143 validate that billing_project is non-null whenever create_required_groups or create_optional_groups is true. (2) TS port: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/config.ts line 122 reads groups_billing_project into cfg.groups.billingProject, but nothing consumes it — groups.ts constructs GoogleGroup with only id/displayName/description/initialGroupConfig/customerId, and the shared component (/home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/google-group/src/index.ts) accepts no provider, billing project, or quota project argument. No `new gcp.Provider` exists anywhere in the TS 0-bootstrap, and no gcp:billingProject/gcp:userProjectOverride appears in any stack config or Pulumi.production.yaml.example. No validation of the setting exists. (3) Go port: the config loader in main.go never reads groups_billing_project (repo-wide grep for billing_project/billingProject in Go 0-bootstrap source returns nothing), groups.go passes no billing/provider args to group.NewGroup, /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/group/group.go has no provider handling, and no NewProvider call exists anywhere in the Go port. (4) Possible equivalents ruled out: helpers/foundation-deployer/deploy.sh in both ports exports GOOGLE_BILLING_PROJECT, but only AFTER the 0-bootstrap stage completes (for downstream stages), so it cannot cover group creation which runs during 0-bootstrap; USER_PROJECT_OVERRIDE is never set anywhere in either port or the library, and billing_project is ignored by the provider unless user_project_override is enabled. The only BillingProject usages in pulumi-library (cai-monitoring) are unrelated resources. Severity note: the feature is opt-in (create_required_groups/create_optional_groups default false), so only users who enable group creation are affected — but for those users the documented feature will fail at deploy time with Cloud Identity quota-project errors when using user ADC credentials, and there is no config plumbing, provider option, or documented workaround in either port. 'Major' is a fair rating for a fully broken documented feature present in the reference implementation.

#### [GO] No Cloud Build private worker pool component in the Go library (TS has cb-private-pool)
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's default CI/CD (build_cb.tf) provisions a private worker pool via 0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool (peered network, optional VPN-HA to on-prem, VPC flow logs) and exports cloud_build_private_worker_pool_id which 1-org's cloudbuild.allowedWorkerPools org policy consumes. The Go port's Cloud Build alternative (build_cloud_build.go.example + pkg/cicd) has no worker pool support at all — grep for WorkerPool in pulumi-library/go and the port finds nothing except the org-policy consumer — so the Cloud Build option cannot be deployed with the private-pool security posture, and 1-org's enforce_allowed_worker_pools depends on an externally created pool ID. The TS library ships packages/cb-private-pool with PrivateWorkerPoolConfig, VpnConfig and flow-logs support, so this is Go-side drift.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/{main,network,vpn_ha}.tf; 0-bootstrap/outputs_cb.tf:51 cloud_build_private_worker_pool_id. Go: no WorkerPool hits in pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd or pulumi_go-example-foundation (only the policy consumer in 1-org/policies.go:146). TS: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool/src/index.ts.

**Verifier reasoning:** The claim is fully substantiated on both sides.

TF side confirmed: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/{main,network,vpn_ha,outputs}.tf exists and is wired in via build_cb.tf:147 (`module "tf_private_pool"`), with outputs_cb.tf:51-64 exporting cloud_build_private_worker_pool_id, cloud_build_worker_range_id, cloud_build_worker_peered_ip_range, and cloud_build_peered_network_id. 1-org/envs/shared/org_policy.tf:44,135-146 consumes cloud_build_private_worker_pool_id via data.terraform_remote_state to populate the cloudbuild.allowedWorkerPools org policy, gated by var.enforce_allowed_worker_pools.

Go port confirmed missing: grepping the entire pulumi-library/go tree and pulumi_go-example-foundation for WorkerPool/worker_pool/private-pool/VpnConfig/PrivateWorkerPoolConfig turns up nothing except (a) "WorkloadIdentityPool" in pkg/cicd (an unrelated OIDC/WIF concept for keyless auth, not a Cloud Build compute pool), (b) the IAM role string "roles/cloudbuild.workerPoolOwner" in 0-bootstrap/sa.go (a role grant with no pool to own), and (c) 1-org/main.go + policies.go, which read AllowedWorkerPoolID straight from a plain config value (conf.Get("allowed_worker_pool_id")) rather than any Pulumi-managed resource — i.e., the org-policy consumer exists but nothing in the Go stack can produce that ID. build_cloud_build.go.example even name-drops a `cloud_build_private_pool_subnet` config key in its header comment but never implements or uses it anywhere in the body.

TS side confirmed present: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool/src/index.ts implements CbPrivatePool as a real ComponentResource with PrivateWorkerPoolConfig, VpnConfig, and VpcFlowLogsConfig interfaces, creating the peered Network/subnet (with flow-logs fields), a DNS policy, a GlobalAddress + servicenetworking.Connection + NetworkPeeringRoutesConfig for VPC peering — a faithful port of the TF module's network.tf behavior. It is actually wired up and used in pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_cb.ts.example, which imports CbPrivatePool and returns privateWorkerPoolId as a real stack output.

This is genuine, verifiable Go-side drift: the Go library has no equivalent component, so the Go port's Cloud Build CI/CD alternative cannot stand up the private-pool security posture, and its 1-org allowed-worker-pools policy is left depending on an externally supplied ID with nothing in the Go codebase able to produce one.

**Verifier note / correction:** The gap itself is real and well-evidenced, but "major" is a slightly generous severity label worth tempering: in both ports (not just Go), Cloud Build is an opt-in alternative CI/CD backend shipped as a `.go.example`/`.ts.example` file — GitHub Actions (via Workload Identity Federation) is the port's enabled-by-default path, unlike upstream TF where Cloud Build is the default. So the missing capability only bites a user who deliberately switches the Go port to the Cloud Build option, not out-of-the-box users. Within that scope the severity is legitimate (a security-relevant network-isolation feature is completely absent, not just degraded), so I'd keep it close to major/moderate rather than downgrade it further.

#### [BOTH] No builder-image automation (tf_cloudbuild_builder equivalent): Dockerfile is never built or published
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's build_cb.tf wires the tf_cloudbuild_builder module (Cloud Build trigger + Workflow + Cloud Scheduler that builds and refreshes the terraform runner image in Artifact Registry), pushes the Dockerfile to the tf-cloudbuilder repo via 0-bootstrap/scripts/push-to-repo.sh, and immediately runs the trigger (module.build_terraform_image). Both ports ship a 0-bootstrap/Dockerfile and create an empty 'pulumi-builders'/'tf-runners' Artifact Registry repo, but nothing builds or pushes the image, and no trigger/workflow/scheduler keeps it updated. The Cloud Build trigger yamls in README-CloudBuild.md reference '.../pulumi-builders/pulumi:latest', an image that will never exist, so every Cloud Build run fails to pull its builder.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/build_cb.tf modules tf_cloud_builder (lines 167-185), bootstrap_csr_repo/push-to-repo.sh (187-194), build_terraform_image (205-219). Ports: pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd/cloudbuild.go creates only artifactregistry.Repository (lines 164-188); TS 0-bootstrap/build_cb.ts.example creates only the AR repo; no workflow/scheduler component exists in pulumi-library (grep 'cloudscheduler|Workflow' matches only vpc-service-controls).

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed: 0-bootstrap/build_cb.tf really does wire module.tf_cloud_builder (tf_cloudbuild_builder: builds the Dockerfile via Cloud Build trigger + Workflow + Cloud Scheduler refresh), module.bootstrap_csr_repo (runs scripts/push-to-repo.sh to push the Dockerfile into the tf-cloudbuilder CSR repo), and module.build_terraform_image (runs `gcloud beta builds triggers run` to build the image immediately) — all as part of Terraform's DEFAULT Cloud-Build-based CI/CD.

On the port side, the technical detail in the claim holds up: neither pulumi-library (go/ or ts/) nor either port contains any Workflow, Cloud Scheduler, or trigger-run automation that builds and pushes the 0-bootstrap/Dockerfile into an Artifact Registry repo. pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd/cloudbuild.go creates only artifactregistry.Repository + cloudbuild.Trigger resources (no image build/push step); the TS build_cb.ts.example/build_cloud_build.ts.example likewise only create the `tf-runners`/`pulumi-builders` AR repo. There IS a generic `pkg/gcloud` component in the library (go/pkg/gcloud/gcloud.go, ts/packages/gcloud) that explicitly says 'Mirrors: terraform-google-modules/gcloud/google (local-exec provisioner pattern)' — i.e., built to be the tool for exactly this kind of push-to-repo/build-trigger-run job — but it is never invoked anywhere in 0-bootstrap for this purpose in either port. So the automation gap is real and not hidden behind an equivalent under another name.

However, the claimed scope and severity are inflated. Both ports made an architectural default-provider switch: GitHub Actions with Workload Identity Federation is the DEFAULT CI/CD (build_github_actions.go/.ts, wired into main.go/index.ts), and it runs on standard `ubuntu-latest`/GitHub-hosted runners with zero dependency on any Artifact Registry builder image (verified: no docker/image/artifactregistry references in build_github_actions.go, and .github/workflows/ci.yml uses runs-on: ubuntu-latest). Cloud Build is kept only as an explicitly-labeled 'Alternative'/'Example' path (README-GitHub.md literally says 'This is the default CI/CD configuration'; README.md table marks Cloud Build '📄 Example'; the wiring files are named build_cloud_build.go.example / build_cb.ts.example / build_cloud_build.ts.example — inert suffixes that are never compiled/run unless a user manually renames them and edits main.go/index.ts). So, contrary to how the claim describes it, neither port creates the AR repo or references the Dockerfile at all in its default deployed state — that only happens if a user opts into the non-default Cloud Build path by following README-CloudBuild.md. The 'every Cloud Build run fails to pull its builder' consequence is real only for that opt-in path, not for the foundation's actual default pipeline.

**Verifier note / correction:** Real gap, but severity should be downgraded from major to minor/moderate: the missing build/push/refresh automation for the tf-runner Docker image only affects the explicitly-documented 'alternative/example' Cloud Build CI/CD path, which is opt-in and not wired into main.go/index.ts by default. The default (and only fully-functional) CI/CD path in both ports is GitHub Actions with WIF, which runs on stock GitHub-hosted runners and has no dependency on the Dockerfile or Artifact Registry repo at all. Users who never touch the Cloud Build alternative never encounter this gap. It remains a legitimate incompleteness in the optional Cloud Build path (the library even has an unused `pkg/gcloud` local-Command component that could implement the equivalent of push-to-repo.sh/build_terraform_image but was never wired up there), and should be fixed or at least documented as a manual prerequisite step in README-CloudBuild.md, but it does not block or break the foundation's default deployment as the claim implies.

#### [TS] Org-admin impersonation and state-bucket IAM grants missing in TS
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF (sa_enable_impersonation=true) grants group_org_admins roles/storage.admin on the state bucket (orgadmins_state_iam) and roles/serviceusage.serviceUsageConsumer at org/parent level; the GitHub example additionally grants each stage SA serviceAccountTokenCreator on itself (self_impersonate). The Go port implements all of these plus org-admin TokenCreator/User on each SA (sa.go sections 8 and 12). The TS port implements none: stateBucketIamMembers is passed as [], and no serviceAccountTokenCreator/serviceAccountUser/BucketIAMMember/serviceUsageConsumer-for-group grants exist anywhere in the TS stage.

**Evidence:** TF: terraform-google-bootstrap v12 main.tf orgadmins_state_iam, org_admin_serviceusage_consumer, org_admin_sa_user/impersonate; 0-bootstrap/build_github.tf.example google_service_account_iam_member.self_impersonate. Checked: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/index.ts line 95 (stateBucketIamMembers: []), sa.ts, build_github_actions.ts — grep for serviceAccountTokenCreator/serviceAccountUser/BucketIAM returns nothing.

**Verifier reasoning:** The claim survives adversarial verification. TF side: 0-bootstrap/main.tf:60 sets sa_enable_impersonation=true on terraform-google-bootstrap ~>12.0; the module (v12.0.0 source verified) then creates orgadmins_state_iam (group_org_admins gets roles/storage.admin on the seed state bucket) and org_admin_serviceusage_consumer (group gets roles/serviceusage.serviceUsageConsumer at org, or folder variant plus folder-level serviceAccountUser under a parent folder). build_github.tf.example:113 creates self_impersonate (each stage SA gets roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator on itself). The Go port implements all of these in 0-bootstrap/sa.go sections 8 and 12. The TS port implements none: 0-bootstrap/index.ts:95 passes stateBucketIamMembers: [] and the library component (pulumi-library/ts/packages/bootstrap/src/bootstrap.ts:171-180) creates bucket IAM only from that array; sa.ts has no TokenCreator/serviceAccountUser grants (its serviceUsageConsumer entries are org-level roles for the bootstrap/proj SAs, mirroring sa.tf, not the group); index.ts org-admins roles (lines 138-167) include neither storage.admin on the bucket nor serviceUsageConsumer; build_github_actions.ts has only workloadIdentityUser WIF bindings. Repo-wide and library-wide greps for serviceAccountTokenCreator, serviceAccountUser, BucketIAMMember, and serviceUsageConsumer-for-group found no equivalent under any name (the sole TS TokenCreator hit is a CAI role-monitoring list in 1-org, not a grant).

**Verifier note / correction:** Two refinements: (1) The TF module's org_admin_sa_user/org_admin_sa_impersonate_permissions are gated on create_terraform_sa && sa_enable_impersonation, and the foundation sets create_terraform_sa=false (main.tf:61), so those grants are NOT actually created in TF — the Go port's org-admin TokenCreator/User on each granular SA is an extension beyond TF parity, and its absence in TS is not a TF-parity gap. The genuine TF-parity gaps are: group storage.admin on the state bucket, group serviceUsageConsumer at org/parent (plus folder-level serviceAccountUser when under a parent folder), and per-SA self-impersonation TokenCreator in the GitHub build. (2) Severity leans moderate rather than major: stage SAs still reach the state bucket via project-level storage roles in sa.ts (mirroring TF sa.tf), and CI auth works via the present workloadIdentityUser bindings — the loss is org-admin break-glass/impersonation workflows and SA self-token-minting for some WIF flows, not pipeline function.

#### [TS] TS README-CloudBuild.md instructions do not match the code: bogus diff target and ignores the two shipped Cloud Build example files
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Step 1 tells the operator to hand-write 0-bootstrap/build_cb.ts, even though the repo already ships build_cb.ts.example (full CI/CD project + triggers + worker pool implementation) and build_cloud_build.ts.example — neither is mentioned in the README. Step 2 shows a diff replacing 'const cbOutputs = await deployCloudbuild(cfg, bootstrapFolder, seedProject, stateBucket, stateBucketKmsKey);' but index.ts contains no such call — the actual calls are deployCICDProject(cfg, bootstrapFolder) at index.ts:104 and deployGitHubActionsWIF at index.ts:127. The two shipped example files also carry mutually inconsistent in-file instructions (build_cloud_build.ts.example header says to rename 'build_cb.ts', a file that does not exist in active form). An operator cannot complete the Cloud Build switch from these docs.

**Evidence:** pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/README-CloudBuild.md:34-65 vs pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/index.ts:104,127 (deployCICDProject/deployGitHubActionsWIF; no deployCloudbuild call); shipped but unreferenced: 0-bootstrap/build_cb.ts.example, build_cloud_build.ts.example (header lines 10-17). Go counterpart README-CloudBuild.md:34-44 correctly references its build_cloud_build.go.example.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified directly against the files cited. pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/index.ts contains no `deployCloudbuild(...)` call anywhere; the real orchestration calls are `deployCICDProject(cfg, bootstrapFolder)` at line 104 and `deployGitHubActionsWIF(...)` at line 127 (both from ./build_github_actions), so README-CloudBuild.md's Step 2 diff (lines 63-64, referencing `deployCloudbuild`/`deployCloudBuildCI`) targets code that doesn't exist. Step 1 (lines 34-56) tells the operator to hand-write a new build_cb.ts from a toy snippet, ignoring two fully-implemented shipped files: build_cb.ts.example (exports deployCloudbuild, mirrors build_cb.tf) and build_cloud_build.ts.example (exports deployCloudBuild, different casing) — neither is named in the README. The two example files are also internally inconsistent: build_cloud_build.ts.example's header instructs renaming/removing a 'build_cb.ts' that does not exist as an active file (only *.ts.example variants exist; the real active default is build_github_actions.ts, unmentioned in that header). By contrast, the Go port's README-CloudBuild.md (lines 34-44) correctly references real files (build_github_actions.go -> .bak, build_cloud_build.go.example -> .go) and a diff target consistent with Go's actual main.go structure, confirming this is a TS-specific documentation defect rather than an intentional shared pattern. An operator following the TS README literally cannot complete the switch. The claim's technical assertions are all accurate.

#### [TS] TS README-GitLab.md is skeletal and omits the WIF provider switch; referenced gitlab-ci.yml has no GCP auth step
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The TS GitLab guide is 43 lines (Go's equivalent is 184 lines; TF's is 983). It never mentions activating build_gitlab.ts.example — the code that creates the GitLab OIDC WIF provider — so an operator following it still has only the GitHub-issuer WIF provider and GitLab jobs cannot authenticate. Its CI/CD variable table lists PULUMI_ACCESS_TOKEN and GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY, but the referenced template build/gitlab-ci.yml declares required variables GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER and GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT instead — and the template's jobs contain no GCP authentication logic at all (no id_tokens block, no gcloud cred-config, nothing consuming any of those variables), so 'pulumi up' against GCP fails. The Go port's README-GitLab.md documents the full switch (activate build_gitlab.go.example, config keys, WIF vars, id_tokens auth snippet); none of that was carried into the TS guide.

**Evidence:** pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/README-GitLab.md (43 lines, variable table lines ~23-27) vs pulumi_ts-example-foundation/build/gitlab-ci.yml (header requires GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER/GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT; jobs only install pulumi + npm ci). Unreferenced code: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_gitlab.ts.example. Complete counterpart: pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/README-GitLab.md:39-172.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified end-to-end. TF: terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/README-GitLab.md is 983 lines and the TF foundation has build_gitlab.tf.example, variables_gitlab.tf.example, outputs_gitlab.tf.example, modules/gitlab-oidc, and build/gitlab-ci.yml — the WIF switch is real, substantial TF functionality. Go port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/README-GitLab.md (184 lines) fully documents the switch — activating build_gitlab.go.example, editing main.go's Config struct/loadConfig/build call, setting gitlab_* pulumi config keys, and gives a complete .gitlab-ci.yml with an id_tokens block and gcloud workload-identity-pools create-cred-config auth step. TS port: README-GitLab.md is exactly 43 lines, never mentions build_gitlab.ts.example by name (confirmed via repo-wide grep — that file is referenced nowhere else in the TS repo, so it's orphaned documentation-wise), never mentions any gitlab_* config keys, and never says to wire it into index.ts. build_gitlab.ts.example itself does contain real, working GCP WIF pool/provider/SA-binding creation code equivalent to the Go example, so the gap is purely in documentation/wiring, not missing engineering — but that's exactly what the claim alleges. The referenced build/gitlab-ci.yml compounds the problem: its header comment requires GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER/GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT while the README's variable table lists different variables (PULUMI_ACCESS_TOKEN, GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY), and the actual preview/deploy jobs contain zero GCP auth logic (no id_tokens, no gcloud cred-config, nothing consuming any GCP variable) — confirmed by reading the full 46-line file. Checked pulumi-library for a rescuing shared component: pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd/gitlab.go exists (a GitLabOIDC component), but pulumi-library/ts/packages has no cicd/gitlab/oidc/workload-identity package at all, so there's no library-level fallback either. Every specific fact in the claim (line counts, missing example activation, mismatched CI variables, absent auth logic) checks out as stated. Severity of 'major' is justified, not inflated: an operator who follows only the TS README as written ends up with a .gitlab-ci.yml that cannot authenticate to GCP at all, so pulumi preview/up would fail in GitLab CI — this isn't a merely informational/cosmetic gap.

#### [TS] TS docs reference stack output names that do not exist (cicd_project_id, {stage}_sa_email)
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TS 0-bootstrap/index.ts exports 'cloudbuild_project_id' and long-form '<stage>_step_terraform_service_account_email' names. But 0-bootstrap/README.md:219 and ONBOARDING.md:201 instruct 'export CICD_PROJECT_ID=$(pulumi stack output cicd_project_id)' — no such output exists (actual: cloudbuild_project_id). README-GitHub.md:134 tells operators to obtain the per-stage CI/CD service account via 'pulumi stack output {stage}_sa_email' — no *_sa_email outputs exist (actual: e.g. networks_step_terraform_service_account_email). These copy-paste commands fail during the documented GitHub secrets setup.

**Evidence:** Actual exports: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/index.ts:174-214 (seed_project_id, cloudbuild_project_id, *_step_terraform_service_account_email, wif_provider_name). Wrong references: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/README.md:219, 0-bootstrap/README-GitHub.md:134, ONBOARDING.md:201.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all points.

1) Actual TS stack outputs (pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/index.ts:172-215) are exactly as the reviewer states: seed_project_id, bootstrap_step_terraform_service_account_email, projects_step_terraform_service_account_email, networks_step_terraform_service_account_email, environment_step_terraform_service_account_email, organization_step_terraform_service_account_email, gcs_bucket_tfstate, projects_gcs_bucket_tfstate, state_bucket_kms_key_id, common_config, required_groups, optional_groups, cloudbuild_project_id, cloud_build_private_worker_pool_id, wif_pool_name, wif_provider_name. There is no `cicd_project_id` output and no `{stage}_sa_email` / `proj_sa_email` output anywhere in this file.

2) The docs do say what the reviewer claims:
   - README.md:219/334 and ONBOARDING.md:201 do `export CICD_PROJECT_ID=$(pulumi stack output cicd_project_id)` — wrong key, actual is `cloudbuild_project_id`.
   - README-GitHub.md:134 does `pulumi stack output {stage}_sa_email` — wrong key, actual is `{stage}_step_terraform_service_account_email`.
   - The same broken `_sa_email` naming also appears in docs/FAQ.md and docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md (`proj_sa_email`), showing this is a systemic naming-convention mismatch between docs and code, not a one-off typo.

3) I checked pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) and the rest of the ts port for any alias/shim that maps these documented names to the real outputs — none exists (grep for `cicd_project_id` / `_sa_email` in pulumi-library returns no matches).

4) On the Terraform side, `cicd_project_id` is a legitimate TF output, but only after running `scripts/choose_build_type.sh github` (or gitlab/terraform-cloud/local), which swaps in `outputs_github.tf.example` → `outputs_github.tf` (defining `output "cicd_project_id"`) in place of the default Cloud-Build `outputs_cb.tf` (`cloudbuild_project_id`). The TF README-GitHub.md instructs users to run that swap script before referencing `cicd_project_id`, so the TF docs are internally consistent. The TS port has no equivalent build-type-swap mechanism — it hardcodes GitHub Actions as default but never renamed the leftover Cloud-Build-era output key to match, so its own docs are self-inconsistent (code says `cloudbuild_project_id`, docs say `cicd_project_id`).

One mitigating nuance for severity: build_github_actions.ts (lines 231-266) auto-provisions the actual needed GitHub Actions secrets (WIF_PROVIDER_NAME, SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL, PROJECT_ID, PULUMI_BACKEND_URL) directly via the Pulumi GitHub provider when `github_owner`/`github:token` are configured, so the primary "happy path" onboarding doesn't strictly require the broken manual `pulumi stack output` commands to succeed end-to-end. But the commands are still documented as required/verification steps in multiple files and will fail exactly as the reviewer describes if run — this is a real, reproducible documentation defect, not a fabricated one.

#### [TS] TS drift: SA self-impersonation binding and iamcredentials API missing
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's GitHub and GitLab build types create google_service_account_iam_member.self_impersonate (roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator for each granular SA on itself, required for WIF impersonation flows) and activate iamcredentials.googleapis.com on the CI/CD project. The Go port mirrors both (sa.go lines 295-317; projects.go line 174 in ActivateApis). The TS port has neither: no serviceAccountTokenCreator binding anywhere in 0-bootstrap (grep matches only 1-org for an unrelated grant) and the CICD project's API list in build_github_actions.ts omits iamcredentials.googleapis.com.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/build_github.tf.example lines 88 (iamcredentials API) and 113-119 (self_impersonate); build_gitlab.tf.example lines 99-100, 171-177. Go: 0-bootstrap/sa.go:295-317, projects.go:174. TS: grep 'serviceAccountTokenCreator|iamcredentials' over pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap returns nothing; build_github_actions.ts API list lines 90-108.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all counts. TF side: 0-bootstrap/build_github.tf.example has iamcredentials.googleapis.com (line 88) and the self_impersonate google_service_account_iam_member resource (lines 113-119, role roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator); build_gitlab.tf.example mirrors this (lines 99-100, 171-177). Go port: sa.go contains an explicit "SA Self-Impersonation" loop granting roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator to each granular SA on itself, and projects.go's CICD ActivateApis list includes iamcredentials.googleapis.com. TS port: read sa.ts in full (216 lines) — it creates the granular SAs and wires org/parent/seed/cicd-project IAM bindings plus billing IAM, but there is no self-referential serviceaccount.IAMMember/tokenCreator binding anywhere. Grepping the entire TS repo for iamcredentials/TokenCreator/SelfImpersonate returns zero hits in 0-bootstrap (only unrelated hits in 1-org and 4-projects). build_github_actions.ts's cicdApis list (lines 90-108) and index.ts's seed-project API list (lines 73-92) both omit iamcredentials.googleapis.com entirely. build_gitlab.ts.example also has no self-impersonation or iamcredentials reference. Checked the generic ParentIamMember/ParentIamRemoveRole helper modules used by sa.ts — they are plain role-fanout wrappers with no special-cased self-binding logic, and the shared pulumi-library (both go/pkg and ts/packages, including bootstrap and parent-iam packages) has no equivalent self-impersonation or iamcredentials logic either. So the functionality is genuinely absent everywhere it could plausibly live in the TS port and its shared library.

On severity: "major" is reasonable to defensible, not inflated. This isn't a cosmetic/output-only gap — roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator on itself plus the iamcredentials API is what actually allows a granular SA to mint short-lived tokens for itself during WIF-based impersonation flows; its absence can cause real permission-denied failures in GitHub/GitLab Actions pipelines that rely on impersonation semantics (as opposed to direct WIF principal bindings, which TS does implement via gh-oidc-binding-${key} in build_github_actions.ts — so direct WIF federation to each SA does work, but self-impersonation token-minting does not). I did not find evidence the severity is overstated, so no correction is warranted.

#### [GO] build_gitlab.go.example grants workloadIdentityPoolAdmin on the SA resource instead of the CI/CD project
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF grants the bootstrap SA roles/iam.workloadIdentityPoolAdmin on the CI/CD project (module cicd_project_wif_iam_member via parent-iam-member with parent_type=project). The TS example matches (gcp.projects.IAMMember on cicdProjectId). The Go example instead creates serviceaccount.NewIAMMember with ServiceAccountId = bootstrapSA.Name, i.e., binds a project-level role on the service-account IAM policy — roles/iam.workloadIdentityPoolAdmin is not a valid role for a serviceAccount resource, so pulumi up fails on this resource (or at best grants nothing), and the bootstrap SA cannot manage the WIF pool on subsequent runs.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/build_gitlab.tf.example lines 161-169. Go: pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_gitlab.go.example lines 130-142 (serviceaccount.NewIAMMember{ServiceAccountId: bootstrapSA.Name, Role: workloadIdentityPoolAdmin}). TS correct: build_gitlab.ts.example lines 115-121 (gcp.projects.IAMMember).

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all three points. (1) TF: 0-bootstrap/build_gitlab.tf.example (lines ~161-169) has module "cicd_project_wif_iam_member" using ./modules/parent-iam-member with parent_type="project", parent_id=local.cicd_project_id, granting roles/iam.workloadIdentityPoolAdmin to the bootstrap terraform-env-sa on the CI/CD project — verified present exactly as described. (2) TS port: build_gitlab.ts.example (section 4, ~lines 113-122) correctly uses `new gcp.projects.IAMMember("bootstrap-wif-pool-admin", { project: cicdProjectId, role: "roles/iam.workloadIdentityPoolAdmin", member: ... })` — matches TF semantics (project-scoped IAM binding). (3) Go port: build_gitlab.go.example lines 132-134 instead calls `serviceaccount.NewIAMMember(ctx, "bootstrap-wif-pool-admin", &serviceaccount.IAMMemberArgs{ServiceAccountId: bootstrapSA.Name, Role: pulumi.String("roles/iam.workloadIdentityPoolAdmin"), ...})` — this sets an IAM policy binding scoped to the service-account resource, not the project. I checked the pulumi-gcp Go SDK (v9.20.0, vendored at /root/go/pkg/mod) and confirmed gcp/projects.IAMMember exists and was available to the Go port (the exact type-analog the TS port uses), so the correct resource existed but was not used. I also checked /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd/gitlab.go (the shared GitLabOIDC component both ports import) — it only creates the per-SA workloadIdentityUser bindings (also via serviceaccount.IAMMember, which is correct for that distinct purpose) and does NOT implement the project-level workloadIdentityPoolAdmin grant for the bootstrap SA anywhere; that grant is coded ad hoc in each port's build_gitlab.*.example file, and only the Go one gets it wrong. No other file in the Go port supplies the correct project-level grant elsewhere. roles/iam.workloadIdentityPoolAdmin is a project-level role for managing Workload Identity Pools and is not assignable on a serviceAccount IAM-policy resource, so applying this as written would fail or grant nothing — exactly as the claim states.

**Verifier note / correction:** The severity label 'major' is directionally reasonable but should be scoped: build_gitlab.go.example (like its .tf.example and .ts.example counterparts) is an inert, opt-in scaffold file — it is not compiled/built by default (the .go.example extension is excluded from `go build`), not wired into main.go, not referenced by any test, and even the config values inside it are placeholder empty strings requiring manual completion before use. So this bug does not affect the default bootstrap deployment at all and only manifests if a user explicitly follows the file's instructions to switch to GitLab CI/CD. Within that opt-in scope, the bug is as severe as claimed (it will fail on apply or grant nothing, diverging from both the TF reference and the sibling TS port), so I would characterize it as "major, but confined to an opt-in example path" rather than a defect affecting the default/critical bootstrap flow.

#### [BOTH] iamcredentials.googleapis.com not enabled on the seed project
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF enables IAM Credentials API on the seed project: the foundation sets sa_enable_impersonation=true, and the upstream module's impersonation_apis local adds serviceusage + iamcredentials to activate_apis. Both ports' seed API lists omit iamcredentials and the library Bootstrap components only auto-add cloudkms. The stage SAs live in the seed project, and WIF-based impersonation (google-github-actions/auth generateAccessToken) requires the API enabled on the SA-owning project, so the default GitHub Actions CI/CD auth flow is likely to fail. (Go enables it on the CI/CD project, but the SAs are in seed; TS enables it nowhere.)

**Evidence:** TF: terraform-google-bootstrap v12 main.tf locals base_apis/impersonation_apis + terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/main.tf sa_enable_impersonation=true. Checked: pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/projects.go ActivateApis (seed list, no iamcredentials), pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/index.ts activateApis, pulumi-library/go/pkg/bootstrap/bootstrap.go appendIfMissing (cloudkms only), ts/packages/bootstrap/src/bootstrap.ts.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified on every point. TF: 0-bootstrap/main.tf:60 sets sa_enable_impersonation=true, and the upstream terraform-google-bootstrap v12.0.0 main.tf (fetched from GitHub to confirm) computes activate_apis = impersonation_apis when that flag is true, where impersonation_apis appends serviceusage.googleapis.com and iamcredentials.googleapis.com — so TF enables iamcredentials on the seed project (and also on the CI/CD project via build_github.tf.example:88). Ports: Go seed list (0-bootstrap/projects.go:74-96) and TS seed list (0-bootstrap/index.ts:72-92) copy only the foundation's raw 21-API list without iamcredentials; the library Bootstrap components (pulumi-library/go/pkg/bootstrap/bootstrap.go:163, ts/packages/bootstrap/src/bootstrap.ts:55) auto-append only cloudkms. Exhaustive grep of all three repos found iamcredentials nowhere else relevant (only VPC-SC restricted-services lists and 4-projects confidential-space lists). Go enables it on the CI/CD project only (projects.go:173); TS enables it nowhere in 0-bootstrap (build_github_actions.ts CI/CD list lines 91-107 also lacks it). Impact is real: both ports create the stage SAs in the seed project (Go sa.go:50-51, TS sa.ts:131-132) and the default compiled-in GitHub Actions flow uses WIF with workloadIdentityUser bindings and SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL impersonation, whose generateAccessToken call requires iamcredentials enabled on the SA-owning (seed) project. Default CI/CD auth would fail on first run. Major severity is reasonable given it breaks the primary documented pipeline flow, though remediation is a one-line API addition.

#### [BOTH] validate_e2e.sh KMS keyring check queries location us-central1 but bootstrap creates the keyring in 'us' — false failure on a correct deployment
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** validate_e2e.sh asserts a KMS keyring exists in the seed project with `gcloud kms keyrings list --location=us-central1`. Both ports create the state-encryption keyring in DefaultRegionKMS, which defaults to 'us' (multi-region, matching upstream TF's default_region_kms). The check therefore returns empty on a correctly deployed foundation, assert_exists records a FAIL, and validate_e2e.sh exits 1 — failing the E2E workflow's Validate Resources job even when everything deployed correctly. Combined with the silently-skipped Phase 3/5 checks, the one E2E signal the ports have is unreliable in both directions.

**Evidence:** Check: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/test/validate_e2e.sh:117-122 (and identical TS copy). Keyring location: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/main.go:257-258 (DefaultRegionKMS default "us") passed via projects.go:61 to /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/bootstrap/bootstrap.go:151-154,212-215; TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/config.ts:104 (default "us") and /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/bootstrap/src/bootstrap.ts:95-98.

**Verifier reasoning:** The claim is accurate and confirmed by direct inspection.

1. TF side: validate_e2e.sh has no upstream analog in terraform-example-foundation (TF uses Terratest Go integration tests instead), so this is a script written for/by the Pulumi ports, not a literal TF port — but it is still bootstrap-area E2E test tooling in both ports, and the claimed defect is real regardless of TF provenance. TF's own terraform.example.tfvars confirms `default_region_kms = "us"` is the standard default, matching what both ports use.

2. Confirmed in both ports:
   - Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/main.go:257-258 defaults `DefaultRegionKMS` to `"us"`, passed through to /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/bootstrap/bootstrap.go:151 (`kmsRegion := args.DefaultRegionKMS`) and used at line 215 (`Location: pulumi.String(kmsRegion)`) when creating the `kms.NewKeyRing`.
   - TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/config.ts:104 defaults `defaultRegionKms` to `"us"`, used in /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/bootstrap/src/bootstrap.ts:95-98 (`location: kmsRegion`) when creating `gcp.kms.KeyRing`.
   - Both test/validate_e2e.sh scripts (Go path line 117-122, TS path line 117-122) query `gcloud kms keyrings list --location=us-central1`, a single hardcoded location string with no fallback to `us` and no other keyring check elsewhere in the script.
   - `assert_exists` fails on empty result, `FAIL` is incremented, and the script's tail (`if [ "$FAIL" -gt 0 ]; then ... exit 1`) causes non-zero exit.
   - Both `.github/workflows/e2e.yml` files have a job literally named "Validate Resources" that runs `./test/validate_e2e.sh` (line ~166), so this script's exit code gates that CI job.

Net effect: on a fully correct, standard-config deployment (keyring created in "us" per default), the keyring lookup at "us-central1" returns nothing, producing a false FAIL and a red/failing "Validate Resources" job even though the actual infrastructure is correct. This is a real, reproducible bug in both ports' E2E tooling.

Severity: I'd keep this at "major" as reasonably characterized (not "critical" only because it affects test/CI signal quality rather than actual production infrastructure, and is a one-line fix), consistent with the reviewer's own framing. No inflation to correct.

#### [GO] Bootstrap StackReference created but never consumed — group-based IAM silently skipped when config not duplicated
_Area: 1-org · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** 1-org/main.go creates the bootstrap StackReference and immediately discards it (`_ = bootstrapRef // Used for StackReference outputs in future enhancements`). All group emails (group_org_admins, audit_data_users, billing_data_users, optional governance groups), bootstrap_folder_name, and networks SA email must be manually re-entered as 1-org stack config. Every IAM binding in iam.go is guarded by `if cfg.X != ""`, so if the operator does not duplicate the values, audit-log viewer/BQ, billing viewer, security reviewer, SCC admin, KMS admin, secrets admin bindings and the bootstrap-folder tag binding are silently omitted. In TF these always flow from bootstrap remote state (remote.tf locals.required_groups); the TS port consumes bootstrapRef.getOutput("required_groups"/"optional_groups"/"common_config").

**Evidence:** Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/main.go:51-57 (unused ref, 'future enhancements' comment), 1-org/iam.go:60,81,114,124,134,163,176,241 (empty-string guards). TF: 1-org/envs/shared/remote.tf:21-34, iam.tf:21-63 (unconditional). TS: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:26-29.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified end-to-end.

TF side confirmed: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/remote.tf pulls org_id, parent_folder, billing_account, project_prefix, folder_prefix, networks_step_terraform_service_account_email, bootstrap_folder_name, and required_groups unconditionally from the 0-bootstrap terraform_remote_state data source (no operator re-entry). iam.tf:21-63 and essential_contacts.tf then consume local.required_groups (group_org_admins, group_billing_admins, audit_data_users, billing_data_users) with zero conditional guards — these bindings are always created. tags.tf:85 and projects.tf:309-314 likewise consume bootstrap_folder_name / networks_step_terraform_service_account_email straight from remote state.

Go port confirmed broken exactly as described: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/main.go:51-57 creates `bootstrapRef` via pulumi.NewStackReference and immediately does `_ = bootstrapRef // Used for StackReference outputs in future enhancements` — it is never called with GetOutput/GetStringOutput anywhere in the file or package. loadOrgConfig instead populates GroupOrgAdmins, GroupBillingAdmins, AuditDataUsers, BillingDataUsers, BootstrapFolderName, NetworksSAEmail, and all GCP*Admin/Viewer fields purely via `conf.Get(...)` (plain stack config). iam.go guards at exactly the cited lines (60, 81, 114, 124, 134, 163, 176, 241) plus tags.go:112 (bootstrap folder tag) and iam.go:265 (networks SA hub-and-spoke roles) all skip silently on empty string. I confirmed no other file/module in the Go port or in /home/user/pulumi-library (go/ or ts/) wires bootstrap outputs into 1-org config — grepping the library for StackReference/required_groups/optional_groups returns nothing.

Crucially, I checked whether the operator is instructed to duplicate these values manually (which would only demote severity, not refute the gap) — the port's own ONBOARDING.md and Pulumi.production.yaml.example never ask for group_org_admins/audit_data_users/billing_data_users/bootstrap_folder_name/networks_sa_email at the 1-org step (only org_id, billing_account, bootstrap_stack_name, domains_to_allow). So under the documented workflow, these bindings will be silently omitted in every default deployment, not just in a misconfiguration edge case — this makes the "major" severity assessment fair, if anything conservative.

One correction to the claim's framing: the "optional governance groups" (security_reviewer, network_viewer, scc_admin, audit_viewer, global_secrets_admin, kms_admin) do NOT actually flow from bootstrap remote state in TF either — TF's own `gcp_groups` variable in 1-org/envs/shared/variables.tf:142 defaults to `{}` (all null) and must be set directly as a 1-org-level input regardless of bootstrap. So requiring manual config for those specific fields in the Go port is not a deviation from TF; only the "required groups" (org_admins/billing_admins/audit_data_users/billing_data_users), bootstrap_folder_name, and networks_step_terraform_service_account_email are the items TF sources automatically and unconditionally — and those are the ones genuinely broken in the Go port.

Also worth noting (does not change the Go verdict): the claim's contrast with TS is weaker than presented. pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:28-29 does call `bootstrapRef.getOutput("required_groups"/"optional_groups")`, but these Outputs are never merged into `cfg` — config.ts:111-112 populates `cfg.requiredGroups`/`cfg.optionalGroups` from local `config.getObject("required_groups"/"optional_groups")`, i.e. also manual stack config, making the TS `getOutput` calls dead code for that piece too. Only `bootstrapFolderName` is genuinely threaded through in TS via `commonConfig.apply(...)` at index.ts:36/430. So TS is only partially better than Go, not fully correct as implied — but that's a separate, TS-side observation, not a refutation of the Go finding.

**Verifier note / correction:** Core claim holds for the Go port: bootstrapRef is created and discarded, and the required-groups/bootstrap_folder_name/networks-SA IAM bindings are genuinely silently skippable and, per the port's own onboarding docs, WILL be skipped by default (not just a `if forgotten` risk). Minor overstatement: TF does not source the "optional governance groups" (security_reviewer, network_viewer, scc_admin, audit_viewer, global_secrets_admin, kms_admin) from bootstrap remote state either — those are manual 1-org-level `gcp_groups` variables in TF too, so the Go port's manual config for that subset is not itself a regression. The real regression is limited to required_groups (group_org_admins, group_billing_admins, audit_data_users, billing_data_users), bootstrap_folder_name, and networks_step_terraform_service_account_email, which TF always auto-populates unconditionally but the Go port requires (and does not document) manual duplication for.

#### [TS] CAI monitoring Cloud Function source (index.js) missing from repo — gitignored
_Area: 1-org · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The TS port wires CAIMonitoring with functionSourcePath './cai-monitoring-function', but that directory contains only package.json — index.js is absent because 1-org/.gitignore line 7 ('*.js') swallowed it. Enabling enable_cai_monitoring uploads an archive with no function code, so the cloudfunctionsv2.Function build fails (entrypoint caiMonitoring not found) and the stage errors. This also means the SCC v2 client migration of the function source (upstream commit a4ea938 changed index.js to require('@google-cloud/security-center').v2) is unverifiable/absent in TS. The Go port ships the full source at 1-org/cai-monitoring-function/index.js and does use the v2 client.

**Evidence:** TF: 1-org/modules/cai-monitoring/function-source/index.js (updated to SCC v2 in commit a4ea938). TS port: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:467 references ./cai-monitoring-function; ls of /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/cai-monitoring-function shows only package.json; /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/.gitignore:7 '*.js' matches the missing file (git check-ignore confirms). Go port has /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/cai-monitoring-function/index.js using SecurityCenterClient from '@google-cloud/security-center'.v2.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim fully confirmed on all points.

1. TF side checked out: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/modules/cai-monitoring/function-source/index.js exists and does use the SCC v2 client (`const { SecurityCenterClient } = require('@google-cloud/security-center').v2;`), matching the described upstream commit a4ea938 behavior. The module is wired into 1-org/envs/shared/cai_monitoring.tf, gated by var.enable_scc_resources_in_terraform, so it's a real (opt-in) feature, not dead code.

2. TS port: exhaustive search (`find ... -iname index.js` across the whole pulumi_ts-example-foundation repo) turns up zero index.js files anywhere. The only file in 1-org/cai-monitoring-function/ is package.json. The shared component in /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/cai-monitoring/src/index.ts (imported via `@vitruviansoftware/foundation-cai-monitoring` at 1-org/index.ts:19/462/467) does `new pulumi.asset.FileArchive(args.functionSourcePath)` against `./cai-monitoring-function` and deploys a gcp.cloudfunctionsv2.Function with `entryPoint: "caiMonitoring"` — so a real index.js exporting `caiMonitoring` is required at deploy time, not optional. git check-ignore confirms 1-org/.gitignore:7 (`*.js`, intended for compiled TS output) also matches this hand-written JS Cloud Function source. Git history nails the causal story: commit fe71529 ("add ... CAI monitoring function") only added 1-org/cai-monitoring-function/package.json — index.js was never committed, almost certainly silently dropped by `git add` due to the gitignore rule and never noticed since gitignored files don't show as untracked in `git status`. There is no alternate name/location/inline-asset implementation anywhere in the port or the shared library that substitutes for it — 1-org/modules/cai-monitoring/index.ts is merely a re-export shim, not a second implementation.

3. Go port comparison confirms the contrast is real: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/cai-monitoring-function/index.js (6899 bytes) is present and is byte-for-byte identical in content lineage to the TF source (uses the v2 SCC client).

Severity check: enable_cai_monitoring defaults to `false` (1-org/config.ts:97, `config.getBoolean("enable_cai_monitoring") ?? false`) and TF's equivalent gate also defaults off, so this only bites when a user opts into the feature — it is not a default/first-run breakage. But when enabled, it is a hard, total failure (Cloud Function build fails outright, no partial functionality), so "major" is a defensible severity for the feature itself even though the blast radius is narrowed to CAI-monitoring adopters only.

**Verifier note / correction:** Minor nuance only, not a refutation: the feature is opt-in (enable_cai_monitoring defaults to false in both TF and the TS port), so this breaks only users who explicitly enable CAI monitoring, not the default/base deployment of 1-org. Severity "major" is reasonable for that feature's users (total, unrecoverable build failure), but reviewers should note it's not a default-path outage.

#### [TS] Org policies cannot be applied at folder scope (parent_folder deployments mutate the whole org)
_Area: 1-org · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF applies every org policy at folder level when parent_folder is set (policy_for = "folder") so the foundation can be sandboxed under a folder; the Go library OrgPolicy takes a ParentID ("organizations/…" or "folders/…") and 1-org/policies.go switches on cfg.ParentFolder. The TS org-policy package (OrgPolicyBoolean/OrgPolicyList/DomainRestrictedSharing) hardcodes parent organizations/<orgId>, and TS 1-org passes cfg.orgId unconditionally — folder-scoped deployment silently enforces policies org-wide.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/org_policy.tf:17-25 (policy_for/folder_id locals). Go: /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/policy/org_policy.go:27-33 + 1-org/policies.go:37-38. TS: /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/org-policy/src/index.ts:23-25,67-69,90-92 (parent: organizations/${orgId} only); 1-org/index.ts:81-124.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified end-to-end. TF (1-org/envs/shared/org_policy.tf:17-25) genuinely switches policy_for between \"organization\" and \"folder\" based on parent_folder, passing organization_id/folder_id accordingly to every org-policy module call. The Go port correctly mirrors this: pulumi-library/go/pkg/policy/org_policy.go takes a generic ParentID, and pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/policies.go:35-38 computes parentID as \"organizations/\"+OrgID or \"folders/\"+ParentFolder and threads it through all policy calls. The TS port genuinely lacks this: pulumi-library/ts/packages/org-policy/src/index.ts's OrgPolicyBoolean/OrgPolicyList/DomainRestrictedSharing components only accept orgId and hardcode parent: `organizations/${orgId}` — folder scoping isn't representable in the API at all. pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/config.ts DOES compute folder-aware parent/parentId/parentType (and these are correctly used elsewhere in the same port — iam.ts's orgOrFolderIAMMember helper, and index.ts:510-511 for Access Context Manager's parent_resource_id/parent_resource_type), but every org-policy call site in 1-org/index.ts (6 call sites: boolean policies loop, deny-vm-external-ip, restrict-protocol-forwarding, essential-contacts-domain-restriction, allowed-worker-pools, domain-restricted-sharing) passes cfg.orgId unconditionally. No alternate folder-scoped policies file or overload exists anywhere in the TS port or pulumi-library/ts. This is a real, narrowly-isolated regression versus both TF and the sibling Go port, and given the whole point of parent_folder is to sandbox the foundation's blast radius, silently falling back to org-wide enforcement is a legitimate major-severity finding, not inflated.</explanation>

Library: /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/policy/org_policy_test.go etc. test components only.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified on all fronts.

(1) TF side: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/test/integration/org/org_test.go is 491 lines, a single TestOrg function with an extensive sequence of assertions matching the description — vmExternalIpAccess DENY policy check (line 181-182), requireOsLogin check (184-186), SCC notification project/name checks (189-201), _Default sink exclusion check on the audit logs project (254-256), CAI monitoring service-account check (304), billing sink names output check (355), and per-env shared_vpc_project_id/shared_vpc_projects output checks (465-478). The TF functionality is real and matches the claim's description.

(2) Go port: confirmed via `find` that 0-bootstrap, 2-environments, 3-networks-hub-and-spoke, 3-networks-svpc, 4-projects, and 5-app-infra all have config_test.go, while the full directory listing of 1-org contains zero files matching *_test*: only Pulumi.yaml, README, and the .go source files (main.go, folders.go, iam.go, policies.go, projects.go, scc.go, logging.go, tags.go, essential_contacts.go, cai_monitoring.go, modules/).

(3) TS port: confirmed test/ contains 0-bootstrap, 2-environments, 3-networks, 4-projects, 5-app-infra config.test.ts files but nothing for 1-org, and the 1-org directory itself has no test files either.

(4) validate_e2e.sh Phase 1 (lines 141-171) does exactly 4 assert_exists checks (common folder, prj-c-logging, prj-c-billing, prj-c-scc project IDs) with no assertions at all about org policies, domain-restricted-sharing, log-export sinks/exclusions, CAI monitoring, essential contacts, or KMS/secrets/interconnect projects — and grep confirms 1-org's projects.go does create org-kms, org-secrets, and org-interconnect projects (plus per-env svpc host projects), none of which validate_e2e.sh checks.

(5) pulumi-library test files (org_policy_test.go, log_export/logging test files, cai_monitoring_test.go in go/, and org-policy.test.ts/log-export.test.ts in ts/) are confirmed to be component-level unit tests, not stage-wiring tests — they cannot substitute for verifying which constraints/sinks/filters 1-org's own code actually wires up.

Every specific data point in the claim (line counts, file names, line ranges, check counts) checks out under direct inspection.

One minor nuance for calibration: the other stages' config_test.go files are lighter-weight config/unit tests (testing config parsing, defaults, struct construction) rather than TF-style live integration tests, so "zero tests" for 1-org is a gap relative to an already-modest bar set by sibling stages, not a gap relative to TF's live-integration rigor (no stage in either port replicates that level). This doesn't change the verdict but tempers how much rigor was actually lost — the delta is "no unit/config test at all" vs "a modest config test," not "no test" vs "full deployed-infra integration test."

#### [BOTH] No resource dependency between the CAI builder service account and the Cloud Function that builds with it
_Area: 1-org/cai-monitoring · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform passes google_service_account.cai_monitoring_builder[0].email into the cai-monitoring module (an explicit dependency) and adds waits before creating the function. Both ports construct the builder SA email as a string derived only from the SCC project id, and the SA itself is created in a separate IAM function with no DependsOn/edge to the CAIMonitoring component — Pulumi may create the Cloud Function before the SA and its logging/storage/artifactregistry roles exist, causing fresh-deploy failures. In TS this is worse: the builder SA is gated on enable_scc_resources while the function is gated on the separate enable_cai_monitoring flag, so enable_cai_monitoring=true with enable_scc_resources=false deterministically references a nonexistent build SA.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/cai_monitoring.tf:24 + sa.tf + modules/cai-monitoring/iam.tf:71-77 (time_sleep). Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/cai_monitoring.go:60-73 (string-built email) vs SA creation in iam.go:290-316 (called later in main.go step 8, no dependency). TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:459-475 (buildServiceAccount interpolated from project id, dependsOn only sccNotifications) vs SA creation in iam.ts:255-276 gated on enableSccResources.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified in all four repos. Terraform (envs/shared/cai_monitoring.tf:24) passes google_service_account.cai_monitoring_builder[0].email into the module, creating a hard dependency: the builder SA (sa.tf:17-23) must exist before the Cloud Function is created, and both are gated on the single enable_scc_resources_in_terraform flag. In the Go port, cai_monitoring.go:62-64 string-formats the SA email from the SCC project id only; the SA is created in iam.go:290-316 inside deployOrgIAM which returns only error (SA resource never exposed), and neither main.go:98 nor the pulumi-library component (go/pkg/security/cai_monitoring.go — BuildServiceAccount is a plain StringInput; iamDeps covers only the function SA's grants) adds any DependsOn edge to the builder SA. In the TS port, index.ts:459-474 interpolates the email from sccNotifications.projectId with dependsOn only [sccNotifications], iam.ts:255-276 creates the SA inside a void function, and the ts library component (ts/packages/cai-monitoring/src/index.ts) likewise takes buildServiceAccount as a bare Input<string>. Additionally confirmed the TS flag mismatch: config.ts:97-98 defines enableCaiMonitoring and enableSccResources as independent booleans (both default false), so enable_cai_monitoring=true with enable_scc_resources=false deterministically deploys a function referencing a nonexistent build SA — upstream TF has one flag for both. Exhaustive greps for cai-monitoring-builder/BuildServiceAccount/CAIMonitoring across both ports and both library trees found no alternative implementation of the ordering.

**Verifier note / correction:** The gap is real but two details are overstated. (1) Terraform only guarantees the builder SA's existence before the function — the SA's logging/storage/artifactregistry role grants (envs/shared/iam.tf:181-191) have no dependency edge into the cai-monitoring module in TF either, and time_sleep.wait_kms_iam covers the function SA's roles, not the builder's; so 'roles exist before build' is not a TF behavior the ports failed to port. (2) In the Go port the missing edge is a latent race, not a likely fresh-deploy failure: the function create waits on the AR repo, bucket object, topic, and SCC source inside the component while the builder SA is a single fast parallel create, so the SA nearly always exists first in practice (and Go gates both sides on the same EnableSCCResources flag, so no flag-mismatch path). The deterministic failure mode is TS-only via the enableCaiMonitoring/enableSccResources flag split. Overall 'major' is fair for TS; the Go half alone would be closer to minor/moderate.

#### [TS] Hub-and-spoke networks-SA IAM grants are dead code (networksStepTerraformSaEmail never populated)
_Area: 1-org/iam · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform grants four roles (compute.instanceAdmin, iam.serviceAccountAdmin, resourcemanager.projectIamAdmin, iam.serviceAccountUser) on the net-hub project to the 3-networks pipeline service account when enable_hub_and_spoke is true. The TS iam.ts guards this on cfg.networksStepTerraformSaEmail, but that field is declared in the OrgConfig interface and never assigned — loadOrgConfig does not read it and index.ts never sets it from the bootstrap stack reference — so the grants can never be created even with enable_hub_and_spoke=true, and the networks stage would lack permissions on the hub project. The Go port at least exposes a networks_sa_email config key.
