TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/iam.ts:233-248 (guard) vs /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/config.ts:57 (field declared) and loadOrgConfig:79-115 (never assigned); no assignment in index.ts. Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/main.go:359 (networks_sa_email config).

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified in full. TF (projects.tf:309-315 with hub_and_spoke_roles at lines 18-23) grants roles/compute.instanceAdmin, roles/iam.serviceAccountAdmin, roles/resourcemanager.projectIamAdmin, and roles/iam.serviceAccountUser on the net-hub project to the networks-step SA (sourced from bootstrap remote state, remote.tf:29) when enable_hub_and_spoke=true. In the TS port, iam.ts:233 guards the equivalent grants on cfg.networksStepTerraformSaEmail, but that field is only declared (config.ts:57) and never assigned: loadOrgConfig (config.ts:72-116) has no config key for it, and 1-org/index.ts reads only common_config/required_groups/optional_groups from the bootstrap StackReference (lines 24-36) before passing cfg unmodified to deployOrgIAM (line 453). Repo-wide greps for the field name, alternative spellings (networks_sa_email, networksSaEmail, networks_step_terraform), and the role strings found no assignment and no alternative implementation in the TS port or in pulumi-library (go/ and ts/ both empty for net-hub/hub-and-spoke IAM). The bootstrap stack even exports networks_step_terraform_service_account_email (0-bootstrap/index.ts:177) but 1-org never consumes it, so the field is always undefined and the four grants are unreachable dead code even with enable_hub_and_spoke=true. The Go port is reachable via the networks_sa_email config key (main.go:359, iam.go:265-271). Severity 'major' is appropriate: it silently breaks the hub-and-spoke topology (3-networks pipeline SA lacks required permissions on the hub project) but does not affect the default Dual-SVPC path.

#### [GO] KMS organization service agent provisioned at project scope instead of org scope
_Area: 1-org/iam · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform runs `gcloud beta services identity create --service cloudkms.googleapis.com --organization <org_id>` to create the org-level KMS service agent (service-org-<org_id>@gcp-sa-cloudkms...) before granting it roles/cloudkms.orgServiceAgent. The Go port instead creates a projects.ServiceIdentity on the KMS project, which provisions the per-project agent (service-<project_number>@gcp-sa-cloudkms...), not the org-level one. The subsequent org IAM binding still references service-org-<org_id>@..., which may not exist, so the binding can fail on fresh orgs — and enable_kms_key_usage_tracking defaults to true. The TS port does this correctly with a command.local gcloud call.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/iam.tf:69-91. Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/iam.go:212-232 (projects.NewServiceIdentity with Project: proj.OrgKMSProjectID). TS correct: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/iam.ts:194-205.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified on all points. TF (1-org/envs/shared/iam.tf:69-91) provisions the ORG-level KMS service agent via `gcloud beta services identity create --organization` before granting roles/cloudkms.orgServiceAgent to service-org-<org_id>@gcp-sa-cloudkms.... The Go port (1-org/iam.go:216-219) instead uses projects.NewServiceIdentity scoped to the KMS project, which provisions the per-project agent (service-<project_number>@...), a different principal; there is no org-scoped ServiceIdentity resource in pulumi-gcp. The org IAM binding at iam.go:224-229 still references service-org-<org_id>@..., which won't exist on a fresh org, so the binding fails — and the feature defaults on (main.go:362: != "false"). Exhaustive search of the Go port and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) for ServiceIdentity, identity-create, service-org-, orgServiceAgent, gcp-sa-cloudkms, and Command resources found no alternative implementation, no Command/gcloud call, and no documented manual pre-creation step; the only other ServiceIdentity uses are unrelated project-scoped CAI-monitoring identities. TS port is correct via command.local.Command (iam.ts:194-205). Severity 'major' is appropriate: it is a deploy-blocking failure of the 1-org stage in the default configuration on fresh organizations, mitigated only if the org-level agent already happens to exist.

Go port confirms divergence: pulumi-library/go/pkg/logging/centralized_logging.go implements all three billing sinks (387-409 storage, 479-502 pubsub, 506-545 project log bucket) and pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org (logging.go:79-80, main.go:350) enables it by default. _Area: 1-org/log_sinks · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

The Go port correctly replicates the filter at /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/logging.go:54-61 and applies it at lines 84, 95, 101. #### [TS] Project log-bucket destination lacks Log Analytics and linked BigQuery dataset; substitutes a non-upstream BQ export sink
_Area: 1-org/log_sinks · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform's project_options create an AggregatedLogs log bucket with enable_analytics = true and a linked BigQuery dataset (ds_c_prj_aggregated_logs_analytics) plus an internal project-level '-la' sink so the logging project's own logs are captured. The TS component creates a ProjectBucketConfig with no enableAnalytics, creates no gcp.logging.LinkedDataset anywhere, creates no internal project sink, and instead adds a direct log-export-to-BigQuery dataset ('audit_logs') that does not exist in the current upstream. The Go library implements analytics, linked dataset, and the internal sink.

TS: /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts:76-132 (no enableAnalytics/LinkedDataset/internal sink; BigQuery sink at 102-132) used by /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:331-340. Go correct: /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/logging/centralized_logging.go:226-306.

**Verifier reasoning:** Every element of the claim checks out against the code. Upstream TF (log_sinks.tf:77-85 + modules/centralized-logging/main.tf:152-179, variables.tf:79-80) creates an AggregatedLogs project log bucket with enable_analytics=true (default), retention_days=30 (default), a linked BigQuery dataset ds_c_prj_aggregated_logs_analytics, and an internal project-level '-la' sink so the logging project's own logs land in the bucket; the linked-dataset resource name is exported and asserted by upstream integration tests. The TS library component (pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts) creates a ProjectBucketConfig with no enableAnalytics and a 365-day default retention, has no gcp.logging.LinkedDataset (zero grep hits for LinkedDataset/enableAnalytics across the entire ts library and ts port), no internal project sink (only an OrganizationSink), and instead adds a direct BQ-export dataset+org sink ('audit_logs') that does not exist in the current upstream module. The TS port's stack output logs_export_project_linked_dataset_name (1-org/index.ts:537) returns that plain BQ dataset id, not a linked-dataset resource name, so parity validation against it would be misleading. The port also names the bucket 'LogBucket' rather than 'AggregatedLogs'. The Go library (go/pkg/logging/centralized_logging.go:226-306) implements EnableAnalytics, NewLinkedDataset, and the internal '-la' sink, confirming this is a TS-only gap. Severity 'major' is fair: this changes deployed logging infrastructure materially (no Log Analytics/SQL querying of aggregated logs, logging project's own logs become a blind spot, an extra non-upstream org-wide BQ export is created, and retention differs 365 vs 30 days).

Go correct: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/projects.go:75-121.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all points. TF (1-org/envs/shared/variables.tf:97-140) defines project_budget with per-project defaults (amount 1000, percents [1.2], FORECASTED_SPEND, optional pubsub topic) and projects.tf passes the four budget args to every project module, so upstream creates ~7-8 billing budgets even with default config. In the TS port, 1-org/index.ts instantiates ProjectFactory ~10 times (lines 130-323) with no budget args; cfg.projectBudget is loaded at config.ts:113 and used nowhere; the library component (pulumi-library/ts/packages/project-factory/src/index.ts:126-147) only creates gcp.billing.Budget when budgetAmount > 0 is explicitly passed, with no default. The Go port wires per-project budgets via budgetFor()/getProjectBudget() (1-org/projects.go:75-121), confirming intended parity. #### [BOTH] Bootstrap stack outputs (required_groups, worker pool, networks SA, bootstrap folder) not consumed — group IAM and Essential Contacts silently skipped by default

TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:28-29 (requiredGroups/optionalGroups fetched, unused) vs config.ts:111-114 (config-only) and iam.ts/essential_contacts.ts conditional skips.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified end-to-end. TF: 1-org/envs/shared/remote.tf:21-43 pulls required_groups, cloud_build_private_worker_pool_id, networks_step_terraform_service_account_email, and bootstrap_folder_name from 0-bootstrap remote state; iam.tf:21-63 and essential_contacts.tf consume required_groups unconditionally (0-bootstrap/outputs.tf:74-77 always emits it), org_policy.tf:135-147 consumes the pool ID, tags.tf:84-87 the bootstrap folder, projects.tf:314 the networks SA. Go port: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/main.go:51-57 creates the bootstrap StackReference and discards it (`_ = bootstrapRef`); no GetOutput anywhere in 1-org; all four value families come only from optional local config (main.go:335-384), and with the shipped Pulumi.production.yaml.example (no group keys) iam.go:60/81/114, essential_contacts.go, tags.go:112, and policies.go:146 all silently create nothing. TS port: index.ts:28-29 fetches required_groups/optional_groups and never uses them; iam.ts and essential_contacts.ts read cfg.requiredGroups/gcpGroups which config.ts:111-114 sources purely from local config defaulting to {}; the example config has no group keys, so both silently no-op; networksStepTerraformSaEmail is declared in the interface but never assigned in loadOrgConfig, so hub-and-spoke SA IAM (iam.ts:233) can never fire. Exhaustively searched both ports, helper/deploy scripts (foundation-deployer/deploy.sh just runs pulumi up), and /home/user/pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) — no component or script fills this gap. Severity major is justified: a user following the shipped configs gets a clean deploy with zero governance group IAM and zero Essential Contacts, with no error or warning, where Terraform always creates them.

**Verifier note / correction:** Gap is real but the title overstates the TS port: TS DOES consume common_config from the bootstrap StackReference (index.ts:31-36), including bootstrap_folder_name, and always creates the bootstrap folder tag binding (index.ts:430-435). The full four-value non-consumption (groups, worker pool, networks SA, bootstrap folder) applies only to the Go port; the TS gap is limited to required_groups/optional_groups (fetched but unused, groups IAM and Essential Contacts silently skipped), cloud_build_private_worker_pool_id being config-only, and networksStepTerraformSaEmail never being populated at all (no config key wired — strictly worse than "must be re-entered").

#### [TS] Default service account deprivilege omitted on env KMS and Secrets projects
_Area: 2-environments · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF sets default_service_account = "deprivilege" on both env projects via project-factory. The TS library ProjectFactory supports a defaultServiceAccount arg (including a 'deprivilege' implementation), but the TS 2-environments module never passes it, so SA handling silently defaults to 'keep'. The Go port passes it with an upstream-matching default of 'deprivilege'. Practical impact today is limited (neither project enables compute.googleapis.com, so the default compute SA is not created), but the security control present in TF and Go is silently absent in TS and would matter if compute APIs are later enabled.

**Evidence:** TF: 2-environments/modules/env_baseline/kms.tf and secrets.tf (default_service_account = "deprivilege"). TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/2-environments/modules/env_baseline.ts lines 60-105 (no defaultServiceAccount) vs library support at /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/project-factory/src/index.ts lines 39, 116-123. Go parity: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/2-environments/main.go lines 192-194 and env_baseline.go lines 123, 162.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified on all three legs. TF sets default_service_account = "deprivilege" on both env projects (terraform-example-foundation/2-environments/modules/env_baseline/kms.tf:28 and secrets.tf:28). The TS port's env_baseline.ts (lines 60-105) constructs both ProjectFactory instances without defaultServiceAccount; the TS library (pulumi-library/ts/packages/project-factory/src/index.ts:38-39, 116-123) supports the arg but applies no internal default and only acts on an exact "deprivilege" value, so the omission silently yields "keep" behavior. An exhaustive grep of the entire TS port and pulumi-library found no equivalent under any other name — the only related hits are the 1-org org policy (iam.automaticIamGrantsForDefaultServiceAccounts, which TF also has, so it is not a substitute for parity) and a DefaultServiceAccounts disable on the unrelated CICD project in 0-bootstrap. The Go port passes DefaultServiceAccount with an upstream-matching "deprivilege" default (2-environments/main.go:192-194, env_baseline.go:123,162), confirming the TS-only omission.

**Verifier note / correction:** Gap is real but severity "major" is inflated; it should be minor. Neither env project enables compute.googleapis.com, so no default compute SA exists to deprivilege, and both TF and the TS port enforce the org policy iam.automaticIamGrantsForDefaultServiceAccounts at 1-org (TS: 1-org/index.ts:74), which would block an automatic editor grant to the default SA even if compute APIs were enabled later. The omitted control is redundant defense-in-depth with no practical exposure today.

#### [TS] No billing budgets created under default configuration
_Area: 2-environments · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's project_budget variable defaults every field (kms_budget_amount=1000, secret_budget_amount=1000, alert_spent_percents=[1.2], spend_basis=FORECASTED_SPEND), so budgets are always created for the env KMS and Secrets projects even with no tfvars input. The TS env stacks read config.getObject("project_budget") || {}, pass undefined budgetAmount to ProjectFactory, and ProjectFactory skips budget creation when budgetAmount is null/undefined (index.ts line 126: `args.budgetAmount != null && args.budgetAmount > 0`). Result: with default config, no google_billing_budget resources exist at all — a divergent default. The Go port replicates the upstream defaults (1000 / [1.2] / FORECASTED_SPEND) when project_budget config is absent.

**Evidence:** TF: 2-environments/modules/env_baseline/variables.tf project_budget optional defaults; kms.tf/secrets.tf budget_* args. TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/2-environments/envs/*/index.ts line 32 and modules/env_baseline.ts lines 77-80, 101-104; /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/project-factory/src/index.ts line 126. Go parity: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/2-environments/main.go lines 149-171.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified end-to-end. TF: env_baseline/variables.tf defines project_budget with default={} and per-field optional defaults (kms/secret budget_amount=1000, alert_spent_percents=[1.2], spend_basis=FORECASTED_SPEND); kms.tf/secrets.tf pass these to the project-factory module (which creates google_billing_budget for non-null budget_amount and activates billingbudgets.googleapis.com), and envs/*/main.tf never overrides — so budgets are always created by default. TS: envs/*/index.ts line 32 uses config.getObject("project_budget") || {} with no default injection; env_baseline.ts lines 77-80/101-104 pass the undefined fields through; pulumi-library ts project-factory index.ts line 126 guards budget creation with `args.budgetAmount != null && args.budgetAmount > 0`, so with default config zero billing budgets are created (the [1.2]/FORECASTED_SPEND fallbacks at lines 139-141 sit inside that guard and never fire). Exhaustive search found no alternative path: gcp.billing.Budget exists only in the library's project-factory and budget packages; the foundation-budget package is declared in package.json but never imported by 2-environments; no Pulumi.yaml/stack config, script, doc, or test seeds project_budget; the TS env KMS/Secrets activateApis also omit billingbudgets.googleapis.com. Go port parity confirmed: 2-environments/main.go defaults ProjectBudget to 1000/[1.2]/FORECASTED_SPEND when config is absent, matching upstream — this is a TS-only divergence.

**Verifier note / correction:** Gap is real, but severity is better rated moderate than major: the missing resources are billing-budget alert guardrails (6 budgets: 3 envs x KMS+Secrets) that nothing else in the foundation consumes, the divergence only affects the no-config default path, and parity is trivially restored by setting project_budget stack config or by adding upstream-matching defaults in env_baseline.ts. The divergence is silent, however, which is the main risk.

_Area: 3-networks · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

(1) TF side: current upstream terraform-example-foundation names the shared-VPC host project `{prefix}-{env}-svpc` (terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/main.tf:27, module 'shared_vpc_host_project') and the network itself `vpc-{env}-svpc` (test/integration/networks/networks_test.go:52). The `prj-{env}-shared-base` / `prj-{env}-shared-restricted` naming cited in the claim is explicitly documented as the OLD pre-v4.0 scheme in terraform-example-foundation/docs/upgrading_to_v4.0.md:15-16 ('prj-ENV-shared-base', 'prj-ENV-shared-restricted' — migrated away from). TF also has a dedicated test/integration/shared/shared_test.go that asserts hub DNS policy, DNS zone, network, subnets (sb-c-svpc-hub-*), and routers for hub-and-spoke mode. (2) Both ports: pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/projects.go:307 and pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:302 create host projects named `%s-%s-svpc` / `${prefix}-${envCode}-svpc` (i.e. prj-d-svpc, prj-n-svpc, prj-p-svpc), matching current upstream, not the old shared-base/shared-restricted scheme. The hub project is named `{prefix}-net-hub`. (3) Both validate_e2e.sh scripts (go:198-206, ts:198-206) filter for `prj-{d,n,p}-shared-base*` / `prj-{d,n,p}-shared-restricted*`, which can never match `prj-{d,n,p}-svpc` or `prj-net-hub`. Grepping both entire port repos and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) turns up zero other place that validates 3-networks resources against live GCP — no networks_test.go/shared_test.go equivalent exists; the only other 3-networks-related test files are trivial unit tests of config defaults (pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/config_test.go, pulumi_ts-example-foundation/test/3-networks.config.test.ts) that assert static strings like default region, not deployed-resource validation. 'hub' never appears anywhere in either validate_e2e.sh. (4) Confirmed this script is the sole 'Validate Resources' step wired into .github/workflows/e2e.yml (both ports) for the real E2E deploy-against-sandbox-org workflow; the script's exit code is driven only by FAIL count, and Phase-3 mismatches only increment WARN (not FAIL), so the workflow reports overall success ('E2E VALIDATION PASSED') while silently skipping all network-stage checks and hub-and-spoke checks in every mode. No counter-evidence found anywhere.

**Verifier note / correction:** The claim is essentially accurate as described; I'd only soften the severity framing slightly: this is a test/CI validation gap (blind spot in an optional, manually-gated E2E smoke test), not a defect in the deployed infrastructure itself — the actual 3-networks Pulumi code appears to create the right resources with current-upstream naming. 'Major' is defensible given it silently defeats CI's ability to catch real network-stage regressions and the hub-and-spoke checks are completely absent from the port's test suite (a genuine coverage regression vs. TF's dedicated shared_test.go), but it is not a production/deployment-correctness bug — nothing about the actual foundation's networking is broken or missing, only its automated verification is neutered.

#### [TS] BGP Cloud Routers (4 per shared VPC) with custom advertised ranges missing
_Area: 3-networks (both topologies) · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF creates region1_router1/2 and region2_router1/2 per shared VPC with bgp.asn = bgp_asn_subnet, advertised_groups ALL_SUBNETS, and custom advertised_ip_ranges (PSC/restricted-API range, plus DNS forwarding range on the hub) — the on-prem route advertisement backbone for interconnect/VPN. Go creates them (hub-cr-* / cr-*-cr5..8 via networking.NewCloudRouter with AdvertisedIpRanges). TS creates only NAT routers with no BGP config; the foundation-cloud-router TS library package exists but is imported by nothing in the port. NAT also diverges: TF allocates manual external NAT addresses (nat_num_addresses), TS hardcodes AUTO_ONLY.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc/main.tf:100-160, nat.tf:34-80. Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go:179-210, 3-networks-svpc/main.go:213-236. TS checked: 3-networks-{svpc,hub-and-spoke}/modules/shared_vpc.ts:224-265 (NAT only, natIpAllocateOption AUTO_ONLY); grep CloudRouter over the TS port returns nothing.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF (3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc/main.tf:100-160, and the equivalent hub-and-spoke main.tf:123-184) creates 4 BGP-enabled cloud routers per shared VPC (region1_router1/2, region2_router1/2) with bgp.asn=var.bgp_asn_subnet, advertised_groups=["ALL_SUBNETS"], and advertised_ip_ranges containing the PSC/restricted-googleapis CIDR plus (for prod/hub) the 35.199.192.0/19 DNS-forwarding range. nat.tf additionally allocates MANUAL_ONLY NAT with var.nat_num_addresses_region1/2 pre-reserved external IP addresses. The Go port faithfully reproduces this (pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go ~lines 210-236, networking.NewCloudRouter with AdvertisedIpRanges/AdvertisedGroups, distinct from the separate NAT routers). The TS port's shared_vpc.ts in BOTH 3-networks-svpc and 3-networks-hub-and-spoke creates only two gcp.compute.Router objects, used exclusively for NAT (no `bgp` field set at all), paired with gcp.compute.RouterNat using natIpAllocateOption: "AUTO_ONLY" (not MANUAL_ONLY with reserved addresses). A grep across the entire TS port for ALL_SUBNETS, advertiseMode, advertisedGroups, advertisedIpRanges, cr5/cr6/cr7/cr8, bgpAsn, natNumAddresses returns zero hits anywhere. Crucially, pulumi-library/ts/packages/cloud-router/src/index.ts DOES contain a fully-built CloudRouter component with bgp/advertiseMode/advertisedGroups/advertisedIpRanges support (mirroring what Go's networking.NewCloudRouter does), and @vitruviansoftware/foundation-cloud-router is listed as a dependency in the TS port's package.json — but no .ts file anywhere in the port imports it (only referenced in package.json and its own test file). The subnet-advertisement BGP routers, the manual NAT address allocation, and the DNS-forwarding advertised range are all genuinely absent from the TS port's deployed resource graph.

This makes the reviewer's 'major' severity assessment reasonable rather than inflated: without the hub perimeter, the spoke perimeters aren't bridged to anything and the hub project (which routes hybrid/shared traffic) has no VPC-SC boundary at all, which is a meaningful security-posture gap versus upstream, not merely a cosmetic/informational one.

#### [BOTH] Hub project gets no VPC Service Controls perimeter
_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** In TF, the shared env passes the hub through the shared_vpc module whose service_control.tf creates access levels and a regular_service_perimeter unconditionally (module has no count on mode), so the net-hub project is protected. The Go hub branch (Env=="shared") creates the hierarchical firewall, hub VPC, PSC, DNS, transitivity, and routers but no vpc_sc.NewVpcServiceControls call — only spokes get perimeters. The TS hub shared index also lacks any perimeter. A security control present in TF is silently absent.

**Evidence:** TF: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/shared/net-hubs.tf:160-193 (mode="hub", access_context_manager_policy_id, perimeter members) + modules/shared_vpc/service_control.tf:75 (unconditional regular_service_perimeter). Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go:50-217 (no vpc_sc in shared branch); TS: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/shared/index.ts has no VpcServiceControls.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/shared/net-hubs.tf calls module.shared_vpc with mode="hub" and passes access_context_manager_policy_id, members, members_dry_run. The shared_vpc module's service_control.tf (lines 29-102) unconditionally creates module.access_level, module.access_level_dry_run, and module.regular_service_perimeter for every caller of the module — there is no count/mode conditional gating these three; only the bridge perimeter (google_access_context_manager_service_perimeter.bridge_to_network_hub_perimeter, line 104-119) is gated on mode=="spoke". Because the hub and each spoke instantiate shared_vpc separately, the hub gets its own dedicated regular_service_perimeter keyed to its own project_number — a genuine, dedicated VPC-SC perimeter protecting the net-hub project, not merely a byproduct of the spokes' perimeters.

Port side confirmed absent: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go's `if cfg.Env == "shared"` branch (lines ~47-216) builds hierarchical firewall, SharedVPCHostProject, hub VPC/subnets, VPC-level firewall policy, PSC, DNS policy, DNS forwarding zone, transitivity appliance, and BGP/NAT routers, then exports outputs and returns — no call to vpc_sc.NewVpcServiceControls anywhere in that branch. The spoke branch further down does call vpc_sc.NewVpcServiceControls (line 377) guarded by `if cfg.PolicyID != ""`. Same asymmetry in TS: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/shared/index.ts builds SharedVpc(mode:"hub") + HierarchicalFirewallPolicy and returns outputs with no VpcServiceControls import/call, while envs/development/index.ts (and by extension nonproduction/production) explicitly imports @vitruviansoftware/foundation-vpc-service-controls and instantiates VpcServiceControls with a comment "// VPC Service Controls (mirrors TF base_env/service_control.tf)".

Checked pulumi-library (go/pkg/vpc_sc/vpc_sc.go and ts/packages/vpc-service-controls) — the component is generic (PolicyID, Prefix, Members, ProjectNumbers, RestrictedServices, Enforce, etc.), not spoke-specific; it could be invoked for the hub with zero new component code, confirming this is an omission rather than an architectural constraint. Grepped the entire go and ts port trees (1-org, 3-networks-svpc, 4-projects, 5-app-infra, helpers, build, scripts) for ServiceControls/ServicePerimeter/AccessLevel/access_context_manager/vpc_sc/VpcSc — the only place a policy container is created is 1-org (the org-level AccessContextManagerPolicy resource itself, plus a matching TS counterpart); no perimeter is ever created for the hub project anywhere in either port.

Severity: the reviewer's "major" rating is reasonable, not inflated — this is a genuine, unconditionally-present TF security control (a real enforced/dry-run VPC-SC perimeter with its own access levels binding the hub project) that is completely and silently missing from both ports' shared/hub deployment path, leaving the net-hub project outside VPC Service Controls while every spoke project is covered.

#### [TS] Hierarchical firewall policy rules and associations diverge from Terraform's delegation design
_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/hierarchical-firewall · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform's policy delegates RFC1918 ingress/egress with goto_next (500/510), allows IAP SSH/RDP (5000), Windows KMS egress (5100), and LB health checks (5200), and associates the policy with six folders (common, network, bootstrap, dev, nonprod, prod). The TS port instead ALLOWS all RFC1918 ingress at priority 100 (terminating evaluation so lower-level VPC firewall denies can never block internal traffic), omits Windows KMS and RFC1918 egress delegation, adds a deny-all-ingress at 65534 that TF does not have, and attaches only the network folder. Security semantics are materially different in both permissive and restrictive directions.

**Evidence:** TF: envs/shared/hierarchical_firewall.tf (5 rules, 6 associations). Port: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/shared/index.ts lines 59-104 (4 divergent rules, targetFolders: [networkFolderName]). Go library hierarchical_firewall.go reproduces the TF rule set faithfully.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified end-to-end. TF (3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/shared/hierarchical_firewall.tf) has exactly the claimed 5 rules (goto_next RFC1918 ingress@500 and egress@510, IAP allow@5000, Windows KMS egress allow@5100, LB/health-check allow@5200 tcp 80/443) and 6 folder associations. The TS port (3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/shared/index.ts:59-104) instead has a terminating ALLOW of all RFC1918 ingress@100, a health-check allow@200 with tcp all ports (broader than TF's 80/443), IAP allow@300, a deny-all-ingress@65534 that TF lacks, no RFC1918 egress rule, no KMS egress rule, and only targetFolders:[networkFolderName]. Refutation attempts failed: the imported library component (pulumi-library/ts/packages/hierarchical-firewall-policy) is a pure passthrough with no default rules/associations; grep across the entire TS port for hierarchical FirewallPolicyAssociation, goto_next, 1688, and 35.190.247.13 found no hierarchical-level equivalent anywhere (only VPC-level NetworkFirewallPolicy resources and a KMS route, which TF also has in addition to the hierarchical rules); the svpc variant uses the same divergent rule set; the local hierarchical module is a re-export of the same passthrough. The Go library (pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/hierarchical_firewall.go) faithfully reproduces TF's 5 rules, confirming the evidence's cross-reference. Security reasoning holds: hierarchical allow terminates evaluation so VPC-level denies cannot block RFC1918 ingress (permissive gap), and hierarchical deny@65534 blocks all other ingress before VPC rules are consulted (restrictive gap); five of six folders get no policy at all. Major severity is appropriate.

**Verifier note / correction:** Claim is accurate with one minor nuance: omitting the goto_next RFC1918 egress delegation rule (510) is by itself near-behavior-neutral, because the hierarchical policy default when no rule matches is already goto_next. The materially significant divergences are the terminating allow@100, the added deny-all@65534, the missing Windows KMS egress allow, the health-check rule allowing all tcp ports instead of 80/443 (an additional divergence not itemized in the claim), and attaching only 1 of 6 folders.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all points. TF: terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/shared_vpc/private_service_connect.tf passes forwarding_rule_target = "vpc-sc" to the upstream private-service-connect module (which creates *.googleapis.com CNAME → restricted.googleapis.com plus A records to the PSC IP, and gcr.io/pkg.dev records — behavior corroborated by the Go library port that replicates it). TS library: pulumi-library/ts/packages/private-service-connect/src/index.ts hardcodes target: "all-apis" (line 37), has no target option in its args interface, and creates no DNS resources; "restricted.googleapis" appears nowhere in the TS library. TS port: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/shared_vpc.ts lines 194-213 create five private ManagedZones with zero RecordSets, and no gcp.dns.RecordSet, cloud-dns recordsets, or restricted-VIP DNS exists anywhere in the TS port (exhaustive grep for RecordSet/CNAME/restricted.googleapis/199.36.153.4 across the repo). The same gap exists in 3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc.ts. Go parity confirmed: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/psc.go and go/pkg/private_service_connect/private_service_connect.go accept ForwardingRuleTarget ("vpc-sc"/"all-apis") and create the CNAME/A record sets. Severity "major" is justified — beyond losing the VPC-SC restricted-VIP path, the empty private zones shadow public DNS for googleapis.com/gcr.io/pkg.dev inside the VPC, which would break Google API name resolution entirely from those networks.

#### [BOTH] Tag-based internet egress route missing — Cloud NAT enabled but cannot egress
_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/routing · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform deletes default internet routes and, when nat_enabled, re-creates the tagged route rt-*-1000-egress-internet-default (0.0.0.0/0 via IGW, tag egress-internet). Both ports delete default routes and enable NAT unconditionally (TF: hub_nat_enabled default false, spokes have no NAT at all) but never create any internet route in this stage, so NAT-ed instances have no path to the internet gateway. Notably the Go 3-networks-svpc stage does create this route, showing intra-repo drift. TS additionally uses AUTO_ONLY NAT IPs instead of TF's MANUAL_ONLY reserved addresses with configurable counts.

**Evidence:** TF: modules/shared_vpc/main.tf routes block (nat_enabled conditional), nat.tf. Checked: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go (no NewRoute; NAT EnableNat true for hub+spokes), pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/networking.go and router.go (no internet route); pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/shared_vpc.ts (only windows-kms route; natEnabled !== false default-on; AUTO_ONLY line 237). Contrast: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go lines 170-177.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified on all points. TF (3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/shared_vpc/main.tf:42-54) deletes default internet routes and, when nat_enabled, recreates the tagged 0.0.0.0/0-via-IGW route rt-*-1000-egress-internet-default; nat.tf uses MANUAL_ONLY with reserved addresses; hub_nat_enabled defaults false and TF spokes get no NAT. Go port (3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go) enables NAT unconditionally on hub (lines 197-210) and spokes (~358-370), deletes default routes via library default (pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/networking.go:70), and contains no compute.NewRoute; library route creators are only transitivity ILB routes (transitivity.go:236), no IGW route under any name in router.go/networking.go/cloud_router.go. TS port (modules/shared_vpc.ts) sets deleteDefaultInternetGatewayRoutes:true (line 151), creates only the windows-kms route (line 159), defaults NAT on (natEnabled !== false, line 224; envs pass natEnabled:true), and uses AUTO_ONLY (lines 237, 258); no 0.0.0.0/0 Route exists anywhere in the TS repo or ts library (no route package). Since Cloud NAT only handles packets whose route next hop is the default internet gateway, the deployed NAT gateways in both ports are non-functional — no instance can egress. The Go svpc stage (3-networks-svpc/main.go:170-177) does create the route, confirming intra-repo drift. Major severity is justified, arguably understated since the ports enable NAT by default (TF defaults off), deploying billable but inert infrastructure.

**Verifier note / correction:** Two minor refinements, neither weakening the claim: (1) the Go library router.go does support MANUAL_ONLY allocation when NatNumAddresses > 0 (lines 107-125), so the AUTO_ONLY drift applies only to TS, exactly as the claim scoped it; (2) the TS gap is broader than claimed — the TS 3-networks-svpc stage (modules/shared_vpc.ts, identical code) also lacks the egress-internet route, unlike the Go svpc stage.

#### [TS] Hub BGP Cloud Routers (cr5–cr8) with ALL_SUBNETS + restricted-API advertisement missing
_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/shared (hub) · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

The TS port creates only NAT routers; the library cloud-router package is never used in stage 3, so the interconnect/VPN examples reference router names that will never exist.

Checked: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/shared_vpc.ts (only gcp.compute.Router for NAT, no bgp advertisement), envs/shared/index.ts, grep for cloud-router in the stage. Go port creates all four with advertised ranges (main.go lines 179-195).

**Verifier reasoning:** Terraform side verified: terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/shared_vpc/main.tf (lines 123-185) creates four hub Cloud Routers (cr5-cr8, 2 per region, count gated on mode != "spoke") with bgp.advertised_groups=["ALL_SUBNETS"] and advertised_ip_ranges = restricted-googleapis CIDR plus 35.199.192.0/19 (environment_code "c" = hub, main.tf line 23); the interconnect/partner_interconnect/vpn-ha examples bind to these routers. TS port verified absent: modules/shared_vpc.ts creates only NAT routers (lines 225, 246, no bgp block), envs/shared/index.ts creates only SharedVpc + HierarchicalFirewallPolicy, and a whole-repo grep for compute.Router/advertisedGroups/ALL_SUBNETS/bgpAsn finds no BGP router anywhere. The library component exists (pulumi-library/ts/packages/cloud-router, full BGP support) and is even listed in the TS port's root package.json, but is never imported by any source file. The dangling-reference point also holds: interconnect.ts.example, partner_interconnect.ts.example and vpn.ts.example reference network.region1Router1.name etc., properties that do not exist on the SharedVpc class, so the hybrid-connectivity examples cannot work. Go port confirmed as contrast: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go lines 179-195 create all four hub BGP routers with ALL_SUBNETS + advertised ranges including 35.199.192.0/19. Severity major is justified: TF creates these routers unconditionally on the hub, they carry route advertisement for all hybrid connectivity, and the TS port's shipped examples are wired to router outputs that never exist.

#### [TS] Hub-and-spoke transitivity (gateway MIG + ILB + routes) entirely missing
_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/shared (hub) · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform's net-hubs-transitivity.tf + modules/transitivity deploy per-region gateway MIGs, internal LBs, next_hop_ilb routes for regional aggregates, a dedicated service account with logging/monitoring roles, and priority 20000/20001 firewall policy rules. The TS port has no implementation anywhere (stage or library) even though its README documents an enable_hub_and_spoke_transitivity option and 1-org/config.ts reads it — a documented feature with no code behind it.

**Evidence:** TF: envs/shared/net-hubs-transitivity.tf, modules/transitivity/main.tf. Checked: grep -i transitivity in pulumi_ts-example-foundation (only README.md line 167 and 1-org/config.ts line 90) and pulumi-library/ts (zero hits). Go port has networking.NewTransitivityAppliance and invokes it.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified on both sides. TF reference: envs/shared/net-hubs-transitivity.tf + modules/transitivity/main.tf deploy exactly what the claim lists — transitivity-gw service account with logging.logWriter/monitoring.metricWriter, per-region COS instance templates (can_ip_forward, iptables user-data), per-region MIGs (size 3), per-region all-ports internal LBs, google_compute_route with next_hop_ilb for regional aggregates, and firewall policy rules at priorities 20000/20001. TS port: exhaustive greps for 'transitiv', nextHopIlb, InstanceGroupManager, canIpForward, iptables, MASQUERADE, cos-cloud, allPorts, internal-LB/appliance/gateway-VM terms across pulumi_ts-example-foundation and pulumi-library/ts found no implementation under any name. The hub stage (3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/shared/index.ts) deploys only SharedVpc + hierarchical firewall and its header comment explicitly says it mirrors net-hubs.tf and hierarchical_firewall.tf — omitting net-hubs-transitivity.tf. pulumi-library/ts/packages has no MIG/ILB/transitivity package; the transitivity component exists only in go/pkg/networking/transitivity.go, invoked by the Go port (3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go:159). The TS flag enableHubAndSpokeTransitivity is defined and parsed in 1-org/config.ts (lines 30, 90) but consumed nowhere — a documented option (README line 167) with no code behind it. Severity 'major' is fair: enabling the documented flag silently does nothing, and transitivity is the mechanism that makes spoke-to-spoke traffic possible in a hub-and-spoke topology (VPC peering is non-transitive). Only nuance: the feature is opt-in and defaults to false in both TF and the port, so default deployments are unaffected.

#### [GO] Transitivity ILB health checks have no firewall allow — backends can never become healthy
_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/shared (hub) · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform's lb-internal module creates a health-check firewall rule allowing GCP health-check ranges (35.191.0.0/16, 130.211.0.0/22) to the backend port (tcp:22). The Go TransitivityAppliance creates the TCP:22 health check but no rule permitting it: the hub's foundation rules only allow ingress from subnet ranges (10.0.64.0/18, 10.1.64.0/18) and the hierarchical policy 5200 rule allows health-check ranges on tcp:80/443 only. All MIG instances stay unhealthy, so the transitivity ILB forwards no traffic — silent functional failure.

**Evidence:** TF: modules/transitivity/main.tf module "ilbs" (GoogleCloudPlatform/lb-internal with health_check port 22; module creates its own hc firewall). Port: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/transitivity.go (health check lines 118-128, fw rules 20000/20001 only match regional aggregates); pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go BuildFoundationRules call line 112; library hierarchical_firewall.go rule 5200 (80/443 only).

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF: modules/transitivity/main.tf calls GoogleCloudPlatform/lb-internal ~7.0 with health_check port 22; although create_backend_firewall=false, the module's separate create_health_check_firewall defaults to true (verified against v7.0.0 source), so TF creates google_compute_firewall.default-hc allowing tcp:22 from 130.211.0.0/22 and 35.191.0.0/16 to the transitivity SA on the hub VPC. Go port: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/transitivity.go creates the TCP:22 health check (lines 118-128) but only firewall policy rules 20000/20001 matching regional aggregates; BuildFoundationRules (firewall.go 411-477) adds only deny-egress/googleapis/subnet-range rules; hierarchical rule 5200 allows hc ranges on tcp:80/443 only (same as TF). Exhaustive greps across pulumi_go-example-foundation and pulumi-library (go and ts) for the hc CIDRs, classic compute.Firewall resources, TargetTags/SourceRanges, and the unused 'allow-transitivity' instance tag found no equivalent allow anywhere — the port creates zero classic VPC firewall rules, so probes fall through to the implied deny ingress. Health checks can never pass and all MIG backends stay permanently unhealthy. The gap is real; only the claimed failure mechanism is overstated (see correction).

**Verifier note / correction:** The missing health-check firewall rule is real, but 'the ILB forwards no traffic — silent functional failure' overstates the impact: GCP internal passthrough NLBs fail open, distributing traffic to all backends when every backend is unhealthy, and next-hop-ILB routes still deliver, so transitivity traffic would likely still flow. The actual impact is loss of health-based gating (dead/replacing instances still receive and blackhole their share of flows), permanently unhealthy backend status, and broken TF parity — degraded and fragile rather than a total outage. Severity is closer to moderate than major.

#### [TS] Private Service Access (private_service_cidr / servicenetworking connection) missing
_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/spoke · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform spokes reserve a per-env VPC_PEERING global address (e.g. 10.16.40.0/21 dev) and create google_service_networking_connection for Cloud SQL and other managed services. The TS Network and SharedVpc components have no PSA support and stage 3 never creates a servicenetworking connection, so Cloud SQL private IP would be impossible in spoke VPCs. (Go implements PSA via EnablePSA, though its CIDR is an auto-assigned /16 rather than TF's configurable per-env /21.)

**Evidence:** TF: modules/shared_vpc/main.tf lines 94-116, envs/*/main.tf private_service_cidr locals. Checked: pulumi-library/ts/packages/network/src/index.ts (no servicenetworking), pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/shared_vpc.ts, envs/*/index.ts. Go: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/networking.go lines 137-159.

**Verifier reasoning:** The claim is fully verified. Terraform side confirmed: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/shared_vpc/main.tf:94-116 creates a VPC_PEERING google_compute_global_address plus google_service_networking_connection gated on private_service_cidr, with per-env /21 CIDRs (dev 10.16.40.0/21, nonprod 10.16.48.0/21, prod 10.16.56.0/21) in envs/*/main.tf. TS side: exhaustive greps of pulumi_ts-example-foundation (all stages) and pulumi-library/ts for servicenetworking, VPC_PEERING, GlobalAddress, reservedPeeringRanges, PSA spellings, and the literal CIDRs found no PSA implementation anywhere in stage 3 or the network/shared-vpc components. The SharedVpc module (3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/shared_vpc.ts) creates only a PrivateServiceConnect component, which is PSC (purpose PRIVATE_SERVICE_CONNECT + all-apis forwarding rule for Google APIs), not PSA, and cannot provide Cloud SQL private IP. The only gcp.servicenetworking.Connection in the TS library is in cb-private-pool (Cloud Build worker pools), unrelated to spoke networks. Notably, the TS 3-networks-svpc README (lines 24, 222) advertises PSA support that the code does not implement, confirming an omission rather than a deliberate design change. The Go comparison in the claim is also accurate: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/networking.go:137-159 implements PSA behind EnablePSA with an auto-assigned /16, and the Go port enables it. Severity 'major' is justified: this is provisioned base infrastructure in the reference, its absence blocks private-IP managed services (Cloud SQL, Memorystore) in all spoke VPCs, and the port's own docs claim the feature exists.

#### [BOTH] Spoke-to-hub perimeter bridge (PERIMETER_TYPE_BRIDGE) missing
_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/spoke · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform creates google_access_context_manager_service_perimeter.bridge_to_network_hub_perimeter joining each spoke project with the net-hub project (dry-run or enforced per enforce_vpcsc). Without it, spoke↔hub traffic between perimeters is blocked once perimeters are enforced — and both ports default to enforced. No bridge perimeter exists in either port or in the shared library.

**Evidence:** TF: modules/shared_vpc/service_control.tf lines 104-119. Checked: grep -i bridge across pulumi-library, pulumi_go-example-foundation, pulumi_ts-example-foundation — zero hits; go/pkg/vpc_sc/vpc_sc.go and ts/packages/vpc-service-controls/src/index.ts only create PERIMETER_TYPE_REGULAR.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side verified: modules/shared_vpc/service_control.tf lines 104-119 creates a PERIMETER_TYPE_BRIDGE perimeter joining each spoke project with net_hub_project_number when mode=="spoke" (enforced or dry-run per enforce_vpcsc). Exhaustive search of both ports and the shared library found no bridge perimeter and no functional equivalent: the only ServicePerimeter creations are in pulumi-library/go/pkg/vpc_sc/vpc_sc.go (line 321) and pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpc-service-controls/src/index.ts (line 290), both hardcoded to PERIMETER_TYPE_REGULAR. Go port (3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go) creates one regular perimeter per spoke and none for the hub; TS port (envs/*/index.ts) passes projectNumbers=[envProjectId] only and creates no hub perimeter. No default ingress/egress policies, no ServicePerimeterResource attachment, and no config default folds the hub project into any perimeter (4-projects' ServicePerimeterResource attaches stage-4 service projects, not the hub). Both ports default enforce to true (Go main.go lines 505-507; TS `enforce_vpc_sc ?? true`). The 'bridge' grep hits in the go port are compiled ELF binaries, not source. Claim stands; severity 'major' is reasonable since the foundation's own cross-project operations (DNS peering to hub, compute/dns APIs) are restricted services.

**Verifier note / correction:** Minor mechanism nuance only: in the ports the hub project is in no perimeter at all (the ports also omit the hub's own regular perimeter that TF creates with mode="hub"), so the failure mode is the enforced spoke perimeter blocking restricted-service Google API calls to/from the hub project, rather than literally "traffic between two perimeters." VPC-SC blocks API-plane traffic, not peered VPC data-plane packets. This does not change the verdict or severity.

#### [BOTH] enforce_vpcsc defaults to true and step service accounts are not auto-added to access levels
_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/vpc-sc · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform defaults enforce_vpcsc = false (explicitly warns not to enforce on first run) and always injects the networks/projects/organization step Terraform SAs into both access levels from remote state. Go defaults EnforceVpcSc to true when unset and TS defaults enforce_vpc_sc ?? true; both take members purely from config with an empty default. First-run deployments therefore enforce a perimeter whose access level may contain no members, locking the deployer SAs out of the spoke projects.

**Evidence:** TF: modules/shared_vpc/variables.tf enforce_vpcsc default false; modules/base_env/main.tf members = concat(step SAs, var.perimeter_additional_members). Ports: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go lines 504-508 (default true) and vpc_sc_members config only; pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/development/index.ts line 58 (?? true) with vpc_sc_members default [].

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified on all points. TF: enforce_vpcsc defaults to false (modules/shared_vpc/variables.tf:200-204) — and in hub-and-spoke, base_env never even passes it, so TF is always dry-run unless code is edited — and both members and members_dry_run always concat the networks/projects/organization step SAs from bootstrap remote state with perimeter_additional_members (modules/base_env/main.tf:167-176, remote.tf:22-24). Go port: main.go:504-508 defaults EnforceVpcSc=true when unset, VpcScMembers comes solely from vpc_sc_members config (line 486) and is passed as both Members and MembersDryRun. TS port: envs/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts use enforce_vpc_sc ?? true and vpc_sc_members || []. The shared pulumi-library components (go/pkg/vpc_sc/vpc_sc.go, ts/packages/vpc-service-controls/src/index.ts) take members verbatim from args with no SA injection; TS enforce is a required arg. No helper, script, doc, or config anywhere in either port or the library injects the step SAs. The TS port is worse than claimed: it creates the perimeter unconditionally and 1-org creates/exports the ACM policy by default, so the default TS first-run path really does create an enforced perimeter with an empty access level. The Go port has a partial mitigation: the perimeter is gated on policy_id config, which the example stack configs leave unset, so a default first-run creates no perimeter; the hazard fires only once the user enables VPC-SC. Even with enforce=false, the ports' dry-run access level lacks the step SAs, diverging from TF where they are members of both levels.

**Verifier note / correction:** Gap is real and severity 'major' is fair overall, with one nuance: for the Go port the enforced-empty-perimeter only materializes after the user opts into VPC-SC by setting policy_id (example configs leave it unset, so no perimeter is created on a literal first run); the inverted default and missing step-SA injection still apply the moment VPC-SC is enabled. For the TS port the claim holds fully on the default path with no gating.

#### [GO] Per-environment subnet/proxy/secondary/PSC ranges hardcoded to the development address plan for every environment
_Area: 3-networks-svpc · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** 3-networks-svpc main.go bakes in 10.8.64.0/18, 10.9.64.0/18, proxy 10.26.2.0/23 and 10.27.2.0/23, and GKE secondary ranges 100.72-75.64.0/18 for all env stacks, and defaults psc_ip to 10.17.0.6 for all envs. TF assigns distinct ranges per environment (nonproduction 10.8.128.0/18 + PSC 10.17.0.7, production 10.8.192.0/18 + PSC 10.17.0.8, distinct proxy and secondary ranges, secondary ranges only in region1). No configuration option exists to set these, so the documented enterprise address plan cannot be reproduced and all three environments collide in IP space (breaks on-prem routing/interconnect designs). TS port assigns distinct per-env CIDRs.

**Evidence:** Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go:85-120 and loadNetConfig (no subnet CIDR config; psc_ip default 10.17.0.6 at main.go:400-402). TF: 3-networks-svpc/envs/nonproduction/main.tf (10.8.128.0/18, PSC 10.17.0.7), envs/production/main.tf (10.8.192.0/18, PSC 10.17.0.8).

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

TF side (verified): 3-networks-svpc/envs/{development,nonproduction,production}/main.tf each hardcode distinct per-env values exactly as claimed — development: subnets 10.8.64.0/18 + 10.9.64.0/18, proxy 10.26.2.0/23 + 10.27.2.0/23, GKE secondary 100.72-73.64.0/18, PSC 10.17.0.6; nonproduction: 10.8.128.0/18 + 10.9.128.0/18, proxy 10.26.4.0/23 + 10.27.4.0/23, PSC 10.17.0.7; production: 10.8.192.0/18 + 10.9.192.0/18, proxy 10.26.6.0/23 + 10.27.6.0/23, PSC 10.17.0.8.

Go port (verified): pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go:85-120 hardcodes the literal development-plan CIDRs (10.8.64.0/18, 10.9.64.0/18, proxy 10.26.2.0/23 + 10.27.2.0/23, GKE secondary 100.72-75.64.0/18) unconditionally for every env (development, nonproduction, production all execute the same code path with no per-env branching on CIDR). loadNetConfig (main.go:348-418) exposes no subnet/proxy/secondary-range config keys at all, and psc_ip defaults to "10.17.0.6" (main.go:400-402) for every env if not explicitly set. The three example stack config files (Pulumi.development/nonproduction/production.yaml.example) only set env, project_id, parent_id (+ optional regions) — none set psc_ip or any CIDR. config_test.go also contains no CIDR/psc_ip coverage. Searched pulumi-library/go (networking, vpc_sc packages) for any override mechanism or per-env default table — none found (only an unrelated 10.8.0.0/20 in a unit test).

Cross-check with the TS port confirms the asymmetry: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc uses TF-mirroring per-env directories (envs/development, envs/nonproduction, envs/production), each with its own index.ts hardcoding genuinely distinct CIDRs and PSC addresses per environment (dev: 10.0.64.0/21 + 10.1.64.0/21, PSC 10.2.0.10; nonprod: 10.0.128.0/21 + 10.1.128.0/21, PSC 10.2.0.20; prod: 10.0.192.0/21 + 10.1.192.0/21, PSC 10.2.0.30) — not colliding, unlike the Go port's single main.go which reuses one literal set of ranges for all three. This confirms the Go port is the outlier/regression, not an intentional design choice shared across languages.

Net effect: deploying all three Go-port environments with defaults produces identical subnet/proxy/secondary/PSC address blocks across dev, nonprod, and prod — exactly as the claim states — and there is no configuration knob to fix it short of editing main.go's literal strings per stack.

Correction on severity: "major" is defensible but the description overstates immediacy slightly. The environments are separate VPC networks (not peered to each other) and dedicated/partner interconnect is disabled by default in both TF (enable_partner_interconnect = false in all three env main.tf) and presumably in the Go port as well (interconnect/partner_interconnect wiring lives in *.go.example stub files, not compiled by default) — so no on-prem BGP route collision occurs out-of-the-box. The real, immediate harm is: (1) fidelity/config-parity loss vs. both TF and the TS port, (2) users cannot reproduce the documented enterprise address plan via config alone, and (3) it becomes a latent trap the moment anyone enables interconnect/VPN/hub-and-spoke peering across environments, which is a normal next step for this reference architecture. That's a real, non-cosmetic gap, so "major" is a reasonable call, not clearly inflated — but it is not (as worded) an active on-prem routing break in the default deployment, since no default path advertises these ranges off-VPC across environments simultaneously.

TS port: all six env entrypoints use config.getBoolean("enforce_vpc_sc") ?? true with vpcScMembers defaulting to [], and the perimeter is created unconditionally from the org stack's ACM policy output. The shared pulumi-library components (go/pkg/vpc_sc/vpc_sc.go and ts/packages/vpc-service-controls/src/index.ts) treat enforce as a required pass-through with no safe default, so nothing in the library mitigates it. Example stack configs and READMEs in both ports set neither enforce nor members and carry no dry-run-first guidance. Net effect: enabling VPC-SC with defaults yields an enforced perimeter whose access level contains no deployer identities — a lockout-class regression versus TF's dry-run-first, SAs-auto-included behavior.

**Verifier note / correction:** Severity "major" is fair overall. One nuance for the Go port only: the perimeter is created only when the optional policy_id config is set (main.go:261), so a truly bare default run creates no perimeter at all; the dangerous enforce-by-default only bites once a user supplies policy_id (which is the only way to get VPC-SC in that port). The TS port has no such gate — the perimeter is created unconditionally.

#### [BOTH] App-infra pipeline machinery (per-repo SAs, sa_roles, networkUser/networkViewer grants, triggers, buckets) absent in default path
_Area: 4-projects · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's 4-projects creates, via modules/infra_pipelines + single_project, per-repository pipeline service accounts (sa-tf-cb-*), Cloud Build triggers, state/log/artifact buckets, the gcp-policies repo, and grants each pipeline SA sa_roles on the sample projects, roles/compute.networkViewer on the BU folder, and roles/compute.networkUser on shared VPC subnets — the identities 5-app-infra deploys with. The Go default path creates only the empty prj-c-bu1-infra-pipeline project (business_unit.go deployInfraPipelineProject); the Cloud Build equivalent (example_infra_pipelines.go) is behind //go:build example and is never invoked by main() even with the tag, and no GitHub-Actions-based replacement (WIF pool/SA for app repos) is provisioned either. TS shared index likewise creates only the project. Result: no deploy identity or IAM is provisioned for stage 5.

**Evidence:** TF: 4-projects/modules/infra_pipelines/main.tf, modules/single_project/main.tf:85-110 (sa_roles, folder_network_viewer, networkUser), business_unit_1/shared/example_infra_pipeline.tf. Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit.go:212-236 (project only), example_infra_pipelines.go:8 (//go:build example; deployInfraPipelines never called). TS: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/shared/index.ts (project only).

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed exactly as described: 4-projects/modules/infra_pipelines/main.tf creates per-repo CSRs, a gcp-policies repo, a cloudbuild bucket, and (via terraform-google-modules/bootstrap//modules/tf_cloudbuild_workspace) a sa-tf-cb-<repo> service account, state/log/artifact buckets, and plan/apply Cloud Build triggers per app-infra repo. modules/single_project/main.tf:85-110 grants each such SA the configured sa_roles on the sample project, roles/compute.networkViewer on the BU folder, and roles/compute.networkUser on shared VPC subnets. business_unit_1/shared/example_infra_pipeline.tf wires this up by default: enable_cloudbuild_deploy = (cloud_builder_artifact_repo != ""), and 0-bootstrap unconditionally creates the Cloud Build module and populates that output, so this path executes by default in the reference deployment. sa_roles is non-trivial (compute.instanceAdmin.v1, iam.serviceAccountUser, iam.serviceAccountAdmin on the svpc/floating/peering projects) — real, load-bearing IAM that 5-app-infra's env_base/confidential_space resources need (e.g., attaching instances to shared VPC subnets, managing instance service accounts).

On the port side, both Go and TS confirmed to implement only the empty project creation: pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit.go:212-236 (deployInfraPipelineProject, called from main.go:106) creates only the prj-c-bu1-infra-pipeline project with API activation — no SAs, CSRs, buckets, triggers, or IAM grants. The Cloud Build equivalent (example_infra_pipelines.go) is gated by `//go:build example`, and grep confirms `deployInfraPipelines` has zero callers anywhere in the Go port. pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/shared/index.ts likewise creates only the ProjectFactory project; the full Cloud Build implementation only exists as inert `.ts.example` files never imported by any compiled .ts file.

I also checked for an alternative GitHub-Actions/WIF-based replacement, since the ports document "Per approved deviation, Go/TS foundations default to GitHub Actions WIF" for CI/CD. pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd (GitHubOIDC, GitLabOIDC, CloudBuild components) is real and is wired into 0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.go, but it only provisions a Workload Identity Pool/Provider and per-SA attribute bindings for the five foundation-level stage repos (bootstrap, org, env, net, proj) — there is no "app-infra" or per-business-unit-app-repo entry in that stageRepos map, and no networkUser/networkViewer/sa_roles-equivalent IAM grants anywhere in 4-projects, 5-app-infra, or pulumi-library for any pipeline identity. 5-app-infra's own README instructs users to authenticate via a WIF provider/SA that is simply never created for that stage. So the reviewer's claim that no GitHub-Actions-based replacement is provisioned for app repos is also accurate — the WIF mechanism exists but doesn't reach this use case.

Severity: "major" is fair, not inflated. This isn't dead/informational code — it's the actual least-privilege deploy identity and IAM plumbing that stage 5 (5-app-infra) needs to run as an automated, scoped pipeline rather than under a human's broad credentials. Its absence means a user following the port's documented GitHub Actions workflow for 5-app-infra has no working deploy identity and must manually reverse-engineer and provision the SA, WIF bindings, and IAM grants themselves — a real functional and security-posture regression versus the TF reference. It does not fully block someone from running `pulumi up` locally under their own elevated credentials, so escalating to "critical/blocking" would be overstated, but "major" is accurate.

#### [TS] App-infra pipelines missing: no per-repo SAs, repos, state/artifact/log buckets, triggers, or their outputs
_Area: 4-projects · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF business_unit_1/shared deploys modules/infra_pipelines (source repos, per-repo pipeline service accounts, CB plan/apply triggers, state/artifact/log buckets, AR IAM, org browser IAM) plus the build_confidential_space_image step, and exports terraform_service_accounts, repos, state_buckets, artifact_buckets, log_buckets, plan/apply_triggers_id, image_name, enable_cloudbuild_deploy — consumed by 4-projects env stacks (sa_roles grants via enable_cloudbuild_deploy) and 5-app-infra. TS shared creates only the pipeline PROJECT and exports only app_infra_pipeline_project_id; single_project's enableCloudbuildDeploy/appInfraPipelineServiceAccounts path is therefore permanently dead. (Go has deployInfraPipelines code in example_infra_pipelines.go but also never calls it from main.go.)

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/4-projects/modules/infra_pipelines/main.tf and business_unit_1/shared/example_infra_pipeline.tf:29-160, outputs.tf (13 outputs). TS checked: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/shared/index.ts (50 lines, one ProjectFactory, one output); 4-projects/modules/single_project.ts:86-99 (unreachable pipeline-SA branch — no caller passes enableCloudbuildDeploy).

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed: 4-projects/modules/infra_pipelines/main.tf + business_unit_1/shared/example_infra_pipeline.tf really do create per-repo CSRs, per-repo pipeline SAs, state/artifact/log GCS buckets, CB plan/apply triggers, AR/org IAM, and the confidential-space image build, exporting the 13 outputs cited (terraform_service_accounts, repos, artifact_buckets, state_buckets, log_buckets, plan_triggers_id, apply_triggers_id, gar_name, artifact_registry_repository_id, default_region).

TS side: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/shared/index.ts is indeed only ~50 lines that create a single ProjectFactory project and return one output (app_infra_pipeline_project_id), exactly as the reviewer states. modules/single_project.ts:86-99 does have an enableCloudbuildDeploy/appInfraPipelineServiceAccounts branch, and grepping all 4-projects callers (shared/index.ts, business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts) confirms none of them ever set enableCloudbuildDeploy, so that branch is indeed unreachable dead code today.

However, the port is not silently missing this by accident — it is a documented, deliberate architectural deviation, not an oversight:
1. A full working implementation of the Cloud Build-based infra_pipelines module DOES exist in the repo, at 4-projects/modules/infra_pipelines.ts.example and business_unit_1/shared/example_infra_pipeline.ts.example, faithfully mirroring the TF module (per-repo CSR, per-repo SA, state/artifact/log buckets, org-browser IAM, AR-writer IAM, workload-identity-admin, etc.). Its header comment states: "Per approved deviation, Go/TS foundations default to GitHub Actions WIF. Cloud Build pipelines are provided as an .example reference. To enable: rename this file... and import it." This is the same non-default-file convention used throughout the TS port for optional/alternate paths (build_jenkins.ts.example, build_gitlab.ts.example, interconnect.ts.example, vpn.ts.example, 5-app-infra/*/index.ts.example).
2. /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/docs/ERRATA.md explicitly documents this as an intended deviation under "CI/CD Tool Support" and "Cloud Build Integration": "Cloud Source Repositories are not used as they are a deprecated Google Cloud product" and "CI/CD is handled entirely by GitHub Actions with Workload Identity Federation (WIF)" rather than Cloud Build triggers/CSR.
3. The replacement mechanism is real and wired up, not just an excuse: 0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.ts and 0-bootstrap/sa.ts set up GitHub Actions WIF service accounts, and build/pulumi-preview.yml + build/pulumi-up.yml are real workflow templates copied into each stage's repo (per the 4-projects and 5-app-infra READMEs), giving 4-projects and 5-app-infra a working, non-Cloud-Build deployment pipeline.
4. 5-app-infra does not consume terraform_service_accounts/repos/state_buckets/etc. either — its real (non-.example) index.ts files are minimal scaffolds returning project_id/region, with the fuller compute-workload example also gated behind an .example file, consistently with the same CI/CD deviation.

So the reviewer's factual assertions are accurate (TF has it, active TS shared/index.ts lacks it, the SA-roles branch is unreachable), but the claim's framing as an unintentional gap/bug ("single_project's ... path is therefore permanently dead") mischaracterizes what is actually a documented, repo-wide, intentional substitution of Cloud-Build/CSR-based pipelines with GitHub-Actions-WIF-based pipelines, with a working reference implementation left in place under the port's established .example convention for operators who want to opt into the Cloud Build path.

**Verifier note / correction:** The gap is real (the active TS code path lacks CSR repos, per-repo pipeline SAs, state/artifact/log buckets, CB triggers, and the confidential-space image build, and single_project.ts's enableCloudbuildDeploy branch is unreachable), but severity is overstated as "major" unintentional breakage. This is a documented, intentional architectural substitution (GitHub Actions + WIF replacing Cloud Build/CSR triggers) applied consistently across the whole TS port and explicitly called out in docs/ERRATA.md, with a complete, working reference implementation already provided at 4-projects/modules/infra_pipelines.ts.example and business_unit_1/shared/example_infra_pipeline.ts.example for operators who want the Cloud-Build path. More accurate framing: minor/informational — dead optional code path plus an .example reference implementation not wired in by default, consistent with the port's documented CI/CD deviation — not a functional regression.

#### [BOTH] Confidential Space workload image build pipeline (Dockerfile, confidential_data, Cloud Build) not ported
_Area: 4-projects · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Upstream commit 3bfcd94 (add confidential space) adds to 4-projects/business_unit_1/shared: a Dockerfile and 'confidential_data' sample workload, module build_confidential_space_image (gcloud builds submit producing <region>-docker.pkg.dev/<cicd-project>/tf-runners/confidential_space_image:latest), plus ~7 supporting IAM bindings (builder roles, artifactregistry.repoAdmin, logging.logWriter, workloadIdentityPoolAdmin, bucket storage.admin) and a time_sleep IAM-propagation guard. Neither port has any of this: no Dockerfile, no confidential_data, no image build, no supporting IAM. Yet both ports' 5-app-infra confidential space stacks reference exactly that image ('tf-runners/confidential_space_image:latest'), which nothing in the ports ever builds — the Confidential Space example VM can never run its attested workload without out-of-band manual steps. Both ports DID port the env-level confidential space project + workload SA (Go 4-projects/confidential_space.go; TS business_unit_1/*/index.ts:302+) and the 5-app-infra confidential_space module, so the feature is half-ported.
