Notably the same dead reference to build/pulumi-ci.yml also appears in README.md:113 and 0-bootstrap/README.md:150 (as a markdown link), so this is a repo-wide stale-reference issue, not confined to ERRATA.md.

**Verifier note / correction:** No correction needed to the core claim; severity of 'major' is reasonable to keep as-is. It is a documentation-accuracy defect (nothing in the build/deploy pipeline consumes ERRATA.md, so it can't break a deployment), but it actively misdirects an operator with a nonexistent file path and a false 'no alternatives exist' claim while fully-built alternatives ship in the same repo — that's more than a cosmetic doc nit.

#### [BOTH] foundation-deployer reduced to a shell script: no resume, no IAM preflight, no vet, no CI orchestration
_Area: helpers · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's helpers/foundation-deployer is a full CLI: (1) -validate performs granular IAM permission preflight against examples/iam/default-permissions.yaml (utils/iam_validate.go + standalone cmd/iam-validate), including SCC notification and tag-key checks; (2) steps-file resume (-steps_file/-list_steps/-reset_step, steps/steps.go) so a failed multi-hour deploy resumes where it stopped; (3) runs 'gcloud beta terraform vet' per stage during deployment (stages/vet.go); (4) orchestrates the real git/CI flow — pushes each stage into its repo, creates plan/env branches, and waits on Cloud Build/GitHub/GitLab pipeline results (utils/git.go, gcp/gcp.go build polling, github/github.go, gitlab/gitlab.go) for build types cb/github/gitlab; (5) retry logic for transient errors (utils/retry.go). The ports' helpers/foundation-deployer/deploy.sh (identical in both) only loops 'pulumi preview/up/destroy' over local stage directories: no validation, no resume state, no policy vet, no git/CI integration, no build-type awareness, and the networks mode is hardcoded to 3-networks-svpc with a 'change this line' comment (TF selects via enable_hub_and_spoke tfvar).

**Evidence:** TF: helpers/foundation-deployer/{main.go,steps/steps.go,stages/vet.go,utils/iam_validate.go,cmd/iam-validate,gcp/gcp.go,github/github.go,gitlab/gitlab.go}, global.tfvars.example (enable_hub_and_spoke, cicd build type). Ports: pulumi_go-example-foundation/helpers/foundation-deployer/deploy.sh (279 lines; STAGES array line 70-77 hardcodes 3-networks-svpc), README.md documents the omissions; TS identical (diff empty).

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed exactly as described: helpers/foundation-deployer is a 35-file Go CLI with -validate (utils/iam_validate.go + standalone cmd/iam-validate, checking TestIamPermissions against org/folder/billing scopes), -steps_file/-list_steps/-reset_step (steps/steps.go), gcloud beta terraform vet per stage (stages/vet.go), git/CI push+branch+poll orchestration for cb/github/gitlab build types (utils/git.go, gcp/gcp.go, github/github.go, gitlab/gitlab.go), and utils/retry.go transient-error retries. global.tfvars.example confirms enable_hub_and_spoke drives network-mode selection.

Both ports' helpers/foundation-deployer contain only deploy.sh (278 lines, byte-identical between go/ts) plus a README documenting the gap. Verified: no resume/state file, no per-step skip logic (script uses `set -euo pipefail` and reruns all stages from the top unless the user manually passes --stage/--stack), no retry logic anywhere in either port or in pulumi-library, and the STAGES array hard-codes "3-networks-svpc" with a literal '# Change to 3-networks-hub-and-spoke if using that mode' comment — confirmed both 3-networks-svpc/ and 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/ directories exist in the port, so this really is a manual-edit requirement vs. TF's tfvar-driven selection.

However, two of the five cited sub-claims are partially covered elsewhere in the port, which the claim omits and which matters for severity:
1. IAM preflight: scripts/validate-requirements.sh DOES perform an IAM/API/tool preflight check (5 org-level roles + billing admin + required APIs), invoked separately from deploy.sh. It is materially less granular than TF's TestIamPermissions-based check against examples/iam/default-permissions.yaml (no per-stage/per-resource permission enumeration, no SCC-notification or tag-key checks, and it warns rather than hard-fails on unverifiable permissions), so the 'no IAM preflight' framing is not quite accurate.
2. terraform vet: both ports ship a real, non-trivial Pulumi CrossGuard policy-library (policy-library/ with actual index.ts/policies/*.ts in the TS port implementing no-default-network, no-sa-key-creation, no-public-ip, require-private-google-access, etc., explicitly documented as 'the Pulumi equivalent of ... gcloud beta terraform vet'). This is a genuine, differently-implemented equivalent for policy enforcement. The real gap is narrower than claimed: it is not invoked automatically by deploy.sh or the build/*.yml CI templates (no --policy-pack flag anywhere), so it exists but isn't wired into the deploy flow.
3. CI orchestration: the custom git-push/branch-create/build-poll logic (utils/git.go, gcp/gcp.go build polling, github.go, gitlab.go) is indeed absent, but the ports substitute native CI definitions (build/pulumi-preview.yml GitHub Actions workflow with WIF auth and PR preview/comment, build/gitlab-ci.yml with branch-to-stack mapping) that achieve the same end-to-end outcome (preview on PR, apply on merge) by delegating orchestration to the CI platform itself rather than reimplementing polling in Go.

Net: the core claim — deploy.sh is a thin, dumb loop lacking automated resume, granular IAM validation, automated policy vetting, and custom CI polling/retry logic, with a hardcoded network-topology comment — is verified true and is a legitimate, well-evidenced functional regression. Severity 'major' is reasonable for a tool meant to safely orchestrate a multi-hour, multi-stage production deployment, though the description overstates the IAM and vet gaps by not mentioning the (weaker/differently-wired) equivalents that do exist in scripts/validate-requirements.sh and policy-library/.

**Verifier note / correction:** Not a full gap on all five sub-points as framed. Confirmed fully missing: automated resume/steps-file, retry logic, and the hardcoded (not tfvar-driven) network-mode selection. Partially missing/mischaracterized: (a) IAM preflight exists via scripts/validate-requirements.sh but is coarser/less granular than TF's TestIamPermissions check (no SCC/tag-key checks, no per-stage yaml-driven permission list) — should be described as 'less granular' not 'no IAM preflight'; (b) a real Pulumi CrossGuard policy-library exists as the terraform-vet equivalent but is not automatically invoked by deploy.sh or the CI templates — should be described as 'vet equivalent exists but isn't wired into the deploy flow' rather than 'no vet'; (c) custom git/CI push-and-poll code is absent but is substituted by native GitHub Actions/GitLab CI pipeline definitions in build/ that accomplish the same preview/apply workflow through the CI platform's own orchestration rather than a bespoke Go implementation.

#### [BOTH] All 13 GKE security constraints have no Pulumi equivalent
_Area: policy-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF actively enforces 13 high-severity GKE constraints: gke_allow_only_private_cluster, gke_allowed_node_sa_scope, gke_container_optimized_os, gke_dashboard_disable, gke_disable_default_service_account, gke_disable_legacy_endpoints, gke_enable_alias_ip_ranges, gke_legacy_abac, gke_master_authorized_networks_enabled, gke_node_pool_auto_repair, gke_node_pool_auto_upgrade, gke_restrict_client_auth_methods, gke_restrict_pod_traffic. The TS policy pack contains no policy touching gcp:container/* resource types, and no equivalent exists in pulumi-library. Any GKE cluster deployed via 5-app-infra workloads would face zero of these guardrails.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/policies/constraints/gke_*.yaml (13 files, all severity: high). Port: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts — grep for container/gke returns nothing; /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/policy-library has no code; /home/user/pulumi-library has no CrossGuard policies.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF side: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/policies/constraints/gke_*.yaml contains exactly the 13 named files, each with `severity: high`, backed by matching Gatekeeper templates in policies/templates/ (gcp_gke_private_cluster_v1.yaml, gcp_gke_legacy_abac_v1.yaml, gcp_gke_node_auto_upgrade_v1.yaml, etc.) — the TF functionality is real and exactly as described.

Port side: exhaustive search found no equivalent anywhere.
- pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts contains only 7 CrossGuard policies (project, IAM, network) touching gcp:organizations/project, gcp:serviceaccount/key, gcp:*/iAMMember|iAMBinding, gcp:compute/instance, gcp:compute/subnetwork — zero references to gcp:container/* resource types.
- pulumi_go-example-foundation/policy-library/ has no code at all, just a README pointing at the TS pack.
- pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) has no CrossGuard PolicyPack code whatsoever (grepped for `policy.PolicyPack`/`@pulumi/policy`/CrossGuard — zero hits in either language). Its `go/pkg/policy` package only wraps org-policy constraints (gcp.orgpolicy.Policy resource), unrelated to CrossGuard resource-validation policies. Mentions of 'gke'/'container' in pulumi-library (vpc_sc.go, network tests) are just restricted-services lists and subnet secondary-range naming, not guardrails.
- Neither port's 5-app-infra, test/, build/, or scripts/ directories contain any container.Cluster resource usage, GKE test scaffolding, OPA/Rego, gatekeeper, config-validator, or 'terraform vet' equivalent that could be enforcing these rules by another name.
- Tellingly, the port's own README (policy-library/README.md) includes an explicit upstream-to-CrossGuard mapping table listing only 4 constraints as ported (external-IP, service-usage org policy, IAM-domain org policy, resource labels) — the port's own documentation does not claim any GKE coverage, corroborating the gap rather than hiding a differently-named equivalent.

On severity: not inflated. Both 1-org/projects.go, 4-projects/business_unit.go (Go port) and 1-org/index.ts (TS port) explicitly enable container.googleapis.com at the project level (mirroring TF's own 1-org/modules/network/main.tf), meaning the foundation is designed to let business-unit teams provision GKE clusters downstream via 4-projects/5-app-infra even though neither the TF reference nor either port currently ships an example GKE cluster in 5-app-infra. That matches upstream's own posture (guardrails shipped proactively, independent of whether the example workload uses GKE), so 'major' is a fair characterization of a real, unmitigated compliance gap for any GKE workload teams add later — not merely an unused/informational stub.

#### [BOTH] BigQuery world-readable dataset constraint missing
_Area: policy-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF enforces bigquery_world_readable.yaml (GCPBigQueryDatasetWorldReadableConstraintV1, severity high) blocking BigQuery datasets readable by allUsers/allAuthenticatedUsers. The port has no policy inspecting gcp:bigquery/dataset:Dataset accesses or BigQuery IAM resources.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/policies/constraints/bigquery_world_readable.yaml. Port: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts — no bigquery reference; nothing in Go port or pulumi-library.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed: policy-library/policies/constraints/bigquery_world_readable.yaml exists exactly as described (GCPBigQueryDatasetWorldReadableConstraintV1, severity high, blocks datasets readable by allUsers/allAuthenticatedUsers).

Port side: exhaustively searched both ports and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) for "bigquery", "Dataset", "allUsers"/"allAuthenticatedUsers", and any OPA/conftest/config-validator residue. Findings:
- The shared CrossGuard policy pack lives solely at pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts (the Go port's policy-library/README.md explicitly says it defers to this same TS pack — there is no separate Go policy implementation). I read the full file: it implements only 7 policies (no-default-network, project-labels-required, no-sa-key-creation, no-public-access, no-public-ip, require-private-google-access, require-flow-logs).
- The one policy that could plausibly cover this — "no-public-access" — only inspects a hardcoded list of resource types: gcp:projects/organizations/folder IAMMember/IAMBinding and gcp:storage/bucketIAMMember:BucketIAMMember. It does NOT include gcp:bigquery/datasetIamMember or datasetIamBinding, and it does not inspect the `accesses` (classic ACL) field on gcp:bigquery/dataset:Dataset — the actual mechanism (specialGroup: allAuthenticatedUsers / iamMember: allUsers in a dataset's access entries) that the TF constraint targets.
- No bigquery-related policy exists anywhere else: not in pulumi-library/go or pulumi-library/ts, not in scripts/build/test directories, and there is no leftover OPA/conftest/terraform-vet mechanism carrying the original constraints forward.
- This is not a moot/dead-code gap: both ports actually provision real gcp.bigquery.Dataset resources (a billing-export dataset and a log-analytics linked dataset in 1-org), and pulumi-library/go/pkg/data defines an additional (currently unused) DataPlatform component creating raw/curated datasets — so the resource type is genuinely in play in this codebase, not hypothetical.

Overall the claim is accurate and the port has zero equivalent, in either language, for the world-readable BigQuery dataset check.

#### [BOTH] Cloud SQL constraints (no public IP, require SSL) missing
_Area: policy-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF enforces GCPSQLPublicIpConstraintV1 (sql_public_ip.yaml) and GCPSQLSSLConstraintV1 (sql_ssl.yaml), both severity high, preventing Cloud SQL instances with public IPs or without SSL enforcement. The port's 'no-public-ip' policy only checks gcp:compute/instance:Instance accessConfigs; no policy examines gcp:sql/databaseInstance settings (ipConfiguration.ipv4Enabled / requireSsl).

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/policies/constraints/sql_public_ip.yaml and sql_ssl.yaml. Port: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts:95-111 (compute instances only); no sql checks anywhere in either port or pulumi-library.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed: policy-library/policies/constraints/sql_public_ip.yaml (GCPSQLPublicIpConstraintV1) and sql_ssl.yaml (GCPSQLSSLConstraintV1) both exist with severity: high, exactly as described.

However, the claim that 'no sql checks anywhere in either port or pulumi-library' is false for the public-IP half. Both pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/policies.go and pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts deploy the GCP organization-policy constraints `sql.restrictPublicIp` and `sql.restrictAuthorizedNetworks` as real gcp:orgpolicy/policy:Policy resources (via pulumi-library/go/pkg/policy's NewOrgPolicy / the TS OrgPolicyBoolean component), applied org-wide/folder-wide in the 1-org stage. This is not a stub — it's a genuine, always-on, API-level enforcement mechanism. It directly mirrors terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/org_policy.tf lines 35-36, where the TF reference itself sets these same two org policies as belt-and-suspenders alongside the OPA GCPSQLPublicIpConstraintV1 constraint. So 'no public IP' is functionally covered in both ports, just via a different (arguably stronger) mechanism than a CrossGuard resource check — this satisfies 'equivalent behavior implemented differently.'

The SSL half of the claim holds up: I grepped both ports and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) exhaustively for ssl/SSL/requireSsl/databaseInstance/ipConfiguration — the only hits were false positives (substring 'ssl' inside 'AccessLevel'/'accessLevel'). There is no native GCP org policy for requiring SSL on Cloud SQL, and no CrossGuard/OPA-equivalent policy checking requireSsl anywhere in either port's policy-library (go port's policy-library is just a README pointing at the TS pack; TS port's index.ts has exactly 7 policies — no-default-network, project-labels-required, no-sa-key-creation, no-public-access, no-public-ip [compute only], require-private-google-access, require-flow-logs — none reference SQL). So that portion of the gap is real.

Neither the TF reference nor either port actually provisions any Cloud SQL/google_sql_database_instance resources anywhere in the repo — this is a landing-zone/foundation repo, so this is a dormant guardrail for downstream workloads (stage 5-app-infra), not something exercised by the foundation's own resources today. That bears on severity, not on whether the gap is real.

**Verifier note / correction:** Narrow the claim to only the SSL constraint. 'No public IP' is NOT missing — both ports enforce `sql.restrictPublicIp` (and `sql.restrictAuthorizedNetworks`) as native GCP organization policies in 1-org, mirroring the TF reference's own org_policy.tf, which is a real and arguably stronger control than the OPA GCPSQLPublicIpConstraintV1 preview-time check. Only the 'require SSL' (GCPSQLSSLConstraintV1/requireSsl) equivalent is genuinely absent from both ports and pulumi-library. Severity should be downgraded from 'major' (claimed for both constraints) to minor/moderate: just one of the two cited high-severity constraints is truly missing, and it guards a resource type not provisioned anywhere in the current codebase.

#### [GO] Go port contains no policy pack at all — only a README pointing at the TS repo
_Area: policy-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The Go port's policy-library/ directory holds a single README.md that instructs users to 'git clone' the TypeScript foundation's GitHub repo to obtain the policy pack. No policy code exists in the Go repo, and the shared pulumi-library has no CrossGuard pack either (go/pkg/policy and ts/packages/org-policy implement GCP Organization Policy constraints — a different mechanism, used by stage 1-org, not preview-time plan validation). A Go-foundation user has no runnable policy enforcement without fetching an external repository, and the Go repo's CI cannot reference a local pack.

**Evidence:** TF: entire /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/ (28 constraints + 81 templates + lib/*.rego). Go port: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/policy-library/ contains only README.md (lines 14-16, 35-42 direct users to clone github.com/VitruvianSoftware/pulumi_ts-example-foundation). Checked /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/policy/org_policy.go and /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/* — no CrossGuard/@pulumi/policy code anywhere.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified end-to-end. (1) TF side confirmed: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/ genuinely contains 28 constraint YAMLs, 81 template YAMLs, and lib/*.rego (3 files) — a real, substantial OPA-based policy pack validated via Config Validator/gcloud beta terraform vet. (2) Go port confirmed empty: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/policy-library/ contains only README.md, which explicitly instructs cloning github.com/VitruvianSoftware/pulumi_ts-example-foundation to get a runnable pack. No CrossGuard code, no policies/, no equivalent exists anywhere else in the Go repo (exhaustive grep for opa/rego/constraint/crossguard/policy-pack/policypack across the whole repo turns up nothing but that README). (3) pulumi-library confirmed to have no CrossGuard pack in either go/ or ts/ subtrees — go/pkg/policy/org_policy.go and ts/packages/org-policy wrap gcp:orgpolicy resources (organization policy constraints applied by 1-org at deploy time), a genuinely different mechanism from preview-time plan validation; no other package name (guard/compliance/validate) hides an equivalent. (4) Confirmed the TS port DOES have a real, working CrossGuard pack (policy-library/index.ts, 7 policies covering project/IAM/network domains) — so the underlying architecture (TS-only, since @pulumi/policy has no Go SDK) is a legitimate reason the Go port can't author its own pack, but the Go repo also does not vendor/copy the shared TS pack locally. (5) Confirmed the consequence claim: the Go repo's actual CI (.github/workflows/ci.yml) and its deployment-pipeline template (build/pulumi-preview.yml) contain zero references to `--policy-pack` or any policy step — there is no automated policy enforcement wired in at all, so a Go-foundation user genuinely gets no runnable local policy enforcement out of the box. This is a real, well-evidenced gap.

**Verifier note / correction:** Severity as stated is largely accurate but slightly overstated in one respect: the claim implies total absence of any enforcement path, when in fact a working CrossGuard pack does exist one directory/repo away (the TS port's policy-library/index.ts is real and functional, not vaporware), and the Go README does document a concrete, usable workaround (clone + point --policy-pack at it). Also worth noting neither the Go nor the TS port's actual CI templates (build/pulumi-preview.yml) currently pass a --policy-pack flag at all — so 'CI cannot reference a local pack' somewhat overstates the delta, since even the TS port's shipped CI isn't wired to invoke its own pack automatically today. The core defect — no colocated/vendored policy code in the Go repo or shared library, forcing a manual cross-repo, cross-language fetch, with materially thinner coverage (7 policies vs. 28+81 TF constraints) than upstream even when fetched — is real and reasonably called 'major' for a foundation whose stated purpose is security/compliance guardrails, but it is not a 'zero enforcement possible' situation.

#### [GO] Go port ships no policy pack code at all
_Area: policy-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The Go port's policy-library/ directory contains only a README that points users to clone the separate TypeScript foundation repo to obtain the CrossGuard pack. Unlike the TF reference (policy-library/ with lib/*.rego plus 28 constraint YAMLs and ~60 templates usable in-place by tf-wrapper.sh), the Go repo is not self-contained: no policy code exists in the repo or in pulumi-library/go. Also note the TS pack implements only 7 policies vs the reference's 28 active constraints.

**Evidence:** TF: policy-library/policies/constraints/*.yaml (28 files), policy-library/lib/constraints.rego. Go port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/policy-library/ contains README.md only (ls). pulumi-library/go/pkg/policy is org-policy constraints (org_policy.go), not CrossGuard. TS port has policy-library/index.ts (144 lines, 7 policies).

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

TF reference (step 1): policy-library/policies/constraints has exactly 28 YAML constraint instances, policy-library/lib/constraints.rego and util.rego exist, and policy-library/policies/templates has 81 constraint-template YAML files (not ~60, but the "usable in-place by tf-wrapper.sh" characterization is accurate — build/tf-wrapper.sh line 278 calls `gcloud beta terraform vet ... --policy-library=...` directly against this directory).

Go port (step 2): pulumi_go-example-foundation/policy-library/ contains only README.md — confirmed by direct listing. That README explicitly says CrossGuard's policy SDK is TypeScript-only and directs users to clone/copy the sibling TS repo's policy-library. Searched the entire Go port tree and pulumi-library/go for any rego/CrossGuard/policy-pack code: pulumi-library/go/pkg/policy/org_policy.go is a GCP-native Org Policy (v2) component wrapper (orgpolicy.Policy), a fundamentally different, coarser mechanism than CrossGuard's preview-time resource-shape validation — not an equivalent implementation, confirming the reviewer's observation. No CI file in the Go port (build/pulumi-preview.yml, pulumi-up.yml, gitlab-ci.yml) wires in any --policy-pack flag either, so the gap is real at the repo level.

Cross-checked the TS port: policy-library/index.ts contains exactly 7 real CrossGuard policies (confirmed by reading the file), matching the claim precisely. Note TS's own CI files also don't invoke --policy-pack automatically, and its README describes a policies/*.ts file layout that doesn't match the actual flat index.ts — a minor doc inconsistency, not relevant to the Go claim.

The Go port's own docs/ERRATA.md explicitly documents this as a known, deliberate design gap ("This port: Does not include policy validation infrastructure... Policy enforcement is handled via organization policies deployed in Stage 1 and via Pulumi's CrossGuard... framework, which can be adopted separately"), and CHANGELOG.md calls policy-library a "skeleton." So the omission is real, not an oversight the port is unaware of.

Mitigating factor for severity: the Go port's 1-org/policies.go does implement ~7 real GCP-native Org Policy constraints (compute.vmExternalIpAccess, iam.allowedPolicyMemberDomains, essentialcontacts.allowedContactDomains, cloudbuild.allowedWorkerPools, etc.) via pulumi-library/go/pkg/policy, providing some overlapping preventive coverage for a subset of what TF's OPA constraints check — but this is server-side/deploy-time enforcement, not CrossGuard's preview-time IaC-diff validation, and doesn't cover the majority of the 28 TF constraints (labels, SA keys, public IP, flow logs, etc. are not covered by these org policies).

**Verifier note / correction:** Real gap, but two details in the claim's evidence are slightly off and severity is arguably a notch high: (1) the reference has ~81 constraint templates, not ~60. (2) Calling the TS repo "the separate TypeScript foundation repo" undersells that it's a sibling repo in the same multi-repo project (pulumi_ts-example-foundation is checked out locally alongside), so a user working across all three repos can reference `../pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library` directly — it isn't an external/unmaintained dependency, and the Go port's own README and ERRATA.md are transparent about this being an intentional architectural choice (CrossGuard's policy SDK is TypeScript-only, so there is no way to write a native Go policy pack even in principle). Also worth noting neither port's CI actually wires `--policy-pack` into pulumi preview/up automatically, so even the "complete" TS pack isn't enforced in the pipeline today — the gap is as much "policy enforcement isn't wired into CI for either port" as it is "Go has no local pack." Severity of "major" is reasonable for the coverage drop (28 to 7 policies) and lack of self-containment, but should be scoped as "known, documented limitation shared in spirit by both ports' CI wiring" rather than framed as Go uniquely and silently lacking any policy story.</correction>


#### [TS] Policy pack is missing PulumiPolicy.yaml and cannot be loaded by the Pulumi CLI
_Area: policy-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** A CrossGuard policy pack requires a PulumiPolicy.yaml file (declaring runtime: nodejs) for 'pulumi preview --policy-pack <dir>' or 'pulumi policy publish' to load it. The TS policy-library contains only index.ts, package.json, tsconfig.json and README.md. As shipped, even the manual usage documented in the README ('pulumi preview --policy-pack ./policy-library') would fail to load the pack, so none of the 7 implemented policies can actually be enforced.

**Evidence:** find across /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation, /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation and /home/user/pulumi-library for 'PulumiPolicy*' returns nothing. Pack contents: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/{index.ts,package.json,tsconfig.json,README.md} only. README usage instructions at policy-library/README.md:53-61.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all fronts.

1. TF side is real: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library contains a genuine constraint set (policies/constraints/*.yaml, policies/templates/*.yaml) used with Config Validator / `gcloud beta terraform vet`. The TS port's own README explicitly maps this to a Pulumi CrossGuard equivalent ("These are the Pulumi equivalent of the upstream Terraform foundation's OPA constraints").

2. TS pack contents match the evidence exactly: only index.ts, package.json, tsconfig.json, README.md. index.ts does define a real `policy.PolicyPack("foundation-policies", {...})` with 7 policies (no-default-network, project-labels-required, no-sa-key-creation, no-public-access, no-public-ip, require-private-google-access, require-flow-logs) — so the logic exists, but the loader manifest does not.

3. I verified the actual Pulumi Go SDK source vendored in the sandbox's module cache (github.com/pulumi/pulumi/sdk/v3@v3.231.0) to check the loading mechanics rather than relying on memory:
   - go/common/resource/plugin/analyzer_plugin.go: `NewPolicyAnalyzer` does `projPath := filepath.Join(policyPackPath, "PulumiPolicy.yaml")` then `workspace.LoadPolicyPack(projPath)`, and returns a hard error ("failed to load Pulumi policy project located at %q") if that fails.
   - go/common/workspace/loaders.go: `LoadPolicyPack` reads that exact file from disk and errors if it's absent.
   - go/common/workspace/paths.go: confirms `PolicyPackFile = "PulumiPolicy"` is the required basename (yaml/yml/json extensions only — no fallback to package.json or Pulumi.yaml).
   This proves conclusively that `pulumi preview --policy-pack ./policy-library` — the exact command the README tells users to run — cannot load the pack without a PulumiPolicy.yaml.

4. Searched exhaustively for any alternative: `find` for PulumiPolicy* across all three repos returns nothing; grepped the whole ts port repo for "policy-library"/"PolicyPack" references outside the directory itself (CI workflows, build/, scripts/, test/) — found zero, so there is no other invocation path that might set the runtime out-of-band. Also checked /home/user/pulumi-library/ts for any policy-pack scaffolding — its "policy"-named packages (org-policy, network-firewall-policy, hierarchical-firewall-policy) are GCP Organization Policy / firewall-policy infrastructure resources, unrelated to CrossGuard analyzer plugins.
   As a side note, the Go port's policy-library (/home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/policy-library) contains only a README.md with zero implementation — worse than TS, but that's a separate issue since this claim is scoped to ts.

Severity check: "major" is well-calibrated, not inflated. This isn't a cosmetic/informational gap — it renders an entire governance feature (7 policies, explicitly documented as the CrossGuard replacement for the upstream OPA layer) completely non-instantiable via the only documented workflow. It doesn't block core infra deployment (hence not "critical"), but the policy-enforcement subsystem is 100% inoperable as shipped, matching "major" accurately.

#### [BOTH] Policy validation is never exercised: no test, CI job, or deploy step runs the CrossGuard pack (TF vets every stage in every test)
_Area: policy-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Even where a policy pack exists (TS port's policy-library/index.ts with 7 policies), nothing runs it: the e2e.yml workflow, ci.yml, build/gitlab-ci.yml, build/pulumi-preview.yml, and helpers/foundation-deployer/deploy.sh all invoke pulumi preview/up without --policy-pack, and no unit test loads the pack. In TF, policy-library validation runs on every stage in every integration test (WithPolicyLibraryPath → gcloud beta terraform vet), so policy regressions fail CI; in both ports a broken or violated policy would never be detected. Additionally the TS README documents a policies/{project,iam,network}-policies.ts structure and 9 policies, but index.ts implements only 7 — 'deletion-policy-prevent' and 'restrict-owner-role' are documented but not implemented.

**Evidence:** TS pack: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts (7 policies; README.md documents 9 across three files that do not exist). No --policy-pack usage: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/build/gitlab-ci.yml, /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/build/gitlab-ci.yml, .github/workflows/e2e.yml and ci.yml in both ports, helpers/foundation-deployer/deploy.sh in both ports. TF comparison: tft.WithPolicyLibraryPath in all 8 suites under /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/test/integration/.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all fronts. (1) TF side: all 8 integration test suites (bootstrap, org, envs, projects, projects-shared, networks, app-infra, shared) call tft.WithPolicyLibraryPath("/workspace/policy-library", ...), so every TF stage is vetted against the OPA policy library on every test run. (2) Port side: the TS policy-library/index.ts defines a real CrossGuard PolicyPack with exactly 7 policies (no-default-network, project-labels-required, no-sa-key-creation, no-public-access, no-public-ip, require-private-google-access, require-flow-logs), while policy-library/README.md documents 9 policies across a policies/{project,iam,network}-policies.ts structure that does not exist on disk — 'deletion-policy-prevent' and 'restrict-owner-role' are documented-only, confirming the doc/impl mismatch. The Go port's policy-library/ contains only a README (no index.ts/PolicyPack at all), pointing users to clone the TS pack manually instead of having an actual implementation. (3) Exercise search: a global grep for '--policy-pack' and 'policyPacks' across both entire repos shows every occurrence is inside README.md documentation/examples — zero occurrences in .github/workflows/e2e.yml, ci.yml, build/gitlab-ci.yml, build/pulumi-preview.yml, or helpers/foundation-deployer/deploy.sh in either port. Grep of test/ directories and pulumi-library/ (go and ts subdirs) for PolicyPack/@pulumi/policy/policy-library returns nothing, confirming no unit test loads the pack either. So the claim's every specific assertion (which files were checked, what they contain, the 7-vs-9 discrepancy, and the TF WithPolicyLibraryPath comparison) is accurate and verified directly against the source. Severity of 'major' is reasonable, not inflated: this is a compliance/security-policy enforcement mechanism that TF treats as a first-class, always-on CI gate, and in both Pulumi ports it is entirely inert (dead code in TS, entirely absent in Go), meaning policy regressions or violations would silently pass through preview/up/CI with no detection — a meaningful parity gap for a security-focused reference architecture.

Public grants through any of these pass the port's policy silently.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/policies/constraints/iam_deny_public.yaml (role: roles/*, target organizations/**). Port: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts:69-76 (iamTypes array).

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/policies/constraints/iam_deny_public.yaml is a GCPIAMAllowedBindingsConstraintV3 (severity high) with target organizations/**, role roles/*, denying allUsers/allAuthenticatedUsers on every IAM binding of every asset type — matches the claim's description exactly.

On the port side, the 'no-public-access' policy lives only in /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts (lines 69-76); the Go port's policy-library/README.md explicitly states the Go port has no separate policy pack and delegates to this same shared TS pack, so 'both ports' really means one shared implementation. Read in full, the policy gates on `args.type` against exactly the 6 hardcoded strings the reviewer cited (projects/organizations IAMMember+IAMBinding, folder IAMMember, storage BucketIAMMember) — there is no generic fallback that scans arbitrary resource types for allUsers/allAuthenticatedUsers regardless of `args.type`. I grepped the entire ts port, go port, and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) repos for 'allUsers', 'allAuthenticatedUsers', 'iam_deny_public', and 'GCPIAMAllowedBindingsConstraintV3' — the only hits are the index.ts implementation itself and its own README/mapping-table mentions; no equivalent check exists anywhere else (no rego port, no separate test-suite scanner, no OPA-style validator).

Critically, this is not just a theoretical scope gap: the foundation codebase itself (shared across both ports via pulumi-library) actually instantiates several of the uncovered resource types, so the hole is exploitable in the real code, not hypothetical: gcp:folder/iAMBinding (pulumi-library go/pkg/iam/folder.go, ts/packages/parent-iam, 0-bootstrap/modules/parent-iam-remove-role), gcp:serviceaccount IAMMember/IAMBinding (0-bootstrap/sa.go, pulumi-library go/pkg/iam/service_account.go), gcp:kms/cryptoKeyIAMMember (4-projects/cmek.go), gcp:artifactregistry/repositoryIamMember (4-projects/example_infra_pipelines.go, pulumi-library go/pkg/cicd/cloudbuild.go), and gcp:pubsub/topicIAMMember (pulumi-library go/pkg/logging/centralized_logging.go, used by both ports' Stage 1 log-sink wiring). None of these are in the 6-item allowlist, so a mistaken allUsers/allAuthenticatedUsers grant on any of them would pass the policy pack silently, exactly as claimed. BigQuery dataset/table IAM, Secret Manager IAM, and Cloud Run/Cloud Functions invoker IAM were not found instantiated anywhere in the current codebase, so those specific sub-claims are more speculative/forward-looking, but the core claim (hardcoded 6-type allowlist vs TF's all-asset-type organizations/** scope) is fully verified and the severity (major) is justified given concrete, currently-provisioned resource types are already unprotected by the policy.

Port side: exhaustive search found no equivalent anywhere.

Notably even upstream Terraform itself (1-org/envs/shared/org_policy.tf) does not set gcp.restrictServiceUsage either — this control is TF's CI-time policy-library/OPA check, not an org policy — so the README's parity claim is a fabricated/incorrect mapping, not just an alternate valid implementation.

The control is genuinely absent from both ports (not implemented under a different name), and the README actively misrepresents this as covered. #### [BOTH] World-open firewall rule constraints (SSH/RDP/any) missing
_Area: policy-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF enforces three GCPRestrictedFirewallRulesConstraintV1 constraints (restrict_fw_rules_ssh_world_open, restrict_fw_rules_rdp_world_open, restrict_fw_rules_world_open, all severity high) blocking ingress rules from 0.0.0.0/0. The TS policy pack has no policy inspecting gcp:compute/firewall:Firewall resources. This matters because the foundation's own 3-networks stage and app workloads create firewall rules; the upstream gate that prevents accidentally world-open SSH/RDP is absent in both ports.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/policies/constraints/restrict_fw_rules_{ssh_world_open,rdp_world_open,world_open}.yaml. Port: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts contains no firewall resource check (the only 'firewall' mention is in a violation message string); Go port and pulumi-library have none.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/policies/constraints/restrict_fw_rules_{ssh_world_open,rdp_world_open,world_open}.yaml all exist, are GCPRestrictedFirewallRulesConstraintV1, severity high, and target INGRESS from 0.0.0.0/0 on port 22/3389/any respectively (verified the underlying gcp_restricted_firewall_rules_v1.yaml constraint template rego logic, which matches asset_type == "compute.googleapis.com/Firewall").

Port side: read the full TS policy-library/index.ts (the only file with actual policy code) — it defines 7 CrossGuard policies (no-default-network, project-labels-required, no-sa-key-creation, no-public-access, no-public-ip, require-private-google-access, require-flow-logs). None inspect gcp:compute/firewall:Firewall, and none inspect gcp:compute/networkFirewallPolicyRule:NetworkFirewallPolicyRule (or the regional variant) either. The Go port's policy-library/ directory contains only a README stating it delegates entirely to the TS pack, so it inherits the same gap. Searched pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) for any component-level validation guarding against 0.0.0.0/0 ingress on SSH/RDP/any — found none (no throw/panic/validate logic in firewall.go or ts network/src/index.ts, which does contain a legacy gcp.compute.Firewall-creating FirewallRules component used by cb-private-pool, but with no guardrail on its inputs). Also confirmed no separate OPA-equivalent check exists anywhere for the modern NetworkFirewallPolicyRule resource type that the ports (and upstream TF itself, via the network-firewall-policy module) actually use for 3-networks/4-projects/peering.

So the claim holds: there is genuinely no policy-as-code gate in either port that would block a future world-open SSH/RDP/any ingress rule, whether expressed via legacy gcp.compute.Firewall or via the modern NetworkFirewallPolicyRule that the foundation's own stages actually create. This is a real coverage gap: nothing stops a later app team (e.g., in 5-app-infra) from introducing an open ingress rule undetected by CrossGuard.

**Verifier note / correction:** One nuance worth noting for calibrating severity: the upstream TF constraint itself only inspects the legacy compute.googleapis.com/Firewall asset type, and terraform-example-foundation has already migrated 3-networks/4-projects to the modern network-firewall-policy module (creating NetworkFirewallPolicyRule assets instead), so this constraint is largely vestigial against the reference repo's own current resources too — it isn't actively firing upstream either. Additionally, none of the currently-shipped Pulumi port code paths create a world-open ingress rule today (all ingress in 3-networks/4-projects is IAP-range-gated with hierarchical deny-all-by-default, mirroring upstream's security posture), so this is better characterized as a missing future-proofing/CI guardrail against configuration drift rather than an active live vulnerability in the ported infrastructure as shipped. "Major" severity is still defensible given it's a broad, org-wide, high-severity preventive control with literally no equivalent (for either legacy Firewall or modern NetworkFirewallPolicyRule) anywhere in the port, but the description should frame it as guarding against future drift rather than implying today's deployed infrastructure is currently exposed.

#### [TS] PSC forwarding rule hardcodes target "all-apis" instead of TF's "vpc-sc" restricted VIP
_Area: pulumi-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF configures private_service_connect with forwarding_rule_target = "vpc-sc" so traffic is steered to the restricted VIP that honours VPC Service Controls; the Go library exposes ForwardingRuleTarget and the Go stages pass "vpc-sc". The TS PrivateServiceConnect has no target argument and hardcodes target: "all-apis", weakening the VPC-SC data-exfiltration control on every network. Relatedly, the TS restricted-API egress allow rule uses pscAddress/32 where TF allows the restricted_googleapis_cidr.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc/private_service_connect.tf (forwarding_rule_target = "vpc-sc") and firewall.tf:56 (dest_ip_ranges = [local.restricted_googleapis_cidr]). Go: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/psc.go ForwardingRuleTarget; hub-and-spoke main.go passes "vpc-sc". TS: /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/private-service-connect/src/index.ts:37 (target: "all-apis", no arg), shared_vpc.ts:293 (pscAddress + "/32").

**Verifier reasoning:** Fully confirmed on all points. TF side: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc/private_service_connect.tf:26 and the hub-and-spoke equivalent both set forwarding_rule_target = "vpc-sc" for the shared_vpc PSC endpoint, steering traffic to the restricted VIP that honors VPC Service Controls. Go port: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/psc.go exposes a real ForwardingRuleTarget field (also used to pick restricted.googleapis.com vs private.googleapis.com for DNS), and both pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go:147 and 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go:124/322 pass "vpc-sc". TS port: pulumi-library/ts/packages/private-service-connect/src/index.ts has no target/forwardingRuleTarget field in PrivateServiceConnectArgs at all and hardcodes target: "all-apis" on the GlobalForwardingRule (line 37). A repo-wide grep across pulumi_ts-example-foundation and pulumi-library/ts for "forwardingRuleTarget", "ForwardingRuleTarget", and "vpc-sc" (as a PSC target string) returns zero hits, and "all-apis" appears exactly once, confirming there is no override path anywhere in the TS port or library — both 3-networks-svpc and 3-networks-hub-and-spoke TS stages call this same component with no target argument. This means the TS foundation routes private/PSC-based Google API traffic to the unrestricted VIP, which does not enforce the VPC Service Controls perimeter — a real weakening of the data-exfiltration control, consistent with the reference behavior specifically being "vpc-sc" for exactly this security-sensitive path. The secondary detail (firewall dest_ip_ranges using pscAddress+"/32" vs TF's restricted_googleapis_cidr local) is actually a non-issue: in TF, local.restricted_googleapis_cidr is literally assigned from module.private_service_connect.private_service_connect_ip (the same single PSC IP, per 3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc/main.tf:20), not a separate broader CIDR block, so TS's pscAddress+"/32" is functionally equivalent there — but this doesn't diminish the primary finding. If anything, the TS PrivateServiceConnect component is even less complete than described: unlike the Go version it creates no CNAME/A DNS records at all pointing *.googleapis.com to the restricted/private VIP (only empty DNS zones are created in shared_vpc.ts), meaning the DNS side of PSC is non-functional in TS regardless of the target bug. Severity of "major" for the specific hardcoded-target claim is justified, not inflated, given this is a core VPC-SC/data-exfiltration control central to the foundation's security model.

**Verifier note / correction:** Minor refinement only: the claim's framing that "TF allows the restricted_googleapis_cidr" as if it were a broader/different range than the PSC address is misleading — in this TF module, restricted_googleapis_cidr IS just the single private_service_connect_ip (same value as pscAddress in TS), so that part of the evidence is not a distinct, additional gap; it's functionally equivalent to the TS pscAddress/32 usage. The core finding (hardcoded "all-apis" vs configurable "vpc-sc") stands fully and is the real, major-severity gap.

#### [BOTH] CAI monitoring CMEK only applied to Artifact Registry — source bucket and Pub/Sub topic encryption omitted; labels accepted but never applied; function_uri output missing
_Area: pulumi-library/cai-monitoring · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF cai-monitoring applies var.encryption_key to the Artifact Registry repo, the cloud-function source bucket (simple_bucket encryption) and the Pub/Sub topic (topic_kms_key_name), and passes var.labels to the AR repo and Cloud Function; it also exports function_uri. Both library components pass encryptionKey only to the Artifact Registry; the bucket and topic are created without CMEK, so enabling CMEK silently leaves two of three data stores Google-managed-key encrypted. Both accept a labels arg that is never referenced, and neither exposes the function URI output.

**Evidence:** TF: 1-org/modules/cai-monitoring/main.tf:72-89 (bucket encryption), module pubsub_cai_feed (topic_kms_key_name), labels on AR/cloud_function, outputs.tf (function_uri). Ports: pulumi-library/go/pkg/security/cai_monitoring.go:235-237 (AR only; Labels field unused, bucket at 249-257 and topic at 275-281 without CMEK); pulumi-library/ts/packages/cai-monitoring/src/index.ts:148,155-176 (same).

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed exactly as described: 1-org/modules/cai-monitoring/main.tf sets kms_key_name = var.encryption_key on the AR repo (line ~78), encryption = enable_cmek ? {default_kms_key_name = var.encryption_key} : null on the cloudfunction_source_bucket module (lines 72-89), and topic_kms_key_name = var.encryption_key on module.pubsub_cai_feed (line ~128). labels = var.labels is passed to the AR repo and to module.cloud_function. outputs.tf exports function_uri = module.cloud_function.function_uri.

Both ports were searched exhaustively (grep for KmsKeyName/kmsKeyName, encryption, Labels/labels, Url/Uri, and the resource constructors themselves) and no equivalent exists anywhere else in either repo or in pulumi-library. Confirmed line-by-line:
- Go (pulumi-library/go/pkg/security/cai_monitoring.go): KmsKeyName set only on the AR repo (lines 235-236); storage.NewBucket (line 249) has no KmsKeyName/encryption field at all; pubsub.NewTopic (line 275) has no KmsKeyName field. The `Labels map[string]string` field (line 88) is declared but never read anywhere in the constructor — dead field. No FunctionURI/Url field exists on the CAIMonitoring struct or in RegisterResourceOutputs.
- TS (pulumi-library/ts/packages/cai-monitoring/src/index.ts): kmsKeyName set only on the AR repo (line 148); the Bucket (line 155) and Topic (line 173) constructors have no kmsKeyName. `labels?: Record<string,string>` (line 66) is declared but never referenced. No functionUri output.
- The library's own Go test file only asserts CMEK on the artifactregistry.Repository (TestNewCAIMonitoring_CMEKEncryptionKey/NoCMEKByDefault) — there is no test covering bucket/topic encryption, corroborating this was an unintentional oversight rather than a deliberate scoping decision.
- Neither port's stage-1-org caller (pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/cai_monitoring.go, pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/modules/cai-monitoring/index.ts) even passes EncryptionKey or Labels today, so the gap is currently latent/unreachable in the shipped config — but the Go docstring for EncryptionKey explicitly promises "CMEK encryption of Artifact Registry, Cloud Storage, and Pub/Sub," so the component's own documented contract is broken, not just an edge case.

The claim is accurate as stated and the search found no alternative implementation anywhere.

**Verifier note / correction:** Gap confirmed exactly as described technically, but "major" severity is somewhat inflated for two of the four sub-claims. Checking the reference TF's actual call site (1-org/envs/shared/cai_monitoring.tf) shows encryption_key/enable_cmek/labels are never overridden from their defaults (null/false/{}) in the real foundation deployment — so out-of-the-box, no TF deployment of this module is actually CMEK-encrypted or labeled either, and the gap doesn't cause a regression relative to what upstream currently deploys. Additionally, the module-level function_uri output is not even propagated up to the 1-org env's outputs.tf in the reference implementation (only artifact_registry_name/asset_feed_name/bucket_name/topic_name are re-exported there), so "function_uri output missing" has essentially no downstream consumer in the reference design — it's an informational parity gap, not a functional one. The core AR/bucket/topic CMEK-coverage gap is legitimate and worth fixing (it silently breaks the component's own documented CMEK guarantee for any consumer that does set EncryptionKey, e.g. under an org policy requiring CMEK), so I'd keep that portion at moderate-to-major, but the labels-unused and function_uri-missing portions are minor/cosmetic given nothing currently consumes them.

#### [TS] CbPrivatePool accepts vpnConfiguration but silently ignores it; pool worker config and existing-network reuse hardcoded
_Area: pulumi-library/cb-private-pool · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF cb-private-pool creates an HA VPN to on-prem (PSK read from Secret Manager, TWO_IPS_REDUNDANCY external gateway, two BGP tunnels, CUSTOM router advertisement of the peered pool range) when vpn_configuration.enable_vpn=true. The TS component declares vpnConfiguration?: VpnConfig in its args but never references it in the constructor — the option is silently dropped. It also hardcodes diskSizeGb:100, machineType:"e2-standard-4", noExternalIp:true and the pool name (cb-pool-<region>), and cannot attach to an existing peered_network_id, all of which are variables in TF (private_worker_pool.disk_size_gb/machine_type/no_external_ip/name/peered_network_id).

Port: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool/src/index.ts:35 (arg declared), constructor lines 45-161 (vpnConfiguration never used; hardcoded workerConfig at 145-149).

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified against the actual source. TF side: 0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/vpn_ha.tf exactly matches the description (HA VPN via terraform-google-modules/vpn/google//modules/vpn_ha, PSK from Secret Manager, TWO_IPS_REDUNDANCY, two BGP tunnels remote-0/remote-1, CUSTOM router_advertise_config advertising the peered pool range), and main.tf:30-52 wires disk_size_gb/machine_type/no_external_ip/name/peered_network_id from variables.tf as claimed.

TS port side: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool/src/index.ts's VpnConfig interface (line 20) only declares enableVpn?: boolean and args.vpnConfiguration is never referenced anywhere in the constructor (grepped the full 162-line file, zero hits in the body). workerConfig at lines 145-149 hardcodes diskSizeGb:100, machineType:"e2-standard-4" (which doesn't even match TF's own default of "e2-medium"), noExternalIp:true, and the pool name is hardcoded to `cb-pool-${region}` at line 142. PrivateWorkerPoolConfig has no peeredNetworkId field, so there's no way to attach to an existing peered network. The only two call sites in the ts port (build_github_actions.ts:139, build_cb.ts.example:113) both hardcode vpnConfiguration:{enableVpn:false}, so the arg is dead code everywhere it's used. I also found a fully-built, unrelated VpnHa component in pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpn-ha (mirrors the same upstream TF module with HaVpnGateway/ExternalVpnGateway/tunnels/RouterPeer) but confirmed via grep it is never imported by CbPrivatePool or any stage code — it's an orphaned component, not an alternate implementation of this functionality, so it doesn't refute the claim.

All cited evidence lines check out precisely. Severity "major" is justified, not inflated: this is a declared, documented option that silently no-ops rather than erroring, which could mislead a user into believing hybrid on-prem connectivity is configured when it isn't, and there is no compensating path anywhere in the ts port or shared library.
