TS counterpart: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool/src/index.ts:141-154.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified end-to-end. TF side: 0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/{main.tf,network.tf,vpn_ha.tf} exists exactly as described — google_cloudbuild_worker_pool with dynamic peered network_config, a peered VPC (network.tf: google_compute_global_address VPC_PEERING range, google_service_networking_connection, DNS policy, firewall rules), and optional HA VPN (vpn_ha.tf). build_cb.tf instantiates it unconditionally as part of the default Cloud Build CI/CD path.

Go side: exhaustive search of pulumi-library/go (all pkg/* dirs, including pkg/cicd/cloudbuild.go which is the CB component) and all of pulumi_go-example-foundation turns up zero uses of gcp/cloudbuild.WorkerPool, zero peered-network/DNS-policy/firewall/VPN code for Cloud Build, and zero component named anything like cb-private-pool/PrivatePool/WorkerPool that creates the resource. pkg/cicd.NewCloudBuild only creates Artifact Registry + CSR/GitHub/GitLab triggers — no worker pool at all. The Go port's 0-bootstrap/build_cloud_build.go.example (gated behind `//go:build example`) is a stub: its doc comment says to set a `cloud_build_private_pool_subnet` config value, but the function body never creates a pool, network, or peering — it just calls NewCloudBuild for triggers/AR. 4-projects/example_infra_pipelines.go (also example-gated) declares PrivateWorkerPoolID as a plain string field with no corresponding creation logic anywhere. 1-org/policies.go:146-150 (this file is NOT example-gated, it's live) wires AllowedWorkerPoolID straight into the cloudbuild.allowedWorkerPools org policy constraint, but that ID can only come from an externally/manually supplied config value in the Go stack — nothing in Go can produce it via IaC.

TS side confirms the asymmetry: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool/src/index.ts is a real, complete ComponentResource — peered Network, gcp.dns.Policy, GlobalAddress + servicenetworking.Connection, NetworkPeeringRoutesConfig, FirewallRules, and gcp.cloudbuild.WorkerPool — and it is actually wired up and instantiated in pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_cb.ts.example. So TS has a working (if example-gated) mirror of build_cb.tf's private pool; Go has nothing but a comment referencing a config key that goes nowhere.

One nuance for severity calibration: this gap only affects the *optional* Cloud Build CI/CD path. Both the Go and TS ports default to GitHub Actions WIF via an explicitly documented 'approved deviation' (build_github_actions.go/.ts is the real, non-example default), where no private worker pool is needed at all. Also, even on the TS side, wiring AllowedWorkerPoolID into the 1-org org policy still requires a manually-set config value rather than automatic StackReference plumbing — so that specific sub-claim ('references a pool the Go stack can never create') is true of Go but is not really a Go-vs-TS differentiator, since TS's default flow has the identical manual-config requirement. The real, clean differentiator is that TS ships a working component + working example wiring; Go ships neither.

**Verifier note / correction:** Core claim holds and severity of 'major' is reasonable but should be scoped: the missing component/library gap is real and complete (confirmed absent from pulumi-library/go and pulumi_go-example-foundation entirely, including the .example file), but it only matters for the optional/alternate Cloud Build CI/CD path — both Go and TS ports default to GitHub Actions WIF, where no private worker pool is required at all, so the default supported deployment path in Go is unaffected. Also note the '1-org references a pool the Go stack can never create' framing applies equally to TS's default path (TS also requires a manually-supplied cloud_build_private_worker_pool_id config value for that org policy) — the real Go-specific gap is that Go has no component and no working example to create the pool at all, whereas TS at least has both, gated behind the same optional Cloud Build path.

#### [TS] CentralizedLogging: folder resource_type unsupported, log-bucket Log Analytics + linked BigQuery dataset missing, and no billing sink for the log-bucket destination
_Area: pulumi-library/centralized-logging · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF centralized-logging takes resources+resource_type (1-org passes "folder" when parent_folder is set) and its logbucket destination supports enable_analytics and linked_dataset_id/description — 1-org passes linked_dataset_id="ds_c_prj_aggregated_logs_analytics". It also creates billing-account sinks for all three destinations. TS CentralizedLogging only creates gcp.logging.OrganizationSink (no folder mode), its loggingBucketOptions has no enableAnalytics/linkedDataset (no gcp.logging.LinkedDataset is ever created — the 'linkedDatasetName' output is actually the unrelated bigqueryOptions dataset), and billing sinks exist only for storage and pubsub, not the log bucket. Go supports folder/billing_account resource types, LinkedDataset, EnableAnalytics, and all three billing sinks.

**Evidence:** TF: 1-org/envs/shared/log_sinks.tf (project_options with linked_dataset_id; resource_type folder|organization); 1-org/modules/centralized-logging/main.tf (log_export_billing for_each over all destinations). Port: pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts:47-100 (orgId-only, ProjectBucketConfig without analytics/linked dataset, billing sinks only at 167-181 and 216-231). Go counterpart: go/pkg/logging/centralized_logging.go:239-251,505-545.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF (1-org/modules/centralized-logging/variables.tf + main.tf, 1-org/envs/shared/log_sinks.tf) genuinely supports resource_type folder|organization (used when parent_folder is set), project_options.enable_analytics/linked_dataset_id/linked_dataset_description (wired to the logbucket submodule, and 1-org passes linked_dataset_id="ds_c_prj_aggregated_logs_analytics" unconditionally), and module.log_export_billing for_each's over all three destinations (pub/sto/prj) when enable_billing_account_sink=true (always true in 1-org).

The TS library (pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts) confirms every part of the claim: CentralizedLoggingArgs has only orgId (no resourceType/folder support), every sink is hardcoded to gcp.logging.OrganizationSink, LoggingBucketOptions has no enableAnalytics/linkedDatasetId fields, no gcp.logging.LinkedDataset resource is ever created, and the exposed `linkedDatasetName` output is indeed sourced from the unrelated bigqueryOptions dataset (line 131/240), not a real bucket-linked dataset. Billing sinks exist only for storage (line 170) and pubsub (line 219) — there is no billing-sink branch for the log-bucket/project destination.

I checked pulumi-library ts/ broadly for an alternate implementation: there IS a generic `log-export` package (ts/packages/log-export/src/index.ts) whose LogExport component already supports folder and billing_account resourceTypes via FolderSink/BillingAccountSink — but centralized-logging never imports or uses it, so this isn't "implemented differently," it's simply absent from this component.

I also found the gap is worse in practice than the ticket states: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts computes the correct parentType/parentId/billingAccount in config.ts (mirroring TF's parent_folder logic and used elsewhere for IAM) but never passes billingAccount (or any folder/resourceType info) into its CentralizedLogging call at all — so today, zero billing sinks are actually created in the TS port's real deployment, not just the log-bucket one.

The Go port (pulumi-library/go/pkg/logging/centralized_logging.go, used by pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/logging.go) genuinely has all of this: ResourceType threaded to folder/org sinks, a real logging.NewLinkedDataset with EnableAnalytics, and a billing-sink block for the project log bucket in addition to storage/pubsub, wired with the correct real values in logging.go.

Severity is not inflated: the missing Log Analytics/linked-dataset and billing-sink behavior affects TF's default/unconditional configuration path, and the missing folder resource_type affects the documented parent_folder deployment mode — this is real, major functional divergence, not an unused informational output.

#### [TS] DnsHub accepts targetNameServerAddresses but never creates the on-prem forwarding zone; zones created without records
_Area: pulumi-library/dns-hub · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The TF foundation's hub DNS (3-networks shared_vpc/dns.tf) creates fz-dns-hub, a forwarding zone sending the corporate domain to var.target_name_server_addresses, plus a peering zone for non-prod envs. The TS DnsHub component declares targetNameServerAddresses in its args but the constructor never references it — no forwarding zone (or peering zone) is created, so on-prem DNS resolution is silently absent for consumers of this component (used by ts 1-org/modules/network). Its three private zones (googleapis.com/gcr.io/pkg.dev) are also created with no record sets. Go has no DnsHub component but the Go port builds forwarding/peering zones via networking.NewDnsZone.

**Evidence:** TF: 3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc/dns.tf (module dns_forwarding_zone with target_name_server_addresses; module peering_zone). Port: pulumi-library/ts/packages/dns-hub/src/index.ts:14 (arg declared), 17-75 (never used; zones without records); used by pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/modules/network/index.ts. Go counterpart: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go:185-207.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

TF side: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc/dns.tf and the hub-and-spoke equivalent both exist exactly as described — a `dns_forwarding_zone` module (name `fz-dns-hub`, type forwarding, `target_name_server_addresses = var.target_name_server_addresses`) created only for prod, and a `peering_zone` module (`dz-<env>-svpc-to-dns-hub`, type peering, targeting the hub/prod network) created for non-prod/spoke environments.

TS port side: pulumi-library/ts/packages/dns-hub/src/index.ts declares `targetNameServerAddresses` in `DnsHubArgs` (line 14) but the constructor body (lines 17-76) never references `args.targetNameServerAddresses` — it only creates a `gcp.dns.Policy` and three private `ManagedZone`s (googleapis.com, gcr.io, pkg.dev) with no forwarding/peering zone and no record sets. Worse than the claim states in one respect: this `DnsHub` class is not even instantiated anywhere in the port — `1-org/modules/network/index.ts` only re-exports it, and no file calls `new DnsHub(...)`, so it is dead code.

More importantly, the actually-used implementation path — `3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc.ts` and `3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/shared_vpc.ts` (used by every env's index.ts) — independently confirms the gap: both read `target_name_server_addresses` from Pulumi config in envs/production, envs/nonproduction, envs/development (and shared/ for hub-and-spoke) purely to pass it through as a stack **output**, but never construct a `gcp.dns.ManagedZone` with `forwardingConfig` or `peeringConfig`. No resource named `fz-dns-hub` or `dz-*-svpc-to-dns-hub` exists anywhere in the TS port. The private googleapis/gcr/pkg-dev zones created in shared_vpc.ts also have no RecordSets, matching the claim.

Notably, pulumi-library/ts DOES contain a fully capable equivalent to Go's `networking.NewDnsZone` — `pulumi-library/ts/packages/cloud-dns/src/index.ts` (`CloudDns` component), which correctly implements forwarding (`targetNameServerAddresses`/`forwardingPath`), peering (`targetNetworkSelfLink`), and `recordsets`. It's even listed as a dependency in the TS port's package.json (`@vitruviansoftware/foundation-cloud-dns`), but is never imported/used by any `.ts` file in the port — so a ready-made fix exists in the library but was never wired in.

Go side confirmed as described: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go lines 183-207 build `dns-forwarding` (type forwarding, `TargetNameServerAddresses: cfg.TargetNameServers`, name `fz-dns-hub`) for env "p" and `dns-peering` (type peering, name `dz-<env>-svpc-to-dns-hub`) otherwise, via `networking.NewDnsZone` from pulumi-library/go. No `DnsHub` component exists in Go at all.

Net: the claim is accurate and, if anything, understates the gap slightly (the DnsHub component being wired into 1-org/modules/network is misleading — it's re-exported but never instantiated; the real gap is that the whole port, via shared_vpc.ts in both topologies, never creates the forwarding/peering zone despite plumbing the config value through as an output). Severity of "major" is justified, not inflated — this isn't an inert informational output; it's a genuinely missing resource (on-prem DNS forwarding/peering) that TF creates unconditionally, silently dropped in the TS port across every environment.

#### [TS] Org-policy components are organization-only; folder-scoped policies (parent_folder deployments) cannot be expressed
_Area: pulumi-library/org-policy · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF org-policy module takes policy_for/organization_id/folder_id, and 1-org computes policy_for="folder" whenever parent_folder is set (org_policy.tf:18-20), applying all constraints at the folder. TS OrgPolicyBoolean/OrgPolicyList/DomainRestrictedSharing hardcode name/parent to organizations/<orgId>, so a folder-parented foundation either fails (missing org-level orgpolicy permission) or silently applies constraints org-wide. The TS port models parentFolder in 1-org/config.ts and branches on it for IAM, but its org policies always target the organization. Go's policy.OrgPolicy takes ParentID (organizations/... or folders/...) and supports both.

**Evidence:** TF: 1-org/envs/shared/org_policy.tf:18-20,52-54 (policy_for/folder_id). Port: pulumi-library/ts/packages/org-policy/src/index.ts:23-31,67-73,90-100 (hardcoded organizations/ parent); pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:82-118 uses these with orgId only despite config.parentFolder existing (1-org/config.ts:26). Go counterpart: pulumi-library/go/pkg/policy/org_policy.go:31-67.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all counts.

TF side: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/org_policy.tf:18-20,52-54 does exactly as described — `policy_for`/`organization_id`/`folder_id` are all derived from `local.parent_folder`, and every `terraform-google-modules/org-policy/google` module invocation (boolean policies loop, list policies, domain-restricted-sharing, etc.) is parameterized this way, so a folder-parented deployment applies all these constraints at the folder rather than the org.

TS port side: pulumi-library/ts/packages/org-policy/src/index.ts hardcodes `parent: \`organizations/${args.orgId}\`` in all three exported components (OrgPolicyBoolean line 25, OrgPolicyList line 69, DomainRestrictedSharing line 92) — there is no folder-parent parameter or overload anywhere in the package (verified the whole package directory, only index.ts + test file exist, no alternate constructors).

pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts calls these three components with `orgId: cfg.orgId` only (lines 82-124, 363), even though 1-org/config.ts (lines 71-76) computes `cfg.parent`, `cfg.parentId`, `cfg.parentType`, and `cfg.parentFolder` precisely for this purpose, and other files in the same stage (iam.ts:38/130 branching on `cfg.parentFolder`, essential_contacts.ts:30 using `cfg.parent`, main index.ts:510-511 exporting `parent_resource_id`/`parent_resource_type`) all correctly honor folder-scoped deployment. This makes org-policy a genuine, isolated inconsistency in the TS port, not a systemic design choice.

Go port/library, by contrast, does implement this correctly: pulumi-library/go/pkg/policy/org_policy.go takes a generic `ParentID` (any `organizations/...` or `folders/...` path) and pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/policies.go:36-38 computes `parentID := "organizations/" + cfg.OrgID; if cfg.ParentFolder != "" { parentID = "folders/" + cfg.ParentFolder }` before calling the shared component — exactly mirroring the TF logic and exactly what the claim says.

Severity: I'd call "major" justified, not inflated. This isn't a cosmetic/output-only gap — it changes where real security-guardrail org-policy constraints (14+ boolean constraints, VM external IP, protocol forwarding, essential-contacts domain restriction, worker-pool restriction, domain-restricted-sharing) actually get attached in GCP. For a folder-scoped foundation deployment (a supported, explicitly modeled mode in the TS port's own config), this either fails outright (deploying principal typically only holds folder-level orgpolicy.policyAdmin, not org-level) or succeeds but silently broadens the blast radius of every listed constraint to the entire organization instead of the intended folder — a meaningful security-scoping correctness bug, not a nice-to-have.

#### [BOTH] Shared-VPC subnet IAM (shared_vpc_subnets → roles/compute.networkUser) and VPC-SC dry-run attach not expressible; TS also never attaches the sample-svpc project to the perimeter
_Area: pulumi-library/project-factory · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF single_project passes svpc_host_project_id + shared_vpc_subnets to project-factory (which grants networkUser on the given host subnets) and additionally grants roles/compute.networkUser on those subnets to app-infra pipeline SAs (google_compute_subnetwork_iam_member), plus vpc_service_control_attach_enabled/attach_dry_run/perimeter_name/sleep_duration (dry-run attach uses google_access_context_manager_service_perimeter_dry_run_resource). Neither library project component supports any of these. Compensation is partial: both ports create the SharedVPCServiceProject attachment inline, and Go attaches the svpc project to the perimeter (enforced mode only); but no SubnetworkIAMMember/networkUser grant exists in either port, dry-run perimeter attach exists in neither, and the TS port attaches only the confidential-space project — its sample-svpc project is never added to the perimeter.

**Evidence:** TF: 4-projects/modules/single_project/main.tf:60-67 (svpc_host_project_id, shared_vpc_subnets, vpc_service_control_*), :100-110 (subnetwork networkUser), 4-projects/modules/base_env/example_shared_vpc_project.tf:43-46. Checked: grep SubnetworkIAMMember over both ports = no hits; pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit.go:100-111 (svpc attach + enforced ServicePerimeterResource, no dry-run); pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/modules/single_project.ts:78-83 (svpc attach only) and business_unit_1/production/index.ts:323 (only confidential-space perimeter attach).

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified end-to-end against source, not just the cited line numbers. TF side confirmed exactly as described: 4-projects/modules/single_project/main.tf:60-66 passes svpc_host_project_id, shared_vpc_subnets, and vpc_service_control_attach_enabled/attach_dry_run/perimeter_name/sleep_duration into the terraform-google-modules/project-factory module, and main.tf:101-110 defines google_compute_subnetwork_iam_member (roles/compute.networkUser) grants to app-infra pipeline SAs on the shared_vpc_subnets, gated by enable_cloudbuild_deploy. base_env/example_shared_vpc_project.tf (lines 42-46) wires attach_enabled=enforce_vpcsc / attach_dry_run=!enforce_vpcsc.

On the port side: read pulumi-library/go/pkg/project/project.go (full file) and pulumi-library/ts/packages/project-factory/src/index.ts (full file) — neither has any svpc_host_project_id, shared_vpc_subnets, or vpc_service_control_* fields/logic. Grepped both ports and the whole pulumi-library for SubnetworkIAMMember/SubnetworkIamMember/networkUser/compute.networkUser — zero real hits (the one grep hit was a compiled Go binary, a strings false-positive, not source). Confirmed pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit.go:100-119 attaches the SVPC sample project to the perimeter only when cfg.EnforceVpcSc is true — no dry-run counterpart exists. Confirmed pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/modules/single_project.ts declares a `sharedVpcSubnets?: string[]` field in SingleProjectArgs that is never referenced in the function body (dead parameter), and that business_unit_1/production, /nonproduction, and /development index.ts (all three env stacks) call gcp.accesscontextmanager.ServicePerimeterResource only for the confidential-space project — the sample-svpc project is never attached to the perimeter in TS, in either enforced or dry-run mode, anywhere. The library's separate vpc_sc/vpc-service-controls components do have a 'dry run' concept (UseExplicitDryRunSpec/membersDryRun/restrictedServicesDryRun), but that's a whole-perimeter spec-level dry run configured once at the network stage — not the per-resource google_access_context_manager_service_perimeter_dry_run_resource attach-a-single-project mechanism the claim is about, so it does not compensate.

**Verifier note / correction:** The claim is accurate but bundles two sub-gaps of very different real-world weight, so 'major' is only partly earned. (1) The shared_vpc_subnets/networkUser subnet grant is real but the TF code path that produces it (pipeline_roles/network_user_role locals) is itself gated by var.enable_cloudbuild_deploy, and both ports have explicitly deprioritized that whole Cloud-Build-pipeline mechanism in favor of GitHub Actions WIF (see pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/example_infra_pipelines.go, gated behind a `//go:build example` tag with the comment 'Per approved deviation, Go/TS foundations default to GitHub Actions WIF. Cloud Build pipelines are provided as an .example reference.'). Under the ports' default/supported configuration this sub-gap has no observable effect, so treating it as major overstates it. (2) The VPC-SC dry-run/attach gap is not similarly excused — it is on the always-active path (svpc/confidential-space project creation happens regardless of the pipeline choice), and here severity is warranted: Go silently drops perimeter protection whenever dry-run mode is used (a common bootstrap phase before flipping to enforced), and TS never attaches the primary Shared-VPC sample project to the perimeter at all, an omission inconsistent with its own Go sibling. I'd keep 'major' for the VPC-SC attach/dry-run half of the claim but flag the networkUser/shared_vpc_subnets half as minor-to-moot given the documented pipeline deviation.

#### [TS] Hub-and-spoke transitivity gateway (MIG + ILB + routes + firewall rules) has no TS equivalent
_Area: pulumi-library/transitivity · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/transitivity deploys per-region gateway MIGs, internal LBs with all-ports forwarding + global access, next_hop_ilb routes for regional aggregates, a dedicated SA, and targeted network-firewall-policy rules (priorities 20000/20001). The Go library implements this (networking.TransitivityAppliance) and the Go port deploys it; the TS library has no transitivity component and the TS 3-networks-hub-and-spoke stage contains no MIG/ILB/route/transitivity code, so spoke-to-spoke transitive routing is absent from the TS port.

**Evidence:** TF: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/transitivity/main.tf. Go: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/transitivity.go; pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go:159-160. Checked: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules contains only shared_vpc.ts + interconnect/vpn examples; grep -ri 'transitivity' over ts 3-networks returns nothing; no ts/packages/transitivity.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified both halves of the claim.

TF side confirmed: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/transitivity/main.tf (181 lines) implements exactly what's described — a service account, per-region MIGs from an instance template, internal forwarding rules with allPorts=true and global access (ilbs), google_compute_route resources with next_hop_ilb, and google_compute_network_firewall_policy_rule resources at priority 20000 (ingress) and 20001 (egress). It's wired into envs/shared/net-hubs-transitivity.tf, gated by var.enable_hub_and_spoke_transitivity (default false).

Go side confirmed present: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/transitivity.go implements the full component (SA, per-region instance templates/MIGs, ILB forwarding rules with AllPorts, routes with NextHopIlb, firewall rules named fw-*-20000-i-a-...-transitivity / fw-*-20001-e-a-...-transitivity), and pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go:159-180 calls NewTransitivityAppliance unconditionally.

TS side: exhaustively searched pulumi_ts-example-foundation (all of 3-networks-hub-and-spoke, all modules including the .ts.example files for vpn/interconnect) and pulumi-library/ts (all 30 packages, including instance-template and compute-instance which are generic building blocks with no MIG/ILB/route/firewall wiring for transitivity). No RegionInstanceGroupManager, ForwardingRule with allPorts, Route with nextHopIlb, or NetworkFirewallPolicyRule at priority 20000/20001 exists anywhere in the TS codebase. The only traces of "transitivity" in the TS port are the config flag `enableHubAndSpokeTransitivity` declared in 1-org/config.ts and mentioned in the stage README — but nothing in 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/*/index.ts reads that flag or instantiates any equivalent resource. It's a dead config field with no implementation behind it, which is arguably worse than a plainly absent flag since it falsely implies the feature exists.

Severity: "major" is justified, not inflated. The gap is a complete, total absence of a whole component (not a stub, partial output, or unused variable) that both the reference TF and the sibling Go port fully implement with resource-level parity. One mitigating fact worth noting for context (not a correction to the claim): in upstream TF this feature is opt-in and defaults to disabled (enable_hub_and_spoke_transitivity = false), so it doesn't break the default deployment path — but that doesn't reduce the severity of the port gap itself, since any TS user who enables the flag (which the TS config layer clearly supports/exposes) gets silent no-op behavior instead of working spoke-to-spoke transitivity.

_Area: test-integration · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** terraform-example-foundation has 2,284 lines of per-stage integration tests (test/integration/{bootstrap,org,envs,shared,networks,projects-shared,projects,app-infra}) that verify deployed GCP state: org-level IAM roles for each of the 5 pipeline SAs, 10 boolean + 3 list org-policy constraints enforced on the parent, VPC-SC perimeter dry-run containing an access level and 116 restricted services, network firewall policy rules (deny-all-egress 65530 / allow-api-egress 1000 with direction, action, logging, dest ranges), 4 DNS zones + DNS policy inbound forwarding per env, 4 Cloud Routers per env with ASN 64514 and advertised ranges, 3 folder log sinks + 3 billing-account sinks each checked for 8 audit-log filters, essential contacts categories, SCC topic/subscription/notification wiring, CAI-monitoring function state, Cloud Build private worker pool config, CSR repos and plan/apply triggers, subnet CIDR ranges, shared-VPC host attachment, IAP firewall rules (22/3389), confidential-space instance config (SEV/MIGRATE/SA).
