**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF side: test/integration/{bootstrap,org,envs,shared,networks,projects-shared,projects,app-infra} total exactly 2,284 lines (verified via wc -l) of terratest/tft-based Go tests that deploy each stage and then query live GCP state via `gcloud` calls wrapped in testutils/gcloud helpers -- e.g. org_test.go:161-186 asserts 10 boolean org-policy constraints are `enforced` plus list-policy/behavior checks on the parent folder via `gcloud resource-manager org-policies describe`; bootstrap_test.go:281-345 asserts each of the 5 pipeline SAs holds specific org-level IAM roles via `organizations get-iam-policy`; networks_test.go enumerates a 116-entry restrictedServices list and checks the live VPC-SC perimeter/firewall config. This is genuine post-apply live-state verification, not static/unit testing.

Port side: both pulumi_go-example-foundation/test/validate_e2e.sh and pulumi_ts-example-foundation/test/validate_e2e.sh are byte-identical (diff shows zero differences), 258 lines, and contain exactly 14 assertion calls (9 assert_exists + 5 assert_count, matching the claim's '~14' figure precisely) checking only resource existence/counts (folder exists, project exists by name filter, KMS keyring exists, bucket exists, SA count >= 4, env-folder count >= 3, etc.). None of these inspect IAM bindings, org-policy enforcement state, VPC-SC perimeter contents, firewall rule properties, DNS zone/policy config, Cloud Router ASN/advertised-ranges, log-sink filters, SCC/CAI/essential-contacts wiring, or CB private-pool/trigger config against live GCP state. I searched both port repos and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) exhaustively for equivalent live verification under any name/keyword (org-policy, vpc-sc, restricted-service, firewall, dns, log-sink, essential-contacts, scc, cai, worker-pool, confidential): the only other test-like artifacts found are (a) config_test.go / *.config.test.ts files in each stage, which are static default-value unit tests with no GCP calls, and (b) pulumi-library's *_test.go / *.test.ts files, which use pulumi.WithMocks / vitest mocks to assert on synthesized resource *inputs* at preview time, never touching a real GCP API. The CI e2e.yml workflow's only 'Validate Resources' step invokes validate_e2e.sh and nothing else; policy-library in both ports is a CrossGuard preview-time (pre-apply) policy pack, structurally unable to verify post-deploy live state and explicitly scoped to different concerns (no-default-network, no-public-ip, etc.) unrelated to the dropped categories. So the claim is accurate: essentially the entire live-state security-control verification layer from the reference implementation is absent from both ports, replaced by a shallow existence-check script.

**Verifier note / correction:** One nuance in the evidence write-up is overstated and should be corrected: the ports' validate_e2e.sh does NOT contain explicit assertions of 'zero IAM bindings / zero org policies / zero VPC-SC / zero firewall / zero DNS / zero log sinks' -- there are no negative/zero assertions at all. The script simply never checks those categories (absence of a check, not an assertion of absence); phases with no deployed resources produce a 'skipped -- stage not deployed' warning rather than a false pass. This is a minor mischaracterization in the claim's description, not a change to the core verdict: the severity assessment of 'major' is fair given that this test suite is the only automated post-deploy verification the ports have, and it provides no coverage at all for the org-policy/IAM/VPC-SC/firewall/DNS/log-sink security-control categories that make up the bulk of the reference implementation's test investment.

#### [BOTH] Per-stage unit tests are largely tautological — they re-implement or locally define the logic they claim to test
_Area: test-unit · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Most port unit tests assert on values defined inside the test rather than exercising stage code, so regressions in the actual programs cannot be caught. Go: 0-bootstrap/config_test.go re-applies defaults manually ('Apply defaults the same way loadConfig does') instead of calling the real loadConfig in main.go; 2-environments, 3-networks-*, and 5-app-infra tests assert on locally declared maps/structs (e.g. envCodes literal) and struct instantiations (assert.NotNil(&SeedProject{})). TS: 2-environments, 3-networks, 4-projects, and 5-app-infra tests import no stage code at all — they define local projectLabels/budgetConfig/defaultAppConfig functions and assert on them; only 0-bootstrap.config.test.ts imports real code (loadConfig). Concrete consequence: 5-app-infra.config.test.ts:42 asserts machineType default 'n1-standard-1', but the actual TS stage code defaults to 'f1-micro' (and TF's integration test verifies f1-micro on the live instance) — the test passes while documenting the wrong default. Roughly: only ~3 of the 11 stage test files (TS 0-bootstrap loadConfig tests; Go 4-projects projectLabels/budgetConfig tests) touch product code.

**Evidence:** Go tautologies: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/config_test.go:27-67 vs loadConfig at 0-bootstrap/main.go:195-258; 2-environments/config_test.go:21-39; 3-networks-svpc/config_test.go:33-46; 5-app-infra/config_test.go:32-48. TS tautologies: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/test/4-projects.config.test.ts:17-34 (local projectLabels), test/5-app-infra.config.test.ts:30-44 ('n1-standard-1') vs 5-app-infra/business_unit_1/development/index.ts.example:18 ('f1-micro' default) and TF test /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/test/integration/app-infra/app_infra_test.go:77-81 (f1-micro).

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified directly against the source files. TF side: terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/variables.tf genuinely defines defaults (project_prefix=prj, default_region=us-central1, etc.), and test/integration/app-infra/app_infra_test.go:77-81 does assert the live instance's machineType is f1-micro, confirming the TF functionality being "tested" is real. Go port: 0-bootstrap/config_test.go (all of it, lines 27-173) never calls loadConfig; it re-implements the same if-empty-then-default logic inline on a bare &Config{} and even has TestSeedProjectStruct/TestCICDProjectStruct that only assert.NotNil(&SeedProject{}) with no call to deploySeedProject. 2-environments/config_test.go and 3-networks-svpc/hub-and-spoke config_test.go likewise declare envCodes/NetConfig defaults locally with no import of stage logic. 4-projects/config_test.go is the genuine exception: it calls the real projectLabels() and budgetConfig() functions defined in 4-projects/business_unit.go. TS port: 2-environments, 3-networks, 4-projects, and 5-app-infra test files define local projectLabels/defaultAppConfig functions inline and import no stage code; only 0-bootstrap.config.test.ts imports and calls the real loadConfig from '../0-bootstrap/config'. The concrete smoking-gun is confirmed exactly as described: test/5-app-infra.config.test.ts:30-44 hardcodes machineType: 'n1-standard-1' inside a locally-defined defaultAppConfig(), while the actual product code at 5-app-infra/business_unit_1/development/index.ts.example:18 reads `config.get('machine_type') || 'f1-micro'`, matching the TF integration test's f1-micro assertion. I also checked pulumi-library (both go/ and ts/) for any component-level test that might independently catch this class of regression — compute-instance.test.ts and compute_instance_test.go only check that the class/struct can be instantiated and exports exist; they never touch a machineType default (that default lives in the stage's business_unit_1 example, not the shared library), so there is no compensating test anywhere in the stack. Minor imprecision in the claim: it says "roughly 3 of 11" stage test files touch product code but only names two (TS 0-bootstrap, Go 4-projects) — I could not find a third file that qualifies, so the real count is 2 of 11, not 3. This is a small overstatement, not a refutation. Severity "major" is reasonable: this isn't cosmetic — it means the unit test suite for 9 of 11 stage files provides no regression protection for the actual Pulumi programs, and it produced a test that affirmatively documents an incorrect default (n1-standard-1 vs f1-micro), which is worse than having no test at all since it looks like verified documentation.

**Verifier note / correction:** Substantially correct as stated; only nitpick is the file count ("~3 of 11" should be "2 of 11" since only TS 0-bootstrap and Go 4-projects actually exercise product code — no third file was found that qualifies).

### MINOR (112)

#### [GO] Billing admins group never granted roles/billing.admin at the org level
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The upstream terraform-google-bootstrap module (used by TF 0-bootstrap main.tf) grants google_organization_iam_member "org_billing_admin" — roles/billing.admin to group_billing_admins at the organization level. The Go port's bootstrap component and sa.go replicate the org-admins grants, the authoritative billing.creator binding, SA billing.user/billing.admin, and the org SA's billing logging.configWriter, but nothing grants the billing admins group billing.admin, leaving the group unable to administer billing.

**Evidence:** Upstream module: terraform-google-bootstrap v12 main.tf resource google_organization_iam_member.org_billing_admin (role roles/billing.admin, member group_billing_admins), consumed by terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/main.tf module seed_bootstrap. Port: grep of pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/sa.go — GroupBillingAdmins appears only in the billing.creator binding (sa.go:371-379); pulumi-library/go/pkg/bootstrap/bootstrap.go has no group grants.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF side: fetched terraform-google-modules/terraform-google-bootstrap v12.0.0 main.tf directly (raw.githubusercontent.com) — it contains resource "google_organization_iam_member" "org_billing_admin" { org_id = var.org_id; role = "roles/billing.admin"; member = "group:${var.group_billing_admins}" }, consumed by terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/main.tf's module "seed_bootstrap" (group_billing_admins = var.groups.required_groups.group_billing_admins). This is distinct from the README prerequisite text about the deploying human user needing billing.admin (that's a one-time bootstrap-operator grant, not this resource) and distinct from the module's separate authoritative billing.creator binding.

Go port side: exhaustively grepped the entire pulumi_go-example-foundation repo (all .go files, not just sa.go) and pulumi-library/go for 'billing', 'GroupBillingAdmins', 'billing.admin', 'OrgBillingAdmin', 'NewOrganizationIAMMember'. GroupBillingAdmins is used only for: (1) creating the Cloud Identity group (groups.go), (2) essential-contacts registration (1-org/essential_contacts.go and 1-org/main.go), and (3) the authoritative org-level billing.creator binding (sa.go:371-379, which mirrors TF's separate google_organization_iam_binding.billing_creator resource, a different role/resource). The only 'roles/billing.admin' grant anywhere in the Go port (sa.go:252) targets the five granular service accounts at the billing-account IAM scope, not the org scope, and not the group. No org-level equivalent for billing.admin -> group_billing_admins exists anywhere in the Go port or in pulumi-library/go/pkg/bootstrap (bootstrap.go has zero billing-related group logic at all). As a sanity cross-check, the TypeScript port shows the identical pattern (billing.admin granted only to SAs via gcp.billing.AccountIamMember, never to the group at org level), corroborating that this is a genuinely missing resource rather than something relocated under an unusual name/abstraction.

Severity: the claimed "minor" rating is not inflated — if anything it's conservative, since this leaves the billing-admins group unable to fully administer billing at the org level via IaC. It's a small, well-scoped fix (one additional org-level IAM member resource), consistent with "minor" severity.

#### [BOTH] Bootstrap helper scripts missing: git_create_branches_helper.sh and choose_build_type.sh
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF ships 0-bootstrap/scripts/git_create_branches_helper.sh (creates and pushes the plan/development/nonproduction/production branches across all stage repos — the branch layout the CI templates depend on) and choose_build_type.sh (validated switching between the cb/github/gitlab/terraform_cloud/local build types, renaming the paired build/outputs/variables/versions example files consistently). Neither port has a 0-bootstrap/scripts directory; branch creation and build-type switching are manual multi-step README instructions (mv single files, hand-edit main.go/index.ts), with no validation. The ports' branch-triggered pipelines still require those branches to exist.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/scripts/{git_create_branches_helper.sh,choose_build_type.sh,push-to-repo.sh}. Ports: ls pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/scripts and pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/scripts — No such file or directory; grep for branch-creation helpers matches only ONBOARDING.md prose.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

TF side: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/scripts/ contains all three scripts as described. git_create_branches_helper.sh walks sibling repo directories (gcp-bootstrap/org/environments/networks/projects/cicd-runner), checks out and pushes plan/production/development/nonproduction branches with seed commits, with build-type-aware special-casing for GitLab's "image" branch. choose_build_type.sh validates a build-type argument against {cb, github, gitlab, terraform_cloud, local} and consistently renames the paired *_<type>.tf / *_<type>.tf.example files (a comment in the script even cross-references the equivalent Go logic in helpers/foundation-deployer/utils/files.go's RenameBuildFiles, confirming TF treats this as first-class, duplicated-for-robustness tooling).

Port side: neither pulumi_go-example-foundation nor pulumi_ts-example-foundation has a 0-bootstrap/scripts directory (confirmed via find). Grepping both repos and all of pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) for git_create_branches, choose_build_type, push-to-repo, checkout -b automation, or any RenameBuildFiles-equivalent turns up nothing except CONTRIBUTING.md boilerplate. The ports do carry an analogous need: 0-bootstrap has multiple build-type variants (build_github_actions.go active by default, plus build_gitlab.go.example, build_cloud_build.go.example, build_terraform_cloud.go.example) that must be swapped. But README-GitLab.md / README-CloudBuild.md spell this out as fully manual, unvalidated steps: `mv build_github_actions.go build_github_actions.go.bak`, `mv build_gitlab.go.example build_gitlab.go`, then hand-editing Config structs, loadConfig(), and the build-call site in main.go via diff-style instructions. Similarly, ONBOARDING.md instructs branch creation via raw `git checkout -b production && git push --set-upstream origin production` (and development/nonproduction) repeated per stage repo, with no script, no repo-name safety check, and no validation of build-type input. The ports' helpers/foundation-deployer/deploy.sh (the Pulumi analog of TF's Go CLI) explicitly does NOT reimplement branch creation or build-file renaming — its own README says it intentionally skips backend/branch mechanics since "Pulumi manages state natively." I checked test/, .github/workflows/, and Makefile targets in both ports for any hidden automation — none exists.

Severity: the reviewer's "minor" label is defensible and not inflated — this is one-time setup/onboarding tooling, not something consumed by steady-state pipelines (the branches themselves, once created by hand, work fine with the existing branch-triggered CI templates). I would not raise it above minor, but arguably it's a legitimate real gap since TF explicitly ships this as reusable, validated tooling and the ports reduce it to error-prone manual multi-repo git commands and hand-edited Go/TS source — worth calling out even if low severity.

#### [BOTH] Build-type example files contain hardcoded empty-string placeholders instead of config wiring
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The alternative build-type examples require hand-editing source constants before they can work: build_cloud_build.go.example line 92 'githubOwner := "" // Replace with: cfg.GitHubOwner'; build_gitlab.go.example lines 84-98 ('gitlabOwner := ""' and all five stageRepos entries are "" with 'Replace with' comments — the referenced cfg fields don't exist in Config); TS build_gitlab.ts.example lines 51-66 identical. The corresponding TF examples are complete: they read var.gh_repos/var.gl_repos from shipped variables_*.tf.example files and work once tfvars are set. The TFC example is similarly partial: TF's build_terraform_cloud.tf.example (319 lines, tfe provider: workspaces, agent pools, variable sets, VCS wiring + tfc-agent-gke module) vs the ports' examples which create only the GCP-side GKE/GCE infra, and no README-Terraform-Cloud is shipped.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/{variables_github.tf.example,variables_gitlab.tf.example,build_terraform_cloud.tf.example,modules/tfc-agent-gke}. Ports: pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_cloud_build.go.example:92, build_gitlab.go.example:84-98; pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_gitlab.ts.example:51-66; build_terraform_cloud.{go,ts}.example import no TFC/tfe SDK.

**Verifier reasoning:** All cited evidence checks out on both sides.

TF side (confirmed complete): variables_github.tf.example and variables_gitlab.tf.example ship fully-typed `gh_repos`/`gl_repos` object variables wired directly into build_github.tf.example/build_gitlab.tf.example via `var.gh_repos.owner`, `var.gl_repos.bootstrap`, etc. Once the .example files are renamed and a tfvars file supplies real values, the config works with no source editing. build_terraform_cloud.tf.example (319 lines) uses the real `tfe` provider to create tfe_workspace, tfe_agent_pool, tfe_variable_set and VCS wiring, backed by modules/tfc-agent-gke, and a companion README-Terraform-Cloud.md is shipped.

Port side (gap confirmed in both Go and TS):
- build_cloud_build.go.example:92 literally has `githubOwner := "" // Replace with: cfg.GitHubOwner` — and notably cfg.GitHubOwner *does* already exist and is wired in main.go/loadConfig, so this placeholder is gratuitous and could have been referenced directly; the example ships broken/no-op until hand-edited despite the real config path already existing.
- build_gitlab.go.example:84-98 has `gitlabOwner := ""` and five `stageRepos` entries all `""`, each with "Replace with: cfg.GitLabX" comments. I grepped main.go and confirmed no GitLabOwner/GitLabRepo* fields exist anywhere in Config — the referenced fields genuinely don't exist. README-GitLab.md documents adding these fields to Config/loadConfig step-by-step, but never instructs the user to also edit the function body's hardcoded "" literals, so even a user who follows the README to the letter is left with a silently-no-op GitLab integration (the function returns empty outputs when gitlabOwner == "").
- pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_gitlab.ts.example:51-66 has the identical pattern (`const gitlabOwner = ""; // cfg.gitlabOwner`, stageRepos all ""), and config.ts confirms no gitlab* fields exist in BootstrapConfig.
- build_terraform_cloud.{go,ts}.example (136/87 lines) create only GCP-side project/GKE-cluster/service-account/IAM-binding infra for a TFC agent; grepping both ports and all of pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) turns up zero references to a tfe provider/SDK, tfe.Workspace, tfe.AgentPool, or tfe.VariableSet — there is no equivalent anywhere for the TF example's actual Terraform Cloud workspace/agent-pool/variable-set/VCS wiring. No README-Terraform-Cloud.md exists in either port (grep confirms only a one-line pointer in README.md acknowledging the omission).

I checked pulumi-library (go/pkg/cicd and all other packages) explicitly for tfe/gitlab-config equivalents and found none, and I compared against other alternative examples in the port (e.g. vpn.go.example) which use a cleanly-passed config struct instead of inline "" placeholders — confirming this hardcoded-placeholder pattern is specific to the build_gitlab/build_cloud_build/build_terraform_cloud examples rather than the port's general convention.

**Verifier note / correction:** The claim is accurate as described; I'd only refine one nuance: for the Cloud Build example, the claim implies the cfg field must be added, but cfg.GitHubOwner *already exists and is already wired* in Config/main.go — so that specific placeholder is an even sillier/lazier oversight (a trivial one-line reference swap, not a missing-field problem) than the GitLab case, where the fields truly don't exist in Config. Severity of "minor" is fair and not inflated: this only affects opt-in, non-default alternative CI/CD backends (GitHub Actions is the working default in both ports), the files are already clearly marked `.example` and require multi-step manual activation regardless, so this adds incremental friction to an already-manual path rather than breaking core functionality.</correction>


#### [TS] CbPrivatePool VPN option is a stub: vpnConfiguration.enableVpn accepted but never implemented
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's cb-private-pool module includes vpn_ha.tf (83 lines): when vpn_configuration.enable_vpn is true it creates HA VPN gateways, tunnels, router interfaces/peers, and reads the PSK from Secret Manager to connect the private pool network to on-prem. The TS library component declares VpnConfig { enableVpn?: boolean } and CbPrivatePoolArgs.vpnConfiguration, but the constructor never reads args.vpnConfiguration — setting enableVpn: true silently does nothing. (Go has no component at all; see separate gap.)

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/vpn_ha.tf and variables.tf vpn_configuration object. TS: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool/src/index.ts lines 20-22 and 45-161 — 'vpnConfiguration' appears only in the interface, never in the constructor body.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed exactly as described: 0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/vpn_ha.tf (83 lines) creates an HA VPN gateway/router, external peer gateway, two VPN tunnels with router interfaces/peers, and reads the PSK from Secret Manager (data.google_secret_manager_secret_version.psk), all gated on var.vpn_configuration.enable_vpn, whose full field set (on_prem_public_ip_address0/1, router_asn, bgp_peer_asn, psk_secret_project_id/name, tunnel0/1_bgp_peer_address/session_range) is declared in variables.tf.

TS side: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool/src/index.ts declares `VpnConfig { enableVpn?: boolean }` (missing all the other TF fields too) and `CbPrivatePoolArgs.vpnConfiguration`, but the 161-line constructor body never references `args.vpnConfiguration` anywhere — verified by direct read and by grep for vpn/HaVpnGateway/ExternalVpnGateway/VPNTunnel/psk/routerAsn/bgpPeerAsn scoped to that package and to the ts port's 0-bootstrap, which returned zero hits. The package.json for cb-private-pool doesn't even depend on the library's own `@vitruviansoftware/foundation-vpn-ha` package, confirming no wiring exists.

I searched hard for an equivalent implemented differently: pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpn-ha does fully implement an HA VPN component (HaVpnGateway, Router, ExternalVpnGateway, VPNTunnel, RouterInterface, RouterPeer) mirroring terraform-google-modules/vpn/google//modules/vpn_ha — but it is never imported by cb-private-pool and is unused anywhere in the ts port (only referenced in package.json as an unused dependency). The ts port also has a fully-working VPN feature in 3-networks-svpc/hub-and-spoke (vpn.ts.example, documented in 0-bootstrap/onprem.md), but that connects the shared-VPC network to on-prem — a distinct feature that TF itself keeps separate (3-networks-svpc/modules/base_env/vpn.tf.example + modules/vpn-ha), not a substitute for cb-private-pool's own vpn_ha.tf which specifically wires the CI/CD private-pool's peered network (vpc-b-cbpools) directly to on-prem. So no equivalent behavior exists anywhere for this specific capability — the gap is real.

On severity: the reviewer's "minor" rating is well-calibrated, not inflated. Even in the upstream TF reference (build_cb.tf) this option is hardcoded to enable_vpn=false in the shipped example — it's an opt-in advanced knob nobody exercises by default. The ts port's analogous build_github_actions.ts also hardcodes `vpnConfiguration: { enableVpn: false }`, and the port's own onprem.md documentation steers users toward the (fully-implemented) stage-3 VPN path for CI/CD-to-on-prem connectivity rather than this cb-private-pool-specific path. So today no code path sets enableVpn:true, meaning the stub causes no currently-observable divergence — it would only bite a user who specifically tries to replicate this narrower TF module feature by hand-editing the stage code, at which point it silently no-ops instead of erroring or building the tunnels. That matches "minor" (real, silent, narrow blast radius) rather than something more severe.

#### [TS] CbPrivatePool hardcodes worker config, drops existing-network branch, and ignores VPN configuration
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** vs TF's cb-private-pool module the TS component: (1) hardcodes workerConfig (machineType e2-standard-4 vs TF default e2-medium, noExternalIp true vs TF default false, diskSizeGb 100 fixed, no custom pool name) with no options exposed; (2) has no peered_network_id branch — enableNetworkPeering with createPeeredNetwork=false silently creates a pool with NO network peering instead of using an existing network, and TF's validation for this combination is absent; (3) accepts vpnConfiguration.enableVpn but never references it — TF's vpn_ha.tf implements a full HA VPN to on-prem (Secret Manager PSK, two BGP tunnels, custom route advertisement); the library has a ts vpn-ha package but it is not wired into the pool. Go has no pool at all (covered separately).

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/variables.tf (private_worker_pool/vpn_configuration objects + validations), main.tf, vpn_ha.tf. Checked: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool/src/index.ts (VpnConfig only declares enableVpn; constructor never reads args.vpnConfiguration; workerConfig literal at lines 145-149).

**Verifier reasoning:** All three sub-claims verified against the code. (1) TF's cb-private-pool exposes name/disk_size_gb/machine_type(default e2-medium)/no_external_ip(default false) in private_worker_pool; the TS component (pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool/src/index.ts lines 141-154) hardcodes workerConfig {diskSizeGb:100, machineType:"e2-standard-4", noExternalIp:true} and pool name cb-pool-${region}, and PrivateWorkerPoolConfig exposes none of these. (2) TF main.tf line 20 uses peered_network_id when create_peered_network=false, with a variables.tf validation for that combination; the TS interface has no peeredNetworkId input (grep over the whole library confirms), so enableNetworkPeering:true + createPeeredNetwork:false silently yields a pool with networkConfig undefined and no error. (3) TF vpn_ha.tf implements HA VPN (Secret Manager PSK data source, two BGP tunnels, CUSTOM route advertisement) gated on enable_vpn; the TS VpnConfig declares only enableVpn and the constructor never reads args.vpnConfiguration; the package.json depends only on foundation-network, not the existing ts vpn-ha package (which is used only in 3-networks stage examples). The TS port (0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.ts lines 129-140) calls the component with enableVpn:false and createPeeredNetwork:true and does not compensate anywhere. No equivalent implementation exists under any other name in the port or library.

**Verifier note / correction:** Severity "minor" is accurate and should not be raised: TF's own 0-bootstrap/build_cb.tf hardcodes the identical call-site values (create_peered_network=true, enable_vpn=false, no worker overrides), so the missing existing-network branch and VPN path are never exercised in the default foundation deployment. The only deployed-behavior differences are machineType (e2-standard-4 vs TF-default e2-medium, a cost delta) and noExternalIp (true vs TF-default false, which is stricter than TF). The remainder is lost configurability of the reusable component plus silent acceptance of no-op inputs (enableVpn:true, createPeeredNetwork:false).

#### [BOTH] Cloud Build stage outputs from outputs_cb.tf not exported
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF exports cloud_builder_artifact_repo, gcs_bucket_cloudbuild_artifacts, gcs_bucket_cloudbuild_logs, cloud_build_worker_range_id, cloud_build_worker_peered_ip_range, cloud_build_peered_network_id, cloud_build_private_worker_pool_id (consumed downstream by 1-org org policy and 4-projects infra pipelines). Go exports none of these (no pool/AR exists — see separate gap — but even cloudbuild-adjacent outputs like an artifact repo path are absent while its 4-projects code expects CloudBuilderArtifactRepo). TS exports only cloud_build_private_worker_pool_id; it creates the tf-runners AR but does not export its path, and worker range/peered-ip/network outputs exposed by its CbPrivatePool component are not surfaced from the stack.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/outputs_cb.tf; consumers 1-org/envs/shared/remote.tf and 4-projects/business_unit_1/shared/remote.tf. Checked: pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/main.go exports (steps 6-9); pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/index.ts return block lines 172-215.

**Verifier reasoning:** Every factual assertion checks out. TF's 0-bootstrap/outputs_cb.tf exports all 7 outputs. Go's 0-bootstrap/main.go exports none of them (only cloudbuild_project_id). TS's 0-bootstrap/index.ts exports only cloud_build_private_worker_pool_id, creates the tf-runners Artifact Registry without exporting its path, and drops the workerRangeId/workerPeeredIpRange/peeredNetworkId outputs that the pulumi-library CbPrivatePool component (ts/packages/cb-private-pool/src/index.ts:40-43) does expose — they die at deployCICDProject's return (build_github_actions.ts:142-145). No equivalent export exists anywhere: I checked both ports' 0-bootstrap files, example/README variants, downstream stages, test suites, and pulumi-library. So the gap is literally real. However, its practical impact is near zero (see correction).

**Verifier note / correction:** The gap is real but should be scoped down and treated as informational rather than minor. (1) For the Go port this is not an independent gap: the default path creates no worker pool, AR repo, or CB workspaces at all — an explicitly documented, approved deviation (docs/ERRATA.md "Cloud Build Integration": CI/CD is GitHub Actions WIF only) — so there are no values to export; the claim itself defers the resource absence to a separate gap. The cited consumer 4-projects/example_infra_pipelines.go:35 (CloudBuilderArtifactRepo) is dead code behind //go:build example with zero callers, and the activatable example_infra_pipeline.go.example consumes only cloudbuild_project_id, which IS exported. (2) The claim overstates TF-side consumption: only cloud_build_private_worker_pool_id (1-org, 4-projects) and cloud_builder_artifact_repo (4-projects) have infra consumers; gcs_bucket_cloudbuild_artifacts/logs and cloud_build_peered_network_id are consumed only by TF integration tests, and worker_range_id/worker_peered_ip_range have no consumers at all. (3) The one output with real cross-port downstream consumers (cloud_build_private_worker_pool_id) IS exported by TS, and TS 1-org/4-projects consumers only read cloudbuild_project_id and the pool ID — both exported. The genuine residue is cosmetic export parity in TS (AR path and pool network details exist as resources/component outputs but are not surfaced), which nothing in either port consumes.

#### [GO] Go Cloud Build / GitLab example files are broken (wrong import path, wrong SA resource path, placeholder owner)
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** build_cloud_build.go.example and build_gitlab.go.example import github.com/VitruvianSoftware/pulumi-library/pkg/cicd, but the library's Go module path is github.com/VitruvianSoftware/pulumi-library/go (packages live under .../go/pkg/...), so following the files' own activation instructions (rename to .go) yields code that cannot compile. build_cloud_build.go.example also builds trigger service-account resource names as projects/{prefix}-b-cicd/serviceAccounts/{email} even though the stage SAs live in the seed project (and the real CI/CD project ID carries a random suffix), and leaves githubOwner hardcoded to "" with a replace-me comment.

**Evidence:** pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_cloud_build.go.example lines 58, 92, 100; build_gitlab.go.example line 49; pulumi-library/go/go.mod module path; working files (projects.go, sa.go) import .../go/pkg/... correctly. TF counterpart: build_cb.tf cloudbuild_sa = google_service_account.terraform-env-sa[each.key].id (seed project SA).

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed by direct inspection and a live compile test. (1) Import path: the library's Go module is github.com/VitruvianSoftware/pulumi-library/go, with cicd published as submodule .../go/pkg/cicd (pulumi-library/go/go.mod, go/pkg/cicd/go.mod); no module exists at the bare root path, and the port's go.mod/go.work contain no require/replace that could resolve .../pulumi-library/pkg/cicd. Following the example files' own activation instructions (rename to .go) and running `go build` in a scratch copy fails with 'no required module provides package github.com/VitruvianSoftware/pulumi-library/pkg/cicd' (build_cloud_build.go.example line 58; build_gitlab.go.example line 49); fixing only the /go segment makes the whole stage compile, so it is the sole compile break. Working files (build_github_actions.go:23, projects.go, sa.go) import the correct .../go/pkg/... path. (2) SA resource path: build_cloud_build.go.example line 100 constructs projects/{prefix}-b-cicd/serviceAccounts/{email}, but the port creates stage SAs in the seed project (sa.go:51 Project: seed.ProjectID) and the CI/CD project ID carries a random suffix by default (main.go:148,233; projects.go:154), so triggers would reference a nonexistent SA resource; the library passes the string through verbatim (pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd/cloudbuild.go:60-62,245,253). TF counterpart build_cb.tf:237 correctly uses the seed-project SA .id (sa.tf:145). (3) githubOwner "" placeholder at line 92 confirmed, though it is explicitly documented replace-me scaffolding. Claimed severity 'minor' is accurate: these are opt-in .example files and the default GitHub Actions path works, but a user activating them per the instructions hits a compile error and then a deploy-time SA error.

**Verifier note / correction:** The core claim (broken import path, wrong SA project in trigger resource names) is fully confirmed. One nuance: the hardcoded githubOwner/gitlabOwner "" placeholders are deliberate, comment-documented scaffolding ("Replace with: cfg.GitHubOwner") rather than an unintentional defect, so that sub-point is a documentation/ergonomics complaint, not a bug per se. Severity 'minor' is appropriate, not inflated.

#### [GO] Go pulumi-library has no HA VPN component at all (TS has one, unused)
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed (completeness-critic)_

**What's missing:** The confirmed gap list states 'library VpnHa component unused | both' for the HA VPN examples, implying both languages have such a reusable component that the example code simply fails to call. That is only true for TS. /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg has no vpn/vpn-ha package whatsoever (checked pkg/networking/*.go and the full pkg listing: app, bootstrap, cicd, cloud_dns, cloud_functions, cloud_router, compute_instance, data, gcloud, group, iam, instance_template, kms, log_export, logging, network_firewall_policy, network_peering, networking, policy, private_service_connect, project, pubsub, security, storage, vpc_sc — no vpn package). Go's /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/vpn.go.example and 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/vpn.go.example both call raw compute.NewHaVpnGateway/NewExternalVpnGateway/NewVPNTunnel directly (confirmed via grep) because there is nothing to call. TS by contrast has a real /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpn-ha package that its example simply doesn't invoke. This is a real asymmetry: Go's port has strictly less reusable VPN infrastructure than TS's, not merely 'unused' abstraction — worth splitting out as a go-specific library-parity finding rather than folding into a 'both' entry.

**Evidence:** TF: no dedicated VPN terraform-google-modules submodule either (3-networks-svpc/modules/base_env/vpn.tf.example uses raw google_compute_ha_vpn_gateway resources), so this is a port-vs-port asymmetry, not a TF-vs-port gap. Checked: /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg (full listing, no vpn package) vs /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpn-ha (exists, has src/).

**Verifier reasoning:** Found by the completeness-critic during final review; not run through the per-claim verifier.

#### [BOTH] Jenkins and Terraform Cloud CI/CD examples ship with no README coverage (and ERRATA denies they exist)
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's 0-bootstrap README contains a full 'Deploying with Jenkins' path and a README-Terraform-Cloud.md. Both ports ship working example code (build_jenkins.go.example/build_jenkins.ts.example, build_terraform_cloud.go.example/.ts.example) whose only documentation is in-file header comments; no README documents these variants, the 0-bootstrap READMEs mention them only in passing ('The Terraform foundation also includes Jenkins and Terraform Cloud examples', Go 0-bootstrap/README.md:414), and both ERRATA files state 'There are no Jenkins or Terraform Cloud equivalents'. Operators cannot discover or evaluate these shipped alternatives from the docs.

**Evidence:** Shipped: pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_jenkins.go.example (190 lines, header activation steps), build_terraform_cloud.go.example; pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_jenkins.ts.example, build_terraform_cloud.ts.example. No README-Jenkins/README-Terraform-Cloud in either port (full md file listing). Denial: both docs/ERRATA.md CI/CD sections. TF baseline: terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/README.md Jenkins section and README-Terraform-Cloud.md.

**Verifier reasoning:** The port-side observation is accurate but the claim's TF baseline for Jenkins is wrong, which changes the picture materially.

1) Terraform Cloud (TF baseline confirmed real): terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/README.md:185-189 has a live 'Deploying with Terraform Cloud' section linking to README-Terraform-Cloud.md, and that file, plus build_terraform_cloud.tf.example, still exist. Both Pulumi ports ship build_terraform_cloud.go.example / .ts.example but neither has a README-Terraform-Cloud.md (confirmed via directory listing of both ports' 0-bootstrap/), nor any mention in docs/. So for Terraform Cloud specifically, the claimed gap (shipped example code, zero dedicated README) is real.

2) Jenkins (TF baseline claim is FALSE): terraform-example-foundation currently has NO Jenkins documentation or code at all -- no README-Jenkins.md, no 'Deploying with Jenkins' section in README.md, no build_jenkins.tf.example, no jenkins-agent module. `git log` shows commit 6b3863e 'refactor!: remove jenkins support (#1470)' (2026-06-18) deleted README-Jenkins.md, the jenkins-agent module, build_jenkins.tf.example, and build/Jenkinsfile -- and this commit is an ancestor of the current HEAD (2026-07-01) on this checked-out branch. So the claim's core evidence ('TF's 0-bootstrap README contains a full Deploying with Jenkins path') is factually wrong for the repo state being compared against; there is no upstream Jenkins doc left for the ports to have parity with.

3) Independent of TF parity, both ports do have an internal documentation-consistency gap: they follow a 'one README per CI/CD example' pattern for Cloud Build (README-CloudBuild.md) and GitLab (README-GitLab.md), shipping the corresponding .example files, but do NOT extend that pattern to the Jenkins/TFC .example files they also ship. Worse, Go's 0-bootstrap/README.md:414 explicitly (and incorrectly) states 'The Terraform foundation also includes Jenkins and Terraform Cloud examples. These are not ported' -- false, since build_jenkins.go.example and build_terraform_cloud.go.example are literally present in the repo. The TS port doesn't even mention Jenkins/TFC in its CI/CD Providers table at all (checked ts 0-bootstrap/README.md lines ~287-295), a more complete omission than Go's inaccurate note. Both ERRATA.md files were checked: Go's says 'There are no equivalent README-Jenkins.md ... or README-Terraform-Cloud.md files' (true, scoped to README files); TS's says 'There are no Jenkins or Terraform Cloud equivalents' (broader and inaccurate, since .ts.example equivalents do exist). Searched pulumi-library/go and /ts -- no Jenkins/TFC-related components there either, so the README gap is absent everywhere it could live.

Net: the reviewer is right that both ports ship build_jenkins.*.example and build_terraform_cloud.*.example with only in-file header comments and no dedicated README, and that the main READMEs/ERRATA mischaracterize this (one denies the code was ported when it was; the other omits it from the table entirely). But roughly half the claim's framing (treating Jenkins as an equal parity failure against a still-fully-documented TF feature) is wrong -- TF dropped Jenkins entirely, so there's no live TF Jenkins doc to be missing parity with. Severity is fairly called minor: these are explicitly non-default, optional CI/CD alternatives (GitHub Actions is the golden path), gated behind clear manual rename steps documented in the file headers, so the practical operator impact is low discoverability/clarity, not a broken or blocked workflow.

**Verifier note / correction:** Narrow the claim to Terraform Cloud only, and drop/reframe the Jenkins parity argument: upstream terraform-example-foundation removed all Jenkins support (README-Jenkins.md, jenkins-agent module, build_jenkins.tf.example, Jenkinsfile) in commit 6b3863e before the current HEAD, so there is no current TF 'Deploying with Jenkins' README section to be missing parity with. The Jenkins .example files in both ports are actually ahead of/orphaned from current upstream, not lagging it -- the real issue there is just that the ports' own README (Go) inaccurately claims the Jenkins/TFC examples 'are not ported' and TS's README omits them from its CI/CD table entirely, both of which are documentation-accuracy bugs distinct from a TF-parity gap. The genuine TF-parity gap is limited to Terraform Cloud, where upstream still ships a full README-Terraform-Cloud.md that neither port equivalents.

#### [BOTH] New 'local' build type (no-CI/CD bootstrap option) not ported
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Upstream commit 2688fe4 (2026-06-19) added a supported 'local' build type: build_local.tf.example (no dedicated CI/CD project; cicd_project_id = seed project), outputs_local.tf.example, versions_local.tf.example, and 'local' added to scripts/choose_build_type.sh. Neither port offers this option: the bootstrap build-type choices are build_cloud_build/build_cb, build_github_actions (active default), build_gitlab, build_jenkins, build_terraform_cloud examples only, and main.go/index.ts unconditionally provision the GitHub Actions CI/CD project and WIF. There is also no choose_build_type.sh equivalent (ports' scripts/ has only validate-requirements.sh). A user wanting workstation-only deployment with no CI/CD infra has no supported path.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/build_local.tf.example, 0-bootstrap/outputs_local.tf.example, 0-bootstrap/versions_local.tf.example, 0-bootstrap/scripts/choose_build_type.sh (commit 2688fe4). Checked: file listings of /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap and /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap (no *local* variant); grep 'build_local|buildLocal|local build' across both ports empty; /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/main.go:71-72 always calls deployGitHubActionsBuild.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF side: commit 2688fe4 ("feat: add local build type (#1473)", 2026-06-19) genuinely exists in /home/user/terraform-example-foundation and adds exactly what the claim describes: build_local.tf.example (sets cicd_project_id = seed_project_id, no dedicated CI/CD project, only creates the gcp-projects state bucket), outputs_local.tf.example, versions_local.tf.example, and 'local' added to the build_types array in scripts/choose_build_type.sh.

Port side: exhaustive search of both /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation and /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation 0-bootstrap directories shows only cloud_build/cb, github_actions (active default), gitlab, jenkins, and terraform_cloud variants (as .go.example/.ts.example files) — no local/*.example counterpart in either port. grep for build_local, buildLocal, seed-as-cicd, noCicd, skipCicd, singleProject-as-cicd, workstation-only, etc. across both ports AND /home/user/pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) returned nothing relevant — no equivalent implemented under a different name. Both main.go and index.ts unconditionally call deployCICDProject followed by deployGitHubActionsBuild/deployGitHubActionsWIF with no branch to skip CI/CD project creation. Neither port's scripts/ directory has a choose_build_type.sh equivalent (only validate-requirements.sh), and the helpers/foundation-deployer in both ports has no 'local' build-type handling either (only shell 'local' variable keyword usages, unrelated). So the gap is real and matches the claim's description almost exactly.

On severity: the reviewer's 'minor' label is reasonable and, if anything, not overstated. This is a small, purely additive convenience feature added upstream just ~2 weeks before the reference date (2026-07-03), so the port lag is expected/small in scope. The feature itself is tiny (skip one project + a WIF/OIDC setup, reuse seed project ID) and switching to any non-default build type in the ports already requires manual file renaming and hand-editing main.go/index.ts (there's no automated switcher for any provider, not just 'local') — so adding local support would be a similarly small, mechanical change, not evidence of a deep architectural gap. I would not upgrade the severity beyond 'minor'; it does represent one entire (if niche) supported deployment mode being unavailable, but it's easily worked around by a user manually skipping the CICD deploy call, and it isn't consumed/depended upon by any other stage.

**Verifier note / correction:** None needed — claim and severity both hold up as stated.

#### [BOTH] No ordering dependency between created groups and IAM bindings that reference them
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's module seed_bootstrap has depends_on = [module.required_group], guaranteeing the org-admin/billing-admin group bindings happen after the groups exist when create_required_groups=true. In Go, deployGroups returns no resources and nothing depends on it — org-admin group IAM members (sa.go section 7) and the billing.creator binding are created concurrently with group creation and can fail on first deploy. In TS, group resources are wired only into the Bootstrap component's dependsOn; the org-admin IAMMember loop in index.ts has no dependency on them.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/main.tf line 107. Checked: pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/groups.go (returns error only) + main.go call order; pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/index.ts lines 96 and 161-167.

**Verifier reasoning:** The claim is verified in every particular. TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/main.tf line 107 has depends_on = [module.required_group] on module seed_bootstrap, which internally binds group_org_admins (org_admins_org_iam_permissions) and group_billing_admins (roles/billing.creator) at the org level, guaranteeing groups exist before the bindings when create_required_groups=true. Go port: deployGroups in 0-bootstrap/groups.go discards the group.NewGroup resource handles and returns only error; sa.go section 7 (lines 275-291, OrganizationIAMMember for group:GroupOrgAdmins) and section 10 (lines 371-379, billing.creator OrganizationIAMBinding for group:GroupBillingAdmins) use plain config strings with no pulumi.DependsOn (the only DependsOn in the stage is projects.go:130, unrelated), so Pulumi's parallel engine can attempt the IAM bindings before the groups exist. TS port: groups.ts does return a dependsOn array, but index.ts wires it only into the Bootstrap component (line 96), and the shared library component pulumi-library/ts/packages/bootstrap/src/bootstrap.ts creates no group IAM bindings whatsoever (it takes no group args), so that wiring protects nothing; the org-admin IAMMember loop at index.ts:161-167 passes no resource options and has no dependency on the created groups. Neither the go/pkg/group nor ts google-group library components contain retry/wait logic that would compensate. No equivalent ordering mechanism exists anywhere in either port or the shared library. The claimed severity of minor is accurate: the race only manifests when create_required_groups=true (default false in both ports), causes a transient first-deploy failure, and self-heals on a retry once the groups exist.

#### [TS] Org admins group over-granted at org level vs TF baseline
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF grants group_org_admins exactly org_admins_org_iam_permissions = [resourcemanager.organizationAdmin, billing.user] (+orgpolicy.policyAdmin when org_policy_admin_role=true). The TS port additionally grants resourcemanager.tagAdmin, resourcemanager.tagUser, logging.configWriter, securitycenter.notificationConfigEditor, accesscontextmanager.policyAdmin and essentialcontacts.admin to the group — a divergent, more-permissive posture. Go matches TF exactly.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/main.tf locals org_admins_org_iam_permissions; Go: sa.go section 7. TS: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/index.ts lines 138-167.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified on all points. TF (0-bootstrap/main.tf:32-34,56,63) grants group_org_admins exactly resourcemanager.organizationAdmin + billing.user, plus orgpolicy.policyAdmin only when org_policy_admin_role=true. The six extra roles cited (tagAdmin, tagUser, logging.configWriter, securitycenter.notificationConfigEditor, accesscontextmanager.policyAdmin, essentialcontacts.admin) exist in TF only as org-step service-account grants (0-bootstrap/sa.tf:40-52), never group grants; no other .tf file grants IAM to group_org_admins. The TS port (pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/index.ts:138-167) grants all six extra roles to the group in BOTH branches of the orgPolicyAdminRole conditional, while its sa.ts still grants them to the org SA too — so this is strictly additive over-provisioning of the human group, not a relocation or consolidation. The Go port (0-bootstrap/sa.go:275-288, section 7) matches TF exactly. pulumi-library has zero org-admins group IAM logic, so nothing there offsets the divergence.

**Verifier note / correction:** Severity 'minor' is appropriate; note the practical impact is limited because the group already holds resourcemanager.organizationAdmin (which includes organizations.setIamPolicy), so the extra grants widen declared posture and break TF parity without materially expanding effective privilege.

#### [GO] SA email output names diverge from TF/TS and from Go's own docs
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Go exports service-account emails as <key>_step_terraform_service_account_email using the short map keys (org_step_..., env_step_..., net_step_..., proj_step_...), while TF and the TS port export organization_step_/environment_step_/networks_step_/projects_step_terraform_service_account_email. Go's own ONBOARDING.md (line 300) tells users to run `pulumi stack output organization_step_terraform_service_account_email`, which will fail against the actual exports.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/outputs.tf; Go: pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/main.go line 84 (ctx.Export(key+"_step_terraform_service_account_email")) vs pulumi_go-example-foundation/ONBOARDING.md line 300; TS: index.ts lines 175-179 use the long names.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all points. TF (0-bootstrap/outputs.tf lines 22-42) and the TS port (0-bootstrap/index.ts lines 175-179) both export the long names (organization_step_/environment_step_/networks_step_/projects_step_terraform_service_account_email). The Go port (0-bootstrap/main.go line 84) exports key+"_step_terraform_service_account_email" over the deployIAM map in sa.go whose keys are bootstrap/org/env/net/proj, yielding org_step_/env_step_/net_step_/proj_step_ names that diverge from TF, TS, and Go's own ONBOARDING.md line 300, which instructs `pulumi stack output organization_step_terraform_service_account_email` — a command that would fail against the actual exports. No alias, remap, or second export of the long names exists anywhere in the Go repo or in pulumi-library (grepped both go/ and ts/). Go's other docs (README-GitHub.md line 129, README-GitLab.md line 172, docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md, docs/FAQ.md) reference yet a third scheme ({stage}_sa_email) that also matches nothing. Severity 'minor' is fair: the outputs are informational/CLI-consumed only — no downstream stack references them programmatically — so impact is limited to broken documented commands and cross-port naming parity.

#### [TS] TS build_cloud_build.ts.example promises a private worker pool it never creates; triggers lack pool/log/artifact wiring
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The example's header lists "5. Private Worker Pool — VPC-peered for secure builds" but the function creates no worker pool and its Cloud Build triggers don't reference one (TF's tf_workspace sets enable_worker_pool=true + worker_pool_id for every stage trigger). It also creates no per-stage log or artifacts buckets (TF tf_cloudbuild_workspace creates both and 0-bootstrap exports them).

**Evidence:** pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_cloud_build.ts.example (header lines 20-27 vs body — no WorkerPool/CbPrivatePool, triggers have no worker pool option). TF: 0-bootstrap/build_cb.tf module tf_workspace (enable_worker_pool, log_bucket_name, artifacts_bucket_name).

**Verifier reasoning:** The claim holds for the cited file. TF side verified: 0-bootstrap/build_cb.tf creates a private worker pool (module.tf_private_pool -> google_cloudbuild_worker_pool) and per-stage tf_cloudbuild_workspace modules with enable_worker_pool=true, worker_pool_id, log_bucket_name, and artifacts_bucket_name; outputs_cb.tf exports the pool ID and both bucket maps. In pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_cloud_build.ts.example, header line 26 promises '5. Private Worker Pool — VPC-peered for secure builds' but the body creates only the CI/CD project, CSR repos, filename-based triggers with no worker-pool option, and an Artifact Registry repo — no WorkerPool/CbPrivatePool, no log or artifacts buckets. The referenced cloudbuild-tf-plan.yaml/apply.yaml files do not exist in the TS repo, so the pool cannot be wired via build YAML either. Additionally, the example's CloudBuildOutputs lacks privateWorkerPoolId while 0-bootstrap/index.ts:212 exports cicdOutputs.privateWorkerPoolId (consumed by 1-org's cloudbuild.allowedWorkerPools policy), so adopting the example as written would break that export. Exhaustive greps of the TS port and pulumi-library confirmed no per-stage build-logs/build-artifacts buckets exist anywhere in TS 0-bootstrap.

**Verifier note / correction:** The gap is confined to the build_cloud_build.ts.example file, not the TS port as a whole: the private worker pool component (CbPrivatePool, wrapping gcp.cloudbuild.WorkerPool with VPC peering, mirroring TF's cb-private-pool module) exists in pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool and is created by both the default active provider build_github_actions.ts and the sibling example build_cb.ts.example, with the pool ID exported from 0-bootstrap and consumed by 1-org. What is genuinely absent everywhere in the TS port is the tf_cloudbuild_workspace equivalent: no trigger wiring to the pool and no per-stage log/artifacts buckets. Claimed severity 'minor' is accurate given it is an opt-in example file and the pool exists in the default path.

#### [TS] TS reads but never wires bucket_tfstate_kms_force_destroy, kms_key_protection_level, and random_suffix
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** config.ts loads bucketTfStateKmsForceDestroy, kmsKeyProtectionLevel and randomSuffix, but index.ts never passes kmsPreventDestroy or keyProtectionLevel to the Bootstrap component and hardcodes randomSuffix: true, so these documented options are silently ignored (KMS keys always protected, protection level always SOFTWARE, suffix always on). Go wires all three (sa/projects.go: KMSPreventDestroy, KeyProtectionLevel, RandomSuffix).

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/variables.tf bucket_tfstate_kms_force_destroy; main.tf kms_prevent_destroy/key params. Checked: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/config.ts lines 105/114/117 vs index.ts Bootstrap call lines 51-96 (randomSuffix: true literal; no kmsPreventDestroy/keyProtectionLevel); pulumi-library/ts/packages/bootstrap/src/bootstrap.ts supports both args.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified line-by-line. TF: bucket_tfstate_kms_force_destroy exists (0-bootstrap/variables.tf:93) and is wired as kms_prevent_destroy = !var.bucket_tfstate_kms_force_destroy (main.tf:67). TS port: config.ts loads all three values (lines 105/114/117) but a repo-wide grep confirms no consumer exists anywhere — index.ts:59 hardcodes randomSuffix: true and the Bootstrap call (index.ts:51-96) omits kmsPreventDestroy and keyProtectionLevel; no alternative wiring exists in sa.ts, build_github_actions.ts, or elsewhere. The shared library component (pulumi-library/ts/packages/bootstrap/src/bootstrap.ts lines 16/29/30, defaults at 49-50, applied at 99/108/111) supports all three args, so the library is not the gap — the TS stage simply never passes them, meaning KMS keyring/key are always protect:true and protection level always SOFTWARE regardless of user config. Go wires all three (0-bootstrap/projects.go:63,103-104,154 — claim said sa/projects.go, actual path is 0-bootstrap/projects.go, immaterial). Claimed severity of minor is appropriate: it silently ignores documented config, but defaults match TF's effective behavior for typical users; it only bites users who set bucket_tfstate_kms_force_destroy=true (teardown blocked by protect), kms_key_protection_level=HSM, or random_suffix=false.

**Verifier note / correction:** Gap is real but slightly overstated as a TF-parity issue: only bucket_tfstate_kms_force_destroy is an actual variable in terraform-example-foundation's 0-bootstrap. kms_key_protection_level and random_suffix are not TF 0-bootstrap variables (grep of the TF repo's 0-bootstrap finds neither) — they are port-added config options, so for those two the defect is dead config and Go/TS parity divergence, not upstream Terraform parity. Severity minor stands.

_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed (completeness-critic)_

Neither example actually deploys the tfc-agent workload onto the cluster (no Kubernetes provider resource, no secret with the TFC agent token) — the cluster it provisions would never actually run a Terraform Cloud agent — and both drop the NAT/router, firewall, DNS policy, and service-identity impersonation resources entirely. Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_terraform_cloud.go.example (only project/SA/cluster/nodepool/org-IAM/impersonation-loop, no kubernetes deployment). TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_terraform_cloud.ts.example (same, even thinner — no per-stage impersonation loop, no kubernetes resources at all).

#### [BOTH] WIF wildcard repo binding can never match (silent no-op grant)
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Both ports add a branch TF doesn't have: when a stage's GitHub repo is unset or "*", the SA binding member becomes principalSet://.../attribute.repository/<owner>. The repository claim is always "owner/repo", so this attribute value never matches and the stage SA silently gets no WIF access. Go additionally omits attribute.repository_owner from the provider attribute mapping, so no owner-scoped attribute is even available. TF's build_github.tf.example always binds attribute.repository/<owner>/<repo>.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/build_github.tf.example sa_mapping. Checked: pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.go lines 77-84 + pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd/github.go AttributeMapping (no repository_owner); pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.ts lines 204-207.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified in full. TF reference (/home/user/terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_github.tf.example line 37) always binds attribute.repository/<owner>/<repo>, with all five repos required by the gh_repos object type in variables_github.tf.example — TF has no wildcard branch. Both ports add one: Go (/home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.go lines 77-84) and TS (/home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.ts lines 204-213) emit member principalSet://iam.googleapis.com/<pool>/attribute.repository/<owner> when a stage repo is unset or "*". GCP principalSet attribute members are exact-match and GitHub's repository claim is always "owner/repo", so this member can never match — the stage SA silently gets no WIF access while pulumi up succeeds and the code comment claims "Wildcard: any repo under this owner". The branch is reachable: repo configs are optional (conf.Get / pulumiConfig.get, no validation) in both ports. The Go sub-claim also holds: pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd/github.go lines 106-111 map only google.subject, attribute.actor, attribute.aud, attribute.repository — attribute.repository_owner is absent, so Go cannot even express an owner-scoped binding; the TS inline provider maps attribute.repository_owner (line 195) but never uses it in the binding. No compensating binding exists anywhere in either port or the library (grep for workloadIdentityUser/principalSet found only unrelated 5-app-infra confidential-space code), and pulumi-library/ts has no cicd/github package. Claimed severity "minor" is fair: the defect is fail-closed (no security exposure), the READMEs document all stage repos as Required, and unset repos also skip GitHub secrets provisioning so the stage pipeline would be unwired regardless — impact is a confusing CI auth failure plus a misleading no-op IAM binding, not privilege escalation.

#### [BOTH] project_deletion_policy not applied to the seed project
