Both ports read the config value and apply it to the CI/CD project, but neither passes it to the library Bootstrap component for the seed project, which silently defaults to PREVENT — a DELETE/ABANDON setting is ignored for the seed project.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/main.tf line 68. Checked: pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/projects.go deploySeedProject (no DeletionPolicy in BootstrapArgs call) vs deployCICDProject (passes it); pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/index.ts Bootstrap call (no deletionPolicy) vs build_github_actions.ts deployCICDProject.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified in full. TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/main.tf:68 passes project_deletion_policy to module "seed_bootstrap" (variable defaults to "PREVENT"). Go port: deploySeedProject in /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/projects.go (lines 55-108) omits DeletionPolicy from bootstrap.BootstrapArgs even though the library supports it (/home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/bootstrap/bootstrap.go:69-70, defaulting to "PREVENT" at lines 127-129), while deployCICDProject does pass DeletionPolicy: pulumi.String(cfg.ProjectDeletionPolicy) at projects.go:155. TS port: the Bootstrap call in /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/index.ts:51-96 passes no deletionPolicy, and the library defaults it to "PREVENT" (/home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/bootstrap/src/bootstrap.ts:45), while build_github_actions.ts:69 passes deletionPolicy: cfg.projectDeletionPolicy for the CI/CD project. No stack transformations or alternative mechanisms exist in either port that would apply the configured policy to the seed project. Severity "minor" is fair: the silent default equals the TF default, so behavior diverges only when a user sets DELETE/ABANDON (e.g., teardown flows), in which case the seed project ignores it and remains PREVENT.

#### [TS] Billing-account log sinks not created (billingAccount never passed to CentralizedLogging)
_Area: 1-org · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF sets enable_billing_account_sink = true in module logs_export, creating billing-account-level sinks to the log destinations, and outputs billing_sink_names. The TS centralized-logging library supports billing sinks via args.billingAccount, but 1-org/index.ts constructs CentralizedLogging without it, so no BillingAccountSink is ever created and the exported billing_sink_names is always an empty object.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/log_sinks.tf:44-46 (enable_billing_account_sink = true). TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:328-354 (no billingAccount arg) vs pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts:168-181,217-231 (billing sinks gated on args.billingAccount).

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all counts. TF (log_sinks.tf:44-46) does set billing_account and enable_billing_account_sink=true on module.logs_export, and outputs.tf:117-119 exports billing_sink_names, which the centralized-logging module populates via google_logging_billing_account_sink resources for project/storage/pubsub destinations. In the TS port, 1-org/index.ts:328-354 constructs `new CentralizedLogging(...)` without a billingAccount field, even though cfg.billingAccount is already used 8+ other times in the same file for other components (lines 133,156,179,202,225,245,271,304). The shared TS library (pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts) gates BillingAccountSink creation for storage (~168-181) and pubsub (~217-231) destinations strictly on `if (args.billingAccount)`, so with no billingAccount passed, no BillingAccountSink resources are ever created, and the exported `billing_sink_names` (index.ts:544-545) always resolves to `{}`. A full grep of the TS port and pulumi-library/ts found no alternate spelling or separately-wired mechanism that achieves this. As a control, the Go port does wire this correctly (main.go:328,350, logging.go:79-80 pass BillingAccount and EnableBillingAccountSink into the shared component, and pulumi-library/go/pkg/logging/centralized_logging.go:387,479,528 create the sinks), confirming this is a TS-specific omission, not a deliberate design divergence shared by both ports. Severity "minor" is reasonable — no failure/crash, purely an additive informational output/optional security-logging feature with no other apparent consumer in the ported code.

#### [GO] Essential Contacts resources named by nondeterministic map iteration — churn across deployments
_Area: 1-org · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** deployEssentialContacts builds contactMap (a Go map keyed by email) and creates resources named essential-contact-0..N by iterating the map with an incrementing index. Go map iteration order is randomized per process, so contact→resource-name assignment can change on every pulumi up, causing spurious delete/recreate of org-level Essential Contacts. TF keys the for_each by email so resource identity is stable; naming by email (or sorting) would fix this.

**Evidence:** Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/essential_contacts.go:108-124 (for email, categories := range contactMap with idx counter). TF: 1-org/envs/shared/essential_contacts.tf uses for_each keyed on stable values.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

TF side: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/essential_contacts.tf builds `categories_map` then transposes it into `local.contacts_list` (a map keyed by email), and the `google_essential_contacts_contact` resource uses `for_each = local.contacts_list` with `email = each.key`. Terraform's for_each resource addressing is keyed by the stable map key (email), so contact→resource identity never depends on iteration order.

Go port side: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/essential_contacts.go builds `contactMap` (map[string][]string keyed by email, lines 45-105), then at lines 108-124 does `idx := 0; for email, categories := range contactMap { ...NewContact(ctx, fmt.Sprintf("essential-contact-%d", idx), ...); idx++ }`. This exactly matches the cited evidence — the Pulumi logical resource name (which determines URN/identity for diffing) is an incrementing index assigned by Go's randomized map-iteration order, not by the email itself. Since Go intentionally randomizes map iteration order per range (varies across process runs), the email assigned to "essential-contact-0" vs "essential-contact-1" etc. can differ between `pulumi up` invocations, producing property diffs against prior state on resources whose logical name maps to a different email than last run.

I checked /home/user/pulumi-library (both go/ and ts/) for any shared Essential Contacts component that might implement stable keying instead — none exists (only unrelated "contactcenterinsights.googleapis.com" string matches). I confirmed main.go's only call site (line 116) uses this function as-is, so there's no other determinism-adding wrapper. I also checked the TS port's essential_contacts.ts: it uses the identical `idx`-based naming pattern, but iterates a plain object/Record via `Object.entries`, whose iteration order in JS is deterministic (insertion order) for non-numeric string keys — so the TS port does not share this specific nondeterminism bug, consistent with the claim being scoped to "go" only.

One minor nuance: the claim's phrase "delete/recreate" may overstate the mechanism — I could not confirm locally whether the underlying provider treats `email` as ForceNew (ContactArgs in the vendored pulumi-gcp SDK has no visible replace-on-change annotation in the Go SDK comments, and GCP's Essential Contacts API supports an Update/Patch call for email/categories). It's plausible the actual observed churn is spurious in-place property updates each deploy rather than full delete+create — but either way the described defect (unstable, non-deterministic resource↔email identity causing spurious diffs across deploys) is real and present in the code exactly as cited.

**Verifier note / correction:** The mechanism may be slightly overstated: it likely causes spurious per-deploy property diffs/updates on the essential-contact-N resources (email reassigned to a different logical resource) rather than guaranteed full delete+recreate, since email/categories may be patchable in the underlying provider rather than ForceNew. This doesn't change the "minor" severity rating, which is reasonably calibrated (org-level Essential Contacts churn, small resource count, no ports elsewhere lack the same issue since TS uses deterministic object iteration).

#### [TS] Log sink filters set to empty string — exports ALL logs instead of TF's audit/vpc/firewall/dns filter
_Area: 1-org · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF applies a logs_filter restricting exported logs to cloudaudit activity/system_event/data_access/access_transparency/policy plus vpc_flows, firewall and dns_queries logs on the storage, pubsub, and project sinks. TS 1-org passes loggingSinkFilter: "" to every destination, so every org log entry is exported to all destinations — a significant cost/content divergence from the reference behavior.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/log_sinks.tf:21-30 (logs_filter) used at lines 52-84. TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:334,339,344,352 (loggingSinkFilter: "").

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF (1-org/envs/shared/log_sinks.tf:17-30) defines a logs_filter restricting exports to cloudaudit activity/system_event/data_access/access_transparency/policy plus compute vpc_flows/firewall and dns.googleapis.com dns_queries, and applies it to storage_options, pubsub_options, and project_options (lines 53, 68, 79). The TS port's 1-org/index.ts (lines 328-354) instantiates CentralizedLogging with loggingSinkFilter: "" on all four destinations (log bucket, BigQuery, storage, pubsub — lines 334, 339, 344, 352), and no equivalent filter string (searched for logsFilter/logFilter/cloudaudit/vpc_flows/dns_queries) exists anywhere else in the TS 1-org stage. The shared TS component (pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts) passes filter: args.X.loggingSinkFilter ?? "" straight to gcp.logging.OrganizationSink — since "" is not null/undefined the ?? never substitutes a real default, so the sinks genuinely get an empty (match-all) filter. Cross-checking the Go port (pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/logging.go:54,84,95,101) shows it defines the identical cloudaudit/vpc_flows/firewall/dns_queries filter string and wires it into all sinks correctly, proving this was achievable and was simply dropped in the TS port. The claimed "minor" severity is reasonable/not inflated — it's a real cost and log-volume divergence (exports all org logs rather than the curated audit/network subset) but not a security regression or functional break.

#### [TS] Per-project billing budgets not wired for any 1-org project
_Area: 1-org · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF creates a billing budget (default 1000 USD, alert at 120% forecasted) on every 1-org project (audit logs, billing export, kms, secrets, interconnect, scc, net hub, env network projects) via project_budget defaults; the Go port passes budgets via budgetFor(...). The TS ProjectFactory supports budget args, but 1-org/index.ts constructs all eight+ projects without any budget parameters, so no budgets exist.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/variables.tf:105-140 (defaults 1000/1.2/FORECASTED_SPEND), projects.tf budget args per module. Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/projects.go:45-96. TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:130-323 (no budget* args on any ProjectFactory).

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all three fronts.

1. TF side: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/variables.tf:97-140 defines `project_budget` with per-project optional fields (net_hub, shared_network, interconnect, org_secrets, org_billing_export, org_audit_logs, common_kms, scc_notifications), each defaulting to amount=1000, alert_spent_percents=[1.2], alert_spend_basis="FORECASTED_SPEND". projects.tf wires `budget_alert_pubsub_topic`/`budget_alert_spent_percents`/`budget_amount`/`budget_alert_spend_basis` into every project module (lines 59-62, 93-96, 128-131, 162-165, 196-199, 230-233, 273-276, 297-301). Claim's TF description is accurate.

2. Go port: confirmed equivalent exists — `budgetFor()` and `getProjectBudget()` in /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/projects.go convert a `PerProjectBudget` struct (mirroring each upstream variable, defaults 1.2/FORECASTED_SPEND) into a `project.BudgetConfig` passed to every `createProject(...)` call for all 8 projects (logging, billing_export, scc, kms, secrets, interconnect, net_hub, shared_network).

3. TS port: /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/project-factory/src/index.ts (lines 44-51, 125-145) shows `ProjectFactory` fully supports `budgetAmount`, `budgetAlertSpentPercents`, `budgetAlertPubsubTopic`, `budgetAlertSpendBasis` and creates a `gcp.billing.Budget` when `budgetAmount` is set — so the component-level capability exists (also mirrored in the standalone `budget` package). However, /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts constructs all 8 projects (orgAuditLogs, orgBillingExport, commonKms, orgSecrets, interconnect, sccNotifications, networkHub, and the 3 per-environment network projects) with zero budget* arguments — grepping the entire file for "budget" only turns up `activateApis` entries for "billingbudgets.googleapis.com" (API enablement), never an actual budget config. Notably, /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/config.ts defines and loads `projectBudget: Record<string, any>` from Pulumi config (`project_budget`) but this field is never referenced anywhere in index.ts — it's dead/unused config, strongly corroborating that budget wiring was dropped for this stage specifically. By contrast, the exact same budget plumbing IS correctly wired in this same TS port's other stages (2-environments/modules/env_baseline.ts, 4-projects/modules/single_project.ts), confirming this is an isolated omission in 1-org, not a missing capability across the whole port.

Severity: the claimed "minor" is accurate/not inflated. Missing budgets don't block any deployment or break functionality — this is a cost-monitoring/alerting gap (no billing overspend notifications for the 8 shared org/network projects), appropriately minor rather than critical.

#### [TS] create_unique_tag_key option is a no-op (identical ternary branches)
_Area: 1-org · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF appends a random suffix to the environment tag key short_name when create_unique_tag_key = true (needed to avoid collisions when the org already has an 'environment' TagKey, e.g. parent-folder test deployments); the Go port implements the random suffix. TS index.ts reads cfg.createUniqueTagKey but both ternary branches return "environment", so the option silently does nothing and re-deployments collide.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/tags.tf:47-56 (key_suffix + random_string). Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/tags.go:41-43. TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:406 (shortName: cfg.createUniqueTagKey ? "environment" : "environment").

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all three points.

1. TF side is real: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/tags.tf:47 defines `key_suffix = var.create_unique_tag_key ? "-${random_string.tag_key_suffix.result}" : ""`, consumed at line 20 as `shortname = "environment${local.key_suffix}"`, backed by a `random_string.tag_key_suffix` resource (lines 50-54) and a `create_unique_tag_key` variable (envs/shared/variables.tf:179), with tfvars comments explaining it's for avoiding TagKey collisions in parent-folder/test deployments.

2. Go port correctly implements it: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/tags.go:41-55 conditionally creates a `random.NewRandomString` resource when `cfg.CreateUniqueTagKey` is true and builds `keySuffix` as `"environment-<random>"`, otherwise leaving it `"environment"` — faithful port of the TF logic, even citing "H14 / create_unique_tag_key" in a comment.

3. TS port is a genuine no-op: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:406 reads `cfg.createUniqueTagKey` but both ternary branches evaluate to the literal string `"environment"`. Grepping the file and the whole ts port confirms there is no `@pulumi/random` import, no `RandomString`, and no other reference to `createUniqueTagKey` beyond config plumbing (config.ts:36,96) — the config value is read and then discarded. I also checked /home/user/pulumi-library/ts (the shared component library, per instructions) for any factored-out TagKey/suffix helper; the only `RandomString` usage there is in project-factory for GCP project-ID uniqueness, an unrelated concern — no equivalent tag-key-suffix logic exists anywhere reachable by the TS port. So the gap is real and exactly as described.

On severity: the reviewer's "minor" label looks appropriate, maybe even slightly generous toward the port. This isn't merely a cosmetic/informational gap — the whole point of create_unique_tag_key is to prevent an "AlreadyExists" API error on `google_tags_tag_key`/`gcp.tags.TagKey` when re-deploying (e.g., parallel/parent-folder test stacks) into an org that already owns an `environment` TagKey. Since the branches are identical, setting the flag to true provides zero conflict-avoidance and any second deployment attempting the same org will hit a duplicate-TagKey creation error — a real (if narrow, only-hit-in-specific-multi-deployment scenarios) functional bug, not just dead code. "Minor" is a fair severity given it only manifests in the specific parent-folder/multi-deployment scenario the option exists for, and does not affect standard single-org deployments.

#### [TS] 1-org package.json missing declared dependencies for imported packages; relies on repo-root install with a conflicting version pin
_Area: 1-org/build · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** index.ts imports @vitruviansoftware/foundation-project-factory and @vitruviansoftware/foundation-org-policy, and modules/network/index.ts imports foundation-network and foundation-dns-hub, but 1-org/package.json declares none of them (only cai-monitoring and centralized-logging). Resolution works only if dependencies are installed at the repo root (root package.json declares them, but has no workspaces field), so a standalone `npm install && pulumi up` in 1-org fails at module resolution. The root also pins foundation-centralized-logging to 0.5.0 while 1-org requires ^0.5.1 (the version adding billingSinkNames). The package name is also a copy-paste "0-bootstrap".

**Evidence:** TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:14-16,19 and modules/network/index.ts vs /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/package.json (deps list); root /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/package.json (no "workspaces"; centralized-logging "0.5.0").

**Verifier reasoning:** Every element of the claim verified. 1-org/index.ts (lines 14-15) imports @vitruviansoftware/foundation-project-factory and foundation-org-policy, and 1-org/modules/network/index.ts (lines 6-7) imports foundation-network and foundation-dns-hub, yet 1-org/package.json declares none of them (only cai-monitoring ^0.2.0 and centralized-logging ^0.5.1, plus Pulumi base packages), and its name is the copy-pasted "0-bootstrap". The repo root package.json declares all packages but has no "workspaces" field, and no alternative mechanism exists (no pnpm/lerna/yarn workspace config, no file: deps, no committed node_modules); resolution in-monorepo works only because the Makefile "install" target installs at root and Node walks up to root node_modules. Critically, the documented deployment workflow in 1-org/README.md copies 1-org/* into a standalone repo and runs npm install there — the four undeclared packages are then absent and pulumi preview/up fails at module resolution; the CI template (build/pulumi-up.yml) runs npm ci, which also fails since 1-org commits no package-lock.json. The version conflict is real too: root pins centralized-logging to exact "0.5.0" while 1-org needs ^0.5.1 — pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging is at 0.5.1 and billingSinkNames exists only in 0.5.1; 1-org/index.ts:545 consumes it, so a root-only install silently yields an undefined billing_sink_names output (ts-node transpile-only masks the type error).

**Verifier note / correction:** Claim is accurate but slightly understates scope and severity. (1) The defect is not unique to 1-org: e.g. 2-environments/envs/development/package.json also declares zero foundation packages (and is also named "0-bootstrap") while 2-environments/modules imports foundation-project-factory — the missing-deps pattern is repo-wide. (2) The centralized-logging 0.5.0-vs-^0.5.1 conflict only bites in root-only installs; when 1-org's own npm install succeeds, nearest-first resolution gives it 0.5.1. (3) "Minor" is at the low end: the documented standalone workflow (copy 1-org to its own repo, npm install, CI npm ci) is the primary deployment path and it fails outright at module resolution (and npm ci fails for lack of a package-lock.json), so "moderate" would also be defensible — though it is a build-time packaging bug with a trivial fix, not incorrect infrastructure.

#### [BOTH] CAI monitoring CMEK only wired for Artifact Registry; bucket and Pub/Sub topic encryption plus random_suffix option missing
_Area: 1-org/cai-monitoring · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform's cai-monitoring module applies var.encryption_key to the Artifact Registry repo, the function source bucket (enable_cmek), and the Pub/Sub topic (topic_kms_key_name), and supports random_suffix (default true) on resource names. Both library components accept an EncryptionKey/encryptionKey arg but apply it only to the Artifact Registry repository — the source bucket and topic have no KMS args — and neither supports a random suffix (fixed names ar-cai-monitoring, top-cai-monitoring-event, fd-cai-monitoring), so re-deployments/recreates can collide with soft-deleted or existing resources.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/modules/cai-monitoring/main.tf:29,36-38,53-57,82-84,122-133 and variables.tf:38-48,68-72. Go: /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/security/cai_monitoring.go:228-257,275-281 (KMS only on AR; fixed names). TS: /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/cai-monitoring/src/index.ts:142-176 (same).

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF module (1-org/modules/cai-monitoring/main.tf) wires var.encryption_key to the Artifact Registry repo (line 56), the source bucket via enable_cmek (82-84), and the Pub/Sub topic via topic_kms_key_name (128), and embeds a random_id suffix in every resource name (ar-/bkt-/fd-/top- names, lines 53/77/108/126). In both ports the component lives only in pulumi-library: Go (go/pkg/security/cai_monitoring.go) applies EncryptionKey solely to the AR repo (235-237) — bucket (249-257) and topic (275-278) have no KMS args — despite the arg's doc comment claiming coverage of Cloud Storage and Pub/Sub; TS (ts/packages/cai-monitoring/src/index.ts) is identical (kmsKeyName only at line 148). Both use fixed physical names (ar-cai-monitoring, top-cai-monitoring-event, fd-cai-monitoring, bkt-cai-monitoring-sources-<projectId>) with no random suffix. Exhaustive greps of both port repos and the whole library for kmsKeyName/topicKmsKeyName/RandomId/randomSuffix and alternate CAI implementations found no equivalent under any name; the port stages (go 1-org/cai_monitoring.go:66-73, ts 1-org/index.ts:462-474) call these components without passing an encryption key, and the local modules are a stub/re-export. Severity "minor" is fair: upstream's own 1-org stage also never passes enable_cmek/encryption_key (defaults false/null), so the CMEK half is an unexercised component-parity gap (though a silent footgun for library consumers), while the missing name suffix does affect default deployments (recreate/collision risk).

**Verifier note / correction:** One detail is slightly off in the claim: TF's random_suffix variable (default true) only gates the SCC source display name; the random hex suffix on resource names (AR repo, bucket, feed, topic) is unconditional in TF. This makes the naming-parity gap marginally stronger than claimed, not weaker. Additionally, note the ports' stage code never passes an encryption key (matching upstream stage defaults), so the CMEK gap only affects library consumers who set EncryptionKey/encryptionKey directly.

#### [BOTH] CAI monitoring roles_to_monitor default diverges from upstream (network roles dropped, tokenCreator added, not configurable)
_Area: 1-org/cai-monitoring · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform's roles_to_monitor default is [owner, editor, resourcemanager.organizationAdmin, compute.networkAdmin, compute.orgFirewallPolicyAdmin] and is a configurable variable. Both ports hardcode [owner, editor, resourcemanager.organizationAdmin, iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator] with no config option, so grants of compute.networkAdmin and compute.orgFirewallPolicyAdmin no longer raise SCC findings (both library defaults match upstream but are overridden by the stage code).

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/modules/cai-monitoring/variables.tf:56-66. Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/cai_monitoring.go:29-34. TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:468-473. Library defaults (correct but bypassed): /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/security/cai_monitoring.go:48-54; /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/cai-monitoring/src/index.ts:37-43.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified end-to-end. Upstream: roles_to_monitor defaults to [owner, editor, resourcemanager.organizationAdmin, compute.networkAdmin, compute.orgFirewallPolicyAdmin] (1-org/modules/cai-monitoring/variables.tf:56-66) and is wired into the Cloud Function's ROLES env var (main.tf:165); the 1-org/envs/shared invocation does not override it, so those 5 roles are the effective monitored set. Both ports pass a hardcoded divergent list [owner, editor, resourcemanager.organizationAdmin, iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator] (Go: 1-org/cai_monitoring.go:29-34,72; TS: 1-org/index.ts:468-473), and the Go port's comment falsely claims it matches upstream. The pulumi-library components in both languages carry the correct upstream default (go/pkg/security/cai_monitoring.go:48-54; ts/packages/cai-monitoring/src/index.ts:37-43) but only apply it when no list is passed (go lines 152-154), so the stage override wins and lands in the function's ROLES env var (go lines 316, 338). Exhaustive greps of both port repos for rolesToMonitor/roles_to_monitor variants and for networkAdmin/orgFirewallPolicyAdmin/serviceAccountTokenCreator found no config-driven path or alternative implementation preserving the upstream set. Net effect: grants of compute.networkAdmin and compute.orgFirewallPolicyAdmin no longer raise SCC findings in either port, and serviceAccountTokenCreator grants raise findings upstream never would.

**Verifier note / correction:** The core divergence is real, but the "not configurable" sub-claim is overstated: upstream's 1-org/envs/shared stage also does not expose roles_to_monitor as a stage-level variable (it is only a module variable), and the Pulumi library components equally expose RolesToMonitor as a component arg — configurability is at parity. The actual defect is solely that both ports' stage code overrides the correct library default with a divergent hardcoded list. Claimed severity "minor" is appropriate.

#### [GO] Divergent defaults: enable_scc_resources defaults to true and log_export_storage_versioning defaults to true
_Area: 1-org/config · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform defaults enable_scc_resources_in_terraform=false (SCC must be manually activated first — deploying SCC notification/CAI resources against an un-activated SCC fails) and log_export_storage_versioning=false. The Go port inverts both: enable_scc_resources defaults true (`!= "false"`), so a default deployment attempts SCC + CAI resources and can fail on orgs without SCC activated; log_export_storage_versioning also defaults true. The TS port matches TF (both false).

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/variables.tf:23-27 (default false), 81-85 (default false). Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/main.go:349 (EnableSCCResources: != "false") and main.go:372 (LogExportStorageVersioning: != "false"). TS matching TF: config.ts:98,107.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed. Terraform defaults both flags to false (variables.tf:23-27 enable_scc_resources_in_terraform=false, gating scc_notification.tf, cai_monitoring.tf, iam.tf:154/183, sa.tf:19; variables.tf:81-85 log_export_storage_versioning=false, consumed at log_sinks.tf:61). The Go port inverts both: main.go:349 uses conf.Get("enable_scc_resources") != "false" and main.go:372 uses the same pattern for versioning — conf.Get returns "" when unset, so both effectively default to true. No Pulumi.yaml or Pulumi.<stack>.yaml in the Go port sets these keys, and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) has zero references to either flag, so nothing overrides the inverted default. Consequences are real: main.go:89-102 unconditionally runs deploySCCNotification (scc.go:49 creates a securitycenter V2OrganizationNotificationConfig, which fails on orgs without SCC activated) and deployCAIMonitoring when the flag is true; logging.go:87 applies versioning to the log export bucket. The TS port matches TF (config.ts:98,107 use ?? false). Claimed severity 'minor' is fair, if anything slightly understated for the SCC half since a default deploy can hard-fail on orgs without SCC activation, though the failure is loud and trivially configurable away.

#### [TS] Smaller default/behavior divergences: US location fallbacks, enforce_allowed_worker_pools default true, deterministic global bucket name
_Area: 1-org/config · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

(b) enforce_allowed_worker_pools defaults true (TF default false) — harmless only while no worker pool id is set. (c) The log export bucket name is the deterministic `${projectPrefix}-c-logging-bucket` with no random suffix (TF: bkt-<random-suffixed-project-id>-org-logs-<rand>), a globally-shared GCS namespace collision risk that can fail bucket creation. TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:500 (?? "US"), 342 (bucket name), config.ts:101 (?? true); /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts:139 (?? "US").

(a) TF coalesces log_export_storage_location and billing_export_dataset_location to local.default_region (log_sinks.tf:56,96); the TS port instead falls back to "US" (index.ts:500 for the billing dataset; storage location passed unset to pulumi-library/ts centralized-logging which applies ?? "US" at src/index.ts:139-140) even though cfg.defaultRegion exists in config.ts:84 and is used elsewhere (index.ts:465). (b) TF defaults enforce_allowed_worker_pools to false (variables.tf:57-61); TS defaults it to true (config.ts:101). Both gate the org policy on a worker pool id also being set (TF org_policy.tf:138; TS index.ts:117), so it only changes behavior once cloud_build_private_worker_pool_id is configured — matching the claim's caveat. (c) TF names the log export bucket bkt-<random-suffixed-project-id>-org-logs-<random> (log_sinks.tf:55); TS uses the fully deterministic `${projectPrefix}-c-logging-bucket` (index.ts:342) with no suffix anywhere in the port or the TS library (library uses the name verbatim). The Go port confirms the intended pattern: main.go:439-444 falls back both locations to DefaultRegion, and the Go library auto-generates bkt-logs-<projId>-<rand4> when no name is given (pulumi-library/go/pkg/logging/centralized_logging.go:319-326). No alternative implementation refutes any part. Severity "minor" is fair overall, though (c) is a real deploy-time failure mode: with the default prefix the bucket name is identical across all deployments, so any second organization deploying the TS port with defaults gets a guaranteed global GCS name collision.

#### [GO] Essential Contacts resource names assigned from nondeterministic map iteration — churn across updates
_Area: 1-org/essential_contacts · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** deployEssentialContacts builds contactMap (a Go map) and names resources essential-contact-0..N while ranging over the map; Go map iteration order is randomized per process, so the email-to-index assignment can change on every pulumi up, causing contacts to be deleted/recreated (transient loss of notification routing and noisy diffs). Terraform keys the for_each by email (stable); the TS port uses insertion-ordered Object.entries (stable).

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/essential_contacts.tf:40-46 (for_each = local.contacts_list keyed by email). Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/essential_contacts.go:108-124 (idx++ over map range). TS stable: essential_contacts.ts:73-82.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed. TF (1-org/envs/shared/essential_contacts.tf:40-46) keys google_essential_contacts_contact with for_each over transpose(categories_map), i.e. stable per-email resource addresses. The Go port (/home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/essential_contacts.go:108-124) names resources essential-contact-<idx> with idx++ while ranging over contactMap, a plain Go map[string][]string, with no key sorting anywhere in the file. Go randomizes map iteration order per process and each pulumi up is a new process, so email-to-URN assignment is nondeterministic; with 4+ emails always present (org_admins, billing_admins, billing_data_users, audit_data_users), reassignment across runs is near-certain. Because email/parent are force-new on essentialcontacts.Contact, swapped assignments cause delete/recreate churn, not in-place updates. Refutation attempts failed: the function is live (called from 1-org/main.go:116); /home/user/pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) contains no essential-contacts component at all (zero grep hits); no aliases or ignoreChanges mitigate it. The TS port (essential_contacts.ts:73-82) iterates Object.entries(contactMap) whose insertion order is fixed by the deterministic addContact call sequence, so it is stable per-run as claimed. Claimed severity "minor" is accurate: state converges, but updates show noisy replacement diffs with transient contact deletion (brief notification-routing gaps, possible duplicate-email apply conflicts).

#### [TS] log_export_storage_retention_policy option not wired (declared but never loaded or passed)
_Area: 1-org/log_sinks · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform supports log_export_storage_retention_policy {is_locked, retention_period_days} on the log export bucket. The TS OrgConfig interface declares logExportStorageRetentionPolicy but loadOrgConfig never reads it from config and index.ts never passes retentionPolicy to CentralizedLogging storageOptions (the component supports it), so bucket retention cannot be configured in the TS port. Go wires it via log_export_storage_retention_days/locked.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/variables.tf:87-94 and log_sinks.tf:57-59. TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/config.ts:50-53 (declared), loadOrgConfig:79-115 (not read), index.ts:341-348 (not passed); component support at /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts:34-37,145-148. Go: main.go:432-437.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified on all points. Terraform declares log_export_storage_retention_policy (variables.tf:87-94, default null) and wires it into the centralized-logging module's storage_options (log_sinks.tf: retention_policy_enabled/is_locked/period_days). In the TS port, config.ts:50-53 declares logExportStorageRetentionPolicy on the OrgConfig interface, but loadOrgConfig never reads any retention config key and never populates the field, and index.ts (~line 341) passes storageOptions without retentionPolicy. The shared library component (pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts:34-37, 145-148) fully supports storageOptions.retentionPolicy but only applies it when the caller passes it. A repo-wide grep of the TS port and pulumi-library/ts for "retention" found no alternative wiring under any name — the only TS-port hits are the dead interface declaration and a README sentence. The Go port does wire it (main.go:432-437 reads log_export_storage_retention_days/locked; logging.go passes RetentionPolicyEnabled/IsLocked/PeriodDays). Claimed severity "minor" is fair: the TF default is null (retention disabled), so default behavior matches; only users explicitly configuring log-bucket retention are affected, silently.

#### [TS] create_unique_tag_key is a no-op (no random suffix on tag key)
_Area: 1-org/tags · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform appends an 8-char random suffix to tag key short names when create_unique_tag_key=true (needed to avoid org-wide shortname collisions in shared/test orgs). The TS port evaluates `cfg.createUniqueTagKey ? "environment" : "environment"` — both branches identical — so the flag does nothing and repeated deployments into the same org collide on the tag key shortname. The Go port implements the suffix via random.RandomString.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/tags.tf:47,50-54 (key_suffix + random_string). TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:406. Go correct: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/tags.go:42-55.

**Verifier reasoning:** The claim is fully verified. Terraform (1-org/envs/shared/tags.tf:20,47,50-54) appends "-" plus an 8-char lowercase random_string to the tag key shortname when create_unique_tag_key=true. The TS port's only 1-org tag key (pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:404-408) uses the literal no-op ternary `cfg.createUniqueTagKey ? "environment" : "environment"`, so the flag (parsed at config.ts:96 and documented in the stage README) has zero effect; @pulumi/random is declared in the stage's package.json but never imported/used, corroborating dropped logic. Exhaustive greps found no alternative implementation: no tags component exists anywhere in pulumi-library (go or ts), the library's random-suffix code covers only projects/buckets/log sinks, and the only other TagKeys in the TS repo (4-projects peering SSH/RDP firewall tags) are unrelated. The Go port implements the suffix correctly at 1-org/tags.go:42-55 as the claim states. Claimed severity "minor" is accurate, not inflated: the flag defaults to false, and the gap only causes a shortname collision failure when redeploying with the flag enabled into an org that already has an "environment" tag key (shared/test orgs).

_Area: 2-environments · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

TS 2-environments/README.md omits required_groups, project_budget, and folder_deletion_protection which envs/*/index.ts read. TF documents all of these in 2-environments/README.md and modules/env_baseline variables. Combined with the stale ERRATA that claims Assured Workloads is not implemented, the feature is completely undiscoverable from the ports' documentation.

TS: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/2-environments/envs/development/index.ts:23-32 vs README.md:168-174. (1) TF side confirmed: terraform-example-foundation/2-environments/README.md has a dedicated '## Assured Workloads' section, and modules/env_baseline/variables.tf + modules/env_baseline/README.md fully document assured_workload_configuration, project_budget, project_deletion_policy, folder_deletion_protection as module inputs. (2) Go port confirmed: 2-environments/main.go:132-209 reads org_id, billing_account, project_prefix, folder_prefix, org_stack_name, project_deletion_policy, default_service_account, folder_deletion_protection, random_suffix, project_budget (object), assured_workload_enabled/location/display_name/compliance_regime/resource_type, and parent_folder — 15 keys total — while README.md's 'Configuration Reference' table (lines 93-98) lists only org_id, billing_account, org_stack_name, project_prefix. The Assured Workloads feature is fully implemented as real infrastructure in env_baseline.go:188-211 via gcp/assuredworkloads.NewWorkload, wired end-to-end from config through to resource creation. (3) TS port confirmed: envs/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts:23-32 read org_id, billing_account, project_prefix, folder_prefix, parent, required_groups, project_deletion_policy, folder_deletion_protection, project_budget — but README.md's config table (lines 168-174) lists only org_id, billing_account, org_stack_name, project_prefix, folder_prefix, default_region, project_deletion_policy — omitting required_groups, project_budget, folder_deletion_protection exactly as claimed (and also 'parent'; it also documents 'default_region' and 'org_stack_name', neither of which index.ts actually reads — the TS doc table is stale/inaccurate in both directions). TS's modules/env_baseline.ts also implements Assured Workloads (gcp.assuredworkloads.Workload) with a full assuredWorkloadConfiguration arg, but none of the three envs/*/index.ts files pass that argument, so in TS the feature is currently unreachable from any entry point, not just undocumented. (4) The stale-ERRATA sub-claim is confirmed: both docs/ERRATA.md files state 'Stage 2 ... does not include Assured Workloads resource definitions ... workload creation must be added manually' — directly contradicted by the working implementation in both ports (Go fully wired; TS implemented in the module but not yet wired to any stack entry point). No hidden documentation elsewhere (per-env READMEs, Pulumi.*.yaml.example files) covers these keys either — they show the same truncated tables and omit the extra config knobs. Checked pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) for any alternate home for this functionality — Assured Workloads is not there; it's implemented directly against the gcp provider SDK in each port's own 2-environments code.

**Verifier note / correction:** Not really a correction so much as an addendum: for the TS port specifically, the Assured Workloads gap goes one step beyond documentation — the module (env_baseline.ts) supports it, but no envs/*/index.ts currently plumbs an assuredWorkloadConfiguration through from config, so today a TS user literally cannot enable it via `pulumi config set`, whereas the Go port can. The claimed 'minor' severity is reasonable for what is largely a documentation-only defect (nothing crashes, defaults are sane, the primary consequence is discoverability), though the TS wiring gap plus the actively-wrong ERRATA text arguably pushes this slightly past a pure cosmetic doc nit — a case could be made for 'moderate' rather than 'minor' — but 'minor' is not an inflated/incorrect severity call and doesn't need to be walked back.

#### [TS] Assured Workload configuration not wired into any environment stack
_Area: 2-environments · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

Also note the TS module's displayName fallback is `${env}-workload` instead of TF's default "FEDRAMP-MODERATE".

**Evidence:** TF: 2-environments/envs/production/main.tf assured_workload_configuration block; modules/env_baseline/variables.tf defaults. TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/2-environments/envs/production/index.ts lines 20-33 (no assuredWorkloadConfiguration arg, no config read); modules/env_baseline.ts lines 119-133. Go parity: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/2-environments/main.go lines 174-180.

**Verifier reasoning:** Every element of the claim verified. TF wires assured_workload_configuration into the production env_baseline module (envs/production/main.tf lines 28-34, enabled=false, display_name "FEDRAMP-MODERATE") and exports assured_workload_id/resources. The TS module (2-environments/modules/env_baseline.ts lines 26, 119-133) fully supports assuredWorkloadConfiguration, but no env program (development/nonproduction/production index.ts) passes it and no Pulumi config key reads it — confirmed by grepping all *.ts and all Pulumi.yaml/Pulumi.*.yaml.example files in the port and both go/ and ts/ of pulumi-library (only an API-enablement string in vpc-service-controls; no component). The production stack also omits the assured_workload_id/resources exports that TF's production outputs.tf has. The Go port does read assured_workload_enabled/location/display_name/compliance_regime/resource_type from config with TF-matching defaults (main.go lines 174-206) and creates the workload (env_baseline.go 188-210), confirming the parity assertion. **Verifier note / correction:** The gap is real and "minor" is the right severity, with two contextual notes: (1) the TS port's docs/ERRATA.md (lines 79-85) already documents Assured Workloads as a known divergence, so this is an acknowledged omission rather than an undocumented oversight — though the ERRATA understates the port, since the module DOES contain the resource definition and only the stack wiring/config plumbing is missing; (2) the practical burden difference vs TF is small — TF users must also edit main.tf (flip enabled=false to true), whereas TS users would add a ~7-line argument object to one call site; the Go port is actually more convenient than TF (pure config, no code edit).

#### [TS] Assured Workloads support unreachable: config never wired and outputs not exported
_Area: 2-environments · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's production env accepts var.assured_workload_configuration (enabled/location/display_name/compliance_regime) and outputs assured_workload_id and assured_workload_resources; Go parses assured_workload_* config keys. The TS env_baseline module implements the Workload resource, but no env entrypoint (production/development/nonproduction) reads any assured_workload config or passes assuredWorkloadConfiguration, and the two outputs are missing from the returned exports — the feature cannot be enabled.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/2-environments/envs/production/main.tf (assured_workload_configuration) and outputs.tf:37,42. Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/2-environments/main.go:173-178. TS checked: 2-environments/envs/production/index.ts:20-41 (no assuredWorkloadConfiguration arg, exports omit assured_workload_*); module support exists at 2-environments/modules/env_baseline.ts:115-133.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

TF side (as cited): envs/production/main.tf hardcodes `assured_workload_configuration = { enabled = false, location = "us-central1", display_name = "FEDRAMP-MODERATE", compliance_regime = "FEDRAMP_MODERATE", resource_type = "CONSUMER_FOLDER" }` passed into module "env" (env_baseline), and envs/production/outputs.tf:37-44 re-exports `assured_workload_id` / `assured_workload_resources` from the module. Note: this is NOT a root Terraform *variable* — there's no `assured_workload_configuration` entry in envs/production/variables.tf; it's a literal block a user must hand-edit in main.tf to enable (per 2-environments/README.md:71: "edit main.tf ... update assured_workload_configuration.enable to true").

TS side: grepped all of 2-environments/envs/{production,development,nonproduction}/index.ts and the shared pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) for "assured"/"Workload". The only TS hits are in modules/env_baseline.ts (the Workload resource implementation, optional param `assuredWorkloadConfiguration?`, and outputs `assuredWorkloadId?`/`assuredWorkloadResources?`) — exactly as the reviewer said. None of the three env index.ts files reference "assured" at all: they never pass `assuredWorkloadConfiguration` into `deployEnvBaseline(...)`, and production/index.ts's return block only exports `env_folder`, `env_kms_project_id`, `env_kms_project_number`, `env_secrets_project_id` — the two assured_workload outputs are indeed absent. docs/ERRATA.md even documents this as an intentional omission ("Stage 2 ... does not include Assured Workloads resource definitions"), though that note is itself stale since env_baseline.ts actually does implement the resource — it's just unreachable from any entrypoint. pulumi-library has no relevant equivalent (only unrelated `assuredworkloads.googleapis.com` API-enablement string in vpc_sc). So the claim is accurate: the feature is implemented in the module but wired to nothing in TS, making it unusable via any entrypoint.

On severity: "minor" is the right call, if anything slightly generous toward the reviewer being fair rather than inflating. This is an opt-in, disabled-by-default (enabled=false) compliance feature for FedRAMP-regulated customers only; upstream itself doesn't expose it as a discoverable tfvar — enabling it upstream already requires hand-editing main.tf. Nothing else in the reference repo consumes assured_workload_id/resources downstream (grep across the whole TF repo outside 2-environments found zero references), so the missing exports are purely informational with no consumers. The fix is small (add the optional config block + call param + two export lines to production/index.ts, mirroring what Go's main.go does for the config-plumbing half, though notably even Go's main.go never calls ctx.Export for these two outputs either — so TS is not uniquely worse on the output-export front, only on the entrypoint-wiring front). Severity is appropriately minor, not inflated.

#### [TS] Required 'parent' config missing from example stack configs; bootstrap common_config fetched but unused
_Area: 2-environments · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF derives the environment folder parent from bootstrap remote state (common_config.parent_id) with no user input. The TS stacks require config.require("parent"), but the shipped Pulumi.<env>.yaml.example files list only org_id, billing_account, remote_state_bucket (which the program never reads) and project_prefix — so following the example yields an immediate missing-required-config failure, and the commonConfig/requiredGroups outputs fetched from the bootstrap stack reference are dead code instead of being used to derive parent.

**Evidence:** TF: 2-environments/modules/env_baseline/remote.tf (parent from bootstrap common_config). TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/2-environments/envs/production/index.ts lines 16-17 (unused commonConfig/requiredGroups), line 27 (config.require("parent")); /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/2-environments/envs/production/Pulumi.production.yaml.example (no parent key).

**Verifier reasoning:** Every element of the claim checks out. TF derives parent from bootstrap remote state (terraform-example-foundation/2-environments/modules/env_baseline/remote.tf:19, common_config.parent_id) with no user input. The TS port hard-requires config.require("parent") in all three env programs (2-environments/envs/*/index.ts:27), while the shipped Pulumi.<env>.yaml.example files contain only org_id, billing_account, remote_state_bucket (never read by any code — the programs use StackReferences), and a commented project_prefix — no parent key. The stage README's own stack-init instructions also omit parent, so following either shipped guide fails immediately with a missing-required-config error. The commonConfig and required_groups outputs fetched from the bootstrap StackReference (index.ts:16-17) are dead code — requiredGroups is re-read from config at line 29. The port even proves the intended pattern elsewhere: 1-org/index.ts:35 does `commonConfig.apply(c => c.parent_id || cfg.parent)`, exactly the TF-equivalent derivation that 2-environments omits. Exhaustive search of the TS repo (scripts, helpers, tests, docs) and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) found no code that derives or auto-populates parent for this stage; the only mitigation is docs/ENVIRONMENTS.md:55,65 which documents `pulumi config set parent`. Claimed severity of minor is accurate: no infrastructure is missing, and manual config restores parity, but the out-of-box example flow is broken and TF's automatic derivation (which prevents placing env folders under the wrong parent) is absent.

#### [TS] Stack references to bootstrap/org hardcoded, not configurable
_Area: 2-environments · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** All three TS env stacks call new pulumi.StackReference("bootstrap") and new pulumi.StackReference("org") with no config override. The org reference is load-bearing (tag values for the folder tag binding come from it). On Pulumi Cloud or project-scoped backends, a bare name "org" resolves within the current project (2-environments-*) and will not find the 1-org stack, failing the deployment until the source is edited. The port's own 1-org stage exposes bootstrap_stack_ref config for exactly this, and the Go 2-environments stage requires an org_stack_name config value — so this is TS-internal and Go/TS drift.

**Evidence:** TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/2-environments/envs/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts lines 13-14. Contrast: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts line 25 (bootstrap_stack_ref config) and /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/2-environments/main.go lines 40-42 with config key org_stack_name.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all points. (1) Upstream TF makes remote state configurable: remote_state_bucket is a required variable in all three env dirs (terraform-example-foundation/2-environments/envs/*/variables.tf:17) consumed by modules/env_baseline/remote.tf to locate bootstrap and org state. (2) All three TS env stacks hardcode new pulumi.StackReference("bootstrap") and new pulumi.StackReference("org") (pulumi_ts-example-foundation/2-environments/envs/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts:13-14); a repo-wide grep finds no code reading org_stack_name/bootstrap_stack_name anywhere in the TS port, and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) contains zero StackReference occurrences, so no equivalent exists under any name. (3) The org ref is load-bearing: its tags output drives gcp.tags.TagBinding in 2-environments/modules/env_baseline.ts:108-111. Project names diverge (2-environments-development vs 1-org per Pulumi.yaml), so bare names fail to resolve on Pulumi Cloud/project-scoped backends. (4) Drift confirmed: 1-org/index.ts:25 reads bootstrap_stack_ref config, and the Go port requires org_stack_name (pulumi_go-example-foundation/2-environments/main.go:136, used at lines 40-42). Aggravating detail the claim missed: the TS port's own docs instruct setting these keys — 2-environments/README.md tells users to `pulumi config set org_stack_name ...`, per-env READMEs document a required bootstrap_stack_name, and Pulumi.development.yaml.example sets remote_state_bucket — yet no code reads any of them, so users following the README set config that is silently ignored. Claimed severity "minor" is appropriate: deployment fails until a two-line source edit per stack, but nothing is misprovisioned.

#### [GO] assured_workload_id and assured_workload_resources computed but never exported
_Area: 2-environments · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** deployEnvBaseline populates outputs.AssuredWorkloadID and outputs.AssuredWorkloadResources when assured_workload_enabled is set, but main.go only exports env_folder, kms project id/number, and secrets project id per environment. TF's production env exports assured_workload_id and assured_workload_resources, so downstream consumers/operators lose those identifiers in the Go port.

**Evidence:** TF: 2-environments/envs/production/outputs.tf:37-46 and modules/env_baseline/outputs.tf:37-46. Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/2-environments/env_baseline.go:207-210 (values computed) vs main.go:63-66 (only 4 exports per env).

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed: 2-environments/modules/env_baseline/outputs.tf:37-46 defines assured_workload_id and assured_workload_resources (sourced from google_assured_workloads_workload.workload[0]), and envs/production/outputs.tf:37-46 re-exports both at the root module level. Neither output is consumed downstream anywhere else in the TF repo (grep across the whole repo shows only the two outputs.tf definitions) — they exist purely for operator/CLI visibility (`terraform output`).

Go port confirmed: env_baseline.go computes both values into the EnvBaselineOutputs struct (AssuredWorkloadID at line 207, AssuredWorkloadResources at line 210) guarded by `if cfg.AssuredWorkload.Enabled`, but main.go's export loop (lines 63-66) only calls ctx.Export for env_folder, env_kms_project_id, env_kms_project_number, and env_secrets_project_id — assured workload fields are never passed to ctx.Export anywhere in the file or elsewhere in the Go port (verified via repo-wide grep). No alternate name/spelling is used. This is a genuine, verifiable gap exactly as described.

Note for context (not a refutation): the TS port has the identical gap — modules/env_baseline.ts computes and returns assuredWorkloadId/assuredWorkloadResources from the module, but envs/production/index.ts's returned export object only includes env_folder/env_kms_project_id/env_kms_project_number/env_secrets_project_id, omitting the assured workload fields too. This shows the omission is a consistent pattern across both ports rather than a Go-specific slip, but since the claim is scoped to 'go' only, this doesn't refute it.

Severity: 'minor' is accurate and not inflated. Since nothing downstream in TF itself consumes these two outputs (confirmed via grep — they're never referenced by any other module/stage), this is a pure informational/CLI-visibility loss for operators who want to look up the Assured Workload resource ID via `pulumi stack output`, not a functional/wiring break. 'Minor' correctly reflects that.

_Area: 3-networks · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_
