READMEs checked: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/README.md:107-117 and pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/README.md:146-152 (no mention of the .example files). TF baseline: terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/{dedicated_interconnect,partner_interconnect,vpn-ha}/README.md.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF baseline confirmed exactly as described: terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/{dedicated_interconnect,partner_interconnect,vpn-ha}/README.md each give detailed setup steps, and 3-networks-svpc/README.md has "Using Dedicated Interconnect" (lines 92-101), "Using Partner Interconnect" (103-113), and "OPTIONAL - Using High Availability VPN" (115-138) sections that explicitly instruct renaming interconnect.tf.example/partner_interconnect.tf.example/vpn.tf.example to activate them.

Both ports do ship equivalent ready-made code: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/{interconnect,partner_interconnect,vpn}.go.example and pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-{svpc,hub-and-spoke}/modules/{interconnect,partner_interconnect,vpn}.ts.example all exist and were verified on disk.

Confirmed the READMEs as cited: Go 3-networks-svpc/README.md lines 107-117 say only "extend the main.go" / link to generic GCP docs, with zero mention of interconnect.go.example, partner_interconnect.go.example, or vpn.go.example. The Go Partner Interconnect section (111-113) indeed has no activation steps at all — just descriptive text and a generic link. TS 3-networks-svpc/README.md (146-152) is the same pattern ("extend the shared environment deployment"); it doesn't even have a "Using Partner Interconnect" header, only Dedicated Interconnect and HA VPN. The hub-and-spoke READMEs in both languages don't mention interconnect/VPN/on-prem connectivity at all.

I looked hard for a mitigating equivalent elsewhere and found one partial one: both ports ship 0-bootstrap/onprem.md, which does give concrete "rename vpn.ts.example → vpn.ts / vpn.go.example → vpn.go" and "rename interconnect.ts.example → interconnect.ts (or .go)" instructions with PSK secret setup steps mirroring the TF flow. However, this doesn't refute the claim: onprem.md is an orphan document — grepped for and confirmed it is not linked from the top-level README, 0-bootstrap/README.md, or any 3-networks-* README in either port — so an operator following the normal stage-by-stage path would never discover it. It also never once mentions partner_interconnect.go.example/partner_interconnect.ts.example by filename anywhere in either repo (confirmed via repo-wide grep), so the Partner Interconnect gap is total and unmitigated everywhere.

Net: the claim is accurate. The shipped .example implementations for Interconnect/VPN exist but the canonical stage READMEs operators would consult (3-networks-svpc, 3-networks-hub-and-spoke, in both Go and TS) never point to them, instead directing hand-written extension of main.go/shared deployment against generic GCP docs — same as described. The claimed "minor" severity is fair, not inflated (it's a documentation/discoverability issue, not a functional gap, since the working code does ship); if anything the Partner Interconnect sub-case is slightly worse than "minor" in isolation since it has zero documented activation path anywhere in the repo, but that's consistent with what the reviewer already called out.

**Verifier note / correction:** Minor nuance only: it is not literally true that the examples are "never referenced" anywhere in the repos — 0-bootstrap/onprem.md in both ports does name-check vpn.*.example and (dedicated) interconnect.*.example with concrete rename/activate steps. But that file is an unlinked orphan (not referenced by any other README) and still never mentions the partner_interconnect.*.example files at all, so it doesn't meaningfully change the practical impact the reviewer describes for the cited 3-networks stage READMEs.

#### [GO] Cross-stage values (SVPC host project IDs, DNS hub project) must be hand-copied into config; DNS peering breaks if dns_project_id unset
_Area: 3-networks · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** 3-networks-svpc requires project_id via conf.Require and reads dns_project_id via conf.Get with no default, instead of consuming 1-org's exported shared_vpc_projects/<env>_network_project_id outputs. Because project IDs carry a random suffix (random_suffix default true), the operator must look up generated IDs and copy them into each network stack's config; if dns_project_id is omitted, dev/nonprod DNS peering targets the malformed 'projects//global/networks/vpc-p-svpc' and deployment fails. TF wires these automatically from remote state; the TS port resolves the host project from the org StackReference (shared_vpc_projects output).

**Evidence:** Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go:354 (conf.Require("project_id")), :360 (dns_project_id no default), :203 (peering target uses cfg.DNSProjectID). TF: 3-networks-svpc/envs/*/remote.tf. TS: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/envs/development/index.ts:14-15.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified end-to-end. TF: 3-networks-svpc/modules/base_env/remote.tf resolves both shared_vpc_project_id and dns_project_id automatically from data.terraform_remote_state.org.outputs.shared_vpc_projects[var.env] (and [\"production\"] for the DNS hub), so no hand-copying occurs in TF. Go port: main.go:354 uses conf.Require(\"project_id\") and :360 uses conf.Get(\"dns_project_id\") with no default anywhere in loadNetConfig (every other optional field — region1/2, domain, org_stack_name, psc_ip, etc. — gets a hardcoded fallback, but DNSProjectID does not). Line 203 confirms the vulnerable interpolation: fmt.Sprintf(\"projects/%s/global/networks/vpc-p-svpc\", cfg.DNSProjectID), which yields \"projects//global/networks/vpc-p-svpc\" if unset. The only StackReference in the file (org, line 246) is used exclusively for access_context_manager_policy_id, never for project IDs. Crucially, this is a real, avoidable gap, not a structural necessity: 1-org/main.go in the same Go repo already exports both shared_vpc_projects (map) and per-env <env>_network_project_id outputs, and 4-projects/main.go (same repo) already consumes orgStack.GetStringOutput(\"%s_network_project_id\") automatically via StackReference — proving both the data and the established pattern exist in this codebase but were simply not applied to 3-networks-svpc. The TS port confirms the pattern works: envs/development/index.ts:13-15 pulls envProjectId from orgRef.getOutput(\"shared_vpc_projects\") automatically. Further corroboration: the Go port's own README.md documents the manual workaround (`pulumi config set project_id \"prj-d-svpc\" # from Stage 1 output`) and its Configuration Reference table omits dns_project_id entirely, and helpers/foundation-deployer/deploy.sh has no logic to auto-populate project_id/dns_project_id for this stage — so the failure mode described (malformed peering target when dns_project_id is left unset, which the docs don't even mention) is plausible in practice. No equivalent auto-wiring exists in pulumi-library (go/ or ts/) that the port could be silently relying on. The claimed severity of \"minor\" appears reasonably calibrated (not inflated) — it's a one-time setup/documentation gap that fails loudly at apply time via a GCP API rejection rather than silently corrupting infrastructure, though if anything it is slightly underrated given dns_project_id isn't even documented as a required key.

#### [TS] Hierarchical firewall rule set diverges: org-level deny-all-ingress added, TF's monitoring goto_next / windows-activation rules dropped
_Area: 3-networks (both topologies) · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's hierarchical policy contains goto_next delegation rules for monitoring ranges (500/510), allow IAP SSH/RDP (5000), allow windows KMS activation 35.190.247.13/32 (5100), and allow LB/health-check ranges (5200) — no hierarchical deny. TS replaces this with allow-RFC1918/health-check/IAP rules plus a deny-all-ingress at priority 65534. Because hierarchical denies override VPC-level allows, the TS deny-all can block traffic that TF permits via per-VPC rules, and Windows activation egress is not allowed at any level unless the per-VPC option is enabled.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/envs/shared/hierarchical_firewall.tf:30-100. TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/envs/shared/index.ts:22-61 and 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/shared/index.ts:63-102 (deny at 65534, no goto_next/windows rules).

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed exactly as cited: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/envs/shared/hierarchical_firewall.tf (lines 30-106) defines delegate-rfc1918-ingress/egress as goto_next (priority 500/510), allow-iap-ssh-rdp (5000), allow-windows-activation EGRESS to 35.190.247.13/32:1688 (5100), and allow-google-hbs-and-hcs (5200) — with no deny rule anywhere in the module, so unmatched traffic implicitly falls through to lower-level (VPC) policy. The module (modules/hierarchical_firewall_policy/main.tf) confirms no implicit deny is injected.

TS side confirmed as cited: both 3-networks-svpc/envs/shared/index.ts (lines 22-61) and 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/shared/index.ts (63-102) use a HierarchicalFirewallPolicy with allow rules for RFC1918 (100), health-check (200), IAP (300), and an explicit deny-all-ingress at 65534 — no goto_next action used anywhere, no windows-activation rule.

I checked every place equivalent functionality could hide:
- pulumi-library/ts/packages/hierarchical-firewall-policy/src/index.ts: the shared TS component DOES support "goto_next" as a typed action (so it's not a library limitation) — the two callers simply choose not to emit those rules or the windows-activation rule. This is a caller-code divergence, not a missing capability.
- pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/hierarchical_firewall.go + pulumi_go-example-foundation's 3-networks-svpc/hub-and-spoke main.go: the Go port faithfully reproduces TF's exact ruleset (goto_next 500/510, allow-IAP 5000, allow-windows-activation EGRESS 5100, allow-HBS/HCS 5200, no deny). This confirms the omission is TS-specific, not a limitation shared by the whole project.
- A related but separate mechanism exists: TF's `windows_activation_enabled` var (default false) only ever adds a *route* (not a firewall allow) in shared_vpc modules on both TF and TS sides — identical behavior, not a gap. Separately, TF's 4-projects/modules/base_env/example_peering_project.tf adds a firewall EGRESS allow for 35.190.247.13:1688 gated on windows_activation_enabled for peered projects; TS mirrors this exactly in 4-projects/business_unit_*/*/index.ts (`fw-*-peering-base-0-e-a-allow-win-activation-all-tcp-1688`). So the windows-activation allow is NOT "unimplemented everywhere" — it works for the peering-project pattern in TS exactly as in TF. Only the org-wide, always-on hierarchical-level allow (which TF relies on to cover VMs sitting directly on shared/hub VPCs, not just peered projects) is genuinely missing from TS.

Additional context that bears on severity (not part of the cited evidence, but directly relevant): TF's hierarchical policy is associated to six folders (common, network, bootstrap, development, production, nonproduction — the last three pulled via remote state from 2-environments), giving it foundation-wide reach. TS's policy (`targetFolders: [networkFolderName]`) is associated only to the network folder — it never references the 2-environments stack outputs (env_folder) for dev/nonprod/prod, even though those outputs exist and TF-equivalent data is available. So in TS, the whole divergent ruleset (goto_next, deny-all, missing windows rule alike) only actually governs the network folder's own projects, not the workload folders — which already carry equivalent IAP/health-check/windows-activation rules directly at the project/VPC-firewall level (4-projects peering rules). That materially narrows the real-world blast radius of the cited divergence versus what the claim's "org-level deny-all can block traffic TF permits" framing implies.

**Verifier note / correction:** The core factual claim is correct: TS's two 3-networks envs/shared/index.ts files do drop TF's goto_next/windows-activation hierarchical rules and add an org-level deny-all-ingress (65534) instead, and this is TS-specific (the Go port matches TF exactly, and the shared TS library component even supports goto_next, so it's a caller choice, not a capability gap). However, treat "severity: minor" as about right, not inflated, for two reasons the original evidence doesn't mention: (1) the goto_next omission by itself has no behavioral effect — TF's own module has no implicit deny, so goto_next is functionally a no-op restated explicitly, and TS's absence of it changes nothing by itself; the only rules with real teeth are the added deny-all and the missing windows-activation EGRESS allow. (2) TS's hierarchical policy is associated only with the "network" folder (not also common/bootstrap/development/production/nonproduction as in TF), so its reach is far narrower than TF's — it doesn't govern the workload folders at all, and those already get equivalent IAP/health-check/windows-activation-gated firewall rules provisioned directly at the project level in 4-projects. So the practical risk is confined to the network folder's own (typically non-workload) projects rather than being an org-wide regression.

#### [TS] Proxy-only subnets and GKE secondary ranges missing from env VPCs
_Area: 3-networks (both topologies) · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF env stacks create REGIONAL_MANAGED_PROXY proxy-only subnets (subnet_proxy_ranges) and GKE pod/service secondary ranges (subnet_secondary_ranges) per region; the Go port creates both (Role/Purpose subnets, SecondaryRanges). TS env stacks pass only two primary subnets with no secondaryRanges and no proxy subnets, even though the Network component supports role/purpose/secondaryRanges — internal L7 load balancing and GKE alias-IP setups have no addressing.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/envs/production/main.tf:22-63 and hub-and-spoke envs/shared/net-hubs.tf proxy subnet blocks. Go: 3-networks-svpc/main.go:84-120, hub-and-spoke main.go:234-269. TS: 3-networks-svpc/envs/production/index.ts:32-49 and hub-and-spoke envs (2 primary subnets only).

**Verifier reasoning:** TF confirmed: 3-networks-svpc/envs/{production,nonproduction,development}/main.tf and 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/{shared,production,nonproduction,development} all define subnet_proxy_ranges (wired to the base_env module's google_compute_subnetwork with purpose=REGIONAL_MANAGED_PROXY, role=ACTIVE) and subnet_secondary_ranges (GKE pod/svc CIDRs). Go port mirrors this: 3-networks-svpc/main.go and 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go both set SecondaryRanges and Role/Purpose=REGIONAL_MANAGED_PROXY blocks for every env. TS port: checked all 7 env index.ts files (svpc: development/nonproduction/production; hub-and-spoke: shared/development/nonproduction/production) — every single one passes exactly two primary subnets with no `secondaryRanges` and no proxy-purpose subnet entries. This is not a naming/alternative-implementation issue: the shared TS Network component (pulumi-library/ts/packages/network/src/index.ts) explicitly defines `role`, `purpose`, and `secondaryRanges` on SubnetConfig and correctly threads them through to gcp.compute.Subnetwork (purpose:, role:, secondaryIpRanges:), and SharedVpc (3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc.ts) exposes a top-level `secondaryRanges` arg that is never populated by any env caller. So the capability exists and works correctly in the shared library but is simply never invoked by any TS env stack — a straightforward, consistent omission confirmed identically in both topologies.

**Verifier note / correction:** No correction to the substance is needed, only a note on severity: I verified that neither the TF reference, the Go port, nor the TS port's 5-app-infra stage actually consumes these secondary-range names or proxy subnets downstream (no ip_range_pods/ip_range_services/gke-pod/gke-svc/proxy references anywhere under 5-app-infra in any of the three repos). The upstream TF module's own inline comments call these "...proxy-only subnet example" — i.e. reserved example addressing for future GKE alias-IP / internal L7 LB use, not wired into any consuming resource within this repo's stages. So the claimed "minor" severity is accurate, not inflated: it is a genuine infrastructure-parity gap (real GCP subnet resources will be absent from TS deployments) but it does not break any existing wiring or downstream stage today.

#### [TS] VPC-SC ingress/egress policies (and dry-run variants) not wired from config in any env stack
_Area: 3-networks (both topologies) · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF base_env exposes ingress_policies/egress_policies variables fed into the perimeter, and the Go port loads vpc_sc_ingress/egress policy config into VpcServiceControlsArgs. The TS vpc-service-controls library supports ingressPolicies/egressPolicies(+DryRun), but no TS env stack passes them and there is no config key to set them — perimeters can only be all-or-nothing.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/shared/net-hubs.tf (egress_policies/ingress_policies) and base_env variables. Go: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go:385-388 + loadNetConfig. TS checked: 3-networks-svpc/envs/production/index.ts:64-72 and hub-and-spoke envs — VpcServiceControls called without ingress/egress args; no vpc_sc_*_policies config parsing anywhere in the TS port.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all three points. (1) TF side is real: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/{shared,development,nonproduction,production}/variables.tf and main.tf define egress_policies/ingress_policies (+ _dry_run variants) and wire them through base_env -> shared_vpc -> service_control.tf into the perimeter; envs/shared/net-hubs.tf even concats a hardcoded dedicated-interconnect egress policy with var.egress_policies. Same pattern exists in 3-networks-svpc. (2) TS port: grepped every env stack (3-networks-hub-and-spoke and 3-networks-svpc, all of development/nonproduction/production/shared) plus the whole repo for ingressPolic/egressPolic/vpc_sc_ingress/vpc_sc_egress — zero hits outside the library's own type definitions. Every env's VpcServiceControls(...) call only sets policyId/prefix/members/membersDryRun/projectNumbers/restrictedServices/enforce; the only vpc_sc-prefixed config keys read anywhere are vpc_sc_members, vpc_sc_project_numbers, and enforce_vpc_sc. The shared library (pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpc-service-controls/src/index.ts) does expose optional ingressPolicies/egressPolicies/ingressPoliciesDryRun/egressPoliciesDryRun args and threads them into the ServicePerimeter status/spec, confirming the capability exists in the component but is simply never invoked or exposed via config in the TS ports. This matches the claim exactly.

One correction to the claim's framing: the Go port is not actually the clean counterexample the evidence implies. Go's NetConfig struct does carry VpcScIngressPolicies/VpcScEgressPolicies(+DryRun) fields that get passed into VpcServiceControlsArgs (main.go:385-388 in both 3-networks-hub-and-spoke and 3-networks-svpc), but loadNetConfig() never populates them from any config key (no conf.GetObject('vpc_sc_ingress_policies', ...) or equivalent exists anywhere in the Go port, and the .yaml.example files don't document such a key either). So in practice the Go port's fields are always nil/empty too — it has inert plumbing, not working config wiring. The real difference is that TS lacks even the pass-through call site, whereas Go has an unused pass-through; neither port lets an operator actually set these values via Pulumi config today. This doesn't change the verdict for TS specifically, since the described end-state (no way to configure ingress/egress policies in the TS port) is accurate.

Severity: 'minor' is fair, and if anything the TF defaults reinforce that — egress_policies/ingress_policies default to [] in the reference and are described as advanced VPC-SC exception carve-outs (e.g., for dedicated/partner interconnect scenarios), which are themselves only offered as opt-in .ts.example modules in the TS port (mirroring TF's enable_dedicated_interconnect defaulting to false and Go's own .go.example files). So this gap only bites the subset of users doing advanced perimeter carve-outs, not baseline deployments — 'minor' is not inflated.

**Verifier note / correction:** Real gap for TS, but the claim's implicit comparison ('Go loads it correctly, TS doesn't') is not quite right: Go also fails to wire ingress/egress policies from any config key (loadNetConfig never populates VpcScIngressPolicies/VpcScEgressPolicies) — it has unused struct fields passed into the perimeter call but no way to set them either. TS just lacks even that pass-through. Net effect for TS operators is as described (all-or-nothing perimeters, no config key), and 'minor' severity is appropriate given TF's own defaults are empty lists for what is an advanced/optional carve-out feature.

#### [GO] parent_id falls back to placeholder 'organizations/123456789012'
_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** loadNetConfig defaults ParentID to the placeholder literal "organizations/123456789012" when parent_id is not configured, and FirewallAssociations falls back to that value; deploying the shared (hub) stack without parent_id would attempt to associate the hierarchical firewall policy with a bogus organization instead of failing fast with a clear config error (the svpc variant correctly uses conf.Require("parent_id")).

**Evidence:** Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go:525-527 (placeholder default) and 531-533 (association fallback) vs 3-networks-svpc/main.go:357 (conf.Require("parent_id")).

**Verifier reasoning:** The claim is confirmed by direct inspection, not refuted.

TF reference: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/shared/remote.tf defines `local.parent_id` from the bootstrap remote-state output `common_config.parent_id` (no hardcoded fallback anywhere in TF for this value). The hierarchical firewall policy module itself takes `parent`/`associations` as explicit required module inputs (envs/shared/hierarchical_firewall.tf passes `local.common_folder_name` and folder locals), so there is no placeholder-default pattern in the reference implementation — any missing upstream value would surface as a Terraform plan-time error.

Go port: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go confirms the exact lines cited:
- Line 480: `ParentID: conf.Get("parent_id")` (optional, not Require)
- Lines 525-527: `if c.ParentID == "" { c.ParentID = "organizations/123456789012" }`
- Lines 531-533: `if len(c.FirewallAssociations) == 0 { c.FirewallAssociations = []string{c.ParentID} }`

This ParentID is then fed directly as the `Parent` argument of the real `compute.FirewallPolicy` resource inside pulumi-library's `networking.NewHierarchicalFirewallPolicy` (/home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/hierarchical_firewall.go, line 53), and FirewallAssociations becomes the attachment-target list for `compute.FirewallPolicyAssociation` resources (same file, line 62-67). This code path only executes when `cfg.Env == "shared"` (the hub stack), matching the claim.

Contrast confirmed: 3-networks-svpc/main.go line 357 does `ParentID: conf.Require("parent_id")` — no placeholder fallback — so the inconsistency between the two Go stages is real.

Cross-check: the TS port (/home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/shared/index.ts:26) uses `config.require("parent")`, i.e., it fails fast and does not have this placeholder-fallback bug — so this is a go-port-specific defect, not a general design choice across ports. No Pulumi.yaml config-schema `required` declaration exists to compensate (checked go/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/Pulumi.yaml — plain, no schema).

Severity: the reviewer's own "minor" label is accurate, not inflated. Deploying with parent_id unset won't silently succeed against a real wrong org (the placeholder "organizations/123456789012" is very unlikely to match any real customer org), so the practical outcome is a GCP API error (permission-denied/not-found) at apply time rather than actual misconfiguration of live infra — but that error will be far more confusing than Pulumi's clear "missing required configuration variable 'parent_id'" message that `conf.Require` would have produced (as svpc does). That matches "minor" severity: a real fail-fast/DX regression, not a correctness/security issue in practice.

#### [TS] Example stack configs missing required 'parent' key
_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/config · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Both shared and env index.ts call config.require("parent") (and require org_id), but the shipped Pulumi.*.yaml.example files contain only org_id and remote_state_bucket (a key the stage never reads). Following the examples, pulumi up fails with a missing required configuration error.

**Evidence:** Port: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/shared/index.ts line 26 and envs/development/index.ts line 26 (config.require("parent")) vs envs/shared/Pulumi.shared.yaml.example and envs/development/Pulumi.development.yaml.example (no parent key).

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed. All four env index.ts files in pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke (shared, development, nonproduction, production — each at line 26) call config.require("parent"), yet none of the shipped Pulumi.*.yaml.example files contain a parent key; they ship only org_id and remote_state_bucket, and remote_state_bucket is never read by any .ts file in the stage. No alternate supply path exists: the Pulumi.yaml files have no config block, helpers/foundation-deployer/deploy.sh sets no config, build/pulumi-up.yml and pulumi-preview.yml inject nothing, and the stage README's own setup commands set org_id/billing_account/org_stack_name/env_stack_name but never parent (docs/ENVIRONMENTS.md mentions `pulumi config set parent` only for 2-environments and 3-networks-svpc, not this stage). The Terraform reference has no such defect — its example tfvars cover all required variables and TF has no `parent` variable at all — so the mismatch is port-introduced. A user copying the example config fails at pulumi preview/up with a missing-required-configuration error. Claimed severity "minor" is accurate: the failure is immediate, self-explanatory, and fixed with one `pulumi config set`.

**Verifier note / correction:** Two refinements, neither refuting the claim: (1) the required `parent` config is actually dead — SharedVpcArgs declares `parent` (modules/shared_vpc.ts:19) but the module never consumes args.parent, so the ideal fix is removing the require rather than adding the key to examples; (2) the scope is understated — nonproduction and production index.ts (line 26 each) have the same defect, and sibling stages (2-environments, 3-networks-svpc) show the same example-vs-code mismatch pattern.

#### [GO] Shipped stack config examples do not satisfy required config keys; no shared-stack example
_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/config · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** main.go requires env_code (conf.Require) and hub_project_id (conf.Require), but Pulumi.development/nonproduction/production.yaml.example set only env, project_id (a key the code never reads) and parent_id — pulumi up with the provided examples fails on missing env_code/hub_project_id, and spoke_project_id is also absent. There is no Pulumi.shared.yaml.example even though the hub deployment is triggered by env=shared.

**Evidence:** Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go lines 474-477 (Require env_code/hub_project_id) vs Pulumi.development.yaml.example / Pulumi.production.yaml.example (env, project_id, parent_id only); ls shows no Pulumi.shared.yaml.example. TF equivalent: terraform.example.tfvars files match declared variables.

**Verifier reasoning:** Every element of the claim verified against the Go port and could not be refuted. (1) /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go lines 474-477 unconditionally call conf.Require("env"), conf.Require("env_code"), conf.Require("hub_project_id") in loadNetConfig, so pulumi up fails with "missing required configuration variable" unless both env_code and hub_project_id are set. (2) All three shipped examples (Pulumi.development/nonproduction/production.yaml.example) set only env, project_id, and parent_id (plus commented-out region1/region2); grep confirms `project_id` is never read anywhere in the stage's code — it is a dead key. Pulumi.yaml provides no config defaults. (3) No Pulumi.shared.yaml.example exists, yet the hub deployment is gated on cfg.Env == "shared" (main.go line 50); the TF reference has a dedicated envs/shared root module plus shared.auto.example.tfvars. (4) TF side checks out: common/shared/access_context .auto.example.tfvars keys (remote_state_bucket, domain, perimeter_additional_members, target_name_server_addresses, access_context_manager_policy_id) all match declared variables in envs/*/variables.tf, so the reference examples work as claimed. (5) No alternate path satisfies the keys: repo-wide grep for env_code/hub_project_id finds no CI script, helper, or build step that sets them for this stage, and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) is a component library that cannot supply stack config. The gap is actually slightly worse than claimed: the stage README's config reference and its `pulumi config set` instructions also omit env_code entirely, so even following the README fails; and spoke_project_id (conf.Get, used at main.go lines 224-360 as the project for every spoke resource) is absent from examples, yielding empty-project spoke resources even after fixing the Require failures. Claimed severity "minor" is fair: it is an example/docs defect that breaks the out-of-box experience but fails fast at config-load with a self-explanatory Pulumi error naming the missing key, and is fixable with two `pulumi config set` commands.

**Verifier note / correction:** Claim is accurate as stated; one nuance: spoke_project_id is read via conf.Get (optional in code, README wrongly marks it required), so its absence does not cause the pulumi up failure — env_code and hub_project_id do. Additionally the README also omits env_code, so the gap extends beyond the .yaml.example files.

_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/flow-logs · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

TF exposes vpc_flow_logs (aggregation_interval, flow_sampling, metadata, metadata_fields, filter_expr), dns_vpc_flow_logs, hub_dns_enable_inbound_forwarding, hub_dns_enable_logging, hub_firewall_enable_logging, firewall_policies_enable_logging as stage inputs (envs/shared/variables.tf:38-146, modules/base_env/variables.tf:101). Repo-wide greps for flow-log/logging keywords in both ports and the shared library found no alternative implementation. **Verifier note / correction:** Mostly accurate, one overstatement: in the Go port two logging toggles ARE configurable — main.go:492-501 reads firewall_policies_enable_logging (hierarchical firewall policy logging, matching TF's variable of the same name) and dns_enable_logging (DNS query logging) from Pulumi config with default true. The real Go-port gaps are: vpc_flow_logs tuning options (absent from library and stage), hub_dns_enable_inbound_forwarding (hardcoded true), and hub_firewall_enable_logging for VPC firewall rules (hardcoded true in the BuildFoundationRules call). The TS-port portion of the claim is fully accurate.

#### [BOTH] Interconnect/VPN examples create attachments/tunnels without BGP sessions and with fewer tunnels
_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/on-prem examples · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform's interconnect_attachment and vpn_ha modules configure a router interface and BGP peer (peer name/ASN, session ranges) per attachment/tunnel, and the VPN example builds 4 gateways × 2 tunnels (8 total). The Go example creates 4 interconnect attachments and only 2 VPN tunnels (region 1 only, second gateway unused) with no RouterInterface/RouterPeer resources anywhere (its secretmanager lookup also passes the secret name as the project). The TS examples create 4 attachments/4 tunnels, also without any RouterInterface/RouterPeer, so BGP would never establish.

**Evidence:** TF: modules/dedicated_interconnect/main.tf (interface+peer blocks), modules/vpn-ha/main.tf (4×2 tunnels with bgp_peer). Ports: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/interconnect.go.example and vpn.go.example (grep RouterInterface/RouterPeer: none; only r1 tunnels); pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/modules/{interconnect,vpn}.ts.example (4 VPNTunnel, no bgp resources).

**Verifier reasoning:** Every element of the claim verified. TF: modules/dedicated_interconnect/main.tf creates 4 attachments each with interface+peer (BGP) blocks via the upstream interconnect_attachment module; modules/vpn-ha/main.tf creates 4 vpn_ha instances (one per existing shared-VPC router) x 2 tunnels = 8 tunnels, each with bgp_peer address/asn and bgp_session_range. Go port: interconnect.go.example creates 4 InterconnectAttachments with no RouterInterface/RouterPeer (PeerASN/PeerName fields unused); vpn.go.example creates only 2 tunnels (region 1), discards the region-2 gateway, has no BGP resources, leaves BgpPeerASN/EnvSecretProjectID unused, and passes the secret NAME as the Project in LookupSecretVersion (line 63). TS port: 4 attachments / 4 tunnels (vs TF's 8), all 16 BGP peer-address/range config fields declared but never consumed, no RouterInterface/RouterPeer. Whole-repo greps of both ports and the shared library for RouterInterface/RouterPeer/router_peer and gcloud BGP commands confirm absence, with one exception: pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpn-ha does implement RouterInterface+RouterPeer per tunnel, but no port source file imports it (it is only an unused package.json dependency in the TS port), it builds a different topology (its own new router + 1 gateway instead of binding to the 4 existing shared-VPC routers), the Go library has no equivalent at all, and nothing anywhere covers interconnect-attachment BGP. Without RouterInterface/RouterPeer, HA VPN tunnels establish IPsec but exchange no routes (HA VPN requires BGP) and dedicated interconnect attachments are non-functional, so a user activating these examples gets dead hybrid connectivity.

**Verifier note / correction:** Claim is essentially fully accurate. One nuance: "no RouterInterface/RouterPeer resources anywhere" is true of the port repos themselves, but the shared TS library (pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpn-ha) does contain a VpnHa component with per-tunnel RouterInterface/RouterPeer — it is just never imported by either port's examples and does not replicate the TF topology, and no interconnect equivalent exists. Claimed severity "minor" is fair given these are opt-in .example files, though arguably understated since activating them yields tunnels/attachments that deploy cleanly but can never pass traffic.

_Area: 3-networks-svpc/dns · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

Go port: 3-networks-svpc/main.go:409-411 fabricates a ["10.0.0.1"] default when the target_name_servers config key is absent, and main.go:185-192 feeds it into the production forwarding zone; the config type is []string so per-server forwarding_path is inexpressible, and the library's only knob (networking/dns.go:54 ForwardingPath, a single zone-global string) is never set by the port. The same defect is duplicated in 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go:534-535, and the TS port shares the identical 10.0.0.1 fabrication (envs/production/index.ts:76), so the claim's go-only scoping actually understates the gap. Claimed severity of minor is fair: the fabricated target would break resolution of the forwarded domain in a production deploy left at defaults (TF's [] default instead fails/forces explicit configuration), but this is example scaffolding where operators supply real values, and the forwarding_path loss only removes the ability to force "private" routing (unset means GCP default behavior, which matches the upstream example's own "default" setting).

#### [BOTH] Interconnect examples create attachments without BGP interfaces/peers; partner 'preactivate' ignored
_Area: 3-networks-svpc/interconnect-examples · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's dedicated_interconnect module (via cloud-router//interconnect_attachment) creates the attachment plus a router interface and BGP peer (peer_name/peer_asn) per VLAN; the partner_interconnect module sets admin_enabled = var.preactivate. Both ports' examples create only the InterconnectAttachment resources: PeerASN/PeerName (Go) and peerAsn/peerName (TS) are declared but never used, no RouterInterface/RouterPeer is created, and the partner examples declare Preactivate/preactivate but never set adminEnabled. On-prem BGP sessions would never come up when the examples are activated.

**Evidence:** TF: modules/dedicated_interconnect/main.tf (interface + peer blocks), modules/partner_interconnect/main.tf (admin_enabled = var.preactivate). Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/interconnect.go.example, partner_interconnect.go.example. TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/interconnect.ts.example, partner_interconnect.ts.example.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified in full. TF's dedicated_interconnect module (3-networks-svpc/modules/dedicated_interconnect/main.tf) passes interface{name} and peer{name, peer_asn} blocks to the cloud-router//interconnect_attachment submodule for all 4 VLAN attachments, creating a router interface and BGP peer per attachment; partner_interconnect/main.tf sets admin_enabled = var.preactivate (default false). Both ports' example files create only the 4 InterconnectAttachment resources: Go declares PeerASN/PeerName and TS declares peerAsn/peerName but never uses them; grep confirms zero RouterInterface/RouterPeer usage anywhere in either port, and AdminEnabled/adminEnabled appears nowhere in either port, so the declared Preactivate/preactivate fields are dead code. The shared pulumi-library has no interconnect component at all (its only RouterInterface/RouterPeer usage is in ts/packages/vpn-ha for VPN tunnels; the cloud_router/cloud-router packages contain no interconnect code). The Go example's docstring falsely claims it deploys attachments "with BGP sessions on existing Cloud Routers." If activated as-is, dedicated attachments would have no BGP interface/peer so on-prem sessions would never establish, and partner attachments would always be admin-enabled (pulumi-gcp defaults adminEnabled to true), inverting TF's default preactivate=false behavior.

**Verifier note / correction:** The gap is real and 'minor' is a fair severity: these are inactive .example scaffolding files requiring manual adaptation, and both ports' docs/ERRATA.md already documents that Interconnect resource definitions are not fully ported and "must be added manually." However, the examples are misleading rather than merely incomplete — they claim to mirror the TF examples, declare dead config fields (peerAsn/peerName, preactivate), and the Go docstring falsely advertises BGP sessions. Also note the partner behavior is not just "preactivate ignored" but inverted: pulumi-gcp's adminEnabled defaults to true, whereas TF's preactivate defaults to false.

(3) Go port: repo-wide grep for windows/activation/35.190.247.13 finds no route and no config option in 3-networks-svpc (or 3-networks-hub-and-spoke); the only compute.NewRoute in 3-networks-svpc/main.go is the tag-based egress-internet 0.0.0.0/0 route, which is not equivalent (requires the egress-internet tag, unlike the untagged KMS route). The Go library (/home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/) has only a hierarchical firewall policy rule allowing egress to 35.190.247.13 (hierarchical_firewall.go lines 154-163), which mirrors TF's separate firewall rule and does not substitute for the route; library NewRoute usage exists only in transitivity.go for hub-and-spoke. The 4-projects Go stage's WindowsActivation option (peering.go lines 284-294) is a network firewall rule in a different stage, also not a route. Notably the Go port's 3-networks-svpc/README.md line 30 documents the KMS route capability that the code does not implement, making this a documented-but-missing feature. Claimed severity "minor" is fair: the TF option defaults to false and only affects deployments that enable Windows KMS activation.

#### [TS] Ingress/egress policies and custom restricted services not wired into the stage
_Area: 3-networks-svpc/vpc_sc · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF envs expose ingress_policies, egress_policies (and _dry_run variants) and custom_restricted_services(_dry_run) variables that flow into the perimeter. The TS library VpcServiceControls supports ingressPolicies/egressPolicies/restrictedServicesDryRun, but the env programs never pass them and provide no config surface — restrictedServices is hardcoded to DEFAULT_RESTRICTED_SERVICES. Users cannot configure perimeter exceptions or custom service lists without editing code. (Go wires all of these from config.)

**Evidence:** TF: envs/production/variables.tf (ingress/egress policies vars), modules/base_env/variables.tf custom_restricted_services. TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/envs/production/index.ts lines 64-72 (no ingress/egress/custom services args) vs /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpc-service-controls/src/index.ts (args supported).

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified on all points. TF side: 3-networks-svpc/envs/production/variables.tf defines ingress_policies/egress_policies (+_dry_run), and modules/base_env defines custom_restricted_services(_dry_run); all flow into the restricted perimeter in base_env/main.tf (lines 163-164, 196-204). TS side: the library component (pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpc-service-controls/src/index.ts lines 157-169) supports ingressPolicies/egressPolicies/dry-run variants and restrictedServices(DryRun), but all three env programs (production/development/nonproduction index.ts) hardcode restrictedServices: DEFAULT_RESTRICTED_SERVICES and pass none of the policy args. A repo-wide grep of the TS port and library found no config keys, alternative wiring, or internal config reads for these features (the only ingress/egress hits are unrelated firewall rules), and Pulumi.production.yaml.example contains no related keys. The Go port confirms the contrast: main.go reads vpc_sc_restricted_services (with default fallback) and ingress/egress policies (+dry-run) from stack config and passes them to the component. Severity 'minor' is accurate: TF defaults for these variables are empty lists, so default deployments are equivalent — the gap is a missing configuration surface (perimeter exceptions and custom service lists require code edits), not a resource-parity break.

#### [BOTH] No equivalent of the 60s VPC-SC propagation wait / perimeter ordering
_Area: 3-networks-svpc/vpc_sc · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF inserts time_sleep.wait_vpc_sc_propagation (60s create and destroy) depending on every network resource before creating the service perimeter, to avoid the perimeter enforcing mid-deploy. Neither port sequences the perimeter after the network resources (Go creates VpcServiceControls with no DependsOn; TS likewise), and neither implements a propagation delay, so with enforce=true (both ports' default) resource creation can race the perimeter.

**Evidence:** TF: modules/shared_vpc/service_control.tf lines 48-99. Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go lines 262-274 (no DependsOn); /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/vpc_sc/vpc_sc.go. TS: envs/*/index.ts VpcServiceControls call; /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpc-service-controls/src/index.ts.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified on all points. TF (modules/shared_vpc/service_control.tf:48-99) inserts time_sleep.wait_vpc_sc_propagation (60s create AND destroy) depending on every network resource (VPC module, PSA, routers, NATs, DNS, peering zone, firewall rules, PSC), and the regular_service_perimeter module depends on that sleep — guaranteeing the perimeter is applied only after the network exists plus a 60s propagation buffer, and that destroys wait 60s after perimeter removal. Neither port has any equivalent: Go's main.go:262-274 calls vpc_sc.NewVpcServiceControls with no pulumi.DependsOn and only config-derived inputs (no data dependency on network resources), and the library component (pulumi-library/go/pkg/vpc_sc/vpc_sc.go) creates AccessLevels and the ServicePerimeter with no delay/ordering mechanism; TS envs/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts construct VpcServiceControls with no dependsOn and inputs only from config/stack references, and pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpc-service-controls/src/index.ts likewise has none. Exhaustive greps of both port repos and the shared library for sleep/propagation/wait/delay/dependsOn found no equivalent under any name (the only wait construct, centralized-logging's waitIamMembership, mirrors a different TF sleep). Both ports default enforce to true (Go main.go:385; TS `?? true`), so the perimeter can be created in parallel with network resources and enforce mid-deploy — exactly the race the TF sleep prevents.

**Verifier note / correction:** Severity 'minor' is fair for the missing wait itself (a transient deploy/destroy race, not a persistent misconfiguration), but the claim understates one aggravating divergence: upstream TF defaults enforce_vpcsc=false (modules/shared_vpc/variables.tf:185, explicitly recommending dry-run first), whereas both ports default enforce to true, making the race materially more likely on a first deploy than in the reference implementation.

#### [BOTH] HA VPN examples omit BGP sessions and most tunnels; library VpnHa component unused
_Area: 3-networks-svpc/vpn-example · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's vpn-ha module creates 4 HA VPN gateways (one per Cloud Router) with 2 tunnels each (8 tunnels) plus router interfaces and BGP peers per tunnel via the vpn_ha module. The Go example creates 2 gateways and only 2 tunnels (region 2 tunnels missing entirely), the TS example 2 gateways/4 tunnels; neither creates any RouterInterface/RouterPeer, so the accepted BGP peer address/range/ASN fields are dead config and no routes would ever be exchanged. The TS library has a complete VpnHa component (tunnels + interfaces + peers) that the port does not use. The Go example also passes the secret name as the Secret Manager project (Project: pulumi.StringRef(cfg.VpnPskSecretName)) and never uses EnvSecretProjectID.

**Evidence:** TF: modules/vpn-ha/main.tf (4x vpn_ha modules, 8 tunnels, bgp_peer blocks). Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/vpn.go.example (2 tunnels, no RouterPeer, secret lookup bug). TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/vpn.ts.example (4 tunnels, no RouterPeer); /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpn-ha/src/index.ts (RouterInterface/RouterPeer supported, unused).

**Verifier reasoning:** Every element of the claim checks out. TF (/home/user/terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/vpn-ha/main.tf) instantiates the terraform-google-modules vpn_ha module 4 times — one HA VPN gateway per Cloud Router (region1 cr1/cr2, region2 cr1/cr2) — with 2 tunnels each (8 total), each tunnel configured with bgp_peer address/ASN and bgp_session_range; the upstream module also creates router interfaces and BGP peers per tunnel. The Go port example (/home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/vpn.go.example) creates 2 gateways but only 2 tunnels, both in region 1 (the region-2 gateway has no tunnels), creates no RouterInterface or RouterPeer, leaves BgpPeerASN and the region-2 router names as dead config, and has the exact cited secret bug: line 63 passes the secret name as the Secret Manager project (Project: pulumi.StringRef(cfg.VpnPskSecretName)) while EnvSecretProjectID (line 45) is never used. The TS port example (/home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/vpn.ts.example) creates 2 gateways and 4 tunnels with no RouterInterface/RouterPeer, so all 16 BGP peer address/range fields plus bgpPeerAsn are dead config (its secret lookup is correct, matching the claim's Go-only attribution of that bug). The TS library (/home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpn-ha/src/index.ts) has a complete VpnHa component creating HaVpnGateway, VPNTunnel, RouterInterface, and RouterPeer per tunnel; the TS port lists @vitruviansoftware/foundation-vpn-ha in package.json but never imports it. The Go library (/home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/) has no VPN component at all. Repo-wide greps of both ports and the library for RouterPeer/RouterInterface/VpnHa/vpn found no equivalent implementation under any other name (the hub-and-spoke Go example repeats the same 2-tunnel no-BGP pattern). The claimed severity of minor is fair: these are opt-in .example files outside the default deployment path, though a user who activates them would get a VPN with no BGP route exchange.

#### [GO] 'keys' stack output is always an empty array — CMEK crypto key IDs never surfaced
_Area: 4-projects · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** main.go exports "keys" from projects.CMEKKeys, but no code ever assigns CMEKKeys (deployCMEKStorage returns only BucketName and KeyringName), so the export always falls to the empty-array branch. TF's base_env outputs keys = values from module.kms.keys, which downstream users need to reference the CMEK crypto key.

**Evidence:** Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/main.go:123-127 (CMEKKeys nil branch), business_unit.go:186-193 and cmek.go:126-129 (CMEKKeys never set). TF: 4-projects/modules/base_env/outputs.tf 'keys' output from module.kms.keys.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

TF side: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/4-projects/modules/base_env/outputs.tf:72-75 defines `output "keys" { value = keys(module.kms.keys) }`, backed by example_storage_cmek.tf's `module "kms"` (terraform-google-modules/kms) with `keys = [var.key_name]`. The cited TF functionality is real.

Go port side: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/cmek.go creates a `kms.NewCryptoKey` (var `cryptoKey`, line 62) used internally for the IAM binding and bucket encryption, but `CMEKResult` (cmek.go:32-35) only carries `BucketName` and `KeyringName` — the crypto key's ID/name is never returned. business_unit.go:190-192 assigns only `result.CMEKBucket` and `result.CMEKKeyring` from `cmekResult`; `result.CMEKKeys` (declared business_unit.go:39) is never assigned anywhere in the repo (verified via grep — only declaration and the two main.go reads exist). main.go:123-127 therefore always hits the `else` branch, exporting `keys` as an empty array regardless of whether CMEK is enabled.

I also checked whether the shared pulumi-library provides an equivalent that the port might be using under another name: /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/kms/kms.go does implement a `Kms` component whose `Keys map[string]*kms.CryptoKey` field exposes exactly this data — but 4-projects/cmek.go does NOT use that shared component at all; it hand-rolls KeyRing/CryptoKey directly and drops the key reference. No alternate spelling (KeyID, CryptoKeyID, KeyIDs, etc.) carries this value anywhere in 4-projects, and no downstream stack (5-app-infra) reads a "keys"/"key" output from the 4-projects stack, so nothing is currently broken by consumption — supporting the reviewer's own "minor" severity rather than something higher.

Conclusion: the claim is accurate as stated, including the correctly-assessed "minor" severity (informational output gap, no found downstream consumer). No correction needed.

_Area: 4-projects · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

TS (4-projects/business_unit_1/production/index.ts, and identically in nonproduction/development) declares envRef := new StackReference('env-...') but only ever consumes envRef.getOutput('env_folder'); it never reads env_kms_project_id anywhere. Instead it calls deploySingleProject('cmek-example', ...) to spin up a brand-new ad-hoc project and places both the KeyRing/CryptoKey and the Bucket inside that new project. I confirmed 2-environments (TS) does export env_kms_project_id/env_kms_project_number (envs/production/index.ts:38-39, env_baseline.ts:138-139), so the output is genuinely available but simply unused by 4-projects -- this is not a case of the upstream output being absent from the port. I also checked pulumi-library for a possible equivalent implemented under another name: there is a shared ts/packages/kms component (foundation-kms) that mirrors terraform-google-modules/kms/google, and it is even listed as a dependency in package.json, but it is never imported/used anywhere in the TS 4-projects code, which instead constructs gcp.kms.KeyRing/CryptoKey directly inline. No alternative spelling, indirection, or shared-library helper surfaces the env KMS project anywhere in TS 4-projects. All of the claim's evidence citations check out exactly as given.

**Verifier note / correction:** Severity as claimed (minor) is reasonable and not clearly inflated: this is example/demonstration code (the CMEK bucket is explicitly a pattern showcase, not a dependency consumed by other stages/stacks), so the practical blast radius is low even though the divergence is a real, functional one (ad-hoc project sprawl instead of centralized env-KMS project reuse) rather than merely an unused informational output.

#### [TS] Sample shared-VPC project not attached to the VPC-SC perimeter (no enforce/dry-run attach)
_Area: 4-projects · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF attaches both the example shared_vpc project and the confidential-space project to the environment's VPC-SC perimeter, switching between enforced and dry-run attachment based on enforce_vpcsc (vpc_service_control_attach_enabled / _attach_dry_run). TS attaches only the confidential-space project (always enforced mode) and never attaches the main sample application project, leaving it outside the perimeter; there is no dry-run attach path at all.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/4-projects/modules/base_env/example_shared_vpc_project.tf:43-46 and example_confidential_space_project.tf:92-93. TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/production/index.ts:322-326 (only confidential space ServicePerimeterResource); same in development/nonproduction.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

TF side (verified): 4-projects/modules/base_env/example_shared_vpc_project.tf:43-46 and example_confidential_space_project.tf:92-93 both pass vpc_service_control_attach_enabled = local.enforce_vpcsc ? "true" : "false" and vpc_service_control_attach_dry_run = !local.enforce_vpcsc ? "true" : "false" into the single_project module, which forwards them (single_project/main.tf:63-66) into the upstream terraform-google-modules/project-factory module. That module implements the actual enforced-vs-dry-run perimeter attachment. So TF genuinely attaches BOTH the sample shared_vpc project and the confidential-space project to the perimeter, and switches each between enforced/dry-run based on enforce_vpcsc.

TS side (verified across production/development/nonproduction in pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/*/index.ts): only one `gcp.accesscontextmanager.ServicePerimeterResource` exists per environment file (line ~323), named "*-confidential-space-perimeter-attachment", attaching only confidentialSpaceProject.projectNumber. The sharedVpcProject (created earlier in the same file, e.g. line 37) is never passed to any ServicePerimeterResource or equivalent attach mechanism anywhere in the file, in the shared single_project module (4-projects/modules/single_project/index.ts, which has no perimeter/vpc_service_control logic at all), or elsewhere in the port. There is also no `enforce_vpcsc`/`enforceVpcsc` config or conditional anywhere in 4-projects (grep across the whole ts port only finds enforceVpcSc used in stage 3-networks-svpc/3-networks-hub-and-spoke, to build the perimeter's own enforced/dry-run access levels — it never reaches 4-projects). The pulumi-library ts VpcServiceControls component (used to construct the perimeter in stage 3) is not invoked from 4-projects either, and it does not offer an "attach existing project to existing perimeter in dry-run vs enforced mode" primitive matching project-factory's attach flags — it's a whole-perimeter constructor, not a per-project attach helper.

So both parts of the claim hold: (1) the sample shared_vpc project is never attached to the perimeter at all, and (2) the confidential-space project's attachment is unconditional/always-enforced with no dry-run path, since it uses the plain ServicePerimeterResource against the perimeter's status directly with no enforce_vpcsc branching.

Severity: claimed "minor" is reasonable, arguably could be called out as more than cosmetic since it's a real security-perimeter coverage gap for the sample app project (a security-boundary difference, not just naming/cosmetic), but "minor" is a defensible characterization for an example/sample project in a foundation template, so I would not push back hard on severity.

#### [TS] single_project: sharedVpcSubnets arg unused; subnet networkUser and folder networkViewer grants missing
_Area: 4-projects · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF single_project grants the app-infra pipeline SAs roles/compute.networkViewer on the BU folder and roles/compute.networkUser on the specified shared-VPC subnets (shared_vpc_subnets var) so pipeline-deployed workloads can attach to the host VPC. The TS deploySingleProject declares sharedVpcSubnets in its args interface but never reads it, and creates neither IAM grant.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/4-projects/modules/single_project/main.tf:93-108 (google_folder_iam_member.folder_network_viewer, google_compute_subnetwork_iam_member). TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/modules/single_project.ts:28 (sharedVpcSubnets declared) — no other reference in file; only projects.IAMMember for saRoles at lines 86-99.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

TF side (terraform-example-foundation/4-projects/modules/single_project/main.tf:93-110): the module does create `google_folder_iam_member.folder_network_viewer` (roles/compute.networkViewer, one per app_infra_pipeline_service_accounts entry, on var.folder_id) and `google_compute_subnetwork_iam_member.service_account_role_to_vpc_subnets` (roles/compute.networkUser, one per (repo × shared_vpc_subnets) pair via the `network_user_role` local, scoped to var.shared_vpc_host_project_id). base_env/example_shared_vpc_project.tf wires real values through (`shared_vpc_subnets = local.subnets_self_links`, `enable_cloudbuild_deploy`, `app_infra_pipeline_service_accounts`), so this is live, exercised functionality in the reference, not dead code.

TS side (pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/modules/single_project.ts): `sharedVpcSubnets?: string[]` is declared in `SingleProjectArgs` (line 28) and never read anywhere else in the file — confirmed via grep of the whole file/repo. The only IAM logic present is the project-level `gcp.projects.IAMMember` loop for `saRoles` (lines 86-99); there is no `gcp.organizations.IAMMember`/folder IAM grant for compute.networkViewer, and no `gcp.compute.SubnetworkIAMMember` (or equivalent) for compute.networkUser anywhere in the file, the rest of the ts port (grepped for networkViewer/networkUser/NetworkViewer/NetworkUser/SubnetworkIAMMember across all .ts), or pulumi-library's ts/ or go/ trees (no matches at all). There's also an orphaned `4-projects/modules/single_project/index.ts` (class-based `SingleProject`) but it's not imported by any caller (all three business_unit_1 stacks import `deploySingleProject` from `single_project.ts`) and it also contains no such IAM logic — just a thin ProjectFactory wrapper.

So the gap is real: the TS port has no path, under any name, that grants folder-level networkViewer or subnet-level networkUser to the app-infra pipeline SAs.

Severity note: I'd soften "minor" slightly in one direction and firm it up in another. On one hand, currently no caller in the TS port actually populates `enableCloudbuildDeploy`/`appInfraPipelineServiceAccounts`/`sharedVpcSubnets` in business_unit_1's three index.ts files, so today this code path is entirely inert — nothing in the shipped example config exercises it, which argues for "minor"/cosmetic in the current repo state. On the other hand, the module's args interface advertises this as supported functionality (mirroring TF's app-infra CI/CD pattern), and a real adopter who wires those args expecting parity with the TF module would silently get project-level SA roles but *not* the folder/subnet network grants needed for the pipeline SA to actually attach resources to the shared VPC — a real permissions gap for any production use of the app-infra pipeline against shared VPC, not just an unused informational output. So "minor" is defensible for today's shipped state but arguably undersells the risk for intended real-world use; I'd call it minor-to-moderate rather than purely cosmetic.

**Verifier note / correction:** Core claim holds as stated. Minor refinement: severity is fair for the current repo state (the feature is currently unreachable dead code since no caller populates enableCloudbuildDeploy/appInfraPipelineServiceAccounts/sharedVpcSubnets), but understates the risk for the module's intended use — a user wiring those args to mirror TF's app-infra pipeline pattern would get partial IAM (project SA roles only) without the folder networkViewer / subnet networkUser grants needed for shared-VPC attachment, which is a real access-control gap rather than merely a cosmetic one.

Claimed severity 'minor' is appropriate: it is an illustrative example and a CMEK-encrypted bucket is still created, but the TS version loses the example's separation-of-duties architecture (dedicated env KMS project) and all location/placement configurability.

#### [GO] 'keys' stack export is always an empty list (CMEKKeys never populated)
_Area: 4-projects/outputs · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

The TS port correctly exports [cmekKey.name].

**Evidence:** TF: modules/base_env/outputs.tf output "keys". Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit.go:39 (field declared, never set — cmek.go CMEKResult has only BucketName/KeyringName), main.go:123-127 (nil check always false → empty export).

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified on all points. TF exports the created KMS key names: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/4-projects/modules/base_env/outputs.tf:72-75 (`output "keys" { value = keys(module.kms.keys) }`), surfaced via each env's outputs.tf. In the Go port, a repo-wide grep for CMEKKeys yields only the field declaration (/home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit.go:39) and the nil-check read (/home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/main.go:123-124) — no assignment anywhere. deployCMEKStorage (/home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/cmek.go) creates the crypto key at line 62 but CMEKResult (lines 30-33) returns only BucketName and KeyringName, and business_unit.go:186-193 assigns only CMEKBucket/CMEKKeyring. So projects.CMEKKeys is always nil and main.go:125-127 always exports keys = [], even when CMEK is enabled and the key exists. The shared library (/home/user/pulumi-library) contains no CMEK/KMS component that could populate the field (only unrelated cai-monitoring hits), and the TS port correctly exports keys: [cmekKey.name] (e.g. /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/production/index.ts:411). Severity 'minor' is accurate, arguably generous: no consumer of the 'keys' output exists in TF's tests, TF's 5-app-infra, or either port — it is a purely informational output, and the fix is a one-line addition of the key name to CMEKResult plus an assignment.

#### [BOTH] 'subnets_self_links' export contains the peering subnet instead of the shared-VPC host subnets
_Area: 4-projects/outputs · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

Both ports export the peering project's own subnet instead, so any downstream consumer expecting the host subnets receives the wrong network. (Both network stacks do export subnets_self_links; 4-projects just never consumes it.)

TF: 4-projects/modules/base_env/remote.tf:25 sets local.subnets_self_links from the 3-networks remote state (shared-VPC host subnets), and outputs.tf:42-45 exports it as the env output subnets_self_links, distinct from peering_subnetwork_self_link (outputs.tf:82-85). Go port: business_unit.go:196-202 sets SubnetsSelfLinks to [PeeringSubnetSelfLink] (or empty), exported at 4-projects/main.go:134; the network StackReference (main.go:41) is only ever read for service_perimeter_name (main.go:63). TS port: business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts:414 exports subnets_self_links: [peeringSubnet.selfLink], duplicating peering_subnetwork_self_link (line 403); netRef.getOutput("subnets_self_links") is never called. Both ports' 3-networks stacks DO export subnets_self_links (go 3-networks-svpc/main.go:305, hub-and-spoke main.go:430; ts 3-networks-svpc/envs/*/index.ts:86-87), so the correct value is available but unconsumed. /home/user/pulumi-library has zero matches for subnets_self_links/subnetsSelfLinks in go/ or ts/ — no hidden equivalent. The export therefore carries the wrong semantics (peering subnet instead of shared-VPC host subnets) in both ports.

**Verifier note / correction:** Claimed severity of "minor" is fair, not inflated. Nuance: in upstream TF this output is functionally consumed by 5-app-infra (modules/env_base/main.tf:34 and modules/confidential_space/main.tf:29 pick the VM subnetwork from projects_env.outputs.subnets_self_links), but neither port's 5-app-infra consumes the 4-projects export (Go takes SubnetworkSelfLink as an arg; TS examples build the shared-VPC subnet path from config and use peering_subnetwork_self_link). So within the port repos it is currently an informational parity export with the wrong value — a booby trap only for downstream consumers that replicate TF's 5-app-infra pattern.

#### [BOTH] Per-environment peering subnet CIDR/region not differentiated (TS hardcoded; Go single default)
_Area: 4-projects/peering · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

TS hardcodes 10.3.64.0/21 and region=default_region in all three env stacks with no config option at all.
