Ports: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts:126 (literal "10.3.64.0/21" in all three); pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/main.go (SubnetIPRange default "10.3.64.0/21") + Pulumi.*.yaml.example (no subnet_ip_range).

**Verifier reasoning:** Every element of the claim checks out. TF assigns distinct per-env ranges (dev 10.3.64.0/21 at business_unit_1/development/main.tf:37, nonprod 10.3.128.0/21, prod 10.3.192.0/21) and exposes subnet_region/subnet_ip_range via base_env variables plus an instance_region override. The TS port hardcodes ipCidrRange "10.3.64.0/21" in all three env stacks (business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts:126) with region taken from default_region and no config key for the CIDR anywhere in the stage. The Go port exposes subnet_region/subnet_ip_range config (main.go:287-294) but defaults every env to us-central1/10.3.64.0/21 with no per-env logic, and none of the shipped Pulumi.{development,nonproduction,production}.yaml.example files set those keys (the README even documents 10.3.64.0/21 as the value to set), so all environments deploy the dev range by default. The shared pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) contains no peering-project component with per-env ranges; the only CIDR hit is the unrelated cb-private-pool component. Claimed severity of minor is accurate: these are sample projects, per-env peerings are non-transitive so nothing fails immediately, and the Go port is fixable via config alone (TS requires a code edit), but the overlap violates the foundation's non-overlapping IP plan and would break hybrid/on-prem routing.

#### [GO] VPC-SC dry-run attachment branch missing; enforce flag read from local config instead of network stack
_Area: 4-projects/vpc-sc · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF always places the SVPC and conf-space projects in the perimeter: enforced mode when enforce_vpcsc, explicit dry-run mode otherwise (vpc_service_control_attach_dry_run = !enforce_vpcsc). The Go port attaches only when its own config enforce_vpcsc=true and does nothing otherwise (no dry-run membership). It also reads enforce_vpcsc from stack config (default true) rather than consuming the network stack's exported enforce_vpcsc, so the two stages can silently disagree.

**Evidence:** TF: modules/base_env/example_shared_vpc_project.tf + example_confidential_space_project.tf (attach_enabled/attach_dry_run pair); base_env/remote.tf enforce_vpcsc from network_env. Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit.go:118-130 and confidential_space.go:96-107 (attach only if cfg.EnforceVpcSc); main.go loadProjectsConfig reads config 'enforce_vpcsc'; pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go:291 exports enforce_vpcsc (unconsumed).

**Verifier reasoning:** Every element of the claim verified. TF: example_shared_vpc_project.tf:43-44 and example_confidential_space_project.tf:92-93 set vpc_service_control_attach_enabled=enforce_vpcsc and vpc_service_control_attach_dry_run=!enforce_vpcsc (project always in perimeter, enforced or dry-run), with enforce_vpcsc sourced from the network stage remote state (base_env/remote.tf:27). Go port: business_unit.go:107-119 and confidential_space.go:87-98 create accesscontextmanager.ServicePerimeterResource only when cfg.EnforceVpcSc is true, with no dry-run branch; grep of the entire Go port and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) found zero uses of ServicePerimeterDryRunResource or any dry-run membership mechanism, and the library project component has no VPC-SC attach logic. 4-projects/main.go:270-274 reads enforce_vpcsc from local stack config (default true) while the netStack StackReference is used only for service_perimeter_name; the 3-networks-svpc/main.go:291 enforce_vpcsc export is unconsumed, so stage 3 (dry-run perimeter, Enforce=false) and stage 4 (default true, enforced attach) can silently disagree. The library perimeter always creates a dry-run spec (UseExplicitDryRunSpec:true), so a dry-run attach target exists — the port just never uses it. Claimed severity of minor is fair: affects only the sample SVPC/conf-space projects and only in the non-default enforce_vpcsc=false path (missing dry-run violation telemetry plus possible stage disagreement).

#### [TS] Confidential VM min_cpu_platform default diverges: 'AMD Rome' vs TF 'AMD Milan'
_Area: 5-app-infra · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's confidential_space module defaults cpu_platform to 'AMD Milan' (required pairing for SEV-SNP capable Confidential Space guidance). The TS example hardcodes minCpuPlatform 'AMD Rome' with no config override; the TF value is also a variable while the TS one is fixed.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/5-app-infra/modules/confidential_space/variables.tf (cpu_platform default "AMD Milan"). TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/5-app-infra/business_unit_1/*/index.ts.example line 155 ("AMD Rome").

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

TF side: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/5-app-infra/modules/confidential_space/variables.tf defines `variable "cpu_platform"` with `default = "AMD Milan"` (line 74-78), which flows into `main.tf` as `min_cpu_platform = var.cpu_platform` (line 105) for the `confidential_instance_template` module call (which also sets `confidential_instance_type = "SEV"` default and `confidential_machine_type = "n2d-standard-2"` default). The business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production}/main.tf callers of `module "confidential_space"` do not override `cpu_platform`, so the reference deployment's effective default is "AMD Milan".

TS side: grepped the entire pulumi_ts-example-foundation repo and pulumi-library (both go/ and ts/) for cpu_platform/cpuPlatform/AMD Milan/AMD Rome/confidential. The shared `InstanceTemplate` component in /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/instance-template/src/index.ts exposes `minCpuPlatform?: string` as a pass-through with no built-in default (so the component itself is neutral). But all three business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts.example files hardcode `minCpuPlatform: "AMD Rome"` (line 155) as a string literal with no Pulumi config lookup or variable — there is no equivalent of a configurable/overridable value anywhere in the TS port or the shared library. This is a genuine, literal divergence from the TF default, and it is not configurable in TS the way it is in TF (a real variable).

For contrast, the Go port's example_confidential_space.go models this as a proper field (`ConfidentialSpaceArgs.CpuPlatform string`, passed to `MinCpuPlatform: args.CpuPlatform`), closer to TF's variable pattern, though that file also appears to be unwired scaffolding (never called from go's main.go), so it doesn't materially affect the ts-specific claim.

Severity assessment: this only affects the `.example` scaffolding files (not a checked-in `index.ts` that's actively deployed), and both "AMD Rome" and "AMD Milan" are valid confidential-VM CPU platforms — the divergence is a default-value/configurability mismatch, not a broken or insecure deployment. "Minor" is an accurate, not inflated, severity label.

#### [TS] Confidential-space example hardcodes 'fake-project' image reference and all-zero image digest
_Area: 5-app-infra · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF derives tee-image-reference from the bootstrap Cloud Build project + tf-runners AR repo (remote state) and takes the attestation image digest from var.confidential_image_digest; Go exposes ConfidentialImageDigest config. The TS index.ts.example hardcodes tee-image-reference to `${region}-docker.pkg.dev/fake-project/tf-runners/confidential_space_image:latest` and pins the attestation condition to sha256:000…0 with no config hook — a placeholder left because the 4-projects image-build pipeline was never ported.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/5-app-infra/modules/confidential_space/main.tf:18 (default_tee_image_reference from cloudbuild_project_id remote state), business_unit_1/production/variables.tf:28. TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/5-app-infra/business_unit_1/production/index.ts.example:131 (attributeCondition sha256:0000…) and ~line 158 (fake-project); grep confidential_image_digest in TS 5-app-infra returns nothing.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

TF side (as cited): `5-app-infra/modules/confidential_space/main.tf:18` computes `default_tee_image_reference` from `local.cloudbuild_project_id`, which comes from `data.terraform_remote_state.business_unit_shared.outputs.bootstrap_cloudbuild_project_id` (line 32) — i.e., derived from the bootstrap Cloud Build project's tf-runners Artifact Registry repo via remote state, region-templated. The `attribute_condition` on the WIF provider (lines 85-89) interpolates `var.confidential_image_digest`, which is a real per-environment variable (`business_unit_1/production/variables.tf:28`, also nonproduction/development) with a `common.auto.example.tfvars` placeholder `IMAGE_DIGEST` and README instructions to populate it from the actual built image's digest via `gcloud artifacts docker images describe`.

TS side: `pulumi_ts-example-foundation/5-app-infra/business_unit_1/{production,nonproduction,development}/index.ts.example` hardcodes `attributeCondition` to `sha256:0000...0000` (line 131) and `tee-image-reference` to `${region}-docker.pkg.dev/fake-project/tf-runners/confidential_space_image:latest` (line 158). Grepping the entire TS 5-app-infra tree for `confidential_image_digest`, `cloudbuild_project_id`/`bootstrap_cloudbuild_project_id`, or `business_unit_shared` remote-state wiring returns nothing — no config knob, no stack reference to the bootstrap/CI-CD project exists anywhere in the TS port for this purpose. The real (non-`.example`) `index.ts` is just a bare scaffold that doesn't touch confidential space at all.

Checked pulumi-library (both `go/` and `ts/`) for any hidden equivalent (shared helper computing tee-image-reference, digest config, etc.) — none exists; the library's `instance-template` package only exposes generic `confidentialInstanceType`/`enableConfidentialVm` passthrough fields, nothing image-reference-specific.

Cross-checked the Go port as a control: `pulumi_go-example-foundation/5-app-infra/example_confidential_space.go` (also an opt-in example gated by `//go:build example`) correctly threads `ConfidentialImageDigest` as a config-sourced field (`main.go:75,86`, `README.md:64/89`, `Pulumi.*.yaml.example:10`) into the attribute condition, and computes `defaultTeeImageRef` dynamically from `args.CloudBuildProjectID` (a stack-reference-sourced field) rather than hardcoding a fake project. This confirms the TS port's hardcoding is a genuine regression relative to both TF and its sibling Go port, not a stylistic difference.

On severity: "minor" is a fair, non-inflated call. The affected code lives entirely in `index.ts.example` files that are explicitly templates a user must copy over the real `index.ts` and edit before deploying (mirrored by the Go port's own build-tag-gated example convention) — it is not on any default deployment path and doesn't block other stages. That said, the gap is real: as shipped, the TS example would try to reference a nonexistent `fake-project` image and an attestation condition that can never match a real digest, so a user following the TS example naively would hit a non-functional confidential-space deployment with no obvious place to plug in the correct values, whereas the Go example at least demonstrates the correct dynamic derivation pattern.

#### [GO] Go 5-app-infra docs are internally inconsistent: ONBOARDING/ERRATA say 'Cloud Run + BigQuery', code and README say Compute instances + Confidential Space; README file table names wrong files
_Area: 5-app-infra · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Go ONBOARDING.md Step 5 says the stage 'deploys sample application resources (Cloud Run, BigQuery)' and Go ERRATA says 'Stage 5 deploys a sample Cloud Run service and BigQuery dataset'. The actual stage (and its own README) deploys Compute Engine instances in the SVPC and peering projects plus optional Confidential Space VMs with a workload identity pool — mirroring TF 5-app-infra/modules/env_base. Additionally the README's File Structure table lists 'env_base.go' and 'confidential_space.go' while the actual files are example_env_base.go and example_confidential_space.go. Operators planning from ONBOARDING/ERRATA get a wrong picture of what Stage 5 provisions.

**Evidence:** pulumi_go-example-foundation/ONBOARDING.md ('Deploying Step 5' section) and docs/ERRATA.md:95-97 vs 5-app-infra/README.md:13-14,109-111 and actual files example_env_base.go/example_confidential_space.go (ls of 5-app-infra). TF baseline: terraform-example-foundation/5-app-infra/modules/env_base/README.md (compute instances).

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed via direct inspection. TF baseline (terraform-example-foundation/5-app-infra/modules/env_base/README.md, modules/confidential_space) deploys Compute Engine instances and Confidential Space VMs — no Cloud Run/BigQuery. The Go port's own 5-app-infra/README.md (lines 13-15) and actual code (example_env_base.go, example_confidential_space.go) correctly mirror this: compute instances in SVPC/peering projects plus an optional Confidential Space VM with a Workload Identity Pool. However, ONBOARDING.md:524 says the stage 'deploys sample application resources (Cloud Run, BigQuery)' and docs/ERRATA.md:95-97 says 'Stage 5 deploys a sample Cloud Run service and BigQuery dataset ... Confidential Space resources are not included' — both false and contradicted by the stage's own README/code. Additionally README.md's File Structure table (lines 109-111) lists 'env_base.go' and 'confidential_space.go' while the actual files (confirmed via ls) are example_env_base.go and example_confidential_space.go. This is a real internal-documentation inconsistency, not a resource gap (the resources exist and are correctly implemented).

**Verifier note / correction:** No correction needed to severity — 'minor' is accurate/not inflated, since this is purely a documentation/naming mismatch (ONBOARDING.md, ERRATA.md, README file table) with no effect on actual deployed infrastructure or code correctness. Worth noting as an aside (not part of the original claim) that the example_*.go files are also gated behind a `//go:build example` tag, so by default `pulumi up` deploys neither the compute instances nor Cloud Run/BigQuery — but that's a separate, undisputed fact and doesn't change the verdict on the documentation-inconsistency claim itself.

#### [BOTH] No multi-zone spreading: all instances pinned to <region>-a; available_zones fabricated or missing
_Area: 5-app-infra · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's terraform-google-modules/vm compute_instance module distributes num_instances across the region's available zones (google_compute_zones data source) and exports the real available_zones list. Both library ComputeInstance components take a single fixed zone and both ports pass `${region}-a`, so multiple instances all land in one zone. The TS example hardcodes available_zones/confidential_available_zones to ['<region>-a']; the Go port does not export them at all.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/5-app-infra/modules/env_base/main.tf module.compute_instance + outputs.tf available_zones. Ports: /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/compute_instance/compute_instance.go (Zone string), /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/instance-template/src/index.ts ComputeInstanceArgs.zone; go example_env_base.go line 98, ts index.ts.example lines 53/168/197-198.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF: env_base/main.tf and confidential_space/main.tf call terraform-google-modules/vm's compute_instance module, which spreads num_instances round-robin across google_compute_zones, and outputs.tf genuinely exports available_zones/confidential_available_zones up through the business_unit stage outputs (not fabricated on the TF side). However num_instances defaults to 1 and is never overridden in the shipped tfvars (the symlinked common.auto.tfvars files point to a nonexistent common.auto.tfvars, only common.auto.example.tfvars exists), so this capability is dormant in the reference example, and the TF integration test only ever reads instances_zones[0].

Port side: pulumi-library/go/pkg/compute_instance/compute_instance.go and pulumi-library/ts/packages/compute-instance/src/index.ts both take a single fixed `zone` field applied to every instance created in the NumInstances loop -- no zone cycling logic, and no google_compute_zones/getZones equivalent exists anywhere in pulumi-library (go or ts) or either port (confirmed via repo-wide grep for AvailableZones/available_zones/GetZones/google_compute_zones). Go's EnvBaseResult in example_env_base.go exports no AvailableZones field at all. TS's index.ts.example hardcodes available_zones/confidential_available_zones to a literal single-entry array `[pulumi.interpolate\`${region}-a\`]`, exactly as claimed -- not derived from any actual zones lookup. Notably the TS example does read num_instances from config (unlike Go's hardcoded 1), so a user setting num_instances>1 there would genuinely get all instances pinned to one zone, a real divergence from TF's spreading behavior. Neither port's test suite references this output.

The claimed 'minor' severity is accurate and not inflated: the available_zones output is purely informational and unconsumed downstream even in TF, and the shipped example configs default to a single instance where the zone-spreading gap can't manifest at all. No severity correction needed.

#### [BOTH] Region fallback diverges: hardcoded us-central1 instead of 4-projects default_region
_Area: 5-app-infra · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF envs compute region as coalesce(var.instance_region, default_region-from-4-projects-remote-state), so an unset region follows the foundation's configured default region. Both ports fall back to a hardcoded "us-central1" when the region config is unset, even though both 4-projects stacks export default_region — a foundation configured for another region would silently place 5-app-infra workloads in us-central1.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/5-app-infra/business_unit_1/*/main.tf (coalesce) + remote.tf (default_region). Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/5-app-infra/main.go lines 88-90; Go 4-projects exports default_region (main.go:133). TS: business_unit_1/*/index.ts line 18 and index.ts.example line 17; TS 4-projects exports default_region (dev index.ts:413).

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified end-to-end.

1) TF side confirmed: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/5-app-infra/business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production}/main.tf all compute `region = coalesce(var.instance_region, local.default_region)`, and remote.tf in each env sets `local.default_region = data.terraform_remote_state.projects_env.outputs.default_region`. So TF's fallback is the foundation's configured default region from 4-projects, not a hardcoded literal.

2) Go port confirmed missing: pulumi_go-example-foundation/5-app-infra/main.go loads `cfg.Region` from Pulumi config and, if empty, hardcodes `c.Region = "us-central1"` (lines ~88-90) — it never reads the `default_region` output from the `projStack` StackReference it creates (only `shared_vpc_project` is read from it). example_env_base.go and example_confidential_space.go both consume `args.Region` for the InstanceTemplate/ComputeInstance region, zone (`Region + "-a"`), and image path — real resources, not just an export. 4-projects/main.go:133 does export `default_region`, confirming the data is available but simply unused downstream.

3) TS port confirmed missing: all three business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts and index.ts.example files use `const region = config.get("region") || "us-central1"` (or `config.get("default_region") || "us-central1"` in 4-projects itself), and none of them call `projectRef.getOutput("default_region")` from the projects StackReference — only shared_vpc_project_id, peering outputs, IAP tags, confidential-space outputs are read from it. 4-projects/business_unit_1/development/index.ts:413 does export `default_region: config.get("default_region") || "us-central1"`, confirming it's available but unconsumed by 5-app-infra. The region value is genuinely used to build InstanceTemplate/ComputeInstance region/zone and to construct the shared-VPC subnetwork self-link (`sb-d-svpc-${region}`), so this is a real functional resource-placement input, not a cosmetic output.

I searched /home/user/pulumi-library (both go/ and ts/) for any region-resolution/coalesce helper or fallback component that might implement this indirectly — found none; the only default_region-adjacent hit is TS's own bootstrap.ts usage, unrelated to 5-app-infra's region resolution.

Conclusion: the claim is accurate as described — both ports hardcode "us-central1" instead of deriving the fallback from 4-projects' default_region output.

On severity: the reviewer labeled this "minor," but the evidence suggests the practical impact is arguably higher than "minor" implies — this isn't just a cosmetic export, it flows into real compute-instance/instance-template region and zone fields and into constructed subnetwork self-links. For any foundation configured with a non-default region (e.g., europe-west1), an unset `region` config in 5-app-infra would cause the port to reference a `us-central1` subnetwork that likely does not exist in the shared VPC host project (since 4-projects created subnets keyed to the real default_region), which would most likely cause an outright resource-creation failure (subnetwork not found) rather than a silent-but-working misplacement — a more disruptive outcome than "minor" suggests, though it only manifests for foundations not using us-central1 and only when the operator fails to explicitly set the region config (a documented, settable workaround exists in both ports' README/config).

**Verifier note / correction:** The gap is real, not refuted. However "minor" likely understates it: because the region flows into real resource fields (instance/template region+zone, and a constructed shared-VPC subnetwork self-link keyed by region) rather than just an informational output, a foundation configured for a non-us-central1 region will likely hit an outright subnetwork-not-found deployment failure if the `region` config is left unset — arguably a moderate/functional severity, not merely a minor/cosmetic divergence. It is mitigated by the fact that operators can work around it by explicitly setting the `region`/`instance_region` config value per stack.

#### [TS] SVPC subnetwork built from naming convention + undocumented config with empty-string fallback, not from remote state
_Area: 5-app-infra · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF selects the shared-VPC subnetwork by filtering the 4-projects subnets_self_links output for the target region. The TS example instead string-builds `projects/${config.get("shared_vpc_host_project") || ""}/regions/${region}/subnetworks/sb-{d|n|p}-svpc-${region}` — relying on a hardcoded naming convention and a shared_vpc_host_project config key that is absent from Pulumi.<env>.yaml.example (which instead documents a remote_state_bucket key the code never reads). If the key is unset, the subnetwork path is `projects//regions/...` and deployment fails.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/5-app-infra/modules/env_base/main.tf (svpc_subnetwork_self_link regex filter over subnets_self_links). TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/5-app-infra/business_unit_1/development/index.ts.example lines 40, 56, 153, 171; Pulumi.development.yaml.example (remote_state_bucket key unused by code, shared_vpc_host_project undocumented).

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF side: terraform-example-foundation/5-app-infra/modules/env_base/main.tf does exactly as described — it pulls data.terraform_remote_state.projects_env (which passes through 3-networks' subnets_self_links via 4-projects/modules/base_env/remote.tf), then does `[for subnet in local.subnetwork_self_links : subnet if length(regexall("regions/${var.region}/subnetworks", subnet)) > 0][0]` to dynamically pick the shared-VPC subnet for the target region.

TS side: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/5-app-infra/business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts.example (lines 40/56/153/171 in each) all string-build `projects/${config.get("shared_vpc_host_project") || ""}/regions/${region}/subnetworks/sb-{d|n|p}-svpc-${region}` instead of consulting any remote state. `shared_vpc_host_project` appears nowhere else in the repo except these occurrences and is absent from every Pulumi.<env>.yaml.example (which only documents `remote_state_bucket`, a key index.ts.example never reads). Grepped pulumi-library (both go/ and ts/) for any region-filter/self-link-selection helper — none exists; no equivalent mechanism lives anywhere else.

I also traced the plumbing that a correct fix would need, and it's broken upstream in the TS port too: 3-networks-svpc/envs/<env>/index.ts correctly exports the full `subnets_self_links` list (svpc.subnetsSelfLinks) mirroring TF, but 4-projects/business_unit_1/<env>/index.ts does NOT forward that — it overwrites its own `subnets_self_links` output with just `[peeringSubnet.selfLink]`, discarding the actual shared-VPC subnet list. So even if 5-app-infra tried to mirror TF's regex-filter approach against the current 4-projects stack reference, it would not find the svpc subnet. This confirms the gap is systemic, not just a naming difference — the dynamic remote-state-derived selection genuinely does not exist in the TS port, at any layer, under any name.

On severity: the claim's 'minor' rating is defensible but arguably understates it slightly. Unlike TF's business_unit_1/development/main.tf (the always-active default file), the TS workload is gated behind a manual index.ts.example -> index.ts copy step, so the default scaffold index.ts doesn't hit this code path. But once a user follows the documented Pulumi.<env>.yaml.example (setting only remote_state_bucket, which does nothing) and enables the example, deployment reliably breaks due to the empty-string fallback producing `projects//regions/.../subnetworks/...`. This is a guaranteed break for anyone following the documented setup for the intended feature, not merely cosmetic, so I'd call it low-to-moderate rather than purely minor, though 'minor' isn't unreasonable given it's gated behind opt-in example code.

**Verifier note / correction:** The gap is real and confirmed at every layer (index.ts.example naming-convention hack, undocumented shared_vpc_host_project key, dead remote_state_bucket key, and — additionally discovered — the 4-projects TS stack's subnets_self_links output itself doesn't forward the real svpc subnet list either, so there's no remote-state path available even if 5-app-infra tried to mirror TF). Severity is plausibly a notch above 'minor' since it guarantees deployment failure for anyone enabling the example workload per the documented config, but the reviewer's 'minor' tag is a reasonable call given this is gated behind an opt-in example file rather than the default active path.

#### [TS] Scaffold reads stack output 'shared_vpc_project' which the TS 4-projects stack does not export
_Area: 5-app-infra · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The active 5-app-infra scaffolds reference projectRef.getOutput("shared_vpc_project"), but the TS 4-projects env stacks export the key as shared_vpc_project_id (the Go port exports shared_vpc_project, and the TS .example files correctly use shared_vpc_project_id). The scaffold's project_id export therefore resolves to undefined — a copy-from-Go inconsistency within the TS port.

**Evidence:** TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/5-app-infra/business_unit_1/{production,development,nonproduction}/index.ts:17 (getOutput("shared_vpc_project")) vs 4-projects/business_unit_1/production/index.ts:399 (shared_vpc_project_id) and 5-app-infra .../index.ts.example:16 (shared_vpc_project_id). Go exports shared_vpc_project at 4-projects/main.go:112.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all fronts.

TF side: terraform-example-foundation's 4-projects/business_unit_1/{production,development,nonproduction}/outputs.tf export `shared_vpc_project` (module.env.shared_vpc_project), and 5-app-infra/modules/env_base/main.tf consumes exactly `data.terraform_remote_state.projects_env.outputs.shared_vpc_project`. So the TF reference genuinely uses the key name `shared_vpc_project`.

TS port: 4-projects/business_unit_1/{production,development,nonproduction}/index.ts (line 399) exports `shared_vpc_project_id: sharedVpcProject.projectId` — i.e., the TS port renamed the key to `shared_vpc_project_id`. But the active 5-app-infra scaffolds — business_unit_1/{production,development,nonproduction}/index.ts, all at line 17 — call `projectRef.getOutput("shared_vpc_project")`, the old/Go-style key. By contrast the corresponding index.ts.example files (the real compute-workload implementations, e.g. business_unit_1/production/index.ts.example line 16) correctly call `getOutput("shared_vpc_project_id")`. This is an internal inconsistency within the TS port itself — confirmed by reading both scaffold and example files side by side.

Go port: 4-projects/main.go:112 exports `shared_vpc_project`, and 5-app-infra/main.go:56 reads `shared_vpc_project` — self-consistent, no bug on the Go side.

pulumi-library: no references to `shared_vpc_project` at all in go/ or ts/, so there's no shared helper that reconciles or aliases the key — nothing masks the bug.

Effect: since Pulumi StackReference.getOutput resolves to `undefined` for an absent key (it doesn't throw), the scaffold's `project_id` export genuinely resolves to undefined when the scaffold is used as-is.

Severity check: the scaffold's projectId variable is used for exactly one thing — the `project_id` output — it is not consumed to parameterize any resource creation in the scaffold, and the file's own header comment explicitly instructs users to replace it with index.ts.example ("copy index.ts.example to index.ts (overwriting this scaffold)") before doing real work. So the blast radius is a broken informational output on an intentionally-throwaway placeholder file, not a broken deployment. "Minor" is an accurate (not inflated) severity rating here — I would not lower it further, but it's also not more severe than claimed.

#### [GO] env_base workload parameters hardcoded (machine_type, num_instances, source image) — TF exposes them as variables

One nuance for severity: I additionally confirmed example_env_base.go is entirely gated behind `//go:build example` and `deployEnvBase` is never called from main.go or anywhere else in the Go repo (grep across the whole repo shows zero call sites) — main.go explicitly documents "Actual compute workloads ... are defined in the example_*.go files which are excluded from compilation by default." #### [BOTH] No 'plan all environments' workflow for feature/plan branches
_Area: build · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF ships github-tf-plan-all.yaml (push to 'plan' branch runs tf-wrapper.sh plan_validate_all: init+plan+validate for every environment) and gitlab-ci.yml's terraform-plan job does the same for $CI_COMMIT_REF_NAME == 'plan'. The ports only preview the single stack matching a PR's target branch (pulumi-preview.yml on pull_request; gitlab preview on merge_request). There is no equivalent that validates all stacks from a non-environment branch, so cross-environment regressions are only caught one environment at a time at PR time.

**Evidence:** TF: build/github-tf-plan-all.yaml, build/gitlab-ci.yml terraform-plan job, tf-wrapper.sh plan_validate_all (lines 197-213). Ports: build/ contains only pulumi-preview.yml (PR-triggered, single stack), pulumi-up.yml, gitlab-ci.yml (no plan-branch job) in both ports.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

TF side (verified): build/github-tf-plan-all.yaml exists, triggers `on: push: branches: [plan]`, and runs `tf-wrapper.sh plan_validate_all "${GITHUB_REF_NAME}" ...` which (per tf_plan_validate_all, lines ~195-213) walks every component/environment directory under the stage and runs terraform init+plan+validate for each. gitlab-ci.yml's `terraform-plan` job does the same thing keyed off `$CI_COMMIT_REF_NAME == "plan"`. This is a real, distinct mechanism from the standard per-branch PR plan job (terraform-plan-mr / github-tf-pull-request.yaml), intended as a pre-check developers can push to a shared `plan` branch to validate a change against all environments before opening the real per-environment PRs (the 0-bootstrap README's `git push --set-upstream origin plan` steps corroborate this workflow).

Port side (both go and ts, exhaustively searched): build/ contains only pulumi-preview.yml (`on: pull_request` to development/nonproduction/production, previews only `github.base_ref`'s single stack), pulumi-up.yml (push-triggered per-branch `pulumi up`), and gitlab-ci.yml (preview on merge_request/target branch only, deploy on commit branch match). No 'plan' branch trigger, no all-environment loop, in either GitHub Actions or GitLab CI templates. Grepped pulumi-library (go/ and ts/), scripts/, helpers/, test/, and .github/workflows/ in both ports for plan_validate_all, plan-all, validate_all, all_environments, and related keywords/patterns — no hits of any kind.

The only adjacent artifact is helpers/foundation-deployer/deploy.sh, a manual local CLI (not CI-triggered, no policy/OPA validation step, loops over all *stages* rather than all *environments within one stage*, requires local credentials and interactive-ish setup) — explicitly documented as a stand-in for TF's separate `helpers/foundation-deployer` Go CLI, not for the plan-all CI workflow. It does not provide an automated, CI-driven cross-environment validation gate equivalent to github-tf-plan-all.yaml/gitlab terraform-plan.

Severity: the reviewer already rates this "minor," which is fair and not inflated — the gap is a missing developer convenience/pre-check tool, not a missing deployment gate: the standard per-environment PR preview/apply flow in the ports still functions and is the actual safety mechanism before any environment is mutated. The absence only removes an optional early-warning step for cross-environment issues before individual PRs are opened, which matches the reviewer's own framing ("cross-environment regressions are only caught one environment at a time at PR time"). No inflation to correct.

#### [BOTH] Shipped GitHub workflows don't match the secrets bootstrap provisions (Pulumi Cloud vs GCS backend mismatch)
_Area: build · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** 0-bootstrap provisions a PULUMI_BACKEND_URL Actions secret per stage repo with the comment that it exists 'so the pipeline templates (build/pulumi-preview.yml, build/pulumi-up.yml) work out of the box', pointing at the self-managed GCS state bucket (proj gets the isolated bucket). But the shipped workflow templates never reference PULUMI_BACKEND_URL and never run 'pulumi login gs://...' — they require a Pulumi Cloud PULUMI_ACCESS_TOKEN instead, which bootstrap explicitly does not provision. Out of the box the workflows therefore fail (missing access token) and the GCS-backend path TF-equivalent state flow has no working workflow variant. Applies identically to both ports' templates.

**Evidence:** Go: 0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.go lines 104-135, 164-171 (PULUMI_BACKEND_URL secret + comment); build/pulumi-preview.yml lines 21-24 and 60-67, build/pulumi-up.yml lines 58-63 (only PULUMI_ACCESS_TOKEN; grep 'PULUMI_BACKEND_URL' in build/ of both ports returns nothing). TS mirrors both files.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all fronts.

TF side (step 1): terraform-example-foundation's 0-bootstrap/build_github.tf.example provisions exactly the secrets the shipped GitHub Actions workflows (build/github-tf-apply.yaml, build/github-tf-plan-all.yaml) consume: PROJECT_ID, WIF_PROVIDER_NAME, SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL, and TF_BACKEND (the GCS state bucket name). The workflows literally sed TF_BACKEND into backend.tf files before running terraform init. So in the reference implementation, bootstrap-provisioned secrets and workflow consumption are in lockstep — this is the real pattern the claim says was broken in porting.

Port side (step 2): Verified in both pulumi_go-example-foundation and pulumi_ts-example-foundation:
- 0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.go (lines 104-171) / build_github_actions.ts provisions WIF_PROVIDER_NAME, SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL, PROJECT_ID, and PULUMI_BACKEND_URL (gs://<bucket>), with an explicit comment that this is "so the pipeline templates ... work out of the box with zero manual setup," while noting PULUMI_ACCESS_TOKEN is deliberately NOT provisioned.
- build/pulumi-preview.yml and build/pulumi-up.yml in both ports (and build/gitlab-ci.yml too) reference only `secrets.PULUMI_ACCESS_TOKEN` for the `pulumi/actions@v5` step; grepping both repos for PULUMI_BACKEND_URL only hits the bootstrap file itself — zero consumers. No `pulumi login gs://...` step, no `cloud-url` action input wiring it in.
- Checked pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) for any GitHubActionsBuild/cicd component that might inject the backend differently — no cloud-url/backend wiring there either.
- Checked scripts/ and helpers/foundation-deployer for a wrapper script (TF-wrapper.sh equivalent) that might inject the backend — none exists.
- Cross-checked 0-bootstrap/README.md: its documented manual setup explicitly tells users to add PULUMI_ACCESS_TOKEN and GOOGLE_CREDENTIALS by hand and never mentions PULUMI_BACKEND_URL at all — confirming the secret is genuinely dead/unused end-to-end, not just in the workflow file.
- Only the disabled build_cloud_build.go.example (an .example file not wired into main.go, requiring manual enablement) shows a correct `pulumi login gs://${_TF_BACKEND}` pattern, and that uses a manually-configured Cloud Build substitution variable, not the auto-provisioned PULUMI_BACKEND_URL secret — so it doesn't provide a working out-of-the-box GCS-backend GitHub/GitLab CI variant either.

This is a genuine, unrefuted internal inconsistency present identically in both ports: bootstrap auto-provisions PULUMI_BACKEND_URL claiming it enables out-of-the-box pipelines, but the actual shipped workflow templates require a manually-supplied PULUMI_ACCESS_TOKEN that bootstrap explicitly does not create, and PULUMI_BACKEND_URL is never consumed anywhere in either port or the shared library.

#### [GO] Terraform-to-Pulumi migration guide (MIGRATION.md) exists only in the TS port
_Area: docs · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The TS port ships docs/MIGRATION.md (232 lines: terraform state inventory, pulumi import workflow, terraform state rm sequencing, cutover checklist). The Go port has no migration or upgrade guide at all, so Go operators moving off an existing terraform-example-foundation deployment have no documented path. The TF repo's own upgrade guides (docs/upgrading_to_v2.0/v3.0/v4.0.md) are Terraform-version specific and not applicable to a fresh port, but the migration-from-Terraform guide is the analogous operator need and only one port has it (Go/TS drift).

**Evidence:** pulumi_ts-example-foundation/docs/MIGRATION.md exists; no counterpart in pulumi_go-example-foundation/docs/ (checked full find of *.md; Go docs dir has ENVIRONMENTS, ERRATA, FAQ, GITHUB_ADMIN, GLOSSARY, SOURCE_FILES, TROUBLESHOOTING, change_resource_hierarchy only). TF reference: terraform-example-foundation/docs/upgrading_to_v*.md.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified in full.

1. TF side: terraform-example-foundation/docs/ does contain upgrading_to_v2.0.md, v3.0.md, v4.0.md (29/143/32 lines) — these are real, and as the reviewer already concedes, they document TF-major-version upgrade breaking changes (no in-place upgrade path v1->v2->v3->v4), not "migrate off Terraform onto something else." So there is no literal TF counterpart to a "Terraform-to-Pulumi migration guide" — that's expected, since TF's own repo would have no reason to document migrating away from itself. This part of the claim is honestly framed as "the analogous operator need," not a direct port of existing TF content.

2. pulumi_ts-example-foundation/docs/MIGRATION.md exists (232 lines confirmed via wc -l, content read in full): covers three migration strategies (Import & Adopt, Side-by-Side, Destroy & Recreate), a Terraform-resource-to-Pulumi-resource mapping table, `pulumi import` / `terraform state rm` sequencing, bottom-up stage migration order, shared-state-bucket considerations, common pitfalls, and a verification checklist.

3. pulumi_go-example-foundation/docs/ was confirmed to contain only ENVIRONMENTS, ERRATA, FAQ, GITHUB_ADMIN, GLOSSARY, SOURCE_FILES, TROUBLESHOOTING, change_resource_hierarchy.md — no MIGRATION.md and no equivalent content under another name. I grepped the entire Go repo (all file types, not just .md) for migrat*/pulumi import/terraform state rm/cutover and got zero genuine hits (only unrelated matches in CHANGELOG.md, a README-GitHub.md, and directory names containing "foundation-..." substrings that happen to match unrelated regex, not actual migration guidance). Go's FAQ.md and TROUBLESHOOTING.md were read in full — they contain a brief "How is this different from the Terraform foundation?" comparison table but nothing about state import/cutover mechanics.

4. Checked /home/user/pulumi-library (both go/ and ts/) for migration-related content — no genuine hits; matches were false positives (GCP API service names like `bigquerymigration.googleapis.com`, and one unrelated "Migrate to the scope-specific constructors" deprecation note in iam/README.md).

This is a genuine Go/TS content-parity drift: TS operators get a documented, actionable playbook for adopting existing Terraform-managed GCP resources into the Pulumi port; Go operators get nothing equivalent anywhere in the Go repo or the shared library.

On severity: I'd keep "minor" as roughly accurate, possibly borderline "low-medium" rather than pure cosmetic — it's a real operational-documentation gap (not just informational output nothing consumes), but it's a missing doc file, not missing functionality/resources/tests, and Go users aren't blocked from migrating (they can adapt the TS guide manually, and the general FAQ table gives some orientation). So "minor" is a fair, not inflated, severity call.

#### [BOTH] Deploy helper lacks retryable-error handling and gcloud preflight checks
_Area: helpers · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Three upstream helper hardening changes have no port equivalent: 2646fa0 added retryable transient-error handling to the deploy helper, 2fcd799 added Cloud Build build retry, and baaf30e added validation that the required gcloud beta components are installed (main.go/stages/validate.go/stages/vet.go). The ports' shared deploy.sh (byte-identical in Go and TS ports) runs 'pulumi up --yes --skip-preview' per stack under 'set -euo pipefail' with zero retry logic and no preflight tool checks, so any transient GCP error aborts a multi-stage foundation deployment midway. Notably the ports' own KMS usage tracking path depends on 'gcloud beta' (TS runs it via command.local.Command) yet nothing verifies the beta component is installed.

**Evidence:** TF: helpers/foundation-deployer commits 2646fa0 (retryable error handling), 2fcd799 (Cloud Build retry), baaf30e (beta components check in helpers/foundation-deployer/gcp/gcp.go, stages/validate.go, stages/vet.go). Checked: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/helpers/foundation-deployer/deploy.sh and /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/helpers/foundation-deployer/deploy.sh (identical, 278 lines, no retry/validate); grep 'retry|beta' in both deploy.sh files empty except billing call in scripts/validate-requirements.sh.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed: 2646fa0 (helpers/foundation-deployer/stages/{apply,data,destroy,vet}.go) adds terratest RetryableTerraformErrors/MaxRetries/TimeBetweenRetries to every terraform.Options call; 2fcd799 (gcp/gcp.go, main.go, stages/*) adds Cloud Build build-retry logic; baaf30e adds gcp.go:IsComponentInstalled plus stages/validate.go:ValidateComponents (checks 'beta' and 'terraform-tools' gcloud components) called from main.go before any stage runs. All three are real and exist as described.

Port side: helpers/foundation-deployer/deploy.sh is confirmed byte-identical (278 lines) across pulumi_go-example-foundation and pulumi_ts-example-foundation, runs `pulumi preview`/`pulumi up --yes --skip-preview`/`pulumi destroy` per stack under `set -euo pipefail` with no retry loop, no catch/backoff, and no component checks. I grepped both ports and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) for retry/backoff/component-check equivalents under any name (retry, backoff, MaxRetries, RetryPolicy, customTimeouts, IsComponentInstalled, gcloud components) and found none — the only 'retry' hits are documentation telling the human operator to manually rerun after a transient error (docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md, 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/README.md), confirming the port authors knew about transient GCP errors but implemented no automated handling. scripts/validate-requirements.sh (a real preflight script both ports do have) checks pulumi/go/gcloud/git/jq versions plus IAM roles and enabled APIs, but never checks gcloud component installation, and is not invoked automatically by deploy.sh or wired into any 'deploy' Makefile target — it's a separate manual `make validate` step. The claim's specific detail that the TS port's KMS-usage-tracking path shells out to `gcloud beta services identity create` via command.local.Command (1-org/iam.ts) is verified true, with no preflight check for the 'beta' component anywhere.

So the core claim holds: no automated retryable-error handling and no gcloud component preflight check exist in either port's deploy path.

**Verifier note / correction:** The gap is real but slightly narrower than described in two respects the reviewer didn't flag: (1) the 'terraform-tools' component check is not actually applicable to the ports — TF uses it because `gcloud beta terraform vet` performs policy validation, but the ports replaced that entirely with Pulumi CrossGuard policy packs (policy-library/), so there's no gcloud terraform-vet dependency to preflight-check in the first place. (2) The 'beta' component dependency is asymmetric between ports: the Go port's KMS org-service-identity step uses the native `gcp.projects.NewServiceIdentity` resource (1-org/iam.go, explicitly commented as a fix to avoid the gcloud shell-out), so it has no gcloud-beta dependency at all; only the TS port's iam.ts genuinely depends on `gcloud beta`. Severity 'minor' looks calibrated correctly rather than inflated: deploy.sh's --stage/--stack flags let an operator resume a failed multi-stage deploy from the point of failure rather than restarting from scratch, and Pulumi's native state management makes partial-apply recovery inherently less fragile than raw Terraform backend/state handling, both of which blunt the real-world impact of the missing automated retry.

#### [BOTH] User permission validation via TestIamPermissions (iam-validate) not ported
_Area: helpers · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Upstream commit 34af7b6 (2026-06-22) added a preflight IAM validator to the foundation-deployer: cmd/iam-validate CLI + utils/iam_validate.go (467 lines) that checks the deploying user's EFFECTIVE permissions on the org, billing account and folder using the TestIamPermissions API against a YAML permission spec (examples/iam/default-permissions.yaml), wired into stages/validate.go. Neither port has any equivalent: helpers/foundation-deployer contains only README.md and a 278-line deploy.sh with no validation step, and grep for TestIamPermissions across both ports and pulumi-library returns nothing. The ports' scripts/validate-requirements.sh only greps role bindings from get-iam-policy output (the older, weaker mechanism), which misses inherited/denied permissions the new API check catches.

**Evidence:** TF: helpers/foundation-deployer/cmd/iam-validate/main.go, helpers/foundation-deployer/utils/iam_validate.go, helpers/foundation-deployer/examples/iam/default-permissions.yaml, helpers/foundation-deployer/stages/validate.go (commit 34af7b6). Checked: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/helpers/foundation-deployer/ and /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/helpers/foundation-deployer/ (README.md + deploy.sh only); grep 'TestIamPermissions|testIamPermissions' across all three port repos empty; /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/scripts/validate-requirements.sh:166 (role-grep approach).

**Verifier reasoning:** Terraform side confirmed: commit 34af7b6 (2026-06-22, "feat: user validation using TestIamPermissions api") adds helpers/foundation-deployer/cmd/iam-validate/main.go, utils/iam_validate.go (467 lines using cloud.google.com/go/{resourcemanager,billing,iam} client libraries and the TestIamPermissions API), examples/iam/default-permissions.yaml, and wires ValidateIAMPermissions() into stages/validate.go's ValidateBasicFields, called from the main "-validate" flag path. This checks effective org/folder/billing permissions for the ADC principal via TestIamPermissions, which correctly accounts for inherited role bindings, group membership, and deny policies -- something a get-iam-policy binding grep cannot do.

Exhaustive search of both ports and pulumi-library found no equivalent under any name: helpers/foundation-deployer in both pulumi_go and pulumi_ts repos contains only README.md and deploy.sh; grep for TestIamPermissions, iam-validate, iam_validate, IAMValidate, default-permissions, orgPermissions/folderPermissions/billingPermissions, preflight/CheckIAM/verifyPermission variants, and the underlying GCP client packages (resourcemanager/apiv3, iam/apiv1/iampb, billing/apiv1) all returned zero hits across pulumi_go-example-foundation, pulumi_ts-example-foundation, and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/). scripts/validate-requirements.sh in both ports independently confirms the reviewer's claim: it only greps gcloud get-iam-policy role bindings for a handful of hardcoded roles (roles/resourcemanager.organizationAdmin, orgpolicy.policyAdmin, etc.) and treats unverifiable bindings as warnings, not the newer effective-permission API check. The ports' own docs (ERRATA.md, foundation-deployer README) document other deliberate TF-vs-Pulumi differences but do not call out IAM permission validation as an intentional omission -- consistent with this simply being a very recent upstream addition (11 days before the stated current date) that hasn't been ported yet, rather than a deliberate design choice.

The claimed "minor" severity is honest and, if anything, could be argued lower rather than higher: I traced the TF code path and confirmed ValidateIAMPermissions/checkOrgPermissions/checkFolderPermissions/checkBillingPermissions never call os.Exit and their errors are only printed ("# Error validating IAM permissions: %v") by the caller, not propagated to halt the deploy-validate flow. It is a pure diagnostic/informational preflight print of missing permissions, not a hard gate blocking deployment. So the gap is real but its practical impact is limited to reduced pre-flight diagnostics quality, exactly matching a "minor" classification.

**Verifier note / correction:** Severity of "minor" is accurate as stated; worth adding that the upstream feature itself is purely advisory (prints missing permissions, never blocks/exits), so the practical user impact of the gap is limited to weaker/less accurate pre-flight diagnostics, not any loss of safety enforcement.

TF: appengine_versions.yaml exists exactly as described (GCPAppEngineServiceVersionsConstraintV1, name service_versions, org-wide match, operational-efficiency scorecard tag). Ports: exhaustive grep (appengine, app.engine, service.version, GCPAppEngine, case-insensitive) across pulumi_go-example-foundation, pulumi_ts-example-foundation, and pulumi-library (both go/ and ts/) found zero equivalent policy. The only 'appengine' hits in the ports are appengine.googleapis.com API-enablement strings in 0-bootstrap (mirroring TF's own activate_apis list) — unrelated to a service-version-limiting check. The TS port's index.ts (which the Go port's own README designates as the canonical shared implementation for both ports) implements only ~9 policies total versus 28 constraint files in the TF reference, and its README's explicit 'Relationship to Upstream Terraform Policies' mapping table does not list appengine_versions/GCPAppEngineServiceVersionsConstraintV1 among covered items, confirming this isn't a naming mismatch but a genuine omission.

#### [BOTH] DNSSEC RSASHA1 constraints missing
_Area: policy-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF enforces dnssec_prevent_rsasha1_ksk.yaml and dnssec_prevent_rsasha1_zsk.yaml (GCPDNSSECPreventRSASHA1ConstraintV1, severity high) forbidding the weak RSASHA1 algorithm for DNSSEC key-signing and zone-signing keys. No port policy examines gcp:dns/managedZone:ManagedZone dnssecConfig.defaultKeySpecs algorithms, and the pulumi-library cloud-dns/dns-hub components contain no such validation.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/policies/constraints/dnssec_prevent_rsasha1_{ksk,zsk}.yaml. Port: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts — no dns reference; /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/cloud-dns and dns-hub checked, no algorithm validation.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed exactly as described: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/policies/constraints/dnssec_prevent_rsasha1_ksk.yaml and _zsk.yaml both exist, kind GCPDNSSECPreventRSASHA1ConstraintV1, severity: high, parameters keyType: KEY_SIGNING / ZONE_SIGNING.

Port side: exhaustively searched both port repos and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) for dnssec, rsasha, managedzone, algorithm, keySpec, DefaultKeySpec, etc.
- ts port policy-library/index.ts (144 lines, full contents read) implements exactly 7 CrossGuard policies (no-default-network, project-labels-required, no-sa-key-creation, no-public-access, no-public-ip, require-private-google-access, require-flow-logs). None touch DNS/DNSSEC.
- go port policy-library/ contains only a README that explicitly delegates to the TS policy pack ("CrossGuard policy packs for Go-based Pulumi programs are written in TypeScript... see the TypeScript foundation's policy-library"). So both ports share the same 7-policy set — no DNSSEC coverage in either.
- pulumi-library DNS components: go/pkg/cloud_dns/cloud_dns.go, ts/packages/cloud-dns/src/index.ts, go/pkg/networking/dns.go, and ts/packages/dns-hub/src/index.ts were all inspected. All of them expose only EnableDnssec/DnssecState (mapping to dnssecConfig.state: on/off/transfer) — none expose or set defaultKeySpecs/algorithm at all, and none validate it.
- Checked actual stage usage (grep for ManagedZone/dnssecConfig across both port repos): the only raw gcp.dns.ManagedZone calls found are private zones in 3-networks-svpc/3-networks-hub-and-spoke shared_vpc.ts for googleapis/gcr/pkg-dev private DNS — these don't set dnssecConfig at all (private zones don't use DNSSEC), consistent with no algorithm-setting code existing anywhere in either port.

So the claim is accurate: there is no RSASHA1-prevention policy equivalent anywhere in either port or the shared library, under any name.

On severity: reviewer's "minor" is a reasonable, not inflated, call for two reasons: (1) this is one specific instance of a much larger, already-systemic gap — the ports' policy-library implements only 7 of the 28 constraints TF defines, so DNSSEC-RSASHA1 isn't a unique oversight but one of ~21 similarly-uncovered TF constraints; (2) practical exploitability is further limited because none of the DNS components in either port expose an algorithm/keySpec parameter at all — a user cannot trigger RSASHA1 through any documented/reusable component path, only via hand-written raw resource code that doesn't currently exist in these repos. Given that, "minor" is defensible and I would not correct it upward or downward.

#### [BOTH] Flow-logs check downgraded from build-failing (high severity) to advisory
_Area: policy-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's network_enable_flow_logs.yaml is severity: high and, like all constraints, fails the build via tf-wrapper.sh validate (exit 33). The port's equivalent 'require-flow-logs' is enforcementLevel: 'advisory' — it only warns and never blocks. This is a deliberate-looking but undocumented enforcement downgrade relative to upstream. (The other ported policies keep 'mandatory'.)

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/policies/constraints/network_enable_flow_logs.yaml (severity: high) + build/tf-wrapper.sh:278 (hard failure). Port: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts:128-142 (enforcementLevel: "advisory").

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim confirmed on both the TF and port sides.

TF side: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/policies/constraints/network_enable_flow_logs.yaml sets `severity: high`. tf-wrapper.sh's `tf_validate()` (build/tf-wrapper.sh:278) runs `gcloud beta terraform vet ... || exit 33` with no severity filtering — i.e. ANY OPA constraint violation (this one included) is a hard build failure, exactly as the claim states. Checking all 27 constraint YAMLs, essentially every one (including the directly analogous `network_enable_private_google_access.yaml` and `iam_deny_public.yaml`) is `severity: high`.

Port side: confirmed in /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts:128-142 — `require-flow-logs` has `enforcementLevel: "advisory"`. This is the sole shared CrossGuard policy pack: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/policy-library/README.md explicitly states the Go port has no policy code of its own and delegates to the TS pack (`--policy-pack /path/to/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library`), so the claim applies identically to both ports as stated. I grepped both port repos and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) for flow-log-related keywords (flowLogs, logConfig, enableFlowLogs) and found no second/alternate blocking check anywhere — this is the only enforcement point.

By contrast, the directly comparable network policy `require-private-google-access` (mapping to `network_enable_private_google_access.yaml`, also severity: high in TF) is kept `enforcementLevel: "mandatory"` in the same file, and so is `no-public-ip` / `no-public-access` — confirming the flow-logs check is singled out for a downgrade rather than this being a pack-wide policy of leniency. The port's own policy-library README (103 lines) documents a partial upstream-mapping table but never mentions or justifies this specific severity/enforcement discrepancy — the downgrade is indeed undocumented.

On severity: I found mitigating context that supports (not undermines) the "minor" label rather than inflating it — actual stage code in both ports (3-networks-svpc/main.go, 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go, and the TS envs/*/index.ts files) explicitly passes `FlowLogs: true` / `subnetFlowLogs: true` for every foundation subnet, so the currently-shipped reference subnets are already compliant; the advisory policy would only fail to catch a future regression (e.g. someone adding a new subnet without flow logs), and even then only warns rather than silently doing nothing. I also found the policy pack isn't wired into any CI/build script in either port (no `--policy-pack`/`policyPacks` references under build/ or .github/ in either repo), meaning the mandatory/advisory distinction currently has no automated teeth either way — a separate, arguably more significant gap, but one that doesn't change the assessment of this specific claim.

#### [GO] Go policy-library is README-only: the CrossGuard pack lives in the TS repo and is not in pulumi-library
_Area: policy-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The Go port's policy-library/ contains only README.md. The README opens with 'This directory contains Pulumi CrossGuard policy pack configuration' but ships no code; operators are told to clone the separate pulumi_ts-example-foundation repo to get the actual pack. The shared pulumi-library has no policy pack package either (go/pkg/policy and ts/packages/org-policy are org-policy components, not CrossGuard packs). This is a defensible design (the CrossGuard SDK is not available for Go authoring), but it is a cross-repo runtime dependency for the documented 'preview-time policy validation' capability, and it directly contradicts the Go ERRATA which says no policy validation infrastructure exists at all.

**Evidence:** ls pulumi_go-example-foundation/policy-library/ → README.md only; README.md lines 1-16 and Usage section instruct cloning github.com/VitruvianSoftware/pulumi_ts-example-foundation. pulumi-library checked: go/pkg/ and ts/packages/ listings contain no CrossGuard policy pack. TF baseline: terraform-example-foundation policy-library (full OPA constraint set, referenced from TF READMEs).

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified directly. TF terraform-example-foundation/policy-library has 112 files of real OPA constraint templates/lib (policies/constraints, policies/templates, lib/constraints.rego, lib/util.rego). The Go port's policy-library/ contains only README.md, which explicitly states CrossGuard is TypeScript-only and instructs cloning github.com/VitruvianSoftware/pulumi_ts-example-foundation to get 'the shared CrossGuard policy pack used by both foundations.' The TS repo's policy-library/index.ts confirms a real, working CrossGuard PolicyPack with 7 concrete validateResource policies (no-default-network, project-labels-required, no-sa-key-creation, no-public-access, no-public-ip, require-private-google-access, require-flow-logs). I checked pulumi-library thoroughly (go/pkg/policy and ts/packages/org-policy): both are deploy-time gcp.orgpolicy.Policy wrapper components (organization policy constraints applied via the GCP Org Policy API), not preview-time CrossGuard policy packs — confirming they are not an equivalent. Grepping all of pulumi-library (go and ts) and the Go port's build/CI (gitlab-ci.yml, pulumi-preview.yml, pulumi-up.yml) and test/ directory for 'crossguard' or 'policy-pack' returns zero hits, so this capability is not wired into any pipeline for either port — it is purely optional/manual, matching the README's manual clone-and-flag instructions.

**Verifier note / correction:** The core gap is real, but one supporting detail in the claim is overstated: it says the README-only design 'directly contradicts the Go ERRATA which says no policy validation infrastructure exists at all.' That's not accurate — the actual ERRATA text reads: 'Does not include policy validation infrastructure... Policy enforcement is handled via organization policies deployed in Stage 1 and via Pulumi's CrossGuard policy-as-code framework, which can be adopted separately.' That is consistent with, not contradicted by, the README's cross-repo CrossGuard instructions — the ERRATA explicitly anticipates CrossGuard as a separately-adopted option, so the two documents agree rather than conflict. Severity of 'minor' as claimed is fair and not inflated — arguably borderline 'informational,' since neither port wires --policy-pack into any CI/build step (grep across gitlab-ci.yml, pulumi-preview.yml, pulumi-up.yml in both repos found zero policy/crossguard references), so nothing is silently broken or enforced-then-missing; it is a documented, opt-in, cross-repo manual step.

#### [GO] Policy validation not wired into pipelines; Go repo ships no policy pack
_Area: policy-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's pipeline (build/tf-wrapper.sh) runs gcloud beta terraform vet against the shipped policy-library/ constraints on every plan. The Go port's policy-library/ contains only a README that points to the TS repo's CrossGuard pack, and the GitHub Actions templates (build/pulumi-preview.yml, build/pulumi-up.yml) never pass --policy-pack, so no policy validation runs in the default CI flow — the guardrail is documentation-only.

**Evidence:** TF: terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/{lib,policies} + build/tf-wrapper.sh:236-260 (tf_validate with policy_source). Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/policy-library/ contains only README.md; grep 'policy-pack' over pulumi_go-example-foundation/build/ returns nothing.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed: terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/{lib,policies/constraints,policies/templates} exists, and build/tf-wrapper.sh's tf_validate() (around line 278) runs `gcloud beta terraform vet "${tf_file}.json" --policy-library="${policy_file_path}"`. This is wired into CI by default: build/github-tf-pull-request.yaml and github-tf-apply.yaml invoke `tf-wrapper.sh plan_validate_all/validate ... "${GITHUB_WORKSPACE}/policy-library" ...` unconditionally.

Go port confirmed absent: pulumi_go-example-foundation/policy-library/ contains only README.md (no PolicyPack code — impossible in Go anyway since Pulumi's Policy SDK is TS/Python-only, as the README itself states). Exhaustive search of pulumi_go-example-foundation/build/*.yml (pulumi-preview.yml, pulumi-up.yml, gitlab-ci.yml) and the repo's actual .github/workflows/*.yml (ci.yml, e2e.yml, release.yml, commitlint.yml) shows zero occurrences of 'policy', 'policy-pack', 'PolicyPack', or 'CrossGuard' anywhere in CI wiring. pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) has no PolicyPack/CrossGuard component either — the only 'policy' hit there is pkg/policy/, a GCP OrgPolicy API wrapper (org_policy.go), a fundamentally different mechanism (declarative org constraints applied at deploy time via the orgpolicy.Policy resource, not a pre-apply plan-vetting step) — it is wired into 1-org/policies.go, but that's not equivalent to terraform-vet/CrossGuard preview-time validation.

Notably, the TS port's own build/pulumi-preview.yml and build/pulumi-up.yml also do NOT pass --policy-pack despite TS actually having a working CrossGuard pack (policy-library/index.ts, 144 lines, implementing no-default-network, no-sa-key-creation, no-public-access, etc.) — so 'guardrail is documentation-only in the default CI flow' is accurate and is not unique to a lazy Go port; it's a foundation-wide gap in the shipped CI templates, which the claim correctly scopes to the Go-specific instance (no pack exists at all, vs. TS's pack existing but unwired).

This is also explicitly self-disclosed by the port authors in docs/ERRATA.md under 'Policy Validation': 'This port: Does not include policy validation infrastructure. Pulumi does not have a direct equivalent of terraform vet. Policy enforcement is handled via organization policies deployed in Stage 1 and via Pulumi's CrossGuard... which can be adopted separately.' This confirms the claim is real and is a known, intentional, documented limitation rather than a hidden defect.

**Verifier note / correction:** The gap is real, but 'minor' severity is arguably fair-to-generous rather than inflated: it's an explicitly documented, deliberate design tradeoff (docs/ERRATA.md), not a silent regression, and a compensating guardrail exists via GCP native Organization Policy constraints (pulumi-library/go/pkg/policy, wired into 1-org/policies.go) enforcing boolean/list constraints like iam.disableServiceAccountKeyCreation and compute.vmExternalIpAccess at the API level — a different but real preventive control layer, distinct from the plan-time CrossGuard/terraform-vet check the claim addresses. Also worth noting: this gap is not truly Go-specific in practice — the TS port, despite having a functioning CrossGuard pack, also fails to wire --policy-pack into its own build/pulumi-preview.yml and build/pulumi-up.yml templates, so 'no policy validation runs in the default CI flow' holds foundation-wide, not just for Go.

#### [TS] README documents two policies (deletion-policy-prevent, restrict-owner-role) that are not implemented
_Area: policy-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The TS README's policy tables list 9 policies including 'deletion-policy-prevent' (projects should use PREVENT deletion policy) and 'restrict-owner-role' (roles/owner should not be used in IAM bindings), and describe a policies/ directory with project-policies.ts, iam-policies.ts and network-policies.ts. index.ts implements only 7 policies inline and no policies/ directory exists — the two documented advisory policies are placeholders with no code. (The Go README omits these two policies from its table, an additional Go/TS doc drift.)

**Evidence:** Documented: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/README.md:11-21,31,39. Implemented: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts (policies: no-default-network, project-labels-required, no-sa-key-creation, no-public-access, no-public-ip, require-private-google-access, require-flow-logs only). No policies/ directory exists.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed literally: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts has only 7 policies (no-default-network, project-labels-required, no-sa-key-creation, no-public-access, no-public-ip, require-private-google-access, require-flow-logs); no policies/ directory or project-policies.ts/iam-policies.ts/network-policies.ts exist; the README documents 9 policies and describes that directory structure. The Go port's README correctly lists only the 7 real ones, confirming the TS-only drift. However, the two 'missing' behaviors are not actually absent from the port's functionality: deletionPolicy defaults to 'PREVENT' directly on every project resource across 0-bootstrap/1-org/2-environments/4-projects (TS) and in pulumi-library/ts's project-factory and bootstrap packages — mirroring how upstream Terraform enforces this (a google_project resource attribute default, not an OPA constraint — TF's policy-library has no such constraint either). And roles/owner is actively monitored via the CAIMonitoring component wired into 1-org/index.ts (rolesToMonitor includes 'roles/owner'), raising SCC findings on grants of that role — exactly matching upstream TF's 1-org/modules/cai-monitoring mechanism (TF's own policy-library also lacks an instantiated 'ban owner role' OPA constraint; that capability only exists as an unused generic template, gcp_iam_allow_ban_roles_v1.yaml).

**Verifier note / correction:** The gap is real but is a documentation-accuracy defect confined to the policy-library README's self-description (wrong policy count, an invented policies/ subdirectory layout), not a missing-compliance-functionality gap. Both underlying safeguards described by the two phantom entries are actually implemented and enforced elsewhere in the TS port — deletionPolicy defaults to PREVENT baked directly into every project resource, and roles/owner grants are monitored via the CAIMonitoring→SCC-findings pipeline — using the same mechanisms (resource defaults + CAI alerting, not OPA/CrossGuard denial) that upstream Terraform itself uses for these two concerns. Severity 'minor' is appropriate for a doc-fix, but the framing should be 'README overstates the policy-library package's own file structure and count' rather than 'two compliance policies are unenforced.'
