#### [TS] Centralized logging: Log Analytics linked BigQuery dataset and folder-scoped sinks unsupported
_Area: pulumi-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's centralized-logging project_options enable Log Analytics (enable_analytics=true) and create a google_logging_linked_dataset (ds_c_prj_aggregated_logs_analytics), and the module supports resource_type=folder for parent_folder deployments; Go implements NewLinkedDataset and folder handling. The TS package creates a ProjectBucketConfig without enableAnalytics, has no LinkedDataset resource, only supports gcp.logging.OrganizationSink (no folder sinks), and its linkedDatasetName output is populated from the separate BigQuery export dataset instead — so the exported logs_export_project_linked_dataset_name is semantically wrong.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/modules/centralized-logging/main.tf:174-176, variables.tf:79-81, outputs.tf:32-34. Go: /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/logging/centralized_logging.go:239-250. TS: /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts:76-132 (no LinkedDataset/enableAnalytics; _linkedDatasetName set from BQ dataset at line 131; OrganizationSink only).

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all three legs. TF centralized-logging module (main.tf:174-187, variables.tf:66-90, outputs.tf:32-34) genuinely supports enable_analytics + linked_dataset_id/description creating a google_logging_linked_dataset, and resource_type genuinely includes 'folder' (used in practice by 1-org/envs/shared/log_sinks.tf:18-19 for parent_folder-scoped deployments, with linked_dataset_id='ds_c_prj_aggregated_logs_analytics' at line 83, exported at outputs.tf:112 as logs_export_project_linked_dataset_name). Go's centralized_logging.go correctly implements EnableAnalytics/LinkedDatasetID via logging.NewLinkedDataset (lines ~239-250) and threads ResourceType through to folder/org sinks. The TS package (pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts) has no enableAnalytics field on its ProjectBucketConfig call, no LinkedDataset resource anywhere in the file, hardcodes an orgId parameter with only gcp.logging.OrganizationSink (no folder/resourceType support at all), and sets _linkedDatasetName from the unrelated BigQuery export dataset (bigqueryOptions.datasetName) rather than any Log Analytics linked dataset -- exactly as described. I additionally verified the folder-scoping gap is live, not theoretical: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/config.ts already computes parentFolder/parentType/parentId (used elsewhere, e.g. iam.ts:38,46,130) but 1-org/index.ts's CentralizedLogging(...) call (~line 328) passes orgId: cfg.orgId unconditionally, ignoring parentFolder, so folder-scoped deployments get an incorrectly org-scoped sink. I also found a separate ts library package (pulumi-library/ts/packages/log-export) that does implement gcp.logging.FolderSink and a resourceType switch, but it is not imported or used by the centralized-logging package that's actually wired into the port -- so the capability doesn't exist where it's needed, and this doesn't refute the claim.

**Verifier note / correction:** The claim and evidence are accurate; only a minor severity nuance. The wrong linkedDatasetName output is purely cosmetic in practice -- no code in the ts port or library consumes logs_export_project_linked_dataset_name as an input, it's a leaf stack output -- so 'minor' fits that part well. But the missing folder-scoped resourceType support is arguably more than cosmetic: it's a live, wired-up gap (config.ts already tracks parentFolder for exactly this purpose) that would silently produce an incorrectly org-scoped sink instead of a folder-scoped one for parent_folder deployments, which is closer to a correctness bug for that topology than a pure omission. Severity could reasonably be bumped from 'minor' to 'moderate' on that basis, though 'minor' is not unreasonable given the primary observable defect (wrong output value) has no consumers.

#### [GO] Private Service Access range auto-allocated (/16, no address) instead of TF's per-env private_service_cidr
_Area: pulumi-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** pkg/networking's EnablePSA creates a GlobalAddress with PrefixLength: 16 and no Address, letting GCP pick an arbitrary /16, and neither NetworkingArgs nor the 3-networks stages expose a private_service_cidr option. TF reserves the exact documented range per environment (dev 10.16.40.0/21, nonprod 10.16.48.0/21, prod 10.16.56.0/21) via address + prefix_length split from var.private_service_cidr. The foundation's IP address plan for service networking is therefore not reproducible with the Go port.

**Evidence:** Go: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/networking.go:138-158 (PrefixLength 16, no Address); pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go:84 (EnablePSA: true, no CIDR). TF: 3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc/main.tf:74-87 (address/prefix from private_service_cidr) and envs/*/main.tf private_service_cidr values.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF side: 3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc/main.tf:73-84 creates google_compute_global_address with address/prefix_length split from var.private_service_cidr, and envs/{development,nonproduction,production}/main.tf set private_service_cidr to 10.16.40.0/21, 10.16.48.0/21, 10.16.56.0/21 respectively (same pattern duplicated in 3-networks-hub-and-spoke). Go port side: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/networking.go's NewNetworking creates the PSA GlobalAddress with only PrefixLength: pulumi.Int(16) and no Address field set (~lines 139-149), and NetworkingArgs has no CIDR-related field at all. pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go calls EnablePSA: true with no CIDR passed anywhere, and its config loader (loadNetConfig) has no private_service_cidr/psa_cidr config key (grep across the repo's Go files and Pulumi.*.yaml stack configs found nothing). I checked the wider pulumi-library for alternative spellings/locations (PrivateServiceConnect component, private_service_connect package, cb-private-pool) — those serve unrelated purposes (PSC endpoint IP for Google APIs, Cloud Build private-pool peering) and DO set a specific reserved Address there, proving the SDK's compute.GlobalAddressArgs supports an Address field and was simply not wired through for the PSA path in pkg/networking. So the gap is real: there is no way in the Go port to pin the PSA range to the documented per-env /21s; GCP will auto-select an arbitrary /16 from the VPC's available space. However, TF's private_service_cidr is consumed only by this one resource (the reserved range's address/name) — it is not referenced by any firewall rule, VPC-SC perimeter, or other downstream resource in the reference implementation. So the practical blast radius is limited to IP-plan documentation/reproducibility and predictability of the reserved range (and reserving a broader /16 instead of the intended /21), not a functional break of other components.

**Verifier note / correction:** Gap confirmed as described; the claimed 'minor' severity is honest and not inflated — the CIDR is unused elsewhere in TF's resource graph, so real-world impact is non-reproducible/undocumented IP allocation and over-broad (/16 vs /21) space reservation, not breakage of other foundation components.

#### [TS] cb-private-pool vpnConfiguration accepted but ignored — no HA VPN to on-prem
_Area: pulumi-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's 0-bootstrap cb-private-pool module builds an HA VPN (gateway, tunnels, router, PSK from Secret Manager) to on-prem when vpn_configuration.enable_vpn = true, so private workers can reach on-prem systems. The TS CbPrivatePool declares a VpnConfig arg ({enableVpn}) but the constructor never references it; the foundation-vpn-ha TS package exists but is wired to nothing. (Verified the Go library/bootstrap also lack this.)

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/vpn_ha.tf:19-32 (module vpn_ha_cb_to_onprem gated on enable_vpn). TS: /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool/src/index.ts:20-22,35 (VpnConfig declared, unused in constructor lines 45-161); 0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.ts:139 passes {enableVpn: false}; grep VpnHa in the port returns only the unused library package.

**Verifier reasoning:** Fully verified against both sides.

TF reference: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/vpn_ha.tf builds a real HA VPN to on-prem (google_secret_manager_secret_version.psk, module "vpn_ha_cb_to_onprem" with peer_external_gateway, tunnels, shared_secret from Secret Manager, router_advertise_config), all gated on `var.vpn_configuration.enable_vpn`. Confirmed as described (though note TF's own top-level build_cb.tf also defaults enable_vpn=false, so it's an opt-in feature even upstream).

TS port: /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool/src/index.ts defines `VpnConfig { enableVpn?: boolean }` and accepts `vpnConfiguration?: VpnConfig` in CbPrivatePoolArgs, but the constructor (lines 45-161) never references `args.vpnConfiguration` anywhere — no VPN gateway, tunnel, router, or secret lookup is created regardless of the flag's value.

The library does contain a real, functionally-equivalent VpnHa component at /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/vpn-ha/src/index.ts (HaVpnGateway, Router, ExternalVpnGateway, VPNTunnel, RouterInterface/RouterPeer for BGP) — this mirrors terraform-google-modules/vpn/google//modules/vpn_ha reasonably well. But it is never imported by cb-private-pool: cb-private-pool's own package.json has no dependency on `@vitruviansoftware/foundation-vpn-ha`, and a repo-wide grep for "vpn-ha"/"VpnHa" inside pulumi-library returns only the vpn-ha package's own test file. It genuinely is "wired to nothing" downstream.

In the TS port itself, 0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.ts:139 hardcodes `vpnConfiguration: { enableVpn: false }` with no config path to flip it, and grep for onPremPublicIp/routerAsn/tunnel0Bgp/pskSecret etc. across the whole TS port returns nothing — so even the interface itself is missing the fields TF requires (on-prem IPs, PSK secret ref, ASNs, BGP session ranges) beyond the single enableVpn bool. Interestingly the port's root package.json lists `@vitruviansoftware/foundation-vpn-ha` as a workspace dependency, but it's never imported anywhere in port source — an orphaned dependency, exactly matching the reviewer's "wired to nothing" characterization.

All cited evidence lines/behavior check out. This is a genuine, verified gap, not a misnamed/relocated equivalent.

**Verifier note / correction:** Severity as claimed ("minor") is reasonable and not inflated — arguably could be called low/borderline-moderate since the port provides no path at all to configure it (missing required fields, not just an ignored flag), but because TF's own reference deployment also defaults enable_vpn=false (it's an opt-in hybrid-connectivity feature only needed by orgs peering private CB workers to on-prem), "minor" is a fair and defensible rating rather than an overstatement.

#### [TS] Budget currencyCode hardcoded to USD (budget package and project-factory)
_Area: pulumi-library/budget · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's budget resources set only units, inheriting the billing account's currency. Both the TS Budget component and the ProjectFactory inline budget hardcode currencyCode: "USD"; for a non-USD billing account the Billing Budget API rejects the request (currency must match the billing account), failing deployment. Go sets units only, matching TF.

**Evidence:** TF: terraform-google-modules/project-factory budget submodule (specified_amount without currency_code as used via 4-projects/modules/single_project/main.tf budget_* args). Port: pulumi-library/ts/packages/budget/src/index.ts:33; ts/packages/project-factory/src/index.ts:135. Go counterpart: go/pkg/project/project.go:239-243 (units only).

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all fronts. (1) TF side: terraform-google-modules/terraform-google-project-factory's modules/budget/main.tf sets only `amount { specified_amount { units = tostring(var.amount) } }` -- no currency_code field exists in that resource block at all, so google_billing_budget inherits the billing account's currency, exactly as claimed. This module is invoked by 4-projects/modules/single_project/main.tf via budget_amount/budget_alert_* passthrough vars. (2) TS port: pulumi-library/ts/packages/budget/src/index.ts:33 and pulumi-library/ts/packages/project-factory/src/index.ts:135 both hardcode `currencyCode: "USD"` inside the gcp.billing.Budget specifiedAmount block, with no currencyCode field exposed on BudgetArgs/ProjectArgs to override it -- confirmed by grepping 'currency' across the entire ts port, pulumi-library/ts, and pulumi_ts-example-foundation, which shows zero other occurrences. (3) Go port: pulumi-library/go/pkg/project/project.go's createBudget (around lines 239-243) builds `SpecifiedAmount: &billing.BudgetAmountSpecifiedAmountArgs{Units: ...}` with no CurrencyCode field set, matching TF/Go parity, and even carries a code comment "Amount is the budget amount in the billing account's currency (e.g. USD)" acknowledging currency should follow the billing account rather than being fixed. This is a genuine TS-only regression: for any non-USD billing account, the Billing Budget API will reject the create call since the specified currency must match the account's currency, causing budget resource creation (and the deployment) to fail.

**Verifier note / correction:** Not a correction to the claim's existence, but a severity nuance: because a currency mismatch causes an outright API rejection (hard deploy failure) rather than a soft misconfiguration, some reviewers would rate this 'moderate' rather than 'minor'. Still, 'minor' is defensible given the narrow blast radius (only non-USD billing accounts, only when budgetAmount is set) and the trivial one-line fix.

#### [BOTH] _Default sink exclusion for routed-in external logs (exclude_external_logs) not implemented
_Area: pulumi-library/centralized-logging · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF centralized-logging issues a terracurl PUT to add an 'exclude_external_logs' exclusion (-logName : "/<logging-project>/") to the logging project's _Default sink so logs routed in from the org/folders are not double-stored in the _Default bucket. Neither the Go nor the TS component (nor either port stage) implements an equivalent (e.g. gcp.logging.ProjectExclusion or a command resource), so external logs are duplicated into the audit project's _Default bucket, inflating cost and retention scope.

**Evidence:** TF: 1-org/modules/centralized-logging/main.tf (terracurl_request.exclude_external_logs). Checked: grep 'exclude_external_logs|exclusions' across pulumi-library, pulumi_go-example-foundation, pulumi_ts-example-foundation = no hits.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. (1) TF reference: 1-org/modules/centralized-logging/main.tf defines resource "terracurl_request" "exclude_external_logs" (gated on var.project_options != null, which is always set in envs/shared/log_sinks.tf's `logs_export` module call), issuing a PUT to https://logging.googleapis.com/v2/projects/<logging-project>/sinks/_Default?updateMask=exclusions with an exclusion filter `-logName : "/<logging-project>/"`. This is not vestigial — terraform-example-foundation/test/integration/org/org_test.go explicitly asserts the _Default sink's exclusions list is non-empty and matches this exact filter, confirming it's load-bearing, tested behavior. (2) Port side: exhaustively grepped pulumi-library/go, pulumi-library/ts, and both pulumi_go-/pulumi_ts-example-foundation repos for exclusion/ProjectExclusion/_Default/exclude_external/updateMask/terracurl-equivalent (pulumi-command local/remote Command), and any raw REST/curl helper. Read the full Go component (pulumi-library/go/pkg/logging/centralized_logging.go, log_export.go) and TS component (pulumi-library/ts/packages/centralized-logging/src/index.ts) end to end — both implement the ProjectOptions/loggingBucketOptions destination (log bucket, linked dataset, sinks, IAM, and even an 'internal project sink' comment explicitly calling out the audit-project-as-blind-spot problem) but never touch or update the _Default sink's exclusions. Both 1-org stages (Go: logging.go, TS: index.ts) invoke this component with the project-bucket destination always populated, so the TF condition for the exclusion would apply here too. No equivalent test exists in either port's test/ directory (only clean_org.sh, no org_test.go analog). The gap is real and present in both ports via the shared library.

**Verifier note / correction:** None needed on the core finding — severity of 'minor' is reasonably calibrated, not inflated. This is a cost/hygiene gap (duplicate storage of externally-routed logs into the audit project's own _Default bucket), not a security or functional-break issue, and it doesn't block deployment or cause data loss, so 'minor' is an accurate (if anything, slightly generous-to-the-port) severity label rather than overstated.

#### [BOTH] CloudFunction accepts eventTriggerResource but never wires a Pub/Sub topic into the event trigger
_Area: pulumi-library/cloud-functions · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Both language components declare EventTriggerResource/eventTriggerResource but build the FunctionEventTrigger with only EventType + TriggerRegion — no PubsubTopic (or resource) is set. For google.cloud.pubsub.topic.v1.messagePublished (the mirrored event-function use case) the API requires the pubsub topic, so any caller using the event-trigger path gets a create-time failure; the arg is silently dropped. (No port stage currently calls this component — cai-monitoring builds its own function — so impact is latent library-level.)

**Evidence:** Mirrors terraform-google-modules/event-function (per package comment). Ports: pulumi-library/go/pkg/cloud_functions/cloud_functions.go:38,103-108; pulumi-library/ts/packages/cloud-functions/src/index.ts:20,56-61.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF: 1-org/modules/cai-monitoring/main.tf sets event_trigger = { pubsub_topic = module.pubsub_cai_feed.id, event_type = \"google.cloud.pubsub.topic.v1.messagePublished\", ... }, matching the description exactly (mirrors terraform-google-modules/event-function). Port: pulumi-library/go/pkg/cloud_functions/cloud_functions.go declares EventTriggerResource (line 38) but the FunctionEventTriggerArgs construction (lines ~103-108) only sets EventType and TriggerRegion, never PubsubTopic — despite the underlying pulumi-gcp SDK type (cloudfunctionsv2.FunctionEventTriggerArgs) having a PubsubTopic pulumi:\"pubsubTopic\" field made for exactly this. The TS twin (ts/packages/cloud-functions/src/index.ts, eventTriggerResource declared line 20, eventTrigger object built lines 56-61) has the identical bug. Neither component's unit tests even pass the resource field, so the omission was never caught. Grepping both port repos (pulumi_go-example-foundation, pulumi_ts-example-foundation) found no caller of this shared component at all; the library's own cai-monitoring implementation builds cloudfunctionsv2 directly and correctly wires PubsubTopic/pubsubTopic (go/pkg/security/cai_monitoring.go:345, ts/packages/cai-monitoring/src/index.ts:234), confirming the claim's note that impact is currently latent/library-level only. Severity of \"minor\" is accurate, not inflated.

#### [GO] ComputeInstance lacks resource_manager_tags — IAP firewall tags from 4-projects cannot be bound to instances
_Area: pulumi-library/compute-instance · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF env_base passes resource_manager_tags (the iap_firewall_tags exported by 4-projects for the peering project's secure-tag-scoped IAP firewall rules) into vm//compute_instance. Go ComputeInstanceArgs has no such field and the Go port's 5-app-infra uses the component without tags, so the sample VM in the peering project never receives the tag values that the tag-scoped INGRESS allow rules require (IAP SSH/RDP to it is blocked). TS ComputeInstance supports resourceManagerTags via params.

**Evidence:** TF: 5-app-infra/modules/env_base/main.tf (locals env_project_resource_manager_tags = iap_firewall_tags; module compute_instance resource_manager_tags). Port: pulumi-library/go/pkg/compute_instance/compute_instance.go:27-34 (no tags field); pulumi_go-example-foundation/5-app-infra/example_env_base.go:96; go port exports iap_firewall_tags (4-projects/main.go:118) but nothing consumes them. TS counterpart: ts/packages/instance-template/src/index.ts:97,120-122.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed exactly as described: terraform-example-foundation/5-app-infra/modules/env_base/main.tf defines local.env_project_resource_manager_tags mapping the 'sample-peering' project to data.terraform_remote_state.projects_env.outputs.iap_firewall_tags, and passes resource_manager_tags = local.resource_manager_tags into module.compute_instance (terraform-google-modules/vm//modules/compute_instance), which sets params.resource_manager_tags on the underlying google_compute_instance_from_template resource.

On the Go side, pulumi-library/go/pkg/compute_instance/compute_instance.go's ComputeInstanceArgs has no tags-related field at all (Project, Zone, Hostname, InstanceTemplate, NumInstances, DeletionProtection only), and NewComputeInstance never sets Params on the compute.InstanceFromTemplateArgs it builds. pulumi-library/go/pkg/instance_template/instance_template.go likewise has no ResourceManagerTags field. This isn't a provider limitation: the underlying pulumi-gcp Go SDK (v9.20.0) does support it — compute.InstanceTemplateArgs has a top-level ResourceManagerTags field and compute.InstanceFromTemplateArgs has Params.ResourceManagerTags (map[string]string) — so the Go component simply never exposes or wires it, while the TS component (ts/packages/instance-template/src/index.ts, which also houses the ComputeInstance class) explicitly supports resourceManagerTags via params.resourceManagerTags.

In the Go port's 5-app-infra, example_env_base.go's EnvBaseArgs even declares an IAPFirewallTags pulumi.StringMapInput field with a comment 'nil for non-peering projects' — clearly intended to carry this value — but it is never referenced anywhere else in the function; it's dead/unwired, and neither instance_template.NewInstanceTemplate nor compute_instance.NewComputeInstance calls pass it through. A repo-wide grep for resource_manager_tags/ResourceManagerTags/resourceManagerTags in pulumi_go-example-foundation returns nothing except that one dead field declaration. I also checked for any alternative mechanism (e.g., a google_tags_tag_binding-style TagBinding resource attached directly to the VM instance) that might achieve equivalent effect by a different name — TagBinding is used elsewhere in the Go port (1-org/tags.go, 2-environments/env_baseline.go for folder/org-level tag bindings) but never for compute instances in 5-app-infra, and TargetSecureTags appears only on firewall-rule matching (4-projects/peering.go, networking/firewall.go), which governs which tag values a firewall rule matches — it does not tag the instance itself. So the gap is real and matches the claim closely.

One nuance worth flagging: the entire VM/compute-instance code path in the Go port's 5-app-infra (example_env_base.go) is gated behind a //go:build example constraint and is excluded from the default build (main.go's default pulumi.Run only does stack references/config and exports project_id/region; it does not create any compute instance unless a developer removes the build tag or builds with -tags=example). So no default 'pulumi up' in the Go port actually creates an under-tagged VM today — the gap only manifests if/when someone enables the example scaffold as their real app-infra implementation. This reinforces rather than undercuts the reviewer's 'minor' severity call, so I would not call the severity inflated.

#### [GO] InstanceTemplate ignores SourceImageFamily/SourceImageProject and defaults Shielded VM off (TS/upstream default on)
_Area: pulumi-library/instance-template · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Go InstanceTemplateArgs declares SourceImageFamily and SourceImageProject but the disk only ever uses args.SourceImage — family/project-based image selection (the TF module's source_image_family/source_image_project, used with default debian family when source_image is empty) is silently dropped, and an empty SourceImage produces a broken template. EnableShieldedVm defaults to false and the Go port's 5-app-infra never sets it, whereas the TS component unconditionally applies the shielded config (secure boot/vTPM/integrity monitoring) mirroring the upstream vm module default — Go/TS drift leaving Go sample workloads unshielded.

**Evidence:** TF: terraform-google-modules/vm//modules/instance_template (source_image_family/source_image_project vars; shielded defaults), used at 5-app-infra/modules/env_base/main.tf. Port: pulumi-library/go/pkg/instance_template/instance_template.go:32-33 (declared), 82-90 (only SourceImage used), 144-150 (shielded only when flag set); grep of pulumi_go-example-foundation/5-app-infra shows EnableShieldedVm never set. TS counterpart: ts/packages/instance-template/src/index.ts:50,68-72.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified against the real terraform-google-modules/vm instance_template module (v15.1.0, the version pinned by 5-app-infra): its main.tf builds the boot disk image as `source_image != "" ? "${source_image_project}/${source_image}" : "${source_image_project}/${source_image_family}"`, so source_image_family/source_image_project genuinely provide a working fallback path when source_image is empty (confidential_space's TF call exploits exactly this by setting the module's `source_image` field to the family name and `source_image_project` to the image project, yielding a valid `project/family` reference). Reading pulumi-library/go/pkg/instance_template/instance_template.go confirms the claim: SourceImageFamily/SourceImageProject are declared in InstanceTemplateArgs but never read anywhere in NewInstanceTemplate — only args.SourceImage feeds the disk's SourceImage. This is a real, reproducible defect: pulumi_go-example-foundation/5-app-infra/example_confidential_space.go sets SourceImageFamily:"confidential-space"/SourceImageProject:"confidential-space-images" but never SourceImage, so the resulting InstanceTemplateDiskArgs.SourceImage is an empty string — a broken template exactly as claimed. The TS counterpart (ts/packages/instance-template/src/index.ts:50) does honor these fields via `args.sourceImage ?? projects/${project}/global/images/family/${family}`, and the TS 5-app-infra example (business_unit_1/production/index.ts.example) relies on that fallback for both env_base (debian-12/debian-cloud) and confidential_space (confidential-space/confidential-space-images) instances, so this is a genuine Go/TS drift, not a false positive. However, the shielded-VM half of the claim is backwards: the actual upstream vm module variable `enable_shielded_vm` defaults to false (confirmed by fetching the module source at the pinned v15.1.0 tag), and terraform-example-foundation's own env_base module call never sets shielded_instance_config, so upstream's real default is UNSHIELDED, matching the Go port's default-false EnableShieldedVm exactly (Go's confidential_space example explicitly sets EnableShieldedVm:true, mirroring the TF confidential_space module which explicitly sets shielded_instance_config — full parity). It is the TS component that deviates from upstream by unconditionally applying shieldedInstanceConfig on every instance template regardless of any flag, which is the opposite framing of what the claim states.

**Verifier note / correction:** The claim conflates two issues, only one of which is a real gap. (1) Real: source_image_family/source_image_project are dead fields in the Go InstanceTemplate component and this does break the shipped example_confidential_space.go (empty SourceImage) — confirmed. But this only affects the optional example files (example_env_base.go / example_confidential_space.go, both gated behind `//go:build example` and excluded from default compilation), not a default/always-deployed path, and env_base's own example is unaffected because it hardcodes a concrete SourceImage. (2) Not a gap, and stated backwards: upstream terraform-google-modules/vm's enable_shielded_vm defaults to false, and TF's env_base module call never overrides it, so 'unshielded by default' is the correct upstream behavior, which the Go port's default-false EnableShieldedVm (set true only for confidential_space, matching upstream) faithfully mirrors. It is the TS component's unconditional shieldedInstanceConfig that diverges from the upstream default, not Go. Severity should be scoped down to the source_image_family/project dead-parameter issue only, and 'minor' is a reasonable-to-slightly-generous severity for a bug confined to an opt-in, non-default-compiled example file.

#### [GO] Kms ignores PreventDestroy and per-key Algorithm/ProtectionLevel; encrypters/decrypters IAM absent in both languages
_Area: pulumi-library/kms · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Go KmsArgs.PreventDestroy is never translated into pulumi.Protect (TS honors it, default true; TF kms module has prevent_destroy) and KeyConfig.Algorithm/ProtectionLevel are declared but never used — no VersionTemplate is set on the CryptoKey, so HSM keys cannot be created and the fields are silently dropped. Additionally, neither Go nor TS supports the encrypters/set_encrypters_for/decrypters/set_decrypters_for IAM args that TF base_env passes (both ports compensate at stage level with a combined cryptoKeyEncrypterDecrypter member, so only the library surface is deficient).

**Evidence:** TF: 4-projects/modules/base_env/example_storage_cmek.tf:22-34 (key_rotation_period, encrypters/decrypters, prevent_destroy). Port: pulumi-library/go/pkg/kms/kms.go:32-40 (fields), 79-90 (CryptoKeyArgs without VersionTemplate/Protect). TS counterpart honors protect + version template: ts/packages/kms/src/index.ts:35-43. Stage compensation: pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/cmek.go:78-80; pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/*/index.ts:369-372.

**Verifier reasoning:** All parts of the claim check out against the actual code.

1) TF side confirmed: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/4-projects/modules/base_env/example_storage_cmek.tf (lines ~20-33) calls the terraform-google-modules/kms/google module with key_rotation_period, encrypters/set_encrypters_for, decrypters/set_decrypters_for, and prevent_destroy = "false".

2) Go library (/home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/kms/kms.go): KmsArgs.PreventDestroy (line 32) and KeyConfig.Algorithm/ProtectionLevel (lines 38,40) are declared but never referenced anywhere else in the file - grep confirms zero other occurrences. The CryptoKeyArgs built at lines 79-84 only sets Name/KeyRing/Purpose/RotationPeriod; there is no VersionTemplate and no pulumi.Protect(...) call or resource option using PreventDestroy. HSM keys (which require VersionTemplate.ProtectionLevel=HSM) cannot be created through this component, and PreventDestroy is silently dropped. The component's own README.md doesn't even document these two fields, corroborating they're vestigial.

3) TS library (/home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/kms/src/index.ts) does honor both: `protect: args.preventDestroy ?? true` (line 43) and a `versionTemplate: { algorithm, protectionLevel }` block (lines 39-42) - confirming the described Go/TS asymmetry.

4) Neither library (go nor ts kms package) has any IAM/encrypter/decrypter code at all - grep for CryptoKeyIAM/IAMBinding/IAMMember/Encrypter/Decrypter in both packages returns nothing.

5) Stage-level compensation confirmed in both ports: pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/cmek.go creates a raw gcp/kms.CryptoKeyIAMMember with role "roles/cloudkms.cryptoKeyEncrypterDecrypter" (lines 78-83); pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/{production,development,nonproduction}/index.ts do the same at line ~369-372.

One nuance worth flagging: neither port's stage code actually instantiates the shared-library Kms component at all (no `kms.NewKms(` call anywhere in the go port; no `new Kms(` anywhere in the ts port) - every stage that touches KMS calls the raw gcp.kms SDK directly instead of the pulumi-library component. The library's kms.NewKms/Kms class is exercised only by its own unit tests. So the missing PreventDestroy/Algorithm/ProtectionLevel/IAM wiring is a real completeness gap in the shared library's public API surface, but it has no live effect on either port's actual deployed stacks today since the deficient code path isn't reachable from any real pipeline - it would only bite a future consumer who tries to use the shared component directly (e.g. for HSM keys or a non-default prevent-destroy).

**Verifier note / correction:** Confirmed accurate as described; only add that the affected library component (kms.NewKms in Go, Kms class in TS) is not actually instantiated by any stage in either port today - both ports bypass it and call the raw gcp.kms SDK directly - so the practical/deployment impact is effectively nil right now and the issue is purely a shared-library API-completeness gap, consistent with (not less than) the claimed 'minor' severity.

#### [TS] FirewallRules silently drops sourceServiceAccounts/targetServiceAccounts
_Area: pulumi-library/network · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The FirewallRules class (in the network package, mirroring network//firewall-rules) declares sourceServiceAccounts and targetServiceAccounts in FirewallRuleConfig but never passes them to gcp.compute.Firewall — only sourceTags/targetTags are wired. Upstream firewall-rules supports source/target service accounts; a caller supplying them gets a rule that applies to all instances instead of the intended SA-scoped set, with no error.

**Evidence:** TF: terraform-google-modules/network//modules/firewall-rules (source_service_accounts/target_service_accounts), used at 0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/network.tf. Port: pulumi-library/ts/packages/network/src/index.ts:127-129 (declared) vs 146-168 (not passed). Go counterpart supports TargetServiceAccounts: go/pkg/networking/firewall.go:69.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF side: 0-bootstrap/modules/cb-private-pool/network.tf:91-107 calls terraform-google-modules/network//modules/firewall-rules with source_service_accounts/target_service_accounts keys in the rule object (both set to null in this particular call, but the module and fields genuinely exist and are supported upstream). Port side: pulumi-library/ts/packages/network/src/index.ts defines FirewallRuleConfig with sourceServiceAccounts (line 127) and targetServiceAccounts (line 129), but the FirewallRules constructor (lines 141-173) only forwards sourceTags/targetTags into gcp.compute.Firewall — sourceServiceAccounts/targetServiceAccounts are read nowhere in the resource args. I grepped the entire monorepo (both ports, and pulumi-library/go and pulumi-library/ts) for sourceServiceAccounts/targetServiceAccounts/source_service_accounts/target_service_accounts and found no alternate wiring anywhere for this specific module. The only real caller today is pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool/src/index.ts (the FirewallRules invocation for the cb-private-pool VPC ingress rule), which — matching upstream — never actually populates sourceServiceAccounts/targetServiceAccounts, so there is no live functional regression in shipped configuration today; the bug is a latent trap for any future/other caller of this component. One nuance in the reviewer's evidence: the cited 'Go counterpart' (go/pkg/networking/firewall.go:69, with TargetServiceAccounts wired at lines 224-225/380-381) is not actually a Go port of the same firewall-rules module — it's NewNetworkFirewallPolicy, the Go equivalent of the separate network-firewall-policy/hierarchical-firewall-policy TF modules (policy-rule resources with a match block), which TS mirrors separately in ts/packages/network-firewall-policy and ts/packages/hierarchical-firewall-policy (both of which DO wire targetServiceAccounts correctly). There is no Go equivalent of the plain firewall-rules component at all in pulumi-library/go. That's a citation imprecision, not a refutation — it actually strengthens the case that omitting the field in ts/packages/network was an oversight, since the codebase demonstrably knows how to wire this exact field elsewhere. The claim, including its 'minor' severity rating, holds as stated.

**Verifier note / correction:** Minor evidence nit only, does not change the verdict: the cited 'Go counterpart' at go/pkg/networking/firewall.go:69 is not a Go port of the firewall-rules module — it belongs to the separate NetworkFirewallPolicy/hierarchical-firewall-policy component family, and there is no Go pulumi-library equivalent of the plain firewall-rules module at all. The core finding (TS FirewallRules silently drops the two fields) is confirmed directly from the TS source and the TF reference regardless.

#### [GO] HierarchicalFirewallPolicy rules are hardcoded — the TF module's data-driven rules variable cannot be expressed
_Area: pulumi-library/networking · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF 3-networks/modules/hierarchical_firewall_policy takes an arbitrary rules map from the stage (envs/shared/hierarchical_firewall.tf). The Go component hardcodes exactly the five foundation rules (500/510 goto_next RFC1918, 5000 IAP SSH/RDP, 5100 Windows KMS, 5200 LB health checks) with no way to add, remove, or modify rules (only a single EnableLogging toggle across the allow rules). Matches today's foundation defaults but cannot express custom hierarchical rules. TS HierarchicalFirewallPolicy is fully data-driven like TF.

**Evidence:** TF: 3-networks-svpc/envs/shared/hierarchical_firewall.tf:33-98 (rules passed as data). Port: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/hierarchical_firewall.go:26-32 (no rules arg), 74-210 (hardcoded rules). TS counterpart: ts/packages/hierarchical-firewall-policy/src/index.ts:9-27.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed: 3-networks-svpc/envs/shared/hierarchical_firewall.tf:17-106 passes a fully data-driven `rules` map (5 entries) into modules/hierarchical_firewall_policy, with only firewall_policies_enable_logging as an external variable. Go port confirmed hardcoded: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/hierarchical_firewall.go's HierarchicalFirewallPolicyArgs has no Rules field (only ParentID, ShortName, Description, Associations, EnableLogging), and the constructor inlines exactly 5 NewFirewallPolicyRule calls (r1..r5) matching the TF defaults verbatim (priorities 500/510/5000/5100/5200, same CIDRs/ports), with EnableLogging only threaded into the three 'allow' rules. Both Go stage call sites (3-networks-svpc/main.go:41, 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go:52) confirm only ParentID/ShortName/Description/Associations/EnableLogging are passed — no rules override possible. I searched pulumi-library/go exhaustively for an alternative implementation: there IS a separate data-driven component, network_firewall_policy/network_firewall_policy.go, with a Rules []FirewallRule field — but its doc comment and resource type (gcp.compute.NetworkFirewallPolicy) show it mirrors a different TF module (terraform-google-modules/network//modules/network-firewall-policy, the VPC-level firewall policy), not the org/folder-level hierarchical_firewall_policy module (which uses compute.FirewallPolicy + FirewallPolicyAssociation, matching the hardcoded component's resource types). That sibling component does not cover this gap. The TS counterpart (ts/packages/hierarchical-firewall-policy/src/index.ts) is indeed fully data-driven (accepts args.rules: HierarchicalFirewallRuleConfig[]), confirming the Go/TS asymmetry described in the claim.

**Verifier note / correction:** None needed — the claimed severity of 'minor' is accurate and arguably even generous: this is a genuine, unambiguous functional gap (loss of configurability for what is a data-driven TF variable) rather than a cosmetic issue, but since the hardcoded rules exactly match today's foundation defaults, no currently-deployed environment's actual firewall behavior differs from TF today — the impact is purely on future extensibility/customization.

#### [GO] Subnet VPC flow-logs configuration hardcoded — vpc_flow_logs interval/sampling/metadata/metadata_fields/filter not expressible
_Area: pulumi-library/networking · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF 3-networks base_env passes var.vpc_flow_logs.{aggregation_interval,flow_sampling,metadata,metadata_fields,filter_expr} per subnet to the network module. Go SubnetArgs has only FlowLogs bool and hardcodes INTERVAL_5_SEC / 0.5 / INCLUDE_ALL_METADATA with no metadataFields/filterExpr, and the Go port passes only FlowLogs:true, so per-environment flow-log tuning present in TF cannot be expressed. TS Network supports the full set (subnetFlowLogsInterval/Sampling/Metadata/MetadataFields/Filter).

**Evidence:** TF: 3-networks-svpc/modules/base_env/main.tf:216-221 (subnet_flow_logs_* from var.vpc_flow_logs). Port: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/networking.go:32-40,111-117; pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/main.go:94,104 (FlowLogs: true only). TS counterpart: ts/packages/network/src/index.ts:16-20,86-92.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

TF side: terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/base_env/main.tf:216-234 passes subnet_flow_logs_interval/sampling/metadata/metadata_fields/filter from var.vpc_flow_logs (defined in modules/base_env/variables.tf:95-110 and envs/shared/variables.tf, an object type with optional fields defaulting to aggregation_interval="INTERVAL_5_SEC", flow_sampling="0.5", metadata="INCLUDE_ALL_METADATA", metadata_fields=[], filter_expr="true") into every subnet in both base_net and restricted_net. This is a real, genuinely per-environment-overridable TF variable, not dead code.

Go port side: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/networking.go defines SubnetArgs with only `FlowLogs bool` (line 39) and, when true, hardcodes AggregationInterval="INTERVAL_5_SEC", FlowSampling=0.5, Metadata="INCLUDE_ALL_METADATA" (lines 111-117) with no metadataFields or filter fields at all. Grepped the entire pulumi-library/go tree and pulumi_go-example-foundation (all stages, including 3-networks-hub-and-spoke and 4-projects/peering.go) for FlowLog, flow_log, metadata_fields, filter_expr, aggregation_interval, flow_sampling — the only hits are `FlowLogs: true` call sites; no alternate name, config field, or stage-level override exists anywhere that plumbs these values through. Confirmed the TS library counterpart (pulumi-library/ts/packages/network/src/index.ts:15-20,86-91) does expose the full set (subnetFlowLogsInterval/Sampling/Metadata/MetadataFields/Filter) with matching defaults, so this is a Go-only gap, exactly as claimed.

On severity: the hardcoded Go values happen to equal the TF defaults exactly (INTERVAL_5_SEC / 0.5 / INCLUDE_ALL_METADATA), so out-of-the-box behavior is identical between TF and the Go port. The gap only manifests if a user wants to override these (e.g., set CUSTOM_METADATA with specific metadata_fields, tune sampling rate, or add a CEL filter_expr) — which the reference repo's envs/shared/variables.tf and base_env module explicitly support as a supported customization point. This is a real, correctly-scoped configurability gap, not a cosmetic or already-covered feature. "Minor" severity is a fair, non-inflated characterization since it's an unexercised customization knob rather than a broken default behavior.

#### [TS] custom_placement_config not supported (library or TS port)
_Area: pulumi-library/simple-bucket · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF 4-projects example_storage_cmek passes custom_placement_config (var.gcs_custom_placement_config, dual-region placement) to cloud-storage//simple_bucket. Neither SimpleBucket/CloudStorage in TS nor the TS port stages support customPlacementConfig; the Go port compensates inline (4-projects/cmek.go sets CustomPlacementConfig), leaving this a TS-side gap. (Also noted: Go's SimpleBucket lacks the storageClass/lifecycleRules/retentionPolicy options that TS exposes, though no active Go-port caller needs them.)

Checked: grep 'customPlacement' — only hit is pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/cmek.go:115; no support in pulumi-library/ts/packages/simple-bucket/src/index.ts or cloud-storage/src/index.ts, none in ts port. Go library SimpleBucket options: go/pkg/storage/storage.go:34-49.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified both halves of the claim. (1) TF side is real: terraform-example-foundation/4-projects/modules/base_env/example_storage_cmek.tf:53 passes custom_placement_config = var.gcs_custom_placement_config into the simple_bucket module, and business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production}/main.tf all set gcs_custom_placement_config to a dual-region data_locations list (uppercased default_region/default_region_2), so this is an actively-exercised feature, not dead code. (2) Port side: pulumi-library/ts/packages/simple-bucket/src/index.ts and cloud-storage/src/index.ts both define SimpleBucketArgs/CloudStorageArgs interfaces with no customPlacementConfig/dataLocations field anywhere (confirmed via full-repo grep for customPlacement, custom_placement, dataLocations, data_locations across pulumi-library -- zero hits). The TS port's own CMEK implementation (4-projects/business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts, around line 376) doesn't even route through the shared library components for this bucket -- it calls gcp.storage.Bucket directly -- and the BucketArgs literal there has no customPlacementConfig, so a dual-region placement is silently dropped in all three TS environments. By contrast pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/cmek.go:113-118 explicitly sets bucketArgs.CustomPlacementConfig when cfg.GCSPlacementRegions has length 2, so the Go port does compensate inline as the reviewer stated. I also confirmed the cmek bucket's output is not consumed anywhere downstream (no references to cmek_bucket/cmekBucket outside the stage itself, no 5-app-infra usage), so this is purely an illustrative example resource whose region-placement fidelity is cosmetic relative to upstream's demo -- consistent with the claimed 'minor' severity, not underrated. The secondary note about Go's SimpleBucket (go/pkg/storage/storage.go) lacking storageClass/lifecycleRules/retentionPolicy that TS's SimpleBucket exposes is also accurate, and correctly caveated as not currently needed by any Go-port caller.

_Area: test-fixtures · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides.

Port side: exhaustively grepped both /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation and /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation, plus /home/user/pulumi-library (go/ and ts/, which contains only reusable infra components like project, iam, bootstrap, cicd — no test-harness/CI-account code). Searches for "ci-account", "ci_account", "ci-foundation", "test_folder", "int_test" returned zero hits anywhere. test/setup_e2e.sh in both ports does only Pulumi.*.yaml.example config templating (org id, billing account, domain, group emails) — no google_service_account/gcp.serviceaccount.Account resource, no org/billing IAM bindings, no folder/project provisioning is defined as code anywhere in the ports. The only artifact referencing the required CI identity is docs/GITHUB_ADMIN.md, which is a manual, non-code instruction ("Create a dedicated Google Cloud Service Account...") listing only 5 roles (folderAdmin, projectCreator, billing.admin, orgpolicy.policyAdmin, iam.organizationRoleAdmin) — materially fewer and different from TF's 14 org roles + owner + billing.admin + logging.configWriter, and it documents manual creation rather than defining it as Pulumi code/manifest. Confirmed GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT is consumed as a pre-existing secret in .github/workflows/e2e.yml with no provisioning code upstream.

Also verified the deletion-policy-override sub-claim: project_deletion_policy config exists in the ports (default "PREVENT", e.g. 0-bootstrap/config_test.go asserts default "PREVENT"; 4-projects, 2-environments, 1-org all read config.get("project_deletion_policy") || "PREVENT"), but grepping the Pulumi.*.yaml.example files, setup_e2e.sh, e2e.yml workflow, and helpers/foundation-deployer/*.sh shows none of them ever sets it (or bucket_force_destroy) to a destructive value for E2E runs — confirming the ports never inject the "make teardown succeed" overrides that TF's test/setup outputs provide automatically.

Severity: the reviewer's own "minor" label looks appropriately calibrated, not inflated. This is test/CI scaffolding, not production foundation code — it doesn't stop someone from deploying the actual foundation stages, only from having a fully-as-code, reproducible E2E test harness bootstrap. If anything one could argue for "minor-to-moderate" since the incomplete/manual role list (5 vs 14+ roles, missing billing-account bindings) could cause confusing permission-denied failures partway through an E2E run, and the missing DELETE-policy injection could cause teardown/destroy failures in CI — but calling it "minor" rather than "major/critical" is fair since it's confined to test tooling.

#### [BOTH] No idempotency / no-drift verification (TF's DefaultVerify plan-check has no port equivalent)
_Area: test-integration · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF integration tests call DefaultVerify after apply, re-planning the stage and asserting Terraform reports no changes (org_test.go, envs_test.go, projects_shared_test.go), which catches non-idempotent resources and perpetual-diff bugs. Neither port has any equivalent: nothing runs `pulumi preview --expect-no-changes` after deployment — not validate_e2e.sh, not deploy.sh, not the e2e.yml workflow. A component producing perpetual diffs (a common porting bug) would go undetected.

**Evidence:** TF: DefaultVerify calls at /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/test/integration/org/org_test.go:109, envs/envs_test.go:71, projects-shared/projects_shared_test.go:84. Ports: grep for 'expect-no-changes' / preview-after-up in /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/{test,helpers,.github} and TS equivalents finds nothing; deploy.sh runs a single preview or up action.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on both sides. TF side: org_test.go:109 (`org.DefaultVerify(assert)` inside DefineVerify), envs_test.go:71, and projects_shared_test.go:84 all call `<blueprint>.DefaultVerify(assert)` with the comment "perform default verification ensuring Terraform reports no additional changes on an applied blueprint" — this is the tft blueprint-test library's built-in re-plan/no-diff assertion run after DefaultApply, i.e. a genuine idempotency/no-drift check baked into the test suite.

Port side: exhaustive grep across pulumi_go-example-foundation, pulumi_ts-example-foundation, and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/ subdirs) for "expect-no-changes", "DefaultVerify", "drift", "idempoten", "no-drift", and automation-API preview patterns (.Preview(), ExpectNoChanges, ResourceChanges/changeSummary in Go; .preview(), expectNoChanges in TS) returned zero hits except one unrelated code comment in pulumi-library/go/pkg/project/project.go about SA creation being idempotent (not a test/check). Inspected helpers/foundation-deployer/deploy.sh in both ports: it runs exactly one action per stack per invocation — either `pulumi preview` or `pulumi up --yes --skip-preview` — never both in sequence to check for a clean second plan. Inspected .github/workflows/e2e.yml in both ports: pipeline is deploy -> validate -> teardown, where "validate" (test/validate_e2e.sh) only asserts resource *existence* via gcloud queries (folders/projects/buckets present via filter/count), never re-runs pulumi preview/up to detect a non-empty diff after apply. The *_test.go files under 0-bootstrap..5-app-infra and the TS *.config.test.ts files are pure unit tests for config-loading logic, not integration/idempotency tests. So the claimed gap is real and the cited evidence is accurate.

On severity: the claim's "minor" label is reasonable and I would not inflate it, but I also wouldn't call it purely cosmetic — TF's DefaultVerify is an active CI assertion that fails the test run on any perpetual diff, and the ports have no equivalent even as a manual/optional step, so a non-idempotent component (a realistic porting bug — e.g. a field left unset that GCP returns a default for, or ordering-sensitive list diffs) would go completely undetected by port CI. That's a genuine testing-coverage gap, but it doesn't affect deployed infrastructure correctness directly, which supports keeping it at "minor" rather than upgrading to major/critical. No correction needed to the claim's substance or severity.

#### [BOTH] clean_org.sh does not clean org-level singletons (ACM policy, SCC notifications, tag keys) that TF's clean_org.sh removes
_Area: test-teardown · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's test/clean_org.sh explicitly deletes the SCC notification and the org's Access Context Manager policy — org-wide singletons that would otherwise collide on the next test run (ACM policy is one-per-org; SCC notification names and tag key short-names are org-unique). The ports' clean_org.sh destroys Pulumi stacks, then sweeps orphaned prj-* projects and fldr-* folders, but never deletes SCC notifications, ACM policies/perimeters, or the org-wide tag keys created by 1-org — if a stack destroy partially fails (the very case the sweep exists for), these leak and block subsequent E2E deploys.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/test/clean_org.sh (gcloud scc notifications delete; gcloud access-context-manager policies delete). Ports: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/test/clean_org.sh and /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/test/clean_org.sh (stacks + projects + folders only; no scc/ACM/tag-key handling; tag keys created in /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/tags.go).

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/test/clean_org.sh runs `gcloud scc notifications delete test-scc-notification --organization ...` and looks up + deletes the org's `gcloud access-context-manager policies` entry, as a defensive cleanup pass distinct from whatever terraform destroy does in 1-org.

Both ports' test/clean_org.sh (identical in go and ts, both read in full) only (1) destroy/remove Pulumi stacks per stage, (2) sweep orphaned prj-* projects (lien removal in go, billing-unlink in ts), and (3) sweep fldr-* folders. No gcloud call or other logic in either script touches SCC notifications, ACM policies, or tag keys.

Searched exhaustively for an equivalent elsewhere: grepped both port repos (.sh/.go/.ts/.md) and /home/user/pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) for scc-notification / access-context-manager / accesscontextmanager / tag-key delete patterns. The ACM AccessPolicy, SCC V2OrganizationNotificationConfig, and TagKey are all created as ordinary Pulumi-managed resources inside 1-org (go: main.go/policies.go/scc.go/tags.go; ts: index.ts), so a *successful* `pulumi destroy` of 1-org would tear them down as a side effect — but nothing in clean_org.sh, helpers/foundation-deployer/deploy.sh, build/*, or scripts/* provides a gcloud-level fallback sweep for when that destroy is partial/fails, which is exactly the failure mode the project/folder sweep steps exist to guard against. Confirmed the resources use static, org-unique names (SCC notification config id defaults to "scc-notify" in both go and ts; tag key shortName is the literal "environment"; ACM policy is a hard one-per-org GCP singleton), so a partial-destroy leak would indeed collide with and block the next E2E run, matching the claim's mechanism.

One correction to the claim's framing: it implies TF's clean_org.sh also cleans tag keys in parallel, but the actual TF script (as quoted in the evidence) does NOT delete tag keys either — only SCC notification and ACM policy. So the tag-key portion of the port gap is a real, independently-identified additional gap, not something mirrored by an equivalent TF line. This doesn't change the verdict (the core SCC-notification/ACM-policy gap is fully verified and real) but the claim overstates TF/port symmetry on that specific point.

**Verifier note / correction:** The claim is essentially correct: both ports' clean_org.sh lack any SCC-notification, ACM-policy, or tag-key cleanup, verified against both port repos and the shared pulumi-library with no equivalent found anywhere. Minor correction: TF's own clean_org.sh does not clean up tag keys either — only SCC notification and ACM policy — so the tag-key portion is an extra gap by analogy, not something TF's script itself parallels. Severity of 'minor' may slightly understate impact: since the ACM policy is a strict one-per-org singleton, a leak from the exact partial-destroy scenario the sweep exists to handle would block all subsequent E2E runs until manually cleaned via a one-off gcloud call (clean_org.sh's `stack rm --force` discards the Pulumi state pointer regardless of whether destroy actually succeeded) — arguably "moderate" — but since the fix is a simple manual gcloud call with no lasting infrastructure damage, "minor" is a defensible judgment call rather than a clear inflation.

The Go README even has an explicit (self-contradictory, since the file exists) note stating Jenkins was not ported. The TS README does not mention Jenkins at all. The only docs touching Jenkins are comparison-table rows in docs/FAQ.md and docs/ERRATA.md that list Jenkins under the Terraform column while showing the Pulumi column as GitHub Actions (or GitHub Actions plus GitLab) -- these docs disclaim rather than advertise Jenkins support.

**Verifier note / correction:** Real finding, but the framing is inflated: the ports do not actively advertise Jenkins as a supported option (both READMEs' CI/CD tables omit it, and the Go README explicitly disclaims it as unported) -- the actual issue is an orphaned, inert build_jenkins example file (never wired into main.go, no pulumi-library component) that upstream deleted on 2026-06-18 but that predates the port's last sync on 2026-05-06. The 4-projects null_resource.jenkins_cicd and tf-wrapper.sh JENKINS-env items cited in the evidence do not apply, since neither port ever had those TF-specific artifacts. Severity "info" is appropriately calibrated (stale example code, not a functional or security issue) and should stay info rather than be escalated.

#### [BOTH] Provisioned CMEK state backend unused by default pipelines (PULUMI_BACKEND_URL secret dangles)
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Both ports provision a PULUMI_BACKEND_URL GitHub secret per stage repo pointing at the KMS-encrypted GCS state buckets created in 0-bootstrap (build_github_actions comments claim pipelines work "out of the box"), but the shipped pipeline templates (build/pulumi-preview.yml, build/pulumi-up.yml) authenticate to Pulumi Cloud via PULUMI_ACCESS_TOKEN and never read PULUMI_BACKEND_URL or run pulumi login. TF stores all stage state in the CMEK-protected seed bucket; in the ports that control is effectively bypassed by default and the provisioned secret is dead configuration.

**Evidence:** pulumi_go-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.go lines 104-172 and pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_github_actions.ts lines 219-266 (secret provisioning) vs both repos' build/pulumi-up.yml and build/pulumi-preview.yml (PULUMI_ACCESS_TOKEN only). TF: seed_bootstrap gcs_bucket_tfstate + backend.tf.example.

**Verifier reasoning:** Claim verified in full. TF side: 0-bootstrap/main.tf sets encrypt_gcs_bucket_tfstate=true with a CMEK state_bucket_kms_key, every stage carries a backend.tf, and TF's GitHub workflows consume secrets.TF_BACKEND and sed it into backend files, so all TF CI state lives in the CMEK-protected seed bucket. Port side: both ports provision a PULUMI_BACKEND_URL GitHub Actions secret per stage repo (go build_github_actions.go:164-171, ts build_github_actions.ts:260-265) pointing at the KMS-encrypted state buckets (library Bootstrap defaults EncryptStateBucket=true; ts index.ts sets encryptStateBucket:true; go projects bucket uses DefaultKmsKeyName), with comments claiming the templates work "out of the box". Yet both ports' build/pulumi-preview.yml and build/pulumi-up.yml authenticate via PULUMI_ACCESS_TOKEN only — no secrets.PULUMI_BACKEND_URL reference, no cloud-url input on pulumi/actions@v5, no pulumi login step — and GitHub secrets are not auto-injected into the job environment, so the secret is dead configuration and state defaults to Pulumi Cloud. Exhaustive grep of both ports and pulumi-library (go and ts) for PULUMI_BACKEND_URL / pulumi login / cloud-url found no consumer anywhere on the GitHub Actions path; gitlab-ci.yml also uses PULUMI_ACCESS_TOKEN. Only the alternative Cloud Build path (README-CloudBuild.md: pulumi login gs://${_TF_BACKEND}) uses the GCS backend, and the go port's docs/ERRATA.md acknowledges Pulumi Cloud is the default — which softens but does not refute the claim. Claimed severity "info" is not inflated; arguably conservative since a CMEK/in-boundary state control from TF is bypassed by default.

#### [TS] TS build_cb.ts.example header promises triggers/workspaces it does not create
_Area: 0-bootstrap · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The file's doc comment says it 'Creates the CI/CD project, Cloud Source Repos, Cloud Build triggers, private worker pool, artifact registry, and per-stage workspaces', but the implementation creates only the project, APIs, one AR repo, and the private pool — no repos (a comment notes CSR deprecation), no triggers, no workspaces. Trigger creation lives only in the separate build_cloud_build.ts.example, whose own header in turn claims a 'Private Worker Pool' (item 5) that it never creates. Users following either file get a partial Cloud Build setup that doesn't match its stated contents.

**Evidence:** pulumi_ts-example-foundation/0-bootstrap/build_cb.ts.example lines 1-5 (header) vs body lines 26-120 (no trigger resources); build_cloud_build.ts.example lines 20-27 ('WHAT THIS CREATES ... 5. Private Worker Pool') vs body (no CbPrivatePool/WorkerPool). TF: build_cb.tf creates all of source+builder+workspaces+pool.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified against the actual files. TF side confirmed: build_cb.tf does create everything the claim says (CSR repos via tf_source, triggers via tf_cloud_builder + tf_workspace, private pool via tf_private_pool, per-stage workspaces via tf_workspace for_each with dedicated log/artifact GCS buckets). On the TS port side: build_cb.ts.example's header (lines 1-5) claims it creates CSR, Cloud Build triggers, and per-stage workspaces, but the body (lines 26-120) only creates the project, API enablement, one Artifact Registry repo, and the CbPrivatePool (a real, working library component from pulumi-library/ts/packages/cb-private-pool) -- no sourcerepo.Repository, no cloudbuild.Trigger, no workspace-equivalent buckets. Separately, build_cloud_build.ts.example's header lists 5 items including 'Private Worker Pool' but its body (through line 162) never instantiates CbPrivatePool or any worker-pool resource -- confirmed by grep, nothing worker-pool related appears in that file. Cross-checking pulumi-library (both go/ and ts/) and the Go port for an equivalent: the Go port's analogous build_cloud_build.go.example has an accurate header (only claims Artifact Registry + triggers, which is exactly what its body delivers via the shared libcicd.NewCloudBuild component in pulumi-library/go/pkg/cicd/cloudbuild.go), and that shared component also does not create a private worker pool -- so 'workspaces' with per-stage log/artifact buckets genuinely does not exist anywhere in either port or the shared library under any name. So the claim is accurate: it is specifically two badly-synced doc headers in the TS port's optional/example files, and the 'per-stage workspace' bucket functionality really is absent everywhere.

**Verifier note / correction:** Minor nuance: the claim's framing ('no repos... no triggers... no workspaces') is slightly stronger than reality for the pair of files taken together -- build_cloud_build.ts.example DOES create Cloud Source Repos (gcp.sourcerepo.Repository, lines 100-105) and per-stage plan/apply Cloud Build triggers (lines 110-145), so those specific capabilities do exist in the port, just not in build_cb.ts.example as its header implies. The claim itself already acknowledges this split ('Trigger creation lives only in the separate build_cloud_build.ts.example'), so this is not a hidden gap on the claim's part, just worth flagging as already-correctly-scoped. The genuinely fully-absent piece is the TF 'per-stage workspace' bundle (dedicated log-bucket + artifacts-bucket per stage), which is missing from both example files and from the shared library. Given both affected files are unused-by-default, explicitly-labeled '.example' opt-in alternates to the port's actual default CI/CD provider (build_github_actions.ts, which is complete and not implicated here), the reviewer's own 'info' severity rating is appropriate and should not be raised -- this is a documentation/header-accuracy defect in optional scaffolding, not a break in the port's default critical path.

#### [TS] Extra boolean org policy enforced that upstream does not set (compute.disableGuestAttributesAccess)
_Area: 1-org/org_policy · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** The TS port enforces compute.disableGuestAttributesAccess in its boolean policy list; this constraint is not part of the Terraform 1-org baseline (14 constraints) nor the Go port. Behavior drift between ports/upstream: guest attributes access is blocked org-wide only in TS deployments, which can break workloads that legitimately use guest attributes (e.g., some GKE/agent flows).

**Evidence:** TF list: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/org_policy.tf:27-42 (14 constraints, no disableGuestAttributesAccess). TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:63. Go matches TF: policies.go:46-65.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified all three legs of the claim. (1) TF baseline: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/org_policy.tf:27-42 lists exactly 14 boolean constraints with no compute.disableGuestAttributesAccess; upstream deliberately removed this constraint in v4.0 (docs/upgrading_to_v4.0.md:11 and CHANGELOG.md:85, issue #1019). (2) TS port: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/1-org/index.ts:63 enforces "compute.disableGuestAttributesAccess": true as an extra 15th boolean org policy applied org-wide (lines 81-87) — despite a comment claiming it matches the Go list. (3) Go port matches TF: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/policies.go:48-64 has the same 14 constraints, and grep for disableGuestAttributesAccess across the entire Go port and /home/user/pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) returns zero hits. This is real behavior drift: only TS deployments block guest attributes access org-wide, a constraint upstream removed specifically because it broke legitimate workloads. Severity "info" is honest (extra hardening, not a missing control), though "low" would also be defensible given it can actively break GKE/agent flows that use guest attributes.

#### [GO] shared_vpc_projects export lacks project numbers
_Area: 1-org/outputs · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform's shared_vpc_projects output exposes, per environment, both shared_vpc_project_id and shared_vpc_project_number (3-networks consumes the number for VPC-SC/service-networking). The Go port exports shared_vpc_projects as env → project-id string only; project numbers of the environment network projects are discarded in deployOrgProjects. The Go 3-networks stage currently sources these via config rather than the stack output, so nothing breaks in-family, but the cross-stage contract is thinner than TF and TS (TS exports {project_id, project_number}).

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/1-org/envs/shared/outputs.tf:127-130 with modules/network/outputs.tf:17-25; consumed in 3-networks-svpc/modules/base_env/remote.tf:18-19. Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/1-org/main.go:151-156 and projects.go:302-334 (number discarded). TS: index.ts:526-528.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified in full. TF exports shared_vpc_projects as env -> {shared_vpc_project_id, shared_vpc_project_number} (1-org/envs/shared/outputs.tf:127-130 with modules/network/outputs.tf:17-25), and 3-networks-svpc/modules/base_env/remote.tf:19 consumes the number. The Go port exports shared_vpc_projects as env -> project-ID string only (1-org/main.go:151-156); the per-env network project number is discarded at 1-org/projects.go:305 (`netProjectID, _, err := createProject(...)` where createProject returns (id, number, error)) and is not exported under any alternative name (only interconnect_project_number and net_hub_project_number exist). pulumi-library (go and ts) has no shared_vpc component that could supply this. Go 3-networks stages source VPC-SC project numbers via the vpc_sc_projects config value (3-networks-svpc/main.go:267,365; 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go:382) rather than the stack output, so nothing breaks in-family — but the cross-stage output contract is thinner than TF and the TS port, which exports {project_id, project_number} per env (1-org/index.ts:526-528). The claimed severity of info is accurate: in the Go port no stage consumes the shared_vpc_projects output at all, making it a pure parity/contract gap with no functional impact.

#### [GO] All three environments deployed in a single stack (no per-environment isolation)
_Area: 2-environments · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF (and the TS port) keep development/nonproduction/production as separate root configurations/stacks, enabling the branch-based per-environment promotion workflow of the foundation CI/CD. The Go port deploys all three environments from one program/stack in a single update, so production cannot be planned/applied independently of development/nonproduction without --target surgery. Outputs are env-prefixed (e.g. development_env_folder) and are consumed consistently by the Go 4-projects stage, so nothing is broken, but the deployment-unit granularity differs from upstream and from the TS port.

**Evidence:** TF: 2-environments/envs/{development,nonproduction,production}/ separate roots. Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/2-environments/main.go lines 52-67 (loop over all envs in one stack). TS parity: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/2-environments/envs/* per-env Pulumi projects.

**Verifier reasoning:** All cited facts verified. TF keeps 2-environments as three separate roots (envs/{development,nonproduction,production}, each instantiating env_baseline for one env), and the TS port mirrors this with three separate Pulumi projects under 2-environments/envs/. The Go port is a single Pulumi project (foundation-2-environments) whose main.go (lines 52-67) hardcodes a loop over all three environments in one stack; there is no env-selection mechanism (no ctx.Stack()-based switching, no env/environment config key in loadEnvConfig), and the single Pulumi.production.yaml.example names the deployment stack, not an environment. A main.go comment explicitly acknowledges "Go deploys all envs in one program." pulumi-library contains no env-baseline component that could provide per-env granularity. Outputs are env-prefixed and consumed consistently by Go 4-projects (main.go:81 uses %s_env_kms_project_id), so nothing is functionally broken — the gap is deployment-unit granularity vs upstream's per-environment promotion model. Claimed severity of info is honest and appropriate.

#### [BOTH] time_sleep 60s destroy-time delay between folder and dependents not replicated
_Area: 2-environments · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF inserts time_sleep.wait_60_seconds (destroy_duration="60s") between the env folder and its dependents (tag binding, KMS/Secrets projects) to absorb GCP eventual consistency during teardown. Neither port replicates any destroy-time delay; both rely purely on dependency-graph ordering (the Go port documents this decision in a comment). Create-time behavior is identical (TF's sleep has no create_duration); only folder destruction may transiently fail. Low impact given folder deletion protection and PREVENT project deletion policy defaults.

**Evidence:** TF: 2-environments/modules/env_baseline/folders.tf time_sleep.wait_60_seconds. Go: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/2-environments/env_baseline.go lines 108-114 (comment, no delay resource). TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/2-environments/modules/env_baseline.ts lines 56-57 (comment only).

**Verifier reasoning:** The claim is accurate in every particular. TF's 2-environments/modules/env_baseline/folders.tf:27-31 defines time_sleep.wait_60_seconds with destroy_duration="60s" (no create_duration), and all three dependents chain through it: google_tags_tag_binding.folder_env (folders.tf:40), the KMS project (kms.tf:34), and the Secrets project (secrets.tf:34). The Go port (/home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/2-environments/env_baseline.go:108-114, also line 152) explicitly documents in comments that the destroy-time delay is not replicated and relies on Pulumi's dependency graph; the TS port (/home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/2-environments/modules/env_baseline.ts:56-57) has a comment only. Exhaustive greps of both ports and /home/user/pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) for time_sleep, Sleep, delay, destroy_duration, pulumiverse time provider, CustomTimeouts, and retry mechanisms found no equivalent under any name in 2-environments or in the shared library components (ProjectFactory etc.). Notably, both ports DO use the pulumiverse time.Sleep provider elsewhere (Go 5-app-infra/example_confidential_space.go:108; TS 5-app-infra index.ts.example files), proving the capability was available and the omission in 2-environments was a deliberate choice, exactly as the Go comment states. The claimed severity of "info" is honest and not inflated: create-time behavior is identical (TF's sleep only acts at destroy), the effect is limited to possible transient folder-deletion failures during teardown due to GCP eventual consistency, teardown is rare given default folder deletion protection and PREVENT project deletion policy, and a destroy retry recovers.

#### [GO] Hierarchical firewall association fallback attaches policy to the entire organization
_Area: 3-networks-hub-and-spoke/hierarchical-firewall · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Terraform parents the policy under the common folder and associates it with exactly six folders derived from remote state (bootstrap, common, network, dev, nonprod, prod). The Go port takes associations from config but silently falls back to [parent_id] — whose own default is the placeholder organizations/123456789012 — attaching the policy org-wide when unconfigured, a broader scope than TF ever uses.

**Evidence:** TF: envs/shared/hierarchical_firewall.tf (parent = local.common_folder_name, 6 associations from remote.tf). Port: pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go lines 52-58 and 525-533 (ParentID default, FirewallAssociations fallback to parent).

**Verifier reasoning:** The claim is verified on both sides. Terraform side: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/envs/shared/hierarchical_firewall.tf parents the policy under local.common_folder_name (line 20) and associates it with exactly six folders (lines 22-29), all resolved from remote state in envs/shared/remote.tf lines 29-34 (bootstrap, common, network, development, nonproduction, production folders). Go port side: /home/user/pulumi_go-example-foundation/3-networks-hub-and-spoke/main.go lines 52-58 create the policy with ParentID straight from config and Associations from cfg.FirewallAssociations; line 525-527 defaults ParentID to the placeholder "organizations/123456789012"; lines 531-533 fall back to []string{c.ParentID} when firewall_associations is unset. The shared library component (/home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/hierarchical_firewall.go) applies whatever associations it is given verbatim and derives nothing from remote state. I searched for any compensating mechanism and found none: the only StackReference in the stack (main.go line 404) fetches only access_context_manager_policy_id; no folder outputs are consumed; firewall_associations appears nowhere in the port's example configs, README, docs, helpers, or scripts (the README even documents parent_id as required with value "organizations/YOUR_ORG_ID" and calls it the "Parent scope for firewall policies", so the documented workflow parents AND associates the policy at the organization node). There is also no Pulumi.shared.yaml.example even though the hierarchical firewall only deploys when env == "shared". The TS port is correctly excluded from the claim: it derives targetFolders from the org stack reference (network_folder_name), so it does not have the org-wide fallback. One nuance: the literal placeholder org "organizations/123456789012" would fail at deploy time (no such org/no permission), so the practical failure mode is the documented path where parent_id is set to the real org and firewall_associations is left unset, silently attaching the policy org-wide instead of to the six foundation folders. The claimed severity of info is honest, if anything slightly understated, since this changes actual firewall attachment scope (extra allow rules for IAP/health-check/KMS ranges apply to resources outside the foundation folders), but it does not overstate the issue.

_Area: 3-networks-svpc/envs · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**Verifier note / correction:** Claim is accurate; two refinements: the defect affects development and nonproduction identically, not just the cited production env; and the claimed 'info' severity is if anything understated rather than inflated — 'low' is defensible since the shipped example config fails the documented first `pulumi up` in three envs, though the error is immediate, clearly messaged, and trivially worked around with a dummy value.
