The file /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/modules/single_project.ts (106 lines, distinct from the unused directory-form index.ts) is a real, used module — all four business_unit_1 stacks import deploySingleProject from it — and it carries the module-level parity points the claim says to look for elsewhere: pipeline sa_roles IAMMember loop, SharedVPCServiceProject attachment, billingbudgets API dedup, TF-matching labels, and budget wiring. Only base_env-level orchestration is inline in the TS stack entrypoints. For the Go port the claim is fully accurate.

#### [BOTH] Custom-route exchange flags diverge from TF network-peering defaults and between ports
_Area: 4-projects/peering · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's network-peering module defaults to not exporting custom routes in either direction. Go sets local export=false/import=true and host export=true/import=false; TS sets exportCustomRoutes=true on BOTH directions (so the spoke exports its custom routes into the host). Routing behavior differs across TF/Go/TS.

**Evidence:** TF: modules/base_env/example_peering_project.tf module "peering" (terraform-google-modules network-peering ~>18.0 defaults). Ports: pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/peering.go:92-113; pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/development/index.ts:147-161.

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified against upstream terraform-google-network v18.0.0: the network-peering module defaults export_local_custom_routes=false and export_peer_custom_routes=false, and derives each side's import flag from the other's export flag, so TF's example_peering_project.tf (which sets neither variable, confirmed by repo-wide grep) yields export=false/import=false in BOTH directions — no custom-route exchange. The Go port (4-projects/peering.go:92-109) sets spoke export=false/import=true and host export=true/import=false, which functionally propagates the Shared VPC host's custom routes into the peered spoke — a real routing behavior change vs TF. The TS port (business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts:147-161, identical in all three envs) sets export=true/import=false on both directions. The shared pulumi-library contains a faithful network-peering component mirroring the TF module (go/pkg/network_peering and ts/packages/network-peering) whose defaults would match TF, but neither port's 4-projects uses it — both call gcp.compute.NetworkPeering directly with the hardcoded divergent flags. No alternative code path corrects this. The divergence from TF defaults and between the two ports is real; severity 'info' is not inflated (arguably understated for Go, where the route import is a live behavioral change).

**Verifier note / correction:** Minor refinement to the claim's TS characterization: because importCustomRoutes=false on both TS peering directions, the spoke's exported custom routes are never actually imported by the host (export without a matching import on the peer is a no-op). So TS is functionally equivalent to TF's default (no custom-route exchange) despite the divergent flags, while the Go port is the one with a real functional difference: host custom routes propagate into the spoke (host export=true + spoke import=true).

#### [BOTH] Netblock data sources replaced by hardcoded CIDR literals; legacy health-checker note
_Area: 4-projects/peering · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF resolves IAP forwarder and (legacy+current) health-checker ranges at plan time via google_netblock_ip_ranges. Both ports hardcode 35.235.240.0/20 for IAP and 35.191.0.0/16, 130.211.0.0/22, 209.85.152.0/22, 209.85.204.0/22 for the optional LB rule. The values match currently published ranges but will not track Google range changes.

**Evidence:** TF: modules/base_env/example_peering_project.tf data google_netblock_ip_ranges (legacy_health_checkers, health_checkers, iap_forwarders). Ports: pulumi_go-example-foundation/4-projects/peering.go (buildPeeringFirewallRules literals); pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/development/index.ts:245,256,283.

**Verifier reasoning:** Both halves of the claim verify. TF: example_peering_project.tf declares google_netblock_ip_ranges data sources for legacy_health_checkers/health_checkers/iap_forwarders (lines 21-31) and uses them at lines 181, 199, 237. Ports: Go peering.go (lines 257, 276, 312-317) and TS business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts (lines 245, 256, 283) hardcode 35.235.240.0/20 and the four LB/health-check ranges. Exhaustive greps of both ports plus pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) for NetblockIpRanges, range-type strings, gstatic ipranges URLs, and cloud-netblocks found no dynamic equivalent anywhere. The literals exactly match the deduplicated union of what the TF data sources return, so rendered firewall rules are identical today; the residual gap is purely maintenance-tracking, which justifies the claimed 'info' severity — no inflation.

**Verifier note / correction:** The claim's mechanism framing is overstated: for range types iap-forwarders, health-checkers, and legacy-health-checkers, terraform-provider-google's netblock data source returns constants hardcoded in the provider binary (verified in data_source_google_netblock_ip_ranges.go) — only cloud-netblocks/google-netblocks are fetched live from gstatic.com. TF therefore does not track Google range changes at plan time either; it picks them up only on a provider version upgrade. The real difference is merely where the constants live: provider dependency (TF, auto-updates on provider bump) vs. port source code (Pulumi, needs a manual edit). Also, TF's concat emits 35.191.0.0/16 twice while the ports dedupe it — cosmetic only. Severity 'info' is accurate.

#### [BOTH] vpc_flow_logs options not configurable (interval/sampling/metadata_fields/filter_expr fixed)
_Area: 4-projects/peering · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF exposes var.vpc_flow_logs (aggregation_interval, flow_sampling, metadata, metadata_fields, filter_expr) applied to the peered subnet. Go's networking library hardcodes INTERVAL_5_SEC / 0.5 / INCLUDE_ALL_METADATA when FlowLogs=true (no metadata_fields/filter_expr support); TS hardcodes the same values inline. Values match TF defaults, but the TF-supported configuration surface is absent.

**Evidence:** TF: modules/base_env/variables.tf vpc_flow_logs + example_peering_project.tf subnet_flow_logs_*. Ports: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/networking.go:111-115; pulumi_ts-example-foundation/4-projects/business_unit_1/development/index.ts:129-133.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side verified: 4-projects/modules/base_env/variables.tf:56-72 defines vpc_flow_logs (5 fields) and example_peering_project.tf:93-98 applies them to the peered subnet. Port side verified: Go library networking.go:111-117 hardcodes INTERVAL_5_SEC/0.5/INCLUDE_ALL_METADATA with no SubnetArgs fields for any flow-log option (no alternate subnet path exists in the Go port), and TS 4-projects business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production}/index.ts:129-133 hardcodes the same logConfig inline. So the typed configuration surface is genuinely absent at the 4-projects/peering boundary in both ports. However, two strong mitigations: TF's own variable is never exposed at the env root (no tfvars/root variable — TF always deploys these exact defaults, so effective user-facing configurability is the same: a code edit either way), and the TS shared library already supports the full surface, so only the inline call site lacks it. Deployed resources are identical in all implementations. The claim survives as an info-level input-surface parity note only.

**Verifier note / correction:** The gap is real but even narrower than stated. (1) TF's vpc_flow_logs variable is itself unreachable from the user-facing configuration surface: no 4-projects env root (business_unit_1/*/main.tf) passes it and no root variable or tfvars entry exposes it, so TF always deploys the same fixed defaults the ports hardcode — changing it requires a code edit in TF too. (2) For TS, the shared library component (pulumi-library/ts/packages/network/src/index.ts:15-20, 86-92) fully supports interval/sampling/metadata/metadataFields/filterExpr; only the 4-projects peering code creates the subnet inline without using it. The gap for TS is 'existing library capability not wired at this call site', not a missing capability. (3) Deployed infrastructure is byte-identical: metadata_fields=[] and filter_expr="true" are GCP API defaults. Info severity is correct — this is a code-level parity note about an internal module input that TF itself never wires up, with zero behavioral difference.

#### [TS] TF-sensitive outputs (instance names/zones/details) exported without secret wrapping
_Area: 5-app-infra · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF marks instances_names, instances_zones, instances_details, confidential_instances_names and confidential_instances_zones as sensitive outputs. The TS example exports the equivalents as plain (non-secret) stack outputs, so values TF hides from console/state consumers are visible in `pulumi stack output` without --show-secrets. Pulumi's equivalent (pulumi.secret) is not used.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/5-app-infra/business_unit_1/*/outputs.tf (sensitive = true). TS: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/5-app-infra/business_unit_1/*/index.ts.example lines 179-196 (no pulumi.secret). Go exports nothing comparable (see critical gap).

**Verifier reasoning:** Verified on both sides. TF: all three outputs.tf files under 5-app-infra/business_unit_1/{development,nonproduction,production} mark instances_names, instances_zones, instances_details, confidential_instances_names, and confidential_instances_zones with sensitive = true. TS: all three corresponding index.ts.example files return these exact fields as plain pulumi.Output values built via pulumi.all(...)/.apply(...) with no pulumi.secret() call anywhere. Exhaustive grep for pulumi.secret/asSecret/secretOutput/ToSecret across the entire pulumi_ts-example-foundation repo and the entire pulumi-library repo (both go/ and ts/ subtrees) found zero matches relevant to this code path -- the only 'secret' hits are an unrelated .gitignore comment, GCP Secret Manager usage in vpc-service-controls/vpn-ha components, and GitHub Actions secret resources in 0-bootstrap. No alternative wrapper or upstream component supplies secret-marking for these fields before they are returned from the stack. The gap is real and reproducible: `pulumi stack output` (without --show-secrets) would show these values in plaintext in the TS port, whereas `terraform output` masks them by default in TF.

**Verifier note / correction:** No correction needed to severity -- 'info' is an honest, non-inflated rating here. The values in question (VM instance names, zones, self-links) are non-sensitive infrastructure metadata, not credentials or secrets; TF's sensitive=true flag itself only suppresses CLI/console echo (it does not encrypt these particular values in state, since TF state is plaintext regardless), so pulumi.secret would provide comparable console-masking behavior but the underlying risk of omitting it is genuinely low. The claim is accurately scoped as an informational parity/hygiene gap, not a security-critical one.

#### [GO] Go pulumi-library also has no DNS-hub component (asymmetric with TS's dns-hub package); needs a deeper pass to confirm inline parity
_Area: library-parity · verifier confidence: high · confirmed (completeness-critic)_

**What's missing:** TS pulumi-library has a dedicated dns-hub package (/home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/dns-hub) used (imperfectly, per confirmed gap) for the hub project's forwarding/peering DNS zones. Go's pulumi-library has no equivalent package (only cloud_dns, a lower-level primitive, not a hub-specific abstraction). This asymmetry wasn't called out anywhere in the library-parity notes, which enumerated dns-hub only against TS. It appears Go implements hub DNS forwarding directly in the 3-networks-hub-and-spoke stage without a shared abstraction (functionally present per the confirmed-gaps list, which only flags TS as missing hub DNS forwarding), so this is lower severity than the VPN case, but the library-parity dimension should re-verify Go's inline implementation has full parity with TF's dns-hub module behavior (record sets, forwarding target servers, etc.) rather than assuming symmetry.

**Evidence:** /home/user/pulumi-library/go/pkg listing has no dns-hub/dns_hub package; /home/user/pulumi-library/ts/packages/dns-hub exists. Not independently re-verified whether Go's inline hub DNS implementation fully replicates TF's forwarding zone + records; flagged as an area needing a deeper pass rather than a fully confirmed defect.

**Verifier reasoning:** Found by the completeness-critic during final review; not run through the per-claim verifier.

#### [BOTH] No equivalent of the 81-template extensibility catalog
_Area: policy-library · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** TF's policy-library ships ~81 constraint templates (policies/templates/*.yaml) plus lib/*.rego helpers so adopters can enable additional controls (CMEK, resource locations, VPC-SC, IAM key age/type, naming/labels patterns, etc.) by adding a small constraint YAML — the README explicitly directs users to add more constraints from this catalog. The ports offer no comparable ready-made catalog; extending coverage requires writing new CrossGuard TypeScript policies from scratch. Not blocking, but a meaningful loss of the upstream library's extensibility surface.

**Evidence:** TF: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/policies/templates/ (81 files) and lib/{constraints,util,util_test}.rego; README.md:305-309 documents extension workflow. Ports: /home/user/pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts is the entire policy surface; Go port has none.

**Verifier reasoning:** TF side confirmed exactly as described: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/policy-library/policies/templates/ contains 81 constraint-template YAML files, lib/ contains constraints.rego, util.rego, util_test.rego, and the top-level README.md (lines 305-309) explicitly tells adopters to "add more constraints from the samples folder" to extend coverage beyond the 28 constraints actually enabled by default (policies/constraints/ has 28 files, a subset of the 81 templates) — confirming this is a genuine extensibility catalog, not just docs for the enabled set.

Searched both ports and pulumi-library (go/ and ts/) exhaustively for any equivalent: grepped for "template", "constraint", "rego", "catalog", CMEK, VPC-SC, key age, naming/label-pattern keywords across all three repos. Findings:
- pulumi_ts-example-foundation/policy-library/index.ts implements only 7 CrossGuard policies (no-default-network, project-labels-required, no-sa-key-creation, no-public-access, no-public-ip, require-private-google-access, require-flow-logs); its own README table lists 9 (adds deletion-policy-prevent, restrict-owner-role) — a minor doc/code drift, but still nowhere near a catalog.
- pulumi_go-example-foundation/policy-library/ contains only a README that defers entirely to the TS pack (Go has no CrossGuard policies of its own since the Pulumi policy SDK is TS-only).
- pulumi-library/go/pkg/policy and ts/packages/org-policy provide a generic OrgPolicy component wrapping GCP's native Organization Policy API (v2) — a real, useful, and different mechanism (preventive, applied at deploy time in Stage 1-org via 1-org/policies.go / org_policy.tf equivalent). This maps to TF's separate org_policy.tf, not to the policy-library/ OPA constraint-template catalog. It is a curated list of ~8 "common constraints" in its README, not an 81-item extensible template library, and doesn't provide the same detective/plan-validation model (gcloud beta terraform vet / pulumi preview --policy-pack architecture) that the 81 templates support.
- No rego files, no "templates" directory, no per-service constraint catalog (CMEK, VPC-SC allowed regions, IAM key age/type, resource-location, naming/label patterns, etc.) exists anywhere in either port or the shared library.

Conclusion: the claim is accurate on both the TF-side evidence and the port-side absence. Extending policy coverage in the ports genuinely requires hand-writing new TypeScript CrossGuard policy code from scratch; there is no ready-made catalog to draw from. The claimed severity of "info" (not blocking) is itself appropriately conservative and not inflated — I would not lower or raise it.

#### [TS] CloudRouter missing NAT, keepalive interval, and encrypted-interconnect options present in Go
_Area: pulumi-library/cloud-router · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** Go's CloudRouter supports EnableNat with static external NAT addresses (MANUAL_ONLY, matching TF nat.tf's google_compute_address + MANUAL_ONLY pattern), BGP KeepaliveInterval, and EncryptedInterconnectRouter. TS CloudRouter exposes only name/network/region/bgp{asn, advertisements}. The TS port builds NAT inline in shared_vpc.ts but with AUTO_ONLY allocation and no static addresses (TF uses MANUAL_ONLY with reserved external IPs), so the library gap is only partially compensated.

**Evidence:** TF: 3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc/nat.tf (MANUAL_ONLY + google_compute_address). Ports: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cloud-router/src/index.ts:4-14; go/pkg/networking/router.go:30-42,107-144; ts stage compensation pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc.ts (natIpAllocateOption AUTO_ONLY).

**Verifier reasoning:** Every element of the claim checks out on direct inspection.

1. TF side confirmed: /home/user/terraform-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc/nat.tf creates google_compute_router (with bgp.asn = var.nat_bgp_asn), google_compute_address (reserved static external IPs, count = var.nat_num_addresses_region{1,2}), and google_compute_router_nat with nat_ip_allocate_option = "MANUAL_ONLY" and nat_ips pointing at the reserved addresses, for both default_region1 and default_region2. This matches the claim exactly. (No keepalive_interval or encrypted_interconnect_router usage exists anywhere in the TF repo — those two are just extra capabilities the Go component happens to expose, not things TF's nat.tf itself demonstrates.)

2. Go library confirmed: pulumi-library/go/pkg/networking/router.go:30-42 RouterArgs has KeepaliveInterval, EncryptedInterconnectRouter, EnableNat, NatNumAddresses (line numbers match exactly); lines ~93-144 wire EncryptedInterconnectRouter into compute.NewRouter, KeepaliveInterval into bgpArgs, and build compute.Address + compute.RouterNat with MANUAL_ONLY when NatNumAddresses>0. A real Go stage (3-networks-svpc/main.go:230-237) calls NewCloudRouter with EnableNat:true and NatNumAddresses:cfg.NatNumAddresses, i.e. this isn't dead code.

3. TS library confirmed deficient: pulumi-library/ts/packages/cloud-router/src/index.ts exposes only project/name/network/region/bgp{asn, advertisedGroups, advertisedIpRanges} — no NAT, no keepaliveInterval, no encryptedInterconnectRouter field at all. No other package in pulumi-library/ts (searched all ~29 packages) implements RouterNat or reserved compute.Address either.

4. TS stage compensation confirmed partial/divergent: pulumi_ts-example-foundation/3-networks-svpc/modules/shared_vpc.ts:225-262 builds the NAT router/NAT inline via raw gcp.compute.Router/RouterNat, but uses natIpAllocateOption: "AUTO_ONLY" with no nat_ips and no compute.Address resources at all (not even ephemeral placeholders) — diverging from TF's MANUAL_ONLY + reserved-IP pattern. Additionally (beyond what the claim even states) the TS NAT router never sets a bgp block or wires natBgpAsn/nat_bgp_asn through config at all (grep found zero hits), a further divergence from TF's `bgp { asn = var.nat_bgp_asn }` on the NAT router.

Net: this is a genuine, verifiable library gap in ts/cloud-router with only partial compile-level compensation in the stage, exactly as described. The claimed "info" severity is not inflated — if anything it's conservative, since AUTO_ONLY vs MANUAL_ONLY is a real infrastructure behavioral difference (dynamic vs static egress IPs, which downstream firewall allowlisting in enterprise landing zones often depends on), and the TS stage also drops the NAT BGP ASN entirely. I would not call the severity inflated; no correction to the gap's substance is needed.

#### [TS] GoogleGroup lacks owners/managers/members memberships and group types that Go and the upstream module support
_Area: pulumi-library/google-group · verifier confidence: high · confirmed_

**What's missing:** terraform-google-group supports owners/managers/members membership creation and a types variable (security/dynamic/external labels). Go's group component implements all of these; TS creates only the bare group with the discussion_forum label. The foundation's own group usage (0-bootstrap/groups.tf) passes neither, so no active-stage breakage — pure Go/TS library feature drift that surfaces only if a consumer needs memberships or security-typed groups.

**Evidence:** TF: 0-bootstrap/groups.tf:35-57 (current usage); terraform-google-modules/group supports owners/managers/members/types. Ports: pulumi-library/go/pkg/group/group.go:50-75,140-185 vs pulumi-library/ts/packages/google-group/src/index.ts:9-46.

**Verifier reasoning:** Confirmed on all fronts. (1) TF side: 0-bootstrap/groups.tf:35-57 does invoke terraform-google-modules/group/google (a well-known public registry module) for required/optional groups; that module's documented interface exposes owners/managers/members membership lists and a group `types` variable (security/dynamic/external/default label mapping) that this foundation simply doesn't exercise. (2) Go port: pulumi-library/go/pkg/group/group.go fully implements this — GroupArgs has Types, Owners, Managers, Members fields; NewGroup builds label maps from Types and creates cloudidentity.NewGroupMembership resources with OWNER+MEMBER, MANAGER+MEMBER, and MEMBER-only roles respectively. (3) TS port: pulumi-library/ts/packages/google-group/src/index.ts's GoogleGroupArgs has only id/displayName/description/customerId/initialGroupConfig — no types, no owners/managers/members — and the component hardcodes the single discussion_forum label with zero GroupMembership resource creation. A repo-wide grep for 'GroupMembership' across pulumi_ts-example-foundation and pulumi-library/ts returns nothing, confirming there is no equivalent implemented under any other name/file/package. (4) Consumer wiring: both go's groups.go and ts's groups.ts (0-bootstrap) call their respective group constructors passing only id/displayName/description/initialGroupConfig/customerId — neither passes types nor memberships, confirming the claim's own statement that there is no active-stage breakage.

**Verifier note / correction:** None needed to the core claim; severity of 'info' is appropriate and, if anything, is already conservative/accurate — the gap is real but dormant (no current call site needs it), so info/low severity is the right call rather than an inflation.
